# Cascade v3.2: a clean redeploy with root-cause fixes

Status: revision 11 (2026-11-01): revision 10's reviewed design (nine review rounds, the ninth
clean) with the code layout of section 8.1, approved by the founder; the changelogs are at the end.
Implementation is under way on branch `v3.2`. Builds on the v3 contracts as built in `contracts/`
(v3-launch, live since 2026-10-30 with the founder's money only) and on the collected scope of two
pen-test rounds, two external reviews and the economics analysis (`v3.2-scope.md`, 2026-10-31). The
PoCs it answers are on `pentest/vault` (deae7a2), `pentest/frontdoor` (4c7334e), the oracle branch
(4c2b8be) and the bridge branch (1129269).

Deployed on mainnet as `v3.2-launch` on 2026-11-16 (the addresses are in `SECURITY.md`); it replaced
v3-launch, which is empty and is being retired.

Revision 5 cut scope on the owner's delegated decision: the newest machinery of revisions 3 and 4
(exit-limit floors as a live predicate, a heartbeat, a standby adapter pair) gave way to hard bounds
in the setters. Revision 6 folded in the clean fifth review's Low and Info items and two founder
decisions: a 2-of-3 Safe as owner and a guardian on its own device are strong recommendations, not
gates; the founder keeps one Ledger for both keys for now, and section 5 states what that leaves.
Revision 7 added one mechanism after the sixth review: an owner-signed stamp (`stampBySig`) that any
account can relay, so a copied single-Ledger seed stays a stalemate even when the thief sweeps the
keys' gas. Revision 8 folds in the clean seventh review and simplifies the stamp: owner only,
effective as of its signing time.

The contracts are immutable and the front door's vault address is immutable, so v3.2 is a full
redeploy of all six contracts plus four linked libraries and a read-only lens (section 8.1). The
founder's position is one 0.215 TAO deposit still in the old front door's open batch on Base; the
founder decided on 2026-10-31 to cancel it today, so the migration is section 7's path A and the old
vault retires empty.

## Founder decisions (2026-10-31)

The answers to section 10's open questions. Where this list and a recommendation elsewhere in the
spec differ, this list wins.

**Approved as recommended:**

- One scheduler replacing the three registries (question 1).
- solc ≥ 0.8.38 with `evm_version = cancun` and `via_ir` (question 2), with Sourcify and taostats
  verification for that version confirmed before the first deployment.
- A 48-hour parameter delay (question 3).
- A 14-day park timeout, the refund's fee paid by whoever calls (question 4).
- Global outflow at 20% at launch, sharing the 10 TAO floor, hard-bounded to [20%, 50%] (question 5).
- The hard bounds (question 6): outflow ≥ 20% per door and global with its floor in [10, 100] TAO;
  `maxFeeAbs` in [0.01, 0.015] TAO; `maxEntryValue` ≥ 10 TAO; `payoutDelay` ≤ 48 h; the front door's
  `batchInterval` ≤ 24 h and `minDeposit` ≥ 0.03 TAO.
- No E5 (question 7).
- The front door's pool liquidity floors at half the 30-day median, measured in the fork rehearsal
  (ruled 2026-11-13: a quarter of the median for TAO/WETH, half the lowest for WETH/USDC; Appendix B),
  plus a one-time look for a Chainlink TAO/USD feed on Base (question 8).
- Reward vesting in [1, 30] days, default 7, snapshotted per claim (question 10).
- Launch caps unchanged: 40 TAO, 10 TAO batches, the LAUNCH limits (question 13).
- `MIN_REDEEM_VALUE` 0.03 TAO and `MAX_FEE_ABS` 0.015 TAO (question 14).
- The owner from the constructor, every launch value a constructor argument, addresses by plain
  `CREATE` from fresh single-use keys, runtime-code read-backs, no hand-off (question 15).
- The staker's buy floor at 5% of the oracle's value, at every size (question 16).
- The front door's `pause` stopping deposits and USDC sales only, never an exit (question 17).
- Reopen items cancellable by the owner only (question 19).

**The treasury stays `0x34D1f7DC8Cb62280d0bc617b784Bd2c4668809f6`** (question 9): account 3 on the
same Ledger seed as the owner and the guardian, the founder's choice for simplicity, against the
recommendation of a separate seed. The residual is revenue only: a copied seed also takes protocol
revenue (the cut's shares, staker fees, the swept pot, adapter surplus), never user principal
(section 5).

**The Safe and the guardian's own device are recommendations, not requirements** (question 18):
when they happen, it is before `depositCap` or `maxTotalAssets` rise above the founder level.

**The standby root validator** (question 11): confirmed, Yuma (a DCG company), root uid 20, hotkey
`5DXdHixxtCvoa6GHKs2Jgrdzc61882Ftx1zN2sYFQuwgL1S1`
(`0x40c47b6a294686fe9612b5d78dd369f4c77fa3bbf96a4286ede97410ba2e3209`), 9% take. It holds about 10%
of the stake, as diversification. It is listed through the normal paths (`addHotkey`, and the
48-hour scheduled `setStakeHotkey` and `moveStake`), never hard-coded; its take, root stake and uid
are re-read live before deploy, and the monitor watches its take and root stake beside the
primary's.

**The upstream root-hold report** (question 12): drafted, to be filed by the founder.

**The keys today:** owner `0x478965abDe80ef000e0f3e60008B43ac46130bFC` (Ledger account 1), guardian
`0xf996A598B75ab25B140bC50989C76eBB56B0187F` (Ledger account 2), keeper
`0xBE2a34FE20C3006E759537922C807c228707DC23` (a hot key). v3.2 keeps the owner and the guardian; its keeper is a fresh
key (the founder's decision of 2026-11-07), given to the deploy plan as `OPS_V32_KEEPER_ADDRESS`, never `0xBE2a34…`.

**The code layout** (2026-11-01): one vault with three linked libraries (the batch path, the unstake
walk, the payout engine), a read-only lens and a minimal share token, approved (section 8.1). With
it the DELEGATECALL posture changes from "the vault's only DELEGATECALL is to `CascadePayoutMath`"
to "the vault's only DELEGATECALLs are to its fixed, linked libraries (`CascadePayoutMath`, L1, L2,
L3), whose addresses are baked into its bytecode at deploy" (I-32).

## 0. Summary

Five mechanisms, each the root cause of a family of findings, not a patch per symptom:

1. **A stake ledger, and a price that never counts what it cannot see.** The vault keeps
   `bookedStakeRao`, the root stake it put there and measured back, on the stake side only. The
   price counts `min(visible, booked)`: stake that vanished from the listed hotkeys (a validator's
   hotkey swap) stops minting at once, and stake that appeared from outside (a gift) counts only
   after an explicit, vesting adoption. One guard, `stakeMissing()`, closes every minting path and
   every burn at a price a user did not agree to; `redeemDirect` stays open to a leaver who opts in
   explicitly, at the `min(visible, booked)` price, which can underpay the leaver and never the
   stayers. The way back is permissionless: anyone may list a hotkey that holds at least the chain's
   minimum of the vault's stake. Stake under an unlisted hotkey is measured with a precompile read
   that exists live, `getTotalColdkeyStakeOnSubnet(coldkey, 0)`.
2. **The adapter parks what it cannot deliver, and refunds what it cannot park forever.** A batch
   whose conversion or vault delivery fails is not left in CCIP `FAILURE` and not reverted: the
   Bittensor adapter keeps the tokens (conversion failed, or the amount is below the chain's floor)
   or the native TAO (the vault refused), records the batch, and anyone can deliver it later; after
   `PARK_TIMEOUT` anyone can send it back to Base as a refund credited to each depositor. Every
   park path is checks-effects-interactions under one reentrancy lock. ForeverMoney's migration is
   not made safe by parking alone: token parks must be converted during the notice window.
3. **Bounded tolerances where the chain rounds.** The adapter accepts a conversion at least
   `amount` (never exactly), covers a shortfall of at most 1 rao per conversion from a native
   reserve inbound and a token cushion outbound, never below what it owes to parked batches, and
   sweeps surplus to the treasury (permissionless, no owner). Every exit pays its own unstake cost,
   what the chain keeps of the stake its walk takes, never the holders who stay and never the
   reserves (the founder's ruling, 2026-11-06). The fee ceiling is bounded on both sides.
4. **One owner mechanism for anything that loosens safety, hard bounds on everything that could
   freeze an exit, and a small exits machine.** A scheduler in `CascadeBase` replaces the three
   bespoke registries and gates every owner setter that loosens a limit or redirects value, plus
   `transferOwnership`; tightening stays immediate. The exit limits can no longer be set below the
   launch values at all (hard bounds), so no setter is a freeze instrument. The instruments that
   remain (pause, removing the launch chain's door, disabling the primary bridge, a launch-door
   payout cancel) are recorded per actor with one clock each, and nothing that opens a door is
   scheduled or executed except from an open vault that stayed open, save a Reopen (a bridge that
   was active before, or a door the owner removed), which restores only what was trusted; a guardian
   replacement needs only the owner's instruments open. Four states, two clocks, five scenario rows
   (section 3.6). **An owner-signed stamp (`stampBySig`), relayed by any account, stamps the owner's
   clock as of its signing time without the key's gas**: it kills every item pending at that time,
   so a copied single-Ledger seed stays a stalemate against a thief who sweeps the keys' gas
   (without it, a copy is as bad as a theft). What the vault cannot see (a Base-side freeze by the
   same single key) is a stated residual; **a 2-of-3 Safe as owner on both chains is strongly
   recommended before significant non-founder money** (it prevents a copy and cannot be installed
   after one), and until then the answer is the monitor's email on every scheduled item and the
   founder's signed stamp within its delay (sections 5, 7, 9).
5. **Exit limits that scale to a second front door.** A global daily outflow window beside the
   per-door one; share origin by chain; `closeBatch(expectedId, minValue)`; the batch fee pot's
   surplus rolls forward and is swept to an immutable treasury; a cancelled payout batch never
   hands a bounced deposit to the holders and never holds the honest entries hostage to one; the
   Base front door accepts a payout from any bridge it has ever enabled, so a refund never depends
   on a bridge staying enabled.

Plus: oracle pool-depth views with a front-door liquidity floor on deliveries and a cardinality
precondition, a constant oracle floor on the staker's buy leg, an owner-set gas cap and an idle
buffer for the root-unstake hold, caps checked against the adapters' own caps, reward vesting
owner-settable up to 30 days and snapshotted per claim.

The v3.1 vault had 11 bytes of headroom, and a measured prototype of every v3.2 feature comes to
31,035 B against EIP-170's 24,576. The approved layout (section 8.1) keeps every entry point, all
storage, the scheduler, the exits machine and the stamp in the vault, runs the batch path, the
unstake walk and the payout engine in fixed linked libraries, and adds a read-only lens and a
minimal share token: 21,401 B measured, 1,675 B under the project's gate, on solc 0.8.38 with the IR
pipeline. **The vault's only DELEGATECALLs are to its fixed, linked libraries (`CascadePayoutMath`,
L1, L2, L3), whose addresses are baked into its bytecode at deploy.**

## 1. Goals and non-goals

**Goals**

- Every finding in `v3.2-scope.md` fixed at its root, with a test that fails on v3.1 and passes on
  v3.2, and an invariant that would have caught it.
- No new trust: no proxy, no upgrade path, no admin on the adapters, no arbitrary external call,
  no owner power that acts on funds faster than today.
- Every place funds can wait has a permissionless way out, listed in section 6 with exactly which
  outside rails it depends on.
- From every state the owner's and guardian's instruments can create, the honest owner reaches an
  open vault without a redeploy.
- The same product: csTAO at 24 decimals, non-transferable, one price per batch, the same
  front-door flows, the same keeper jobs (with additions), the same web.
- A vault that fits with room to spare for audit fixes: at least 1,500 B of headroom after v3.2.
- A migration the founder can do in an afternoon, with nothing of v3-launch left holding value.

**Non-goals**

- A second front door (the design makes it possible; activating one is a later decision, best under
  the Safe, and first see 3.6 on a second chain's last door).
- Transferable csTAO, a wrapper token, delivery to other chains, a hedged product.
- Changing ForeverMoney's rail or Chainlink's lane; the adapters stay a pipe.
- The independent audit (it comes after v3.2, before other people's money).
- Making a single owner key safe against itself: the machine bounds what the key can do on the
  vault's chain; the Safe is the answer for the rest (strongly recommended; until then, the
  monitor's alert and the founder's signed stamp, section 5).

**Anti-goals (a failure even if it works)**

- A holder's shares are burned, or new shares minted, at a price the vault cannot justify from
  stake it can see, unless the holder asked for exactly that and nobody else pays for it.
- A user's TAO is stuck with no permissionless way out.
- An owner action that no later owner action can undo.
- A fix that is a special case where the invariant could hold in code.

## 3. The design, by finding

### 3.1 Stake accounting integrity

Covers: V1 (hotkey-swap misprice on every minting and burning path), E1, gifted stake, the
runtime 1-rao shortfall, unlisted stake after a swap, the hotkey slots (BT-1), the loose 50% line,
and the reviews' M-1, M-2, M-3 and L-I.

**Root cause.** The vault has two sources of truth for stake and uses the wrong one for pricing.
`totalAssets()` reads the stake live from the listed hotkeys, so anything that moves stake off the
list lowers the price at once, while `bookedStake` (what the vault itself staked) is used only for
the outflow base. Minting and burning at the lowered price moves value from holders to whoever
transacts in the window; the only guard is a 50% insolvency line (the PoCs take 7.27 TAO out of a
40 TAO vault on a direct deposit, 8.89 on three batches, and cost an honest leaver 4.21 on a 10 TAO
exit). A gift raises the price at once, which is why the base needed its own booked cap, and
`bookedStake` drifts because claims and unstakes book balance-side amounts.

**Design: one ledger, priced at `min(visible, booked)`, with one guard and a permissionless way
back.**

State: `uint256 internal bookedStakeRao` (replaces `bookedStake`); constants
`MISSING_TOLERANCE_BPS = 10`, `MIN_MISSING_TOLERANCE_RAO = 1_000_000`; `uint64 lastAdoptAt`.
Views: `visibleStakeRao()` (today's `rootStakeRao`), `bookedStake()`, `missingStakeRao()`,
`surplusStakeRao()`, `stakeMissing()` (= missing > max((booked − pending) × 0.1%, 0.001 TAO), the
queued payouts taken off the base in rao and floored at 0, R6 E-fix),
`unlistedStakeRao()` (= `getTotalColdkeyStakeOnSubnet(coldkey, 0) - visible`, floored).
`grossAssets()` = `min(visible, booked) × RAO + balance - ED_RESERVE`; every mint and burn reads it.

The ledger moves only on stake-side measurements and never underflows:

- `stakeIdle`: `booked += min(stakeOf(h) after - before, rao)`, at most what it staked (rev 21, F3).
- `claimRewards(h)`: `booked += _sub0(visibleStakeRao() after, before)` (an unexpected fall books
  nothing and shows as missing stake, never a reverted claim; 3.10); the cut is computed on the
  whole position delta as today, **except while `stakeMissing()`, when the cut is skipped and the
  whole delta vests** (a cut is a mint); so too, as for every mint, below the 50% line or while the
  vault is underwater (below).
- `_removeStake`: `booked = _sub0(booked, stakeOf(h) before - after)` (M-2): a gift on a listed
  hotkey makes a full removal take more than the ledger holds; floored, the excess arrives as liquid
  balance, which is priced. A 1-rao-short receipt shows on the balance side (3.3), never as missing
  stake.
- `moveStake`, `removeHotkey`: within the set, unchanged (both call `move_stake`, which the root
  unstake hold also gates, 3.8); a scheduled `moveStake(from, to, rao)` names its destination, which
  must be the stake hotkey at execute (else `InvalidParameter`, the item kept: a `setStakeHotkey`
  made at once in its 48 hours cannot redirect a move the guardian reviewed), and is clamped to the
  position at execute (3.10.16).
- `adoptStake()` (keeper, or anyone after `KEEPER_FALLBACK`; refuses unless `surplus >=
  minStakeRao()` and a `STAKE_RAMP` has passed since the last adoption): books the surplus and vests
  it on its own drip (Revision 27), no cut: the adoption drip (`adoptVestingAmount`,
  `adoptVestingStart`, `adoptVestingPeriod`, beside the claim schedule's `vestingAmount`,
  `vestingStart`, `vestingPeriod`) restarts on what is left of it and the surplus, by the claim
  schedule's rule (ruling B7b): the period in force, or what is left of the drip if longer, so a
  carried remainder keeps its own end (3.6). It never restarts the claim schedule, so it never pushes
  rewards that holders already earned later; a second adoption while the first's drip runs moves only
  gift value. `unvestedRewards()` is both drips' sum. It enters the outflow base at once and leaves
  `lastStakeAt` alone (3.10).
- `writeOffMissingStake(uint256 rao)` (owner, scheduled 7 days): `w = min(rao, missing)`; requires
  `w + unlisted <= missing` with `unlisted` read inside a `try` capped at `WRITE_OFF_READ_GAS`,
  45,000,000 gas (R6 C; 100,000, `READ_GAS`, until then), 0 on a failed read: should the precompile
  function ever disappear, this guard is lost, which the write-off's 7-day notice and veto make
  acceptable; the read is always given its whole cap, else `InsufficientGas`, so no gas limit can
  starve it into that 0 (Revision 28); so only the part of the gap that is not sitting under an
  unlisted hotkey can be written off (M-1); then `booked -= w`. **A write-off does not lower the
  price** (it already reads the minimum): it closes the gap and re-opens minting at the depressed
  price, which is why it has the registry delay and the guardian's veto. **The read's own cap (R6
  C, ChatGPT R2).** Bittensor meters `getTotalColdkeyStakeOnSubnet` by the coldkey's positions
  (runtime 490: 1,875 gas, plus 1,250 for each hotkey in its `StakingHotkeys`, 1,250 for each
  position on any subnet and 12,500 for each root position, at 625 gas a database read; three
  metering models since July 2026), and the vault cannot stop others adding positions: the reject
  flag refuses only a hotkey new to the coldkey's staking set, so a stranger stakes under one of the
  vault's listed hotkeys on any subnet and `transfer_stake`s it to the vault's coldkey on the same
  subnet, 0.0001 TAO (`DefaultMinTransfer`) a position, which the vault, touching only root, can
  never remove. About 67 such positions priced the read past 100,000 for good: from then on it
  failed at every gas limit, the bound read 0, and an oversized write-off (an owner's error outside
  the tool, a stolen key, or stake reappearing under an unlisted hotkey in the 7 days) ran, reopening
  mints at a price that leaves out stake the holders still own, while the lens, uncapped, still
  showed the unlisted stake. With no stranger at all, 4 listed hotkeys and 3 swap targets on root
  already cost 106,875. A stranger's dust also pins its hotkey in the coldkey's staking set for good
  (`staking_hotkey_must_remain`), so every hotkey the vault ever listed can be kept there and the
  count only grows with rotations. So the cap is the chain's own ceiling for this read: a coldkey
  holds at most 256 hotkeys (`MAX_STAKING_HOTKEYS`) on at most 128 subnets and root, 1,875 + 256 ×
  (1,250 + 129 × 1,250 + 12,500) = 44,801,875, rounded up. Given whole, it needs about 45.72M left at
  the read and about 46.5M of a transaction (execute's self-call and the read each forward 63/64),
  under Bittensor's 64,998,400 per transaction; ops sends the write-off's execute with an explicit
  gas limit, never the wallet's estimate, and the owner's wallet holds that limit's worth up front
  (only the gas used is paid). The other capped reads keep `READ_GAS`. **The residual:** a later
  runtime that raises this read's metering, the staking-set limit or the subnet limit can exceed any
  constant; the read then fails as a vanished function would, under the same notice and veto.

The guard: `depositDirect` reverts `StakeMissing()`; `redeemDirect(shares, acceptMissing)` reverts
unless the flag is set, and with it burns at `min(visible, booked)` (the leaver may be underpaid and
says so; the stayers never are; the web never passes `true`; no automatic reopening);
`onBridgeMessage` reverts `StakeMissing()` and the adapter parks the batch (3.2); `claimRewards`
runs without the cut; `stakeIdle`, `adoptStake` and `processPayouts` are unaffected (they do not
price shares), and `cancelPayoutBatch` re-mints at `min(visible, booked)`, which reads low while
stake is missing, so it returns at most the burned shares (Task 34c): not a guard, and the owner's
cancel works in every state. The 50% line stays as the bounce condition for batch
deposits, the `VaultInsolvent` revert for `depositDirect` and the cut's skip, as today, joined by
the underwater check below; with the ledger it can trip only after a loss has been written off or
liquid TAO is gone, and the owner cannot bring it about.

**The tolerance's base is the stake that backs shares (R6 E-fix; ChatGPT R2 claim E, the founder's
concern after Fable's check).** Every path prices at `min(visible, booked)`, so a gap within the
tolerance comes off the price the moment it appears; the tolerance decides only whether mints and
unconsented burns may run, never what they are priced at. Until R6 its bps part was 0.1% of the
booked stake, which also holds stake that backs no share: the stake behind queued, unprocessed door
payouts (a door redeem's TAO stays staked until `processPayouts` unstakes it) and the stake-side
rewards still vesting. With the queue most of the booked stake that let a gap far larger than 0.1%
of the shares' backing read as nothing missing: booked 100, queued 99.8, a gap of 0.1 halved the
price, mints still ran (the half-launch line does not refuse at exactly half), and after a relist
the stayers kept about 0.1 of their 0.2 TAO. So the base is now `sub0(booked, pending / RAO)`, the
booked stake less the queued payouts, and the 0.001 TAO floor stays: in that example the tolerance
is 0.001 TAO, the 0.1 reads as missing, and both mints refuse until the swapped hotkey is listed
(`VaultLedger.test_aGapInsideTheOldToleranceWithALargeQueueReadsMissing`; with no queue the rule is
as before, `test_aSwapUnderTheToleranceStillMints`). All of `pending` comes off, though part of it
may be liquid (a bounce, reserved from the balance): that only tightens the tolerance, and the rule
reads no balance a delivery's own value could raise. The vesting rewards stay in the base (they are
small, and a third read at every caller costs bytes and gas). Every reader applies the same rule
from the same raw values: the vault's guard, the claim's reads before and after it, the delivery,
L4's `add` and `remove` (passed `pending` beside `booked`), and the lens, so ops and the monitor
read it from the lens. What remains, a stated residual: inside the tolerance a minter can still gain
up to the gap from the stayers if the swapped stake is listed again, now at most max(0.1% of the
stake backing shares, 0.001 TAO) in TAO (`testFuzz_aMinterInsideTheToleranceGainsAtMostIt`), and as
a share of the stayers' value about 0.1% (a little more while vesting rewards, which stay in the
base, are large), or the 0.001 TAO floor's share of a tiny vault.
Nor is the timing the validator's alone: a minter can exit a little (a direct exit's walk lowers
booked and visible together, so the gap stays while the tolerance shrinks; a payout's walk no longer
does, since it lowers the queue with the booked stake) until it reads as missing, then list the
swapped hotkey itself; and anyone can
pull a gap just past the tolerance back inside with a small stake gift on a listed hotkey (the
keeper's `stakeIdle` raises booked, so the tolerance, the same way), reopening mints at a price
understated by up to the tolerance. A gap needs a listed hotkey's owner to swap a position no larger
than the tolerance; with one listed hotkey a swap moves all of it, far past. The launch keeps the
stake on one hotkey with no small positions elsewhere and processes payouts promptly, and the
monitor warns when `missingStakeRao()` is above dust while `stakeMissing()` is false: the answer is
to list the swapped hotkey at once. The tighter base has one cost, under I-6: a swapped position
below the chain's minimum (which no stranger may list) now closes mints sooner while the queue is
large, until a privileged actor lists it or a gift clears it.

**No mint at an empty vault that is underwater (Task 10c).** `totalAssets()` floors `gross -
pending - unvested` at 0, and only a mint at a supply of 0 writes the schedule down (below) when a
loss eats the unvested remainder, so the vault can owe more than it holds while its assets read 0:
it is *underwater*. With shares outstanding the 50% line sees that (assets 0 < supply); at a supply
of 0 it reads `0 < 0` and never trips, so a mint there would be priced at the launch price and its
money would fill the hole: the treasury's cut, or the first depositor's, and, where the loss reached
the payout reservation, an earlier leaver's queued payout, which the new depositor would pay for
good. So every mint also refuses while the vault is underwater (`CascadeFormulas.underwater`), with
`gross` read before the mint's own money: `pending + unvested > gross && (unvested != 0 || pending >
gross + UNDERWATER_TOLERANCE)`. While any reward still vests, that is any shortfall, `gross <
pending + unvested`. With none vesting, it is a shortfall against the queued payouts of more than
`UNDERWATER_TOLERANCE` (a constant, 1,000 rao), `gross + UNDERWATER_TOLERANCE < pending`. A
shortfall in the schedule needs no tolerance: it closes as the schedule vests (a claim adds as much
to the gross as to the schedule), so it never locks deposits. A shortfall in the payouts within
1,000 rao is a rounding of the chain's (a fresh position's read one rao short; no exit leaves one,
since every exit pays its own cost, 3.3), which nothing would close; the next minter absorbs it.
The refusal takes three forms: `claimRewards` skips the cut and the whole delta vests;
`depositDirect` reverts `VaultInsolvent`; `onBridgeMessage` bounces the batch's deposits (its
redeems still burn). The check runs at every supply but decides only at a supply of 0: with shares
outstanding, underwater means assets 0, which the 50% line already refuses. The refusal lasts until
the hole closes: a hole in the schedule as its remainder vests (for the cut; a deposit writes it
down, next), a hole in the reservation when the TAO comes back (a top-up, or the stake listed
again). **At a supply of 0 the unvested rewards are no holder's (Task 15),** so `depositDirect` and
a delivery, there and before their underwater check, write the schedule down to what the gross
before their money holds beyond the queued payouts (`unvested <= sub0(gross, pending)`): the
remainder restarts on that amount as a claim restarts it (over the period in force, or what is left
of the old schedule if longer, 3.6), and no event is logged. Both drips are written down (Revision
27): the adoption drip first, to what that holds beyond the claim schedule, then, when the claim
schedule alone exceeds it, the claim schedule to it, each restarting there by its own rule, so
neither vests faster than its own schedule. A hole in the schedule then never
refuses a deposit, which mints at the launch price and absorbs no part of the hole; only a payout
shortfall past `UNDERWATER_TOLERANCE` still refuses (`depositDirect` reverts, its write-down with
it; a delivery bounces its deposits and keeps the write-down, the schedule having held nothing the
gross backed). The write-down never runs with shares outstanding, where the schedule is the
holders', and the cut's skip is unchanged.

**No cap on batch deposits (L-4).** The ledger closes the mispriced-mint vector; the front door's
`depositCap` is the batch-side cap; `maxTotalAssets` remains the direct-deposit cap; a rogue door
(7 days of notice from an open vault and a veto) can grow the vault past the beta cap by at most
the adapter's 50 TAO per message, which is growth, not loss. **The door's cap drifts (R6, Fable's
whole-system review L-1; stated, no change).** `depositCap` bounds `netForwarded + the open batch`,
and `netForwarded` falls only by a payout or a refund arriving on Base; a direct exit on Bittensor
never lowers it. So a door deposit taken back by `redeemDirect` (any EOA holder, honest or not) keeps
its cap used: at a 40 TAO cap, four 10 TAO round trips leave the vault holding none of the door's
money and every `deposit` (and the staker's `stake`) reverting `CapExceeded` until the owner raises
the cap (`setCaps`, immediate). No funds move and no exit is touched; the mover pays gas only. The
other way it drifts more slowly: payouts carry yield, so `netForwarded` under-counts what the door's
depositors still hold. Ops and the monitor compare `netForwarded` with the door-origin holders'
value and raise the cap by the difference; a cap on a value the vault reports is for a later version.

**Unlisted stake, the slots, and the permissionless way back (M-3, L-I).**

- `addHotkey(h)`: owner, guardian or keeper at any time; **anyone** while `stakeMissing()` when
  `getStake(h, coldkey, 0) >= minStakeRao()` **and `lastClaimAt[h] == 0`**, i.e. the vault never
  listed `h` (the record is set at first listing and kept after removal; a swap's new hotkey
  passes; round 6 L-3). The reject flag refuses third-party transfers onto hotkeys outside the
  coldkey's staking set, but a hotkey the vault removed after claiming stays in that set
  (`StakingHotkeys` while `BasketClaimed != 0`), so a stranger could seed it; re-listing one is for
  the owner, guardian or keeper. Safe because the price is `min(visible, booked)`: listing a hotkey
  that holds the vault's stake can only raise `visible` toward `booked`.
- The last slot (`MAX_HOTKEYS` stays 4) opens only to an add made while `stakeMissing()`, by anyone
  under the rule above or by the owner, guardian or keeper, of a hotkey holding at least
  `minStakeRao()` of the vault's root stake; other adds fill at most `MAX_HOTKEYS - 1` slots (round
  8 L-2). Once a swap is resolved the keeper frees the slot again (the swapped-away hotkey is empty;
  for a swap of the stake hotkey, after the owner's `setStakeHotkey` has moved it); a stranger who
  puts stake on that hotkey leaves its removal to the owner's `removeHotkey`, a grief with no loss,
  and that is one of I-6's preconditions (round 9 L-4 lists the others). Both this and the slot's purpose
  rest on the reject flag (no
  stranger can put stake on an unlisted hotkey outside the coldkey's staking set, so none can squat
  the last slot with a hotkey of its own), which the mainnet canary confirms (section 9's testnet gate was dropped).
- `removeHotkey(h, forfeit)`: the owner's, as today; the keeper or guardian may at any time remove a
  non-stake hotkey with no stake and unclaimed reward at most `hotkeyDustRao`; anyone may remove a
  non-stake hotkey with no stake and no unclaimed reward while the set is full (`hotkeys.length ==
  MAX_HOTKEYS`) and `stakeMissing()`, its only purpose, so, while nothing is missing or a slot is
  free, no stranger can remove an empty `X` during the owner's scheduled `setStakeHotkey(X)` (round 9
  L-2). While the set is full and stake is missing one can (a pending-item check would block I-6's
  way back whenever `X` is the only empty hotkey): the item then fails `NotListed` at execute and is
  kept, and the owner lists `X` again in a non-reserved slot and executes it within its window, a
  grief with no loss. `HotkeyRemoved`'s `forfeit` is true only for the owner's forfeit (the other
  paths decide by the dust rule, whatever the flag).
- `setStakeHotkey(X)` is immediate when `X` is listed, `getStake(X) > getStake(stakeHotkey)` and
  `getStake(X) >= bookedStakeRao / 2` (it follows stake that already moved, L-1), and scheduled
  (48 h) otherwise; `moveStake` stays scheduled. The test reads the chain's stake, so gifts count: a
  stolen owner key that gifts onto a listed `X` more than the stake hotkey holds and at least half
  the booked stake switches at once (on a fresh vault, booked 0, a dust gift suffices), then can
  consolidate the other positions onto `X` with its own removals. The reject flag limits `X` to the
  coldkey's staking set; no principal is at risk (the harm is the validator's take and the swap
  griefs). So the guardian treats any immediate switch as an alarm, and the monitor alerts on every
  `StakeHotkeySet` not preceded by its `Scheduled` item (T54/T55).

The chain cannot fill the vault's list: with the reject flag live, a stranger's `transfer_stake`
onto an unlisted hotkey is refused, so the list grows only by the vault's own stakes and by one
entry per validator swap. Confirming that for a contract coldkey is the mainnet canary's, and every kind of swap
(all-subnet, per-subnet retaining nominators, a per-subnet split) stays an open risk on Task 14's model (section 9's
testnet gate was dropped).

**Alternatives rejected.** Tightening the 50% line (a symptom check); pricing on `booked` alone (a
real loss would overprice for 7 days); counting gifts at once (the price movable by any outsider);
refusing `redeemDirect` outright while missing (locks a holder in); privileged-only hotkey adds (an
absent operator froze Base-only holders); a `missing / 2` threshold (nothing but the vault's own
stake can create the position); a hold queue in the vault (bytes, and the adapter must park
anyway); an owner-set tolerance (a knob).

**Invariants.** I-1 *no mispriced mint, no unconsented mispriced burn:* no `Deposited`,
`DepositedDirect`, `Redeemed` or `RewardCutMinted` while `stakeMissing()`; a `RedeemedDirect` while
missing carries `acceptedMissing = true` at `min(visible, booked)`; and no `Deposited`,
`DepositedDirect` or `RewardCutMinted` while the vault is underwater (while any reward vests, `gross
< pending + unvested`; with none vesting, `gross + 1,000 rao < pending`; `gross` before the mint's
own money; for a deposit at a supply of 0, `unvested` after its write-down, Task 15), at any supply.
I-2 *ledger conservation, floored:* `booked = max(0, Σ stake-side measurements)`, each claim's
measurement itself floored at 0 (`_sub0(after, before)`, 3.10), so a claim never lowers the ledger;
no exit reverts for a ledger reason. I-3 *gifts do not move the price until adopted or unstaked into
balance.* I-4 *the price never falls* except when visible stake falls below booked (a swap; a move
into an empty position read one rao short), across a simulated loss, by the 1-rao-short read of a
fresh position in `stakeIdle`, or by the chain's cut of the owner's own removal of a sub-minimum
leftover (`removeHotkey`), none of them an exit; and, where one of these leaves the gross short of the
queued payouts and the unvested rewards, by the mint or the owner's cancel that absorbs that hole (at
a supply of 0 a mint goes through only within `UNDERWATER_TOLERANCE`, the cut only with nothing
vesting, a deposit after writing the schedule down to what the gross holds beyond the payouts, Task
15; the owner's cancel there writes nothing down, so the holders it re-mints absorb the hole and then
receive the rewards still vesting). No exit lowers it, at any per-removal fee the chain charges
(I-35): every removal's fee is the exit's own, so a walk costs the holders nothing however many
positions it takes, and no bound on the fee is assumed. A direct exit, flagged or not, is paid its
value less its own cost, a payout chunk's entries bear their walk's cost, and a cancel re-mints at
most the shares each entry's value buys. A write-off never moves it, and no mint at a supply of 0
fills a hole in the schedule, or a payout hole past the tolerance. I-5 *the outflow
base never exceeds `min(visible, booked) - ramping`.* That base holds the stake behind queued,
unprocessed payouts and the stake-side rewards still vesting too (R6, Fable's check of E), so while
the queue is large a door's daily allowance is a larger share of what backs the shares than its bps
says: with payouts unprocessed a rogue door burns 20 of 100, then 20 of the 80 still backing shares,
and so on; at launch the 10 TAO floor dominates anyway. I-6 *permissionless recovery:* while the last
slot is free, from any `stakeMissing()` the handler caused by a swap, a stranger's `addHotkey` of
the swap's new, never-listed hotkey clears it; a stranger can list no hotkey the vault ever listed;
a gift can also clear it, harmlessly, since the price stays `min(visible, booked)`. Its further
preconditions (round 9 L-4): a swapped position below `minStakeRao()` (in a vault with less than 2
TAO booked; since R6's E-fix, wherever less than 2 TAO of the booked stake backs shares, as when
queued payouts are most of it: the tolerance's base is then that small, so such a position closes
mints, and paying the queue does not reopen them, since a payout's walk lowers the booked stake with
the queue; until the keeper, guardian or owner lists it) can be cleared only by a gift, and two swaps with one free slot need an old hotkey
removed first. A third: the old hotkey must be empty of stake and credits; a retained position (a
partial swap) or credits paid as stake leave the way back to the owner's `removeHotkey` (immediate),
not the keeper or a stranger; with the set full and a second swap, mints stay closed until the owner
acts (no loss; consenting exits still run). Monitor carry: alert on stake missing with the set full
and no removable hotkey: the owner must act. I-35 *no exit lowers the holders' price* (the founder's
ruling, 2026-11-06): across every `redeemDirect` (flagged or not), every sent `processPayouts` chunk
and every `cancelPayoutBatch`, at any chain removal fee and any `unstakeMarginRao`, with `A`, `S` the
vault's `totalAssets()` and share supply just before the call and `A'`, `S'` just after, `(A' + 1) ×
(S + 10^6) >= (A + 1) × (S' + 10^6)` (the price exactly, with the virtual offsets and without the
view's floor), and the hole `sub0(pendingPayouts + unvested, gross)` is no larger after the call than
before; the one exception is a cancel that starts with `totalAssets()` reading 0 over a hole I-4's
other exceptions left.

**Tests.** Unit: the four PoCs of `HotkeySwapMisprice.t.sol` inverted (deposits revert;
`redeemDirect` without the flag reverts, with it pays at the floor price and the other holders are
unchanged; a stranger's `addHotkey` at the chain's minimum clears the state, and below it or of a
hotkey the vault once listed is refused); a swap under the tolerance still mints; the 1-rao-short
read never trips the guard; an alpha gift changes nothing; a gift then every holder's full exit (no
underflow; the gift priced as liquid); `adoptStake`'s minimum and daily bound; the write-off bounded
by `missing - unlisted` and price-neutral; no cut while missing; the last-slot rule; the three
removal paths; `setStakeHotkey` under L-1; stake-side booking with `claimToBalance`; no mint at an
empty, underwater vault (`Supply0Underwater.t.sol`: the cut skipped, never while a reward vests; a
deposit, direct or in a batch, writing a hole in the schedule down and minting at the launch price;
a payout shortfall refusing a direct deposit and bouncing a batch's, the tolerance at 1,000 and
1,001 rao; no write-down with shares outstanding).
Fuzz: random swaps against the tolerance; random gifts against full exits. Invariant: handler
actions `swapAway`, `strangerAddHotkey`, `seedRemovedHotkey`, `reAddHotkey`, `giftListed`, `adopt`,
`writeOff`, `depositWhileMissing`, `redeemDirectWhileMissing`; I-1 to I-6. Testnet: section 9.

### 3.2 Delivery under refusal: parking and refund in the Bittensor adapter

Covers: hard question 1, B1, the ForeverMoney migration as far as parking can help, BT-2,
`UnknownPeer`, a batch below the staking floor (L-9), the `stakeMissing` delivery, the
owner-replaceable `alphaVault` request, and the reviews' M-8, M-7, M-B, L-A, L-E, **H-3**
(reentrancy), H-2 (i) and L-7.

**Root cause.** The adapter is all-or-nothing: `ccipReceive` must convert exactly and deliver in
one call, or revert, which leaves the message in CCIP `FAILURE`, whose retry needs Chainlink's
`verifierResults`; a permanent cause makes it unrecoverable.

**Design.**

State: `struct Parked { uint128 amount; uint64 parkedAt; bool converted; }`,
`mapping(bytes32 key => Parked) parked` (key = `keccak256(abi.encode(sourceChain, sender,
recipient, message))`), `parkedNative`, `parkedTokens`; constants `PARK_TIMEOUT` 14 days,
`PARK_GAS_RESERVE` 150,000 (measured live before deploy), `SHORTFALL_TOLERANCE_WEI` 1 rao,
`RESERVE_TARGET` and `TOKEN_RESERVE_TARGET` 0.001 TAO (sweep targets and monitor rules, never
parking conditions, M-B), `REFUND_CHUNK = type(uint32).max`; immutable `treasury`; and, since R4,
immutables `inboundGasBase` and `inboundGasPerEntry`: the lane's inbound gas model, the same pair
the Base adapter holds as its `gasBase` and `gasPerEntry` for what CCIP gives this adapter's
`ccipReceive` (one model in two copies, which the deploy plan feeds from one setting and `verify`
holds equal), read by `deliverParked`'s gas requirement below. The pair **includes**
`PARK_GAS_RESERVE` (the R4 review): it is the whole gas a delivery gets, of which the adapter keeps
the reserve back from the recipient's call, so the reserve is counted once, in the pair (the constructor
refuses a base at or below it), and `parkedDeliveryGas` adds nothing to it; and, since R4 (F-1), `tokenCushion`, the
token cushion **booked**: what `fundCushion(amount)` pulled in (approve it first; `CushionFunded`), less
what covered a send's or a converted refund's mint at most 1 rao short (`ShortfallCovered`). The books
tell the cushion from surplus, never the balance: every token above `parkedTokens + tokenCushion` is
surplus (a mint's rounding in the adapter's favour, a plain transfer), the sweep's. And the view
`canSendPayouts()` (R4, S-H1): false while ForeverMoney's AlphaVault is paused, has a migration
announced (its `depositLiquid` closed for good from the announcement) or has finished one, or when any
of those reads fails, each read capped at `RAIL_READ_GAS` (30,000) and the vault's call at
`RAIL_VIEW_GAS` (150,000), so a read that burns its gas cannot starve the delivery it runs in. It
**never reverts** (the R4 review): each read is a low-level `STATICCALL` that copies back at most one
word, and no code at the AlphaVault's address, a revert, an empty or short answer, or a read out of its
gas each reads false, so the vault's fail-open read of the view is never reached through this adapter
(`IAlphaVault` gains `migrationReadyAt()` and `migrationFinished()`); every send
converts through `depositLiquid`, so the vault burns no door redeem while its primary answers false
(3.5).

**One reentrancy lock (H-3)** over `ccipReceive`, `deliverParked`, `refundParked`, `dropParked` and
`sweepSurplus`; `convertExact` is self-only and not locked (it is called from inside the lock).
Every park path is checks-effects-interactions: the record is deleted and the totals decremented
**before** any call to the recipient, and restored in the `catch`. Without that, a recipient that
reverts and re-enters `deliverParked` for the same key could be paid twice from the native parks.

`convertExact(uint256 amount) external` (self only): `withdrawLiquid(token, amount, amount - 1
rao)`; reverts `ConversionShort` unless the native delta is at least `amount - 1 rao` **and** the
balance after the call is at least `parkedNative + amount` (the rao comes from the reserve, never
from parked native) **and**, since R4 (F-3), the adapter's token balance fell by at most `amount`
(ForeverMoney's `withdrawLiquid` burns exactly `wad`; one that burned more, out of the cushion or a
park, is refused, its burn rolled back with the frame; one that burned less leaves the rest as
surplus, so a 1-wei rounding on ForeverMoney's side parks nothing: the vault-favour review); reverts `BelowConversionFloor` when `amount < getDefaultMinStake() × RAO`
(read inside the frame). Called as `try this.convertExact{gas: _sub0(gasleft(), PARK_GAS_RESERVE)}
(amount)` (L-E, L-7: the reserve saturates at 0; a call with no gas simply fails and parks), so a
failure rolls ForeverMoney's burn back and leaves gas to write the park.

`_deliver(recipient, sender, message, amount)`, inside the lock, in order:

1. Decode `decodeBatch(message)` and check the deposits' sum equals `amount` and that `amount` is
   whole rao (`NotRaoAligned`, as in v3.1); a malformed batch reverts (CCIP `FAILURE`; only a
   malformed sender can produce one).
2. `parked[key]` must be empty, else `AlreadyParked()`.
3. Unless `amount == 0` (a redeem-only batch needs no conversion, L-A): first
   `token.balanceOf(this) >= parkedTokens + tokenCushion + amount` (the booked cushion since F-1),
   else `ReserveShort` and the message reverts
   (R4, C-L1: only tokens that arrived are converted. The router names `amount`; an honest lane
   has just released it, so this holds unless the books are wrong, M-B; a compromised lane naming
   tokens it never delivered would otherwise have the conversion burn the token parks. Before R4
   the check ran only on the failure path). Then `try this.convertExact`. On failure, park the
   tokens (the check still holds: a failed conversion rolls ForeverMoney's burn back): `converted
   = false`, `parkedTokens += amount`, `BatchParked(key, messageId, recipient, sender, amount,
   false, reason)`, return.
4. `try recipient.onBridgeMessage{value: amount, gas: _sub0(gasleft(), PARK_GAS_RESERVE)}(...)`. On
   revert, park the native TAO (`converted = true`, `parkedNative += amount`; a redeem-only batch
   parks the same way with `amount == 0`). Nothing was recorded before the call, so there is nothing
   to restore here; the record is written in the `catch`.

Permissionless functions (each recomputes the key; the keeper rebuilds the message from the Base
OnRamp's `CCIPMessageSent` by the `messageId` in `BatchParked`):

- `deliverParked(...)` (locked): if not converted and `amount > 0`, the token balance must cover
  the other token parks, the booked cushion and this one (`ReserveShort`, C-L1, F-1); if converted,
  the native balance the other native parks and this one (`ReserveShort`, F-5, a books guard); then,
  for a token park, `this.convertExact(amount)`
  without a try (a revert reverts the call and the record stays), then move `amount` from
  `parkedTokens` to `parkedNative` and set `converted`; then **delete the record and
  `parkedNative -= amount`**; then, right before the recipient's call, **`gasleft() >=
  inboundGasBase + inboundGasPerEntry × entries`** (the message's own entries, capped at `GAS_CAP` as
  `gasFor` is; the view `parkedDeliveryGas(message)`; the pair includes `PARK_GAS_RESERVE`, so the
  recipient's call gets at least what a CCIP delivery's would, and the reserve is not added twice), else
  `InsufficientGas` and the whole call reverts, the park exactly as it was, a token park still
  tokens (R4, A-M1/C-M1: the recipient's call gets all the gas but the reserve, and a refusal is
  caught, so a call starved of gas used to "succeed" as a re-park, and a keeper sending at its gas
  estimate converted every token park and delivered none). Then `try
  recipient.onBridgeMessage{value: amount}(...)`: on success `ParkedDelivered(key)`; on revert
  **restore the record as a converted park and `parkedNative += amount`** (`ParkedConverted(key)`):
  a vault that refuses with that much gas is refusing the batch, as before. The requirement is
  `deliverParked`'s alone: a CCIP delivery's gas is the sending side's model, and one starved inside
  `_deliver` parks by design (R3 A-L1), to be delivered here with enough. So the keeper can get
  every token park out of ForeverMoney's token during a migration notice even while the vault is
  paused (M-7); there is no standalone conversion call (L-3).
- `refundParked(...) payable` (locked): after `parkedAt + PARK_TIMEOUT`, builds
  `PAYOUTS(key, REFUND_CHUNK, [(user, amount) per deposit])` with the park's recipient (the vault)
  as the payload's sender and the park's sender (the door) as its recipient, so the door takes it
  as a payout from its vault (else I-9 fails), **deletes the record and decrements the total
  first**, then for a converted park `depositLiquid` (needs ForeverMoney's rail open) and
  the send (once the native balance covers the other native parks and this one, `ReserveShort`, F-5),
  for an unconverted park the send of the tokens as they are, once the token balance covers the other
  token parks, the booked cushion and this one (`ReserveShort`, C-L1, F-1). The refund's send skips
  the conversion-floor check (`_checkAmount`), which only applies to conversions (L-7): a sub-floor
  token park is exactly what the refund is for. Fee from `msg.value` (`quoteRefund(...)`; excess
  kept). `netForwarded` falls by the refund on Base. `ParkedRefunded(key, messageId)`.
- `dropParked(...)` (locked): after the timeout, a park with `amount == 0` is deleted
  (`ParkedDropped(key)`).
- `sweepSurplus()` (locked): sends `balance - RESERVE_TARGET - parkedNative` native and
  `token.balanceOf(this) - parkedTokens - tokenCushion` tokens (each floored at 0) to `treasury`: since
  R4 (F-1) the sweep leaves the booked cushion, not a target, and a plain transfer of the token is
  surplus it sends on. The token cushion is funded with `fundCushion`; `bridge.cushion_low` says when
  the native reserve or the booked cushion is below its target (`TOKEN_RESERVE_TARGET`, a target only).

**The front door's side (H-2 i).** The front door's inbound check becomes `bridgeKnown[msg.sender]`
(ever activated) instead of `bridgeEnabled`: a delivery can only credit tokens that have arrived
(`TokensNotReceived`) and each bridge has its own replay map, so a disabled bridge delivering a
late payout or a refund is bounded exactly as an enabled one; `disableBridge` on the front door
then stops only *sends* (closes) through that bridge. A refund therefore depends on the lane and,
for a converted park, on ForeverMoney's `depositLiquid`, and on nothing Cascade's keys can switch
off.

**The other external calls, audited for the same pattern (H-3).** Vault: `redeemDirect` burns and
updates before paying the caller, under `nonReentrant`; `processPayouts` updates the queue before
`bridge.send` and refunds the caller's unused `msg.value` last, under `nonReentrant`;
`cancelPayoutBatch` makes no external call; the precompile calls are runtime, not EVM code, and
cannot re-enter; the scheduler's `execute` self-calls setters that make no state-changing EVM
external call (the only ones are views: `maxMessageValue()` in `setMessageLimits`,
`setMaxBatchValue` and `setPrimaryBridge`; `moveStake` and `setStakeHotkey` call the precompile).
Front door: `_closeBatch` deletes the open batch before `bridge.send`, calls no treasury, and pays
the caller last, under `nonReentrant`; `requestRedeem`'s self-close is the same path with nothing to
pay; `deliver` measures its own balance and is `nonReentrant`; `onBridgeMessage` and `claim` are
`nonReentrant`. Staker: every swap under `nonReentrant` with balance-delta checks. The Bittensor
adapter was the one gap.

**The batch's redeem requesters.** Never burned; processed at the later delivery's price, or gone
on a refund or drop (the request fee is not returned; the user requests again).

**ForeverMoney's migration, honestly (M-7).** A token park after `executeMigration` is a claim
ForeverMoney will not honour. What parking changes: a batch delivered during the 7-day notice
converts normally, and a token park that exists when the notice begins can be converted by anyone
with `deliverParked` during the notice. The rule, in the keeper and as a critical alert
(`bridge.parked_tokens_during_migration`): the moment `migrationReadyAt() != 0`, pause deposits on
the front door, **flush the open batch at once** (anyone can close it, L-4), convert every token
park, and close nothing afterwards. The residual (section 5): a token park that cannot be converted
during the notice rides on ForeverMoney's token. No send-side rail guard (L-2): ForeverMoney's own
modifiers already make `processPayouts` revert in those states; **an open vault whose payouts
revert because ForeverMoney is paused is a stated residual** (the keeper retries; nothing is lost).

**The residual case for parks.** A park is delivered to the vault or refunded to its door through
this adapter's remote twin. If the vault removed the door (`UnknownPeer`), delivery waits for its
re-activation and the refund still works. If ForeverMoney's rail is closed, a converted park cannot
be turned back into tokens and waits for the vault. The ops rule: never `removeFrontDoor` for a door
with a park outstanding, and never retire an adapter pair until `parkedNative == 0 && parkedTokens
== 0`; `verify` enforces it. A door hold can outlast `PARK_TIMEOUT` (14 days against up to 22): a
refund then is an exit, not a loss (depositors are credited on Base), so the timeout stays, and the
runbook pauses Base deposits at the first removal alert (round 6, Info 10).

**On the replaceable `alphaVault`.** Keep it immutable: parking plus the convert-during-notice
rule removes the stranding; an owner-settable pointer would be a fund-touching change on a shorter
notice than any other; a successor rail is a new adapter pair through the registry.

**Alternatives rejected.** Hold or bounce in the vault; convert-or-revert; parking tokens
`withdrawLiquid` had burned; the cushion as a parking condition; a standalone `convertParked`;
refund by an owner; a lock-free `deliverParked` (H-3).

**Invariants.** I-7 *never below what is owed:* `balance >= parkedNative` and `token.balanceOf >=
parkedTokens` after every call, and each total equals the sum over the records. I-8 *mint XOR
refund:* for every delivered batch exactly one of {delivered to the vault, refunded, still parked,
dropped empty} holds. I-9 *a refund credits each depositor exactly their deposit* and the front
door's identities hold across it. I-10 *refund liveness:* a park older than `PARK_TIMEOUT` can
always be refunded or dropped by anyone given the Bittensor-to-Base lane and, for a converted park
only, ForeverMoney's `depositLiquid` (its gates, the chain's minimum stake under it, its slippage)
and the token cushion's rao (a mint 1 rao short is covered by it, 3.3); a converted park can
always be delivered to the vault once the vault accepts. I-11 *no re-entrant drain:* a recipient that reverts
and re-enters, or re-enters and then reverts, never changes `parkedNative`, `parkedTokens` or the records by more than its own
batch.

**Tests.** Unit: the three stranding PoCs now park or cover; a short payment rolls the burn back;
every vault refusal parks native; a vault or a conversion that burns all its gas still leaves a
record; a sub-floor amount and a failing floor read park tokens; a malformed batch reverts; a
drained cushion does not stop a token park and does make `_collect` revert 1 rao short until topped
up; a top-up that would dip into `parkedNative` parks tokens; `AlreadyParked`; redeem-only batches
in `_deliver` and `deliverParked`; `deliverParked` converts and delivers, converts and stays parked,
reverts when the conversion fails; **a reverting-then-re-entering recipient and a two-level
re-entry are refused by the lock and leave the totals exact (H-3)**; `refundParked` before and
after the timeout, converted and unconverted, below the conversion floor, and through a disabled
Base twin; `dropParked`; `quoteRefund`; `sweepSurplus` never touches a parked total; (R4) a forged
delivery naming tokens that never arrived refused `ReserveShort` beside a token park, the parks,
the totals and the cushion untouched (I-7 under a compromised lane), and books that do not cover a
park refusing `deliverParked` and an unconverted refund the same way; `deliverParked` one gas short
of `parkedDeliveryGas` reverting `InsufficientGas` with the park exactly as it was, a token park
unconverted, and at the requirement a gas-burning recipient still leaving the park converted;
(R4, F-1) with a booked 0.001 TAO cushion beside a token park, forged amounts of 1 rao, a sub-floor
amount, exactly the cushion and the cushion and a rao each refused `ReserveShort`, the park, the totals
and the cushion untouched, and a plain transfer swept as surplus with the cushion left; (F-3) an
AlphaVault that burns a rao more refused (at most the amount burned), an honest delivery parking its tokens and `deliverParked`
reverting with the park unchanged; (F-5) books that do not cover a converted park refusing its
delivery and its refund. Invariant: the
adapter campaign with `pauseVault`, `swapAway`, `fmRoundShort`, `fmPause`, `fmMigrate`,
`raiseFloor`, `drainCushion`, `deliverParked`, `refundParked`, `dropParked`, `sweepSurplus`,
redeem-only batches, **a re-entrant recipient actor** and (R4, the forged-message review) **a
compromised lane**: `forgedToBittensor`, `forgedToBase` and `forgedFromOldRail` (through a second
pair the door knows and the vault has disabled), each lying about the amount, the tokens, the sender,
the recipient or the message, each inside a snapshot, after which no other park, the booked cushion,
a queued payout, a claim, an open deposit, the fee pot or the share price moved beyond what the call
itself carried (and the surplus it may take); a forged redeem-only batch burning holders' shares is
F-2's stated residual (section 5) and excluded; I-7 to I-11. Fork: the real `OffRamp` and
`AlphaVault` over `MockStaking`: parking into a paused vault, token parking with the burn rolled
back, `deliverParked`, a refund through the real Base `OffRamp`.

### 3.3 Bounded tolerances

Covers: scope 6 and 7, B1, B2, B3, V3, and the reviews' L-6, L-7, M-B, the I-19 conflict, and
round 5's L-3 and L-5.

**Root cause.** Three exact-equality checks treat a 1-wei rounding by a system Cascade does not
control as a fault that must block the user, and the fee ceiling (0.1 TAO max, 0.02 default) is
larger than the smallest burn (0.01 TAO), so an exit can be finalised at zero.

**Who pays an exit's cost (the founder's ruling, 2026-11-06).** Holders never pay for someone else's
exit. Every cost an exit causes, the chain's per-removal fee, a short receipt, a rounding, falls on
the party exiting, rounded in the vault's favour; an exit that cannot cover its own cost is refused,
never subsidised. Root unstaking is fee-free today, so this changes no payment now; it decides who
pays the day the chain charges.

**Design.**

- Adapter `_collect` (payout send): `require(minted >= amount - 1 rao)` and the token balance must
  then be at least `parkedTokens + amount` (the cushion pays the rao; parked tokens never do).
- Adapter `convertExact`: 1 rao covered from the native reserve, never from `parkedNative` (3.2).
- Adapter fee overpayments: none exist (the callers pay the quoted fee in the same transaction);
  surplus is swept, not refunded.
- Vault unstake shortfalls and the leaver's cost (round 8 H-1; the ruling above): `_unstake(wei)`
  takes the amount to raise in wei and returns `unmet`, the part of it the stake its removals took,
  at face value (rao × 1e9), did not cover, and `cost`, what the chain kept of that stake:
  `ceilToRao(sub0(stake taken × RAO, received))`, both in wei; liquidity is read once, before the
  walk, and never re-read; the walk stops once `unmet <= T` (3.10.11), `T = SHORTFALL_TOLERANCE_RAO ×
  RAO` (1,000 rao), and reverts `UnstakeShort` past it. `redeemDirect`: `available =
  _sub0(_sub0(balance, ED_RESERVE), pendingPayouts)`; if `available < out`, `(unmet, cost) =
  _unstake(out - available)`; pay `paid = floorToRao(_sub0(out, unmet + cost))`, reverting
  `ZeroAmount` when that is 0, and emit `ShortfallAbsorbed(unmet + cost)` when non-zero (the leaver
  bears its exit's whole cost). `processPayouts`: the fee valve below, whose entries bear their
  walk's cost too. Either way an exit is funded only by the liquid TAO above `ED_RESERVE` and the
  other reservations, read before it, plus its own receipts; reservations still staked (the normal
  case, with `idleBufferBps` 0) are neither counted as cover nor needed as cover.
- Fees: `MIN_REDEEM_VALUE` 0.03 TAO and `MAX_FEE_ABS = MIN_REDEEM_VALUE / 2` = 0.015 TAO as
  constants; `maxFeeAbs` is **hard-bounded on both sides**, `MIN_FEE_ABS = 0.01 TAO <= maxFeeAbs <=
  MAX_FEE_ABS` (revision 5: the lower bound is what keeps a fee ceiling from being a freeze
  instrument, section 3.6; it sits near the launch value, not at 0.002, as L-5 asked). `_deductFee`:
  a burned entry whose net would be 0 reverts the chunk `FeeTooHigh`; only a 0-share entry may be
  dust. **A bounce can never be dust either, at any setting**: a bounced deposit is at least the
  front door's `minDeposit`, which `setCaps` hard-bounds at `>= 2 × MAX_FEE_ABS` = 0.03 TAO (L-5;
  0.04 at launch), and its fee share is at most `MAX_FEE_ABS` (0.015), so I-19 ("bounces are never
  released") and the dust rule do not conflict; the dust branch is defensive.
- **The fee valve and the shortfall rule, one rao-aligned formula (L-3; round 7 L-3).**
  `processPayouts` is `payable`; every amount below is in wei, and `fee`, `charged`, `topUp` and
  `net` are whole rao:

  ```
  ceiling = max(sum * maxFeeBps / 10_000, maxFeeAbs)
  fee     = ceilToRao(bridge.quoteFee(...))           // the chain floors transfers to rao
  charged = msg.value >= fee ? 0                      // the full top-up (R4): the caller pays it all
          : floorToRao(min(fee, ceiling))             // the entries' share, split by _deductFee
  topUp   = fee - charged                             // whole rao
  net     = _deductFee(entries, charged)              // sum - charged, each entry floored to rao
  require(msg.value % RAO == 0)                       // BadValue
  require(msg.value >= topUp)                         // FeeTooHigh
  own     = balance - msg.value                       // the caller's value never counts as cover
  need    = net + charged
  free    = _sub0(_sub0(own, ED_RESERVE), pendingPayouts - remaining)   // read once, before the walk
  if (free < need) {
      (unmet, received, cost) = _unstake(need - free + T)   // unmet <= T at face value, else UnstakeShort
  }
  if (cost != 0) {
      net = _deductFee(entries, charged + cost)       // the entries bear the walk's cost too, pro rata
      require(no entry newly left with 0)             // FeeTooHigh: a walk's cost never makes an entry dust
  }
  require(free + received >= net + charged)           // UnstakeShort: what is sent comes from free and the receipts
  send net + fee; refund msg.value - topUp to the caller, last
  ```

  The walk's stake, taken at face value, decides whether it can serve the chunk; what the chain kept
  of it is the chunk's own cost, which its entries bear pro rata like the fee (each share rounded up,
  each net floored to rao, a bounce included), so a removal fee never falls on the holders and
  refuses a chunk whose entries can pay it only in the walk's whole-removal corner below. The
  receipts decide, not a re-read balance, so other batches' reservations, normally still staked, never block a chunk (round 8 H-1). The walk asks
  1,000 rao more than the chunk needs, which covers the second split's rounding (at most a rao an
  entry) unless the walk ended after a whole removal with less than a rao an entry of that cushion
  left. The walk stops once `unmet <= T`, whether or not other positions remain, so this can happen
  with other positions holding plenty; it needs a removal fee. That chunk refuses `UnstakeShort`
  (never paid from `ED_RESERVE` or another batch's reservation) until the balance or the positions
  move: a deposit, an exit, a stake or a reward. The cushion stays idle at full value, the holders'
  as any liquid TAO, for `stakeIdle`. The entries pay at most the ceiling of the fee and their walk's
  cost; anyone can unfreeze a lonely small chunk when the CCIP fee rises above `maxFeeAbs`'s hard
  ceiling of 0.015 TAO; a chunk whose
  walk's cost would leave an entry nothing (a chain fee of at least 0.00375 TAO a removal for the
  smallest entry) reverts `FeeTooHigh` and is paid once liquid TAO covers it without a walk:
  deposits net the queued payouts (`stakeIdle` never stakes below `pendingPayouts`), or the idle
  buffer. `PayoutChunkSent` logs the walk's cost (`unstakeCost`). About +100 B (the walk's cost:
  Revision 23).
- **The full top-up (R4, an outside review).** The Bittensor adapter refuses a send below the
  chain's live minimum stake (`floor()`, `_checkAmount`: `BelowConversionFloor`), and it checks the
  chunk's **net**, after the entries' fee. Today's floor is 0.002 TAO (read live 2026-11-11) against
  `MIN_REDEEM_VALUE` 0.03 and the 0.04 TAO minimum deposit, but a chain that raised it so that
  `sum >= floor > net` would make every such chunk revert, a top-up of the fee above the ceiling
  would not help, and a bounce-only batch cannot be cancelled (`NothingToCancel`), so it would be
  stuck. So a caller whose value covers the **whole** fee pays all of it: `charged` is 0, `topUp` is
  `fee`, the entries are sent their gross (`net == sum`, each entry whole rao already), and the
  caller is refunded `msg.value - fee`, last. Nothing else moves: the vault pays exactly the `sum` it
  reserved (so no holder's price falls, I-35), no entry is sent less than before (each is raised to
  its gross), and the caller only pays. A caller sending the top-up alone, as the keeper does, is
  charged as before. A walk's cost still falls on the entries (the leaver pays, Revision 23), so a
  chunk that walks at a cost can still fall under a raised floor; it is paid once liquid TAO covers
  it without a walk. **The residual:** a floor raised above the gross itself (about 0.04 TAO for the
  smallest bounce, `minDeposit`; 0.03 for the smallest burn) is refused at the quote, and waits for
  the floor to fall or for a new route (a rail through the registry). The full top-up has no
  separate ask: any caller whose value covers the fee pays all of it. So **the keeper's rule** (and
  the hand route's): send exactly the top-up the quote asks, never more, unless choosing the full
  top-up, which it does when it reads `BelowConversionFloor`; and a quote that falls between the
  read and the send to at or below the value sent makes that caller pay the whole fee (its cost,
  never the entries'). The keeper's code follows at the merge. **The treasury keeps a small holding** (the
  founder's call; a runbook line in the off-chain round), so the supply never reaches 0 and vested
  rewards are never stranded at a supply of 0.
- The web forces "withdraw all" when the remainder would be worth less than `MIN_REDEEM_VALUE`, and
  says a remainder just above it can fall below by delivery if the price drops, in which case that
  entry is deferred and the user requests again (L-H).
- README note (M4): `MAX_FEE_ABS` is a TAO amount while the CCIP fee is a dollar cost; at 14×
  headroom a deep fall in TAO's price leaves payouts waiting until someone pays the excess (nothing
  lost); `payout.fee_ceiling` is the early warning.

**Alternatives rejected.** An owner-set tolerance; a per-entry dust refund to Base; paying from
`ED_RESERVE`; spreading a chunk's liquidity shortfall over its entries (stake the walk cannot take
still refuses); an asymmetric `_collect`; the holders absorbing the chain's cut of an exit's walk
(the earlier rule: a removal fee fell on the stayers, and froze an exit above T); a fixed cost
allowance charged before the walk; refusing every exit whose walk costs anything (a freeze the day
the chain charges); a caller top-up of a chunk's walk cost; charging a chunk's walk cost to its
shares entries only (a bounce-only chunk can walk while other batches' reservations are staked, and
could then never pay).

**Invariants.** I-12 *reserves untouched, per exit:* each exit is funded only by the liquid TAO
above `ED_RESERVE` and the other reservations, read before it, plus its own unstake receipts; after
it, `balance - ED_RESERVE >= min(before - ED_RESERVE, others' reservations)`, `before` being the
balance before the exit less any `msg.value`. I-13 *an exit is paid its value less its own cost, or
reverts:* a direct exit is paid exactly `floorToRao(out - unmet - cost)`, `unmet` (at most 1,000 rao)
being the part of `out - available` its walk did not take at face value and `cost` what the chain
kept of the stake it took, rounded up to rao, or reverts (`UnstakeShort` when more than 1,000 rao is
left untaken, `ZeroAmount` when nothing would be paid); a payout chunk is sent as queued less each
entry's share of `charged + cost` (`ceil(assets × (charged + cost) / sum)`, its net floored to rao),
in queued order, or reverts, never for want of liquidity that other batches' staked reservations
hold, as long as the positions the walk may take hold `need - free + T` at face value (under a root
hold every batch can revert, as in v3.1, which the idle buffer answers, 3.8) and no entry's share
leaves it nothing, except when the walk ends after a whole removal with less than a rao an entry of
T to spare and the second split's rounding leaves the entries more than the receipts; that chunk
refuses `UnstakeShort` until the balance or the positions move; its entries bear at most the
ceiling plus the walk's cost (up to one rao per entry, the rounding the vault keeps: rev 21), any
fee above the ceiling comes from the caller's `msg.value` (the whole fee, when the value covers it:
the entries then bear none and are sent their gross less only a walk's cost, R4), the rest
refunded in the same call, and a
chunk whose walk's cost would leave an entry nothing reverts `FeeTooHigh`. I-13's liveness
(Revision 10 L-1, the campaign's precondition): with no pair held, a due batch whose fee is within
the ceiling or topped up processes whenever the positions the walk may take hold `need - free + T`
at face value and every entry still nets above 0 after its share of the walk's cost, except when
the walk ends after a whole removal with less than a rao an entry of T to spare and the second
split's rounding leaves the entries more than the receipts; that chunk refuses `UnstakeShort` until
the balance or the positions move; stake missing is no precondition (`processPayouts` never reads
it). I-14 *no `PayoutDust` for an entry with shares, none for a bounce at any `minDeposit` the bound
allows, and none from a walk's cost.*

**Tests.** Unit: `setRemoveFeeRao(1)` exits (the leaver short by 1 rao, the chunk's entry short by 1
rao, its cushion idle whole); a dust exit below the shortfall is refused (`ZeroAmount`, nothing
burned); a 1,001-rao and a 50,000-rao fee are the leaver's in one removal (no second position
walked) and the entries' pro rata; a walk across two positions and a whole-position removal larger
than the ask charge every removal's fee to the exit; a chunk whose walk's cost would zero its
smallest entry, a bounce among them, reverts `FeeTooHigh` and is paid once a delivery's deposits
cover it; the walk's whole-removal corner (a 1-rao fee, the stake hotkey holding exactly
`need - free`, a second position 10 TAO) refuses `UnstakeShort` with the second position untouched
and is sent, the price unmoved, once a deposit moves the balance; the emptied vault's tail drains at
fees of 1, 1,000, 1,001 and 50,000 rao with no hole;
the last holder's exit at a fee with a reward vesting leaves no schedule hole; the
`PayoutFeeDust` zero-finalisation now
reverts `FeeTooHigh`; `setFeeLimits` refuses above 0.015 and below 0.01; a 0.029 TAO redeem defers
and a 0.03 TAO one burns; a 0.03 TAO bounce under a 0.015 fee is never dust and `setCaps` refuses a
`minDeposit` below 0.03; a 0.02 TAO fee on a lonely chunk reverts with no value, sends with a top-up
(entries charged the ceiling, the caller refunded the rest), and a re-entrant caller is refused at
the refund; the adapter's over-mint, 1-rao under-mint and 2-rao under-mint; two or more queued
batches at zero idle balance all processed, and a direct exit while a staked payout is queued, both
also under `setRemoveFeeRao(1)` (round 8 H-1); the full top-up (R4) through the real Bittensor
adapter: the floor raised after a bounce-only batch and a share-backed one queue (`sum >= floor >
net`), each refused `BelowConversionFloor` with the top-up alone and sent at its gross with the
whole fee, the caller paying exactly the fee and no one else gaining, and a value one rao short of
the whole fee charged as a top-up, so it still reverts. Fuzz: a removal fee in [0, 0.02 TAO], the entries'
(Revision 23; was a shortfall in [0, 2 × tolerance]). Invariant: I-12 to I-14 and I-35 with the
mock's fee switched on at random, up to 0.02 TAO, the handler running several concurrent
batches at `idleBufferBps` 0 and checking, with the root hold off, that `processPayouts` itself
stays live (the whole-removal corner counted apart, where it must refuse exactly `UnstakeShort`).

### 3.4 Exit limits for one door today and two tomorrow

Covers: scope 2, 3, E5, the README's second-door precondition, the reviews' M-4 and origin notes,
and round 5's L-4.

**Root cause.** The outflow allowance is per front door and the redeem authority is per address,
both designed for one door; a second door multiplies the allowance and lets the same address on
another chain redeem shares it did not deposit, and a deposit made on another chain for an address
that belongs to this one hands those shares to whoever controls the address here.

**Design.**

*Global window.* One global window (`burnWindows[bytes32(0)]`) beside the per-door ones;
`_burnRedeems` clips each redeem to `min(perDoor, global)` (after the holder's shares held a day,
3.5's hold, R4) and adds the burn to both; the global
allowance is `max(globalOutflowBps × base, minDailyOutflow)` (the floor shared);
`setOutflowLimits(bps, minDaily, globalBps)` with **both percentages hard-bounded to [2,000,
5,000]** (revision 5: the 20% launch value is the lower bound, so no outflow setting can freeze an
exit; increases scheduled, decreases to the bound immediate) **and the floor `minDailyOutflow` to
[10 TAO, 100 TAO]** (L-4: lowering it was an unstamped exit delay). At launch both are 20% and the
floor 10 TAO. **The windows are fixed, not rolling (R6 F, ChatGPT R2; the contracts README's soft
spot since v2).** A window opens at the first burn after the last one ended and lasts 24 hours, and
the base does not shrink between burns (burned value stays staked until its payout unstakes it), so
a window's rest burned just before it ends and a fresh allowance just after can go seconds apart:
about two allowances in a span of seconds across a reset (`VaultDelivery.test_claimF_...`), under
two in any 24 hours, and up to eight in 7 days. A fixed window never allows less than a rolling one
would (a live window's burns are a subset of the last 24 hours'), so the run and SOG figures, which
count honest payouts, stand as measured; they assume each delivery lands at or after its window's
reset (`_runAtTheFloor` delivers on the second of each reset), and on the real chain a delivery a
moment before the reset finds a spent window and is deferred whole, so the 59%, 67% and 74% are
best-timing figures (the 36%, a delivery every other day, always clears a window). The doubling
matters for a loss only to a party that captures payouts (a compromised lane, section 5's Chainlink
row; an activated rogue door) and only if the defender answers later than the delivery. A leaky
bucket (`burned` drained at one allowance per day instead of reset, no new storage) would hold a
burst to about one allowance; it is queued, with the pro-rata allowance, for before other people's
money (section 5's list), not made here: it touches the exit path's gas model, the lens and three
off-chain readers, for no protection at launch, where the 10 TAO floor is 25% to 100% of the vault.

*Origin by chain.* `redeemOrigin[user]` (since R4 `holders[user].origin`, beside the door hold's fresh shares in one
slot, 3.5) ∈ {0, a chain id, `DIRECT_ORIGIN`}; `activeDoors[chain]`
counts active doors per chain. A door mint sets origin 0 to the door's chain and never changes a
non-zero origin; a door deposit for a user whose origin is another chain **bounces** (M-4,
`DepositBounced(..., ForeignOrigin)`); a deposit for a `DIRECT_ORIGIN` address mints; a direct
deposit always sets `DIRECT_ORIGIN`; `_burnRedeems` skips a user whose origin is not the door's
chain; `setRedeemOrigin(uint64 chain)` by the holder on Bittensor to any chain with
`activeDoors[chain] > 0` or to `DIRECT_ORIGIN`; the cut and a cancel's re-mint do not touch origin,
so an address whose only shares came from the cut must `setRedeemOrigin` or `redeemDirect` (the
treasury runbook says so). A door replacement on the same chain strands nobody. With one door, a
stranger's `depositFor` can set a fresh address's origin to Base (R6, Fable's review I-3): only an
address whose shares never came from a deposit is exposed, the treasury, so its
`setRedeemOrigin(DIRECT_ORIGIN)` right after deploy is mandatory if the treasury is ever a contract
(until it runs, a stranger's 0.04 TAO deposit for it pins its origin to Base, and a twin at its
address on Base could then redeem the cut's shares); with the launch treasury an EOA, the same key
holds both addresses and nothing is at risk.

*A stated future residual (two doors):* a stranger could pin a fresh address's origin to chain B
by depositing for it there first, so the real user's later Base deposit bounces (a 24-hour bounce;
the user sets the origin from Bittensor or uses another address). Not reachable with one door.

*A stated residual (one door; R6 B, ChatGPT R2; the founder's decision: listed as a known risk, no
contract change before the self-funded launch): a Base contract wallet's Bittensor twin.* Origin by
chain gates doors only, never `redeemDirect`, which burns from and pays `msg.sender` with no origin
check, no door hold and no allowance. So whoever controls a Base holder's address on chain 964 owns
its shares there. An EOA, an EIP-7702 account included, is the same party on both chains. A contract
wallet need not be: a legacy `CREATE` wallet or contract (its address follows its deployer's nonce,
which another party may hold on 964), or a `CREATE2` wallet whose owner set changed after deployment
(a Safe or a Coinbase Smart Wallet redeployed on 964 from its public setup calldata comes up with its
*initial* owners). That twin can `redeemDirect` everything the address holds, door-credited shares
included, at any time: the claim has no deadline (it can wait for the balance to grow), and the
wallet's own door redeem cannot outrun it. It can also `setRedeemOrigin(DIRECT_ORIGIN)` (even while
paused, with no shares) to lock the wallet out of its door (every Base redeem then `RedeemSkipped`,
its fee spent) and keep collecting later Base deposits for the address, which still mint for a
