`DIRECT_ORIGIN` holder. Nobody else loses (the exit is fairly priced and pays its own walk). The
self-funded launch cannot hit it (the founder deposits from Ledger EOAs and never `depositFor`s a
contract address); before other people's money the site must refuse contract-account deposits other
than EIP-7702 ones (or at least any that is not a recognised `CREATE2` wallet with unchanged owners)
or warn properly (the stake form's note today says only that such a wallet "can only leave Cascade
through Unstake here", not that someone else may), and warn before any deposit into an address whose
origin is `DIRECT_ORIGIN`; no site guard helps a wallet that calls the contracts directly, or a third
party's `depositFor`. A contract fix is a redeploy and an open design decision for the paid audit:
an opt-in "door only" lock sent from Base through the front door (direct exit stays the default),
or a registered Bittensor withdrawal address carried in a door message; never a forced Base consent
for every direct exit, since the direct exit is EOA holders' way out when the bridge or a door
fails. `SmartWalletTwin.t.sol` pins the three cases (the take, the lock-out, the harvest).

*E5* not added: `maxEntryValue` is the knob (open question 7).

**Alternatives rejected.** A single global cap; origin per door; origin per share; opt-in from
Base; minting a foreign-origin deposit anyway.

**Invariants.** I-15 *per window, each door's burns ≤ its allowance and all doors' burns ≤ the
global allowance* (per fixed window, not per rolling day: about two allowances can burn seconds
apart across a reset, R6 F). I-16 *the origin rule:* a door on chain C burns only for origin C, never mints
for another chain's origin (it bounces), never changes a non-zero origin; a direct deposit always
leaves `DIRECT_ORIGIN`. I-17 *a direct depositor's shares are never burned by a door unless the
holder set that chain afterwards.*

**Tests.** Unit: two doors on two chains (skips, bounces, `setRedeemOrigin` to a chain with and
without a door, the `DIRECT_ORIGIN` flip, a second Base door burning for the first's depositors, the
cut and a cancel leaving origins alone, the global window binding across doors, each door's own
window still counting); `setOutflowLimits` refusing below 2,000 and a floor outside [10, 100] TAO.
Invariant: a second handler door on another chain; I-15 to I-17.

### 3.5 Front door and payouts: close by id, a closed room rule, the pot rolled forward, bounces neither released nor hostage

Covers: scope 8, BASE-1, E3, the reviews' H-2, L-G and M-3, and round 5's L-7 and L-8.

**Root cause.** `closeBatch()` closes whatever is open; the room rule in `canClose` was a free
grief while `_closeBatch` paid the pot surplus to the caller (R6b U4 brought the rule back, and R7
widened it to the half rule, below);
`_closeBatch` pays the pot surplus to the caller; `cancelPayoutBatch` released bounces (H-2)
or, refusing, held a batch hostage to one (L-G).

**Design.**

- `closeBatch(uint64 expectedId, uint256 minValue) payable`: `WrongBatch(open.id)`, then
  `BatchNotReady`, then an empty roll (returns `msg.value`, ignores `minValue`), then
  `BatchTooSmall()`, then the close. **The half rule (R7; the founder's choice of 2026-11-15, after
  the room rule of R6b U4, which reversed decision 6).** Besides its interval, the deposit list at half
  its limit (R12, below) and a full redeem list, `canClose()` is true, while the open batch holds deposits (`open.totalDeposits != 0`), when any of:
  (a) `2 × open.totalDeposits >= maxBatchValue` (at least half the batch value); (b)
  `open.totalDeposits + minDeposit > maxBatchValue` (no minimum deposit fits: the room rule, which
  also covers a batch at or over a lowered `maxBatchValue`, the old "full by value"); (c)
  `netForwarded + 2 × open.totalDeposits >= depositCap` (at least half the room left under the cap,
  written without a subtraction, so a cap lowered below `netForwarded` cannot underflow); (d)
  `netForwarded + open.totalDeposits + minDeposit > depositCap` (the cap's room rule). Each entry is
  at least `minDeposit` when made, so while the deposits are at least the minimum (b) implies (a) and
  (d) implies (c): (b) and (d) decide alone only after `setCaps` raises `minDeposit` above a batch
  already holding deposits, and (a) and (c) cover normal operation (R8, the R7 review's I-1). A batch
  with no deposits (redeems only, or empty) waits for its interval or a full redeem list whatever the limits.
  Why: a deposit is cancellable for free until the close (since R11 from five minutes after its last
  deposit, while the door is unpaused with its primary enabled, R12), so one address could hold every batch just under
  a limit, refusing every other deposit (`BatchValueFull`, or `CapExceeded`), and cancel before each
  close. R6b's room rule closed such a batch only once no minimum deposit fitted, so the holder kept
  the room at exactly `minDeposit` (9.96 of 10 TAO at launch, the R6b review's Low-1): every honest
  deposit above 0.04 TAO was refused, and the holder cancelled each 0.04 that landed. Any rule
  "closable when the room is below X" lets a holder keep the room at exactly X; the half rule ties the
  close to the holder's own capital instead. **The residual, stated: a holder can still refuse
  deposits above about half a batch** (or half the cap's room). At half less a rao (4.999999999 TAO
  at launch) the batch waits, and a deposit above the room (5.000000001 TAO) is refused; but every
  smaller honest deposit lands and makes the batch closable at once, and to keep it waiting the holder
  must cancel as much as each honest deposit adds, so the honest deposits stay and go with the batch.
  That is the hold that keeps a batch under the line; over the line, the rule binds a holder only
  through a close that lands before its cancel (below), so a holder that timed its cancels between
  the keeper's rounds refused every deposit until someone closed the batch (Revision 34), until the
  cancel cooldown (R11, below) made the close land first.
  The room rule's cap clause was also true for good at a filled cap (`netForwarded + minDeposit >
  depositCap` with nothing in the batch: the steady state of a filled cap, of a cap of 0, and of a
  minimum above `maxBatchValue`), so anyone closed every redeem-only batch at once, each close
  drawing the pot for its bridge fee (the R6b review's Low-3); the deposits condition removes that.
  `setCaps` and the constructor bound `minDeposit` by `MAX_BATCH_VALUE` too (R7; Appendix B), so the
  sums cannot overflow (`setCaps(type(uint256).max, ...)` panicked every close). v3.1 dropped the
  room rule as a free grief because a close then paid its closer the pot's surplus; since v3.2 the
  pot's remainder rolls forward (a closer never gains, I-21) and `minValue` guards the closer against
  a cancel landing first, so an early close only forwards the deposits, and a close that lands while
  the batch is over the line commits the holder's capital. **That close must land in time (R8, the
  R7 review's L-1; availability only).** The keeper's round runs once a minute, so a holder can
  deposit a full batch just after one round and cancel before the next: every honest deposit reverts
  `BatchValueFull` (or `CapExceeded`) for most of each minute, at two Base transactions a minute to
  the holder, and a mistimed cancel leaves its capital committed, which is no loss. `closeBatch` is
  permissionless, so the refused depositor's own close commits the holder: the web offers "close this
  batch, then deposit" when a deposit does not fit and `canClose()` is true. The monitor warns on
  heavy cancelling in a batch (the five-readings `batch.deposits_blocked` misses the flapping).
  **The cancel cooldown (R11, Revision 35; the founder's choice) closes that hold.** `cancelDeposit`
  reverts `CancelTooSoon(cancelableAt)` until `CANCEL_COOLDOWN` (5 minutes, five of the keeper's
  rounds) after the caller's last deposit, before every other check: `deposit` and `depositFor`
  write `lastDepositAt[user]` (the entry's owner, whoever pays; declared after every earlier state
  variable), and `cancelableAt(user)` reads it plus the cooldown. A holder's full batch therefore
  stays at least five minutes, so the keeper's next round (a minute later), or the refused
  depositor's own close, finds it over the line and commits it. One address cannot get round it:
  its top-up restarts the clock for its whole entry. With more addresses a lift over half can still
  be cancelled back within a round, but only out of deposits at least five minutes old, which were
  in the batch at a round at least five minutes earlier, when it was under half: over any five
  minutes the cancels add up to less than half a batch (or half the cap's room), so the batch is
  near full for at most about one round in five, between under-half holds
  (`FrontDoorClose.test_aLiftOverHalfCancelsOnlyAnOldDeposit`), instead of most of every minute.
  That bound holds only through keeper closes that land: an aged entry's one-rao cancel between the
  keeper's read and its close voids a close whose `minValue` is the total it read (`BatchTooSmall`),
  while the fresh fill, uncancellable for five minutes, waits; so the keeper names a lenient
  `minValue` on a batch it closes early (R12; `FrontDoorClose.test_anAgedOneRaoCancelVoids...`, both
  half rules; the keeper that does so is v3.2-wire's). **No cooldown while paused or with the primary
  disabled (R12, Revision 36).** While the door is paused (closes still land, but no deposit can, so
  there is no fresh deposit to hold) or its primary bridge is disabled (no close with entries can
  land: `_primary()` reverts `PrimaryBridgeDisabled`),
  `cancelableAt` reads 0 and the cancel goes at once: I-20 is whole again, and a disable cannot become
  a hold on depositors whose clocks `depositFor` keeps restarting. An unpause, or the primary enabled
  again (its reopen, or `setPrimaryBridge`), brings the cooldown back from the user's last deposit;
  another bridge's disable changes nothing. What is left, stated: the under-half hold above, and the
  honest closer paying the bridge fee for a deposits-only batch (the pot is fed by request fees only;
  a forced early close costs its forcer 25 minimum entries, 1 TAO, or 5 TAO of value, all committed,
  and costs the pot, or the keeper, about 0.0016 ETH a close (interpolated), bounded by the 40 TAO
  cap; the pot pays first, so the owner sweeps the carried pot regularly);
  the deposit list's hold (below) is closed by the list's half rule (R12). The contract cure for what
  is left, a deposit fee the cancel does not return (the contracts README's item 29), stays deferred
  (section 5). The cost: unpaused, a cancel waits at most five minutes; `depositFor(victim, ...)`
  restarts the victim's clock, a grief that gifts the victim a deposit of at least `minDeposit` each
  time (0.04 TAO at launch, about 0.48 TAO an hour to hold one victim's cancel; the contract's floor
  for a gift is `MIN_DEPOSIT_FLOOR`, 0.03 TAO, should `minDeposit` be lowered), bounded by the batch's
  room and the cap's room (a gift tops up the victim's entry, which needs no list slot) and by the
  batch's close, and the pause stops it and frees the victim's cancel at once (`depositFor` is
  `whenNotPaused`; R12). Gas: a
  deposit's write (about 22,100 the first time, 5,000 after, cold), a cancel's read (about 2,100,
  and about 6,500 more for R12's bypass: the pause flag, the primary and its enabled flag, cold).
  Honest batches under half of both limits still
  wait for their interval (`FrontDoorClose`); at the launch's 10 TAO a batch reaching 5 TAO is
  closable at once, so under a heavy flow of deposits the keeper sends up to twice the batches it
  would have. The keeper's and the monitor's copies of the rule follow (off-chain, R7). The door is
  19,275 B with the bypass and the list's half rule (R12; 19,209 B with R11's cooldown, 19,041 B at R7, 19,058
  at R6b, 19,102 before it). **The deposit list's hold
  (R6, Fable's whole-system review L-2; the contracts README's checklist item 29), closed by the list's
  half rule (R12, Revision 36).** Fifty minimum deposits (0.04 TAO each, 2 TAO in all) fill the open
  batch's list, so every other depositor reverts `BatchFull`; a deposit pays no fee into the pot, so
  someone pays a bridge fee to close the full batch. Until R11 a holder's full cancel just before each
  close left the batch not ready (`BatchNotReady`) and its deposit refilled it; under R11's cooldown
  the holder kept the list one entry short at each keeper round with 49 aged entries and cycled one a
  round. Now `canClose()` is true once `2 × open.deposits.length >= maxDeposits` (25 of 50, and 25 of
  49 at an odd limit: `>=`), while the redeem list still needs its full limit (a full one closes
  itself on its pot). With the cooldown that ends the hold: a round that finds the batch waiting found
  at most 24 entries, so a list filled after it holds at least 26 entries too young to cancel, and the
  next round finds half the list or more and commits them. Other depositors are refused `BatchFull`
  for at most a round, at 26 minimum deposits (1.04 TAO at launch) committed a round, which fills the
  40 TAO cap in under 40 minutes. No storage, getter or layout change. The cost: honest batches of 25
  depositors or more close at the next round (it matters only under about 0.2 TAO a depositor, where
  the bridge fee per depositor is about 30 to 40% higher than in a full list); a forced close costs
  its forcer 25 entries (1 TAO) where it cost 2 TAO; and the keeper's Base cap (0.01 ETH a day) is
  spent by about 7 TAO of forced closes instead of about 10. No exit is touched (the redeem list is
  separate) (`EndToEnd.test_theDepositListHoldEndsAtTheKeepersNextRound`,
  `FrontDoorClose.test_theDepositListsHalfIsTheEdge`).
- `_closeBatch`: fee from the pot first, then `msg.value`; the caller gets back only its unused
  `msg.value`; **the pot's remainder rolls forward into the next batch's `feePot`** (L-7: no close
  calls the treasury, so a reverting treasury cannot block the 51st requester's self-close, and the
  pot carries every earlier surplus); the self-close passes `msg.value = 0`; a pot still short makes
  that request revert `InsufficientFee`, and the full batch is closeable at once, so the requester
  closes it with a top-up and requests again. `sweepPot(amount)` (owner) sends to the **immutable**
  `treasury` at most `potCarried`, the remainder carried at the last close and still in
  `open.feePot`: never `totalFeeCredit`, never the open batch's own request fees, which pay for its
  close (round 6, Info 2). `BatchClosed` gains `potCarried`.
- **`MAX_BATCH_INTERVAL = 24 hours`** (from 7 days; revision 5, decision 1): a request is never more
  than a day from its close by the timer, so `batchInterval` is not a freeze instrument.
- **The front door's `pause` stops only `deposit`/`depositFor` and `deliver`**; every exit call
  (`requestRedeem`, `requestRedeemWithDelivery`, `cancelRedeem`, `cancelDeposit`, `closeBatch`,
  `claim`, incoming payouts) works while paused, `cancelDeposit` at once (R12: no cooldown while
  paused, nor while the primary is disabled; under R11 it waited out its five minutes). With
  parking on the other side, a redeem-only
  batch sent during a rail incident parks and is dropped.
- Inbound payouts accepted from any **known** bridge (`bridgeKnown`, ever activated), bounded by
  `TokensNotReceived` and the per-bridge replay map (H-2 i, 3.2).
- **`cancelPayoutBatch` carries bounces forward:** it re-mints every remaining entry with `shares >
  0` the value it is owed, never more shares than it burned (`min(shares, sharesForDeposit(assets,
  assetsBefore, supplyBefore))`, one `totalAssets()` and one supply read before anything is
  released, rounding down; Task 34c), and leaves the batch holding only its remaining 0-share
  entries (in their original order, 3.10; new `count`, `payoutsHash`, `remaining`; `cursor` 0;
  `chunk` continued);
  `pendingPayouts` falls by the share entries' whole value (`reminted`) only; a batch with no
  bounces is deleted as today.
  The bounces then wait in their own queue for `processPayouts`. `PayoutBatchCancelled(key,
  reminted, bouncesCarried)`.
- **Removing a door with queued payout batches (M-4):** left as a stated residual rather than a
  per-door pending-batch counter: the only honest reason to remove a door with payouts queued is a
  rogue-door incident, where holding its payouts is the point, and an honest door replacement is
  done after its queue drains (`verify` lists a door's pending batches and refuses the removal file
  while any exist). The counter would cost bytes to protect against an owner acting outside its own
  runbook. **Activating an already-active door reverts** (`AlreadyActive`), so `activeDoors` never
  double counts.
- **A door burns only shares held a day (R4, B-H1).** The first-come allowance could be taken
  every day at no market risk: a deposit and a redeem of the same shares in one batch (the vault
  credits a delivery's deposits before it burns its redeems), so every honest Base exit behind
  them that day was deferred, for a request fee and a bridge fee's share. So each holder has a
  bucket of fresh shares in its holder record, `holders[holder] = Holder{uint64 origin; uint64 since;
  uint128 fresh}`, the redeem origin's slot (redeemOrigin's before R4), so a new depositor's first mint
  writes one new slot, not two: a slot of its own cost about 25,800 gas of delivery need per new
  depositor, which put a 50-deposit batch past the lane's gas model, and no linear model of 100 entries
  then fits the 7,000,000 cap (the clock is `since`: `at` would be a reserved word in a later
  Solidity, and src compiles with no warning):
  - a mint for the holder by a door's deposit (L1's credit) or by `depositDirect` adds its shares
    to the bucket and moves `since` to the share-weighted mean of the bucket's and now,
    `since + (now - since) × minted / (fresh + minted)` rounded up (R4 S-L3; one `mulDiv`), when the
    bucket's last mint was within `STAKE_RAMP` (1 day, the ramp's constant, now in
    `CascadeConstants.sol`), and restarts the bucket at now with them alone after that. On average each
    share is still held a day, but a stranger's gift moves the clock by its share of the bucket only, so
    no daily gift keeps a holder's own fresh shares held past about a day after their own mint;
  - a door's burn may take only `balance - fresh` (floored at 0), fresh being the bucket's shares
    while `now < since + STAKE_RAMP` and 0 after. A request held whole is `RedeemDeferred`: nothing is
    burned, no allowance is taken, and the holder requests again. One held in part burns the older
    shares only, as a request above the balance is clipped. Shares minted in the same delivery
    count as minted now, so a deposit and a redeem in one batch burn nothing;
  - not written: a `cancelPayoutBatch` re-mint, which hands back shares the holder held before
    (never more than it burned) and adds no capital, and the reward cut, the treasury's share of
    rewards, not a deposit;
  - a stranger's `depositFor(victim)` holds only the gifted shares, never the victim's older
    balance (a per-holder timestamp would have let anyone hold any holder's door exit for 0.04 TAO
    a day), and, by the weighted clock, the victim's own fresh shares no more than about a day;
  - `redeemDirect` is never held: it takes no allowance, so there is nothing to crowd. It lowers the
    bucket to the balance it leaves (R6 A, ChatGPT R2 claim A), so `fresh <= balance` holds
    everywhere (a mint raises both, a door burn never takes the balance below it, a cancel's re-mint
    raises only the balance). Until R6 it left `fresh` as it was, which was wrong, not conservative,
    for the weighted clock: a later mint moved the clock only by its share of `still + minted`, and
    `still` counted shares that had left. Fifty round trips of 100 TAO in one block (`depositDirect`
    then `redeemDirect`, no fee, no hold) left about 5,000 TAO of burned shares in the bucket at
    t0, so a 150 TAO door deposit ten minutes short of a day later moved the clock only to about
    t0 + 42 minutes and was held under an hour, taking the next day's allowance ahead of day-old
    holders (R4 B-H1's crowding back for gas). Now the bucket holds only shares still held: a holder
    with aged shares beside the fresh ones it exits keeps those aged shares held in their place for
    the run, which gains it nothing over redeeming them through the door
    (`VaultDoorHold.test_aDirectExit...`; the invariant `freshOf <= balanceOf`);
  - the bucket's `uint128` (3.4 × 10^38) saturates rather than overflows, and never needs to: a
    mint is at most the share supply, which every mint keeps within 2,000,000 shares a wei of the
    assets (below half the launch price deposits bounce and the cut is skipped; a re-mint never
    exceeds what it burned), so about 4.2 × 10^31 for all the TAO there will ever be, and a balance
    is at most the supply, so a saturated bucket still holds the whole balance;
  - no event changes: off-chain code tells a hold from an allowance deferral by the lens's
    `freshShares(holder)` at the delivery's block, or from the holder's last `Deposited` with shares
    or `DepositedDirect` before it.

  Every first-come statement in this spec (below, section 3.6's row 1, section 5's owner-key row)
  means first-come among shares held a day.
- **No door burn while the primary bridge cannot send (R4, S-H1).** From ForeverMoney's migration
  announcement `depositLiquid` is closed for good, so no payout can be sent again, yet door redeems
  were still burned into a queue that could not pay, which only the owner's cancel undid (and each
  launch-door cancel stamps the clock, killing a pending replacement rail's activation). The vault now
  reads its primary bridge's `canSendPayouts()` once per delivery (a plain staticcall capped at
  150,000; fail-open: a call that fails or answers anything but one word reads open, which only a
  primary bridge with no such view reaches, since the Bittensor adapter's view never reverts and
  fits the cap; that is the one open path) and passes it to L1 (`BatchIn.payoutsOpen`); while it is false
  the day's allowance reads 0, so every door redeem is deferred (`RedeemDeferred`, the shares stay with
  their holders, nothing is queued or taken of a window), the same as a spent allowance. Deposits still
  mint (the notice's flush keeps its purpose) and direct exits are unaffected. ForeverMoney's pause
  closes the rail too, while it lasts. Batches queued before the notice are left as they are (the
  runbook, off-chain). A replacement rail made primary answers for itself, so its door redeems burn.
  **A disabled primary cannot send either (R6 D, ChatGPT R2 claim D).** The adapter's view reads
  only ForeverMoney's state and cannot see the vault's disable, so mid-replacement, with the front
  door's primary switched to the new pair first (or a disable inside that half-switched window), a
  batch delivered through the new bridge, enabled on the vault but not its primary, burned into a
  queue `processPayouts` refused (`PrimaryBridgeDisabled`) until the owner's switch. So the vault
  reads its own `bridgeEnabled[primaryBridge]` first: a disabled primary reads closed before its view
  is asked (the fail-open path is reached only for an enabled primary), and the redeems are deferred
  as above. The switch (`setPrimaryBridge`, immediate) or a Reopen of the primary (7 days) reopens
  the door's burns by itself. Operationally the vault's switch goes first, then the front door's, so
  a planned replacement never reaches the window (runbook section 8). **Two residuals of both
  deferrals.** A bounced deposit delivered while the rail reads closed still queues behind it (a
  bounce cannot be deferred or cancelled) and is paid once the rail is open or the switch is made, as
  for ForeverMoney's notice (runbook 7.7: leave it for the replacement rail). And a deferral drops
  the batch's requests: each holder asks again and pays the request fee again (and again each batch
  while the rail stays closed). They differ by batch under ForeverMoney's pause (Fable's whole-system
  review, I-2): a redeem-only batch carries nothing to convert, so the adapter delivers it and its
  redeems are deferred (dropped), while a batch with any deposit fails its conversion, parks whole and
  is delivered with its redeems after the pause. Under a disabled primary nothing parks for that
  reason, so a mixed batch's redeems are dropped too. The site tells a holder to ask again.
  **A stated residual beside S-H1 (R6b U6, the multi-agent review; Fable: Low): ForeverMoney's
  validator swaps its hotkey.** A full `swap_hotkey` moves every nominator's root stake, ForeverMoney's
  included, and removes the old hotkey, so `depositLiquid` on it reverts `HotKeyAccountNotExists` and
  `withdrawLiquid` reads nothing there; the adapter's three reads (pause, migration notice, migration
  finished) do not change, so `canSendPayouts()` still answers open and door redeems from a
  redeem-only batch burn into a queue `processPayouts` cannot send, while batches with deposits park.
  It clears when ForeverMoney's operator calls `migrateValidator` (instant); otherwise the owner
  cancels. It is bounded by the daily allowance for as long as it lasts, and no principal is lost.
  A fourth rail read (`stakedValueRao`) was considered and not taken: the swap is transient and
  ForeverMoney's whole product waits on the repoint, burned redeems pay by themselves once it comes
  (a deferral would spend the request fee and make the holder ask again), and a precompile-priced read
  could close the view for good. The monitor alerts on a zero stake under ForeverMoney's configured
  hotkey, and the guardian's vault pause stops the burns.
- **A clipped entry leaves a redeemable remainder (R4, S-L1).** When an entry is clipped to the entry
  limit or the day's allowance and the holder would keep more than 0 but less than `MIN_REDEEM_VALUE`
  (no door burns that), the burn is that much less: the holder keeps exactly the least shares worth
  `MIN_REDEEM_VALUE` at the batch's price (`ceil(MIN_REDEEM_VALUE × (S + 1e6) / (A + 1))`), which its
  next request burns. A smart wallet is never left with shares no door will burn. **The residual:** a holder with a day of price exposure
  and enough capital can still crowd the allowance (deposit one day, request the next, rotating its
  capital and its addresses); the hold raises the cost from nothing to a day's exposure on twice
  the capital. An allowance shared pro rata among a delivery's requests is the deeper fix, before
  other people's money. **A second residual (merge 7b, accepted by the controller): the sliver after
  an owner's cancel.** A cancel re-mints each entry its owed value in shares at the cancel's price,
  rounded down (`sharesForDeposit`), so an entry owed exactly `MIN_REDEEM_VALUE` (0.03 TAO, what the
  clip leaves and the smallest a door burns) can come back worth just under it, which every door
  then defers. It is tiny (under 0.03 TAO), arises only after an owner's cancel, and stays with its
  holder: `redeemDirect` pays any amount, and a price rise or another deposit lifts it back over the
  minimum; a smart wallet waits for one of those. Revisit before other people's money. **A third
  residual (Task 42's long run, accepted by the controller): the orphan at a supply of 0.** A holder
  who exits directly with `acceptMissing` while stake is missing is paid at the floor price,
  min(visible, booked), and forfeits its share of the missing stake to the holders who stay (I-1).
  When it is the last holder there are none: once the stake comes back (anyone's `addHotkey` of the
  swapped hotkey, I-6), the vault holds it at a supply of 0, owned by the virtual offset's 1e6 shares
  (42.7 and 49 TAO in the long run's two counterexamples). There is no on-chain recovery at a supply
  of 0: no exit, reward cut or adoption releases it, and a first depositor gets its own deposit less
  rounding, nothing of the orphan (the owner's forfeit path was not traced exhaustively). It counts
  in `totalAssets`, so it uses up `depositDirect`'s cap (`maxTotalAssets`: 42.7 TAO under the
  launch's 44 leaves about 1.3 TAO of direct deposits until the owner raises it), and as stake it
  enters the base of the day's outflow allowance. It also sets the share price at about the orphan
  over 1e6, and the orphan keeps earning, and any donation adds to it, so every later mint rounds
  away up to orphan / 1e6 (0.000044 TAO a mint at 44 TAO, more as it grows; `sharesForDeposit` rounds
  down), in the vault's favour: the value accrues pro rata to the virtual shares and the holders of
  the moment. The treasury's permanent share holding (runbook 3.4) keeps the supply above 0, so the
  forfeit goes to the treasury and the other stayers instead, from where it can be returned off
  chain. And outside that corner (the review's point beyond the diff): a consenting leaver's forfeit
  goes to whoever stays, so a stayer who calls `addHotkey` right after another holder's
  `acceptMissing` exit takes that holder's forfeit. The guard (relist the hotkey before any nudge to
  the floor price) is the controller's carry for the UI and the runbook, and the UI copy needs it
  before other people's money. Revisit before other people's money.
  **A fourth residual (the rerun of Task 42's fix round 2, accepted by the controller): a leaver's
  forfeit vests to whoever holds.** A holder who leaves, through the door or directly, is paid at a
  price that leaves out the rewards not yet vested and those not yet claimed, so it forfeits its
  share of both to the holders who stay; and a door leaver's queued payout stays staked until it is
  paid, earning for those holders meanwhile. All of it reaches the holders as the schedule vests,
  and a claim restarts the schedule on what is left and the claim, over 30 days from then, whatever
  the stake did since the rewards accrued (`_vest`): after a large exit takes stake out, the
  schedule vests faster than the smaller stake now accrues, and whoever holds then gets more than
  its share of the accrual, a new entrant timed after a big exit included. What it takes beyond its
  accrual share is bounded by its share of what the departed stake earned and had not yet vested:
  entrant share x the departed stake's last vesting period of net rewards (about 1.5% of the
  departed stake at 18% a year net), out of the stayers' windfall. No one's principal is at risk, no
  stayer loses what its own capital earned, and the leaver ends worse off than if it had stayed. The
  vault-favour campaign's time-share bound credits the attacker this recapture, funded by the stake
  that left: each of its exits whose walk took booked stake (a payout chunk or a direct exit)
  credits net rate x that stake x 30 days, for 60 days (the rerun's counterexample: its 38.985 TAO
  payout, about 26.2 TAO of it still staked, earned about 0.32 TAO for the holders over 24.5 days,
  but its walk took only 6.19 TAO of stake, crediting 0.0916 TAO, of which the vest above the accrual
  spent 0.023 TAO; the attacker had taken 0.000533 TAO beyond its accrual share). Revisit before
  other people's money (the cure: per-holder reward accounting, a rewards-per-share accumulator).
  A fall in the reward rate does the same with a rate in place of stake (R7, the favour campaign's seed
  9402): the owner moving stake to a weaker validator, or the chain's own rates falling, leaves the
  schedule holding rewards earned at the old rate, so it vests above the new accrual for up to a
  period, and a holder through it takes its share of the difference (0.0027 TAO beyond its time share
  in the campaign's case); the campaign credits it like a departure.
  A fifth residual, an adoption pushing rewards later (Task 42's fix round 4), was fixed before
  launch by the adoption drip (Revision 27). What is left of it lives inside that drip: an adoption
  still re-spreads what is left of the drip over the longer of `rewardVesting` and what it has left, so
  anyone (7 days after the last adoption, with a surplus of at least the chain's minimum stake, about
  0.002 TAO) can push up to a quarter of the drip later, where a timed entrant shares it. It is bounded
  and touches no claimed reward, but the drip is not only gifts: the surplus can be stake that came
  back after a write-off, the former holders' principal. Revisit before other people's money.

**What a holder's exit takes, with the bounds (M-3, L-8).** A Base-side exit is: a request, a close
(within `batchInterval` ≤ 24 h, or sooner when the list is full: 50 requesters close a batch by
themselves), delivery (minutes), the payout delay (≤ 48 h), a payout chunk, a claim. Per delivery
each entry is clipped to `maxEntryValue` (≥ 10 TAO, hard-bounded) and all entries to the day's
allowance, `max(20%, 10 TAO)` of the ramped-in base (hard-bounded below), **one allowance shared
first-come** among shares held a day (the hold above). So a holder of up to 10 TAO whose request fits the day's allowance leaves in one
delivery; a whole-cap holder (40 TAO) asking alone needs four request-days plus the delay; and in
a run, everyone asking at once, the floor still lets about 79% of a door's base be burned in 7
daily deliveries and 96.5% in 15, in arrival order; each day's burns are paid out a payout delay
later. So within the first 7 days, what a rogue door scheduled as the run starts leaves the users
(section 3.6's row 1 SOG), the worst case found is about 36% paid. Its moves are each at once and
stamp nothing. On Base, the thief raises the front door's `batchInterval` to its 24-hour bound, and
rolls the open batch every time it falls due empty (anyone may roll an empty batch), so a delivery
comes only every other day (with fewer requesters than `maxRedeems`, which close a batch by
themselves). On the vault, it raises the payout delay to its 48-hour bound, and since
`processPayouts` reads the live delay, the batches queued under the old delay are stranded. The
defence: one request waiting in the batch when it falls due, anyone's, stops the roll. But only the
requests waiting in a batch are delivered, so about 59% is paid only when every request is waiting as
each batch falls due (the batch interval and the payout delay raised: the first close still waits a
day). The payout delay raised alone leaves about 67% paid; neither raised, about 74%. Guidance:
request your exit within the first 3 days (no user controls when others request). These figures
assume no thief-held shares in the queue. A thief with both keys and capital of its own, its
requests first, takes the first-come allowance ahead of honest holders. About 1.4 times the honest
holdings empties the honest share paid before the rogue door when every request is waiting (about
0.6 times with the rolls, 2.1 with the payout delay alone, 2.8 with neither; 3.8 takes every day's
allowance of the seven). The measured case first loosens the entry limit (a 48-hour scheduled
item), so one entry takes a day's allowance; at the launch's 10-TAO limit the thief would need many
addresses instead. If both keys are stolen, honest holders may get little or nothing out. Keeping
the two keys apart is the defence. The hotkey freeze, a residual of the same class: a thief with
both keys and a validator of its own lists that validator's hotkey, moves the stake hotkey and the
stake onto it (`setStakeHotkey`, `moveStake`: 48 hours, Ordinary, so an honest guardian vetoes it),
then swaps the hotkey on the chain; the stake then sits under a hotkey the vault does not list, and
every exit, direct ones included, is refused `StakeMissing` with no stamp while the vault reads
OPEN. A stranger can re-list a wholly swapped hotkey, and exits work until the next swap; if the
chain lets the old hotkey keep part of the position, the four slots run out after the fourth swap
and the freeze is permanent (on the mock; unverified on the chain: Tasks 63 and 64 ask whether a
partial retain is possible and what a swap costs). Meanwhile, under a whole swap, a payout already
queued is refused `UnstakeShort` until someone re-lists the hotkey; under partial swaps it is paid
from what the listed hotkeys still hold.
This is the worst case found among the
tightenings and rolls; Task 41 checked `test_T6`'s fee limits and `setUnstakeGas` (neither strands
more) and hotkey management (the freeze above). A contract change could restore more; that is the
founder's decision
(Task 65). For Task 65: any change must be sized against the roll of empty batches too, and a longer
wait before a new front door activates is the leading candidate; snapshotting the payout delay does
not cover the rolls. A longer wait does not address crowding: an allowance shared pro rata among the
waiting holders, not first-come, would. **Direct exits on Bittensor (an EOA there) are immediate and
have no window**; "days" bounds in this spec are Base-side bounds.

**Alternatives rejected.** Pro-rata pot return; a closer's tip; a settable treasury; the pot paid to
the treasury inside a close (L-7); a permissionless pot sweep (anyone could empty the pot before a
self-close); refusing the cancel while bounces are unpaid (L-G); a per-door pending-batch counter
(bytes for a runbook violation).

**Invariants.** I-18 *ETH identity:* `balance == feePot + totalFeeCredit`, across every close (the
remainder carried) and `sweepPot`. I-19 *bounces are never released to the holders:* the sum of
unpaid 0-share entries equals the part of `pendingPayouts` they reserve, across every cancel, until
paid (and never dust, 3.3). I-20 *a paused front door refuses no exit call* (R12, Revision 36: whole again, a deposit's cancel
at once, with no cooldown while paused; under R11 it waited up to 5 minutes, `CANCEL_COOLDOWN`, after the
caller's last deposit, refused `CancelTooSoon`, never `EnforcedPause`). I-21 *a closer never
receives more than it sent.* I-36 *a door never burns fresh shares (R4, B-H1):* after a door
burns a holder's shares, the holder still holds at least its fresh shares, those its bucket counts
(the shares minted for it by a door's deposit or `depositDirect` in one run of mints each within
`STAKE_RAMP` of the clock, held while `now < since + STAKE_RAMP`, the clock the run's share-weighted
mean mint time, lowered to the balance a direct exit leaves), and a holder's fresh shares never exceed
its balance (R6 A), so every share the clock weighs is one still held and no share younger than about
a day takes any of the allowance; and (S-H1) no door burns while the primary bridge cannot send
payouts for a cause the vault reads: while the vault has it disabled (R6 D), or while its own
`canSendPayouts()` reads false (ForeverMoney's pause and migration; not a swap of ForeverMoney's
validator hotkey, nor a lane's pause or rate limit, which revert sends that are retried: R6b U6).

**Tests.** Unit: a stale id; `minValue`; the roll; the pot carried forward and nothing to the
requester or the treasury in a close; a reverting treasury and a self-close; a short pot, then a
top-up close and the request; `sweepPot`; the room-rule grief now `BatchNotReady`; `canClose`
against the close rule recomputed from this section (`testFuzz_canCloseMatchesTheRule`, R8);
`setBatchInterval` refusing above 24 h; every exit call on a paused door; a payout from a
known-but-disabled bridge credited; a cancel with a bounce re-minting the shares and leaving a
one-bounce batch that is later paid; a bounces-only cancel reverting `NothingToCancel`; a double
activation reverting; the hold (R4): a deposit and a redeem by one holder in one batch, ahead of
another holder's redeem (the first deferred, the second paid, neither window taken by the first),
a redeem a day and a second after the mint burned, `depositDirect` then `setRedeemOrigin` then a
door redeem held, a cancel's re-mint not held, a stranger's daily gift holding only the gifted
shares, and hourly gifts not holding the holder's own shares past about a day (S-L3); (S-H1) a door
redeem deferred while the primary cannot send (a closed stub rail, and through the real adapters a
ForeverMoney migration notice and pause), a direct exit paying meanwhile, a replacement rail made
primary burning again; (S-L1) an entry clipped to the entry limit and to the allowance leaving 0 or
at least `MIN_REDEEM_VALUE`, and one asking for less burned what it asked. Invariant: `closeBatch` with the id; `cancelQueued` checking carried bounces; I-18 to
I-21; I-36 against the handler's own model of each holder's bucket. Fork: the real-router close
with the id.

### 3.6 Owner powers: one scheduler, hard bounds, and the exits machine

Covers: hard question 9, E4, the write-off's delay, checklist item 20, the size budget, and the
reviews' H-1, M-6, H-A, M-A, L-B, L-D, revision 5's decisions 1 to 4 with H-2 (ii), L-1, L-2, L-6
and L-8, round 5's L-1, L-2 and L-6 with the founder's decisions D1 and D2, and round 6's M-1 (the
signed stamp), L-1 and L-2.

**Root cause.** Every owner setter acts at once, and the only delayed things each have their own
propose/activate/cancel code. A stolen owner key can redirect stake, shorten the guardian's window,
swap the oracle and swapper, or raise the reward cut in one block; and with any delay scheme alone
it can freeze exits, hand ownership away, install its own guardian and then a rogue door, and drain
with nobody able to leave in between (H-1). Revisions 3 and 4 answered with a growing predicate
over the exit limits, a heartbeat and a standby pair, and each review found a hole in the newest
part (a below-floor cut deadlocked governance forever, since the loosening that would reopen needed
an open vault). Revision 5 removes the moving parts: **nothing an owner can set can freeze an exit,
because the setters' bounds do not allow it**, and only four instruments remain, each recorded per
actor.

**The scheduler** (`CascadeBase`; the vault and the front door inherit it; the staker stays a plain
`Ownable2Step`, its routes and fees owner-immediate within their hard caps):

```solidity
enum Delay { Param, Registry, Guardian }                       // 48 hours, 7 days, 8 days
enum Kind  { Ordinary, GuardianSwap, Reopen }
uint64 public constant EXECUTE_WINDOW = 7 days;
struct Item { uint64 scheduledAt; uint64 readyAt; Delay class; Kind kind; bytes4 selector; uint64 readyBlock; }   // one slot (L-1, L-B; readyBlock R1-A L-3)
uint64 public immutable MS_PER_BLOCK;                          // constructor, 61..60,000 ms (no seconds value fits); the deploy calibrated so half the delay in blocks takes 5/6 of it at today's speed (1.67 times faster blocks) than the chain's today (Bittensor 7,200 against 12 s, Base 120 against 0.2 s; Revision 28); kept on the target, the lenses ask it
uint64 internal pendingSince;                                  // transferOwnership's execute; acceptOwnership's gate (R1-A L-1)
mapping(bytes32 id => Item) public scheduled;                   // id = keccak256(data); data must be exactly the selector's canonical length (L-6)
bytes32 private transient _executingHash;                      // the executing item's id, keccak256 of its calldata
function schedule(bytes calldata data) external onlyOwner returns (bytes32);   // kind and class from the selector and, for activations, from whether the target is known; requires gate(kind)
function execute(bytes calldata data) external onlyOwner;       // from readyAt and readyBlock until it lapses (both windows passed, Revision 28); requires gate(kind) and clock(kind) < scheduledAt; deletes; sets _executingHash; self-calls; bubbles a revert
function cancel(bytes32 id) external;                           // owner: any; guardian: Ordinary only
function prune(bytes32 id) external;                            // anyone, once it lapses: past readyAt + EXECUTE_WINDOW and past readyBlock + ceil(EXECUTE_WINDOW * 1000 / (2 * MS_PER_BLOCK))
function stampBySig(uint64 asOf, bytes calldata sig) external;  // anyone relays; the owner's EIP-712 signature; lastOwnerClose = max(lastOwnerClose, asOf)
```

| Kind | Selectors | Gate (for `schedule` and `execute`) | Clock older than `scheduledAt` at `execute` | Who cancels |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ordinary | every Param-class loosening; `activateFrontDoor` / `activateBridge` of a target **never active before**, and of a door **the guardian removed** (round 5 L-6), an owner-removed door the guardian has since removed included (Task 10b); `writeOffMissingStake`; `transferOwnership` | `exitsOpen()` | `max(lastOwnerClose, lastGuardianClose)` | owner, guardian |
| GuardianSwap | `setGuardian` | `!ownerHold()` | `lastOwnerClose` | owner only |
| Reopen | `activateBridge` of a bridge **that was active before**; `activateFrontDoor` of a door that was active before and **the owner removed**, and the guardian has not removed since (made known only by the constructor or an activation, L-1) | none | `lastOwnerClose` (round 6 M-1) | owner only |

Shared rules: `execute` deletes the item, sets `_executingHash` (the item's id, the hash of its
calldata), self-calls and bubbles any revert; `onlyOwner` on a scheduled setter is
`onlyOwnerOrScheduled`, admitting the self-call only while `_executingHash == keccak256(msg.data)`
(`_scheduledCall`, M-6), so a nested self-call with other calldata is refused; no scheduled setter
makes a state-changing EVM external call (3.2); `schedule` reverts when the item's clock is not
older than `block.timestamp` (an instrument earlier in the block, or a signed stamp as of now, would
kill it at birth; round 6 Info 6) and when an activation's target is already active
(`AlreadyActive`, as at `execute`, round 6 L-2); an item lapses once both its window in time
(`EXECUTE_WINDOW` after `readyAt`) and its window in blocks (half `EXECUTE_WINDOW` in calibrated blocks
after `readyBlock`, Revision 28) have passed, and anyone may `prune` it; re-scheduling restarts the delay; a multi-field setter is loosening if any field
loosens; a tightening never needs scheduling and is never refused; `acceptOwnership` is the transfer
item's tail (R1-A L-1): `transferOwnership`'s execute records `pendingSince`, and the pending owner
accepts only while `block.timestamp <= pendingSince + EXECUTE_WINDOW` (else `LapsedItem`),
`exitsOpen()`, and `max(lastOwnerClose, lastGuardianClose) < pendingSince` (else `ExitsNotOpen`);
`transferOwnership(0)` and acceptance clear it, so a pending owner expires, and any closure, or an
owner stamp as of the execute or later, refuses it at once, past any guardian veto; an item is also
ready only from `readyBlock =` the scheduling block `+ ceil(delay * 1000 / (2 * MS_PER_BLOCK))`, half
its delay in calibrated blocks (R1-A L-3; half since the R1 re-review; in milliseconds since Revision
28). An item is never executable until that many blocks have been produced since the block that
scheduled it, so on a chain whose blocks stop in a halt, Bittensor's (the vault's), the guardian and a
relayed stamp always get that many live blocks (a halted chain cannot consume the veto). The count is
cumulative (R6 G, ChatGPT R2): a halt that begins once those blocks are in and spans `readyAt` leaves
the item executable in the first block after the resume, where a cancel races the execute
(`BlockFloor.test_aHaltAfterTheFloorSpanningReadyAtLeavesItReadyAtTheResume`); the guardian's window
was the live blocks before the halt, and a halt that starts earlier holds the item until the rest of
them come after the resume. The founder's response budget is therefore the floor's live blocks (40
hours, 5.8 days, 6.7 days at 12-second blocks), not the full delay. The deploy
calibrates `MS_PER_BLOCK` so half the delay in blocks takes 5/6 of it at today's speed, 1.67 times faster blocks
than each chain's today (Bittensor 7,200 ms against 12 s, Base 120 ms against 0.2 s; Appendix B), so on Bittensor the floor is about 0.83 of each delay in
live blocks at today's speed (40 of a 48-hour item's hours, 140 of a 7-day item's, 160 of an 8-day
item's), stronger than the original half, and still about 0.42 after a 2x speed-up. Base is not so
protected, and never was under R1-A L-3: it gives each block number a fixed timestamp (genesis
plus n blocks of its block time, piecewise across the 0.2-second fork, exact across its past
outages), and an outage's blocks are filled in at their own timestamps (the sequencer's catch-up,
or derivation past the sequencing window), so the front door's `readyBlock` is reached whenever its `readyAt` is,
met by catch-up blocks rather than live ones. After a Base outage the protection is the guardian's
cancel (by L1 forced inclusion too, which keeps the sender for an EOA guardian such as the Ledger
and needs ETH on L1) and the owner's stamp; the front door's window in blocks matters
only if a fork slows Base's blocks to more than twice the calibrated time (a point for the auditor).
`MS_PER_BLOCK` takes 61 to 60,000 ms, else `InvalidParameter`: 60 or less is refused so that a block
time given in seconds, the unit before Revision 28, is never taken for milliseconds (2 on Base would
make a 48-hour item wait about 100 days, 12 on Bittensor about 1,000, behind an immutable). A slower
chain reaches `readyBlock` later, and the lapse never lets that strand an item:
an item lapses only once its window in time and its window in blocks (half `EXECUTE_WINDOW` of
calibrated blocks after `readyBlock`: 2,520,000 on Base, 42,000 on Bittensor) have both passed. On
blocks up to about twice the calibrated block time apart (today's speeds and any faster) the blocks
pass first, so the floor never binds at readyAt and an item lapses exactly as its time window ends, as
before; on a slower chain the floor makes loosening changes wait longer than their delay (in
proportion to the slowdown) and the window in blocks keeps each item executable after its floor. The
margin: delays start lengthening once blocks come more than twice the calibrated time apart, 1.2 times
today's, which is Bittensor above 14.4 s and Base only by a fork (were Base back at 2-second blocks, a
48-hour item would wait about 16.7 days and its window in blocks would last about 58 days). A halt
holds the blocks, so an item whose window in blocks was open when the chain halted keeps the rest
of it after the resume. Exits, pauses, tightenings and cancels read no blocks and are unaffected. The
window and `prune` read both clocks; `renounceOwnership` stays disabled; `Scheduled(id, kind, selector, readyAt,
data)` carries the calldata so the monitor can decode every item's arguments (D1).

**The signed stamp (round 6 M-1, the founder's decision; simplified in revision 8).** With one
Ledger, a copied seed lets the thief run a gas sweeper that drains the owner's and the guardian's
accounts and front-runs every refill, so the founder's own transactions never land (964 has no
private bundles): without a remedy, a copy is a theft. `stampBySig(asOf, sig)`, on the vault and
the front door, takes an EIP-712 signature by the **owner's** key and can be submitted by **any**
account. It sets `lastOwnerClose = max(lastOwnerClose, asOf)` and nothing else: no hold, no funds,
no gas from the key, no power the owner lacks (it can cancel anything). Every kind runs on the
owner's clock (Reopen too, else a copied key's Reopen of a known-compromised bridge would outlive
every stamp), so a stamp kills every item scheduled at or before `asOf`. There is no guardian
stamp: an uncompromised guardian cancels directly, a compromised one would add nothing, and the
owner's stamp kills a superset (round 7 Info 9a).

- *Signature.* Domain `EIP712Domain(string name,string version,uint256 chainId,address
  verifyingContract)` with `"Cascade"`, `"3.2"`, the chain id and the contract, hashed once in the
  constructor into an immutable `DOMAIN_SEPARATOR` (no ERC-5267); type `OwnerStamp(uint64 asOf)`. A
  minimal `ecrecover` with `v` in {27, 28} and `s` in the lower half of the curve order, the signer
  equal to `owner()`. ECDSA only, never ERC-1271 or `SignatureChecker`: a Safe owner relays its own
  transactions, and an EIP-7702-delegated owner on Base must not let delegated code answer.
- *Time (round 7 Info 9b).* `asOf <= block.timestamp <= asOf + STAMP_MAX_TTL` (1 day): the stamp's
  time is never in the future and a stale one cannot surface later. The signer sets `asOf` to the
  signing time, and **a replay can never kill an item scheduled after `asOf`**, since the clock only
  moves to the maximum; so there is no nonce (it would cost a slot and let a copied key burn
  pre-signed stamps). This is the form `max(clock, deadline - STAMP_MAX_TTL)` with the as-of time
  signed directly, so no short validity can back-date it.
- *The CO walk.* Daily stamps (`asOf` = t, t + 1 d, ...) pre-signed for a bot, each relayed as soon
  as its `asOf` passes, kill every item within about a day of its scheduling, while every item waits
  at least 48 h, so none executes. On demand, the founder signs `asOf` = now and any funded account
  relays it. `StampedBySig(asOf, relayer)`. About +350 B in each contract, hand-rolled
  (OpenZeppelin's EIP712 and ECDSA would cost 0.8 to 1.2 KB).

**Ordering, for the honest owner (round 7 Info 5).** An owner instrument (at `block.timestamp`) or
a signed stamp (at its `asOf`) kills every owner-clock item scheduled at or before its time, the
founder's own `transferOwnership` to a Safe included. So stamping actions come first (an owner
pause, launch-door batch cancels) and the items to keep are scheduled after them, and after the
`asOf` of the last stamp the founder signed; replays of that stamp cannot touch them.

**Why `Reopen` is owner-only to cancel, and covers only bridges and owner-removed doors (L-2; round
5 L-1, L-6).** A guardian-cancellable bridge Reopen would let a stolen guardian hold the rail about
15 days; owner-only cancel bounds it at 8, **in the right order**: on day 8 execute `setGuardian`
first and the Reopen second (inside its 7-day `EXECUTE_WINDOW`), since a Reopen landed before the
swap is re-closed by the thief at once. A bridge stays a Reopen because the compromised-rail path
(row 3) must reopen the old one; the trade-off (round 6 L-2) is that a stolen owner key can likewise
Reopen a known bridge the guardian disabled, in 7 days, which the guardian answers by disabling it
again when it lands. A door is removed only when rogue (a buggy one is paused on Base and replaced
from OPEN first), so **a door the guardian removed becomes unknown**: its re-activation is Ordinary
and guardian-cancellable, and a stolen owner key cannot bring it back past an honest guardian
without replacing it first (8 days, then 7). **The guardian may also remove a door the owner removed
(Task 10b).** Otherwise a stolen owner key would remove an active rogue door before the guardian
could, leaving it known, and its Reopen, which the guardian cannot cancel, would bring it back in 7
days. The guardian's removal of a Removed door makes it unknown (it is not counted out again: the
owner's removal did that) and deletes the door's pending Reopen, if any, with `Cancelled` by the
guardian (one door has one canonical calldata and one id, 3.10.8). On the launch chain, while
`launchDoorRemovedBy == Owner`, it makes that hold the guardian's: it records the guardian, stamps
the guardian's clock and emits `ExitsClosed`, as had the guardian removed the launch chain's last
door itself (row 2 SG: the swap on day 8, the door's Ordinary activation on day 15). Were the
owner's record left, the converted door could no longer be reopened, the owner's hold would refuse
the Ordinary activation and `setGuardian`, and nothing might ever clear it (I-26). Which
launch-chain door's removal made the record is not kept, so converting any owner-removed
launch-chain door moves it; the record-making door's Reopen, if that door is still Removed, still
clears it. The owner can never remove an unknown door (`PeerNotActive`), so it cannot turn the
guardian's removal back into its own, nor a door's Ordinary way back into a Reopen. A Reopen of any
owner-removed launch-chain door clears the launch door's hold, whoever made it (the instruments
table); the guardian keeps two answers: remove the reopened door again (the launch chain's last: its
hold and stamp return), or, before the Reopen runs, remove that owner-removed door (the Reopen
dies). No activation of an active door can be pending (`schedule` refuses an active target, and one
canonical calldata is one id), so a guardian's removal of an active door leaves nothing scheduled
earlier to bring it back (round 6 L-2); its removal of an owner-removed door deletes that door's
pending Reopen (Task 10b: the delete round 7 Info 4 dropped while it was unreachable). The accepted
price: a stolen guardian that removes the launch door holds it 15 days and a block when the owner
holds nothing, up to 22 while the honest owner's own disable of the vault's primary is outstanding:
that owner hold refuses `setGuardian` until the primary's Reopen (7 d), so OPEN comes at 15 days and
the door's return at 22 (I-26); a stolen guardian cannot cause that alone. To keep that reachable,
**executing `setGuardian` clears `launchDoorRemovedBy` and `primaryDisabledBy` when either reads
`Guardian`** (a hold lasts no longer than the guardian who made it; R1-B M-1: with the bridge half
missing, a stolen guardian that disabled the primary one block before the swap held the launch door
about 22 days); `setGuardian` to the current guardian is allowed, and is how the owner clears an
honest guardian's door hold (round 6 L-1). The vault may then read OPEN while the launch chain has
no door, with direct exits only until one is activated, the exposure row 4 already states, or while
its primary stays disabled until the Reopen (exits through that bridge wait for it; a contract reads
OPEN with its primary disabled just as with no launch door). The SG bound stays 15 days and a block
(with no owner hold outstanding, above):
a disable in the swap's own block stamps the guardian's clock in it, which refuses an Ordinary item
scheduled in that block, so the door's activation is scheduled a block later (Task 40's review, M-1).

**Hard bounds instead of a limits predicate (decision 1).** The four exit-limit setters keep their
loosening direction (scheduled) but can no longer reach a freezing value: `setOutflowLimits`
percentages in [2,000, 5,000] bps and its floor in [10, 100] TAO; `maxFeeAbs` in [0.01, 0.015] TAO
(a fee above it paid by the caller, 3.3); `maxEntryValue >= 10 TAO`; `payoutDelay` in [1 h, 48 h];
the front door's `batchInterval <= 24 h`. Every launch value sits at a bound or inside the range.
`limitsOpen()`, the setter stamps and the limit rows of the scenario table are gone with them. The
invariant this buys: **from every state the instruments can create, the honest owner reaches OPEN
without a redeploy** (I-26, below), which the revision-4 predicate broke.

**The exits machine (vault).** Two recorded holds per actor, two clocks, four states.

| Instrument | Who | Records | Stamps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pause()` | owner or guardian | `pausedBy` (one pauser: a second pause reverts) | the pauser's clock |
| `unpause()` | owner | clears `pausedBy` | **nothing** |
| `removeFrontDoor` of the launch chain's last active door, or the guardian's of an owner-removed launch-chain door while `launchDoorRemovedBy == Owner` | owner or guardian | `launchDoorRemovedBy` (`activeDoors[launchChain]` reaches 0; the guardian's then replaces the owner's record); a guardian's removal of any door, active or owner-removed, also makes it unknown, and deletes an owner-removed door's pending Reopen | the remover's clock |
| `activateFrontDoor` on the launch chain (Reopen or Ordinary), or `setGuardian` while `launchDoorRemovedBy == Guardian` | scheduled | clears `launchDoorRemovedBy` (only these clear it, L-1, L-6); an activation reverts if already active | nothing |
| `disableBridge` of the primary | owner or guardian | `primaryDisabledBy` | the disabler's clock |
| `activateBridge` of the primary (Reopen), or `setPrimaryBridge` to an enabled bridge **while the current primary is disabled** (H-2 ii; the only time it is allowed, on both chains), or `setGuardian` while `primaryDisabledBy == Guardian` (the primary stays disabled until its Reopen; R1-B M-1) | scheduled / owner | clears `primaryDisabledBy` | nothing |
| `cancelPayoutBatch` of a launch-chain door's batch | owner | nothing lasting (a point closure) | the owner's clock |

```
ownerHold    = pausedBy == Owner    || primaryDisabledBy == Owner    || launchDoorRemovedBy == Owner
guardianHold = pausedBy == Guardian || primaryDisabledBy == Guardian || launchDoorRemovedBy == Guardian
exitsOpen()  = !ownerHold && !guardianHold
```

States **OPEN**, **O** (owner hold), **G** (guardian hold), **OG**. Ordinary items need OPEN at
scheduling and at execution with no stamp in between; GuardianSwap needs `!ownerHold` at both and no
owner stamp in between (so a guardian's pause, disable or removal never delays `setGuardian`, M-A);
Reopen needs no gate, only no owner stamp in between. `schedule` is gated as well as `execute`
because reopenings stamp nothing: an item scheduled during a closure and executed right after the
reopening would otherwise have waited with exits closed. Deliberate trade-offs: an honest owner
schedules its loosenings after its own incident, not during it; a closure by anyone during a notice
kills every pending Ordinary item.

Not instruments, by construction: every limit setter (hard-bounded), `setKeeper` (payouts are
permissionless; the stake and claim fallbacks run after 7 days), `setMaxTotalAssets` and the front
door's `setCaps`/`setMaxBatchValue` (deposits only), `setUnstakeGas` (a floor on forwarded gas),
`removeHotkey`/`moveStake`/`setStakeHotkey` (the walk reaches every listed hotkey), `setIdleBuffer`
up, `setBatchLimits` and `setBatchInterval` (whose loosening is scheduled only as belt-and-braces:
immediate tightenings, such as `setBatchLimits` or `setCaps`, can already make a batch closable),
`setRedeemRequestFee` (capped), `setRewardVesting` up (bounded at 30 days and applied from the next
claim only), the front door's `pause` (deposits and sales only), `sweepPot` (a short pot costs the
next self-close one top-up close, 3.5), and `setFloorFallbacks`, which no longer exists.

The front door's machine is the vault's without doors: `ownerHold = primaryDisabledBy == Owner`,
`guardianHold = primaryDisabledBy == Guardian`. **Removing a second chain's last door records and
stamps nothing** (only the launch chain is in the machine), so that chain's exits could close while
the vault reads OPEN: settle it before such a door goes live.

**The scenario table.** Columns: **SO** a stolen owner key (the honest owner cannot act); **SG** a
stolen guardian key; **SOG** both stolen; **CO** a copied owner key (honest owner and thief both
sign; the thief may sweep the key's gas, so the founder acts through signed stamps relayed from any
funded account; without `stampBySig` a copy under a sweeper is SOG); **HO** the honest owner during
a real incident. Each cell is the outcome and the bound; every cell is a test `T-<row>.<column>` in
section 9. "Days" are Base-side bounds (3.5); direct exits are immediate whenever the vault is not
paused, and "direct exits" means holders with an EOA on Bittensor: a Base smart-wallet holder has
none. While both keys share one Ledger (D2), a stolen seed is the SOG column and a copied one is CO
for both keys.

| # | Instrument | SO | SG | SOG | CO | HO |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | vault `pause` | state O: no takeover progress; every exit frozen until the thief unpauses; principal safe; **bound: indefinite freeze, no loss** (as today; the Safe prevents it) | state G: `setGuardian` schedulable and executable; replaced in 8 days; **bound: 8 days** | O or G at will; nothing to veto them; a rogue door needs **7 OPEN days** (no guardian swap is needed when both keys are held), in which the shared allowance lets about 36% of a door's base be paid out at the floor, first-come, in the worst case found: both keys stolen, each move at once and stamping nothing: the Base `batchInterval` raised to its 24-hour bound and the open batch rolled every time it falls due empty (anyone may roll an empty batch), so a delivery comes only every other day (fewer requesters than `maxRedeems`), and the payout delay raised to its 48-hour bound, which strands the batches queued under the old delay, since `processPayouts` reads the live delay; what is still queued at the rogue door's ready time the thief cancels and takes; the defence: one request waiting in the batch when it falls due, anyone's, stops the roll; but only waiting requests are delivered, so about 59% is paid only when every request is waiting as each batch falls due (the batch interval and the payout delay raised), with the payout delay raised alone about 67%, with neither about 74%; guidance: request your exit within the first 3 days (no user controls when others request); these figures assume no thief-held shares in the queue: a thief with both keys and capital of its own, its requests first, takes the first-come allowance ahead of honest holders, and about 1.4 times the honest holdings empties the honest share paid (about 0.6 times with the rolls, 2.1 with the payout delay alone, 2.8 with neither; 3.8 takes all seven days' allowance); the measured case first loosens the entry limit (a 48-hour scheduled item), so one entry takes a day's allowance, where at the launch's 10-TAO limit the thief would need many addresses instead; with both keys stolen, honest holders may get little or nothing out, and keeping the two keys apart is the defence; the hotkey freeze: a thief with both keys and a validator of its own can freeze every exit, direct ones included, with no stamp, by moving the stake onto its validator's hotkey (48 hours) and swapping that hotkey on the chain (a stranger re-lists a whole swap; permanently if the chain allows partial retains, unverified, Tasks 63 and 64; under a whole swap, a payout already queued is refused `UnstakeShort` until someone re-lists the hotkey; under partial swaps it is paid from what the listed hotkeys still hold); the worst case found among the tightenings and rolls; Task 41 checked `test_T6`'s fee limits and `setUnstakeGas` (neither strands more) and hotkey management (the freeze); a contract change could restore more, the founder's decision (Task 65; any change sized against the roll of empty batches too, a longer wait before a new front door activates the leading candidate; snapshotting the payout delay does not cover the rolls; a longer wait does not address crowding, an allowance shared pro rata among the waiting holders, not first-come, would); **bound: 7 days of open exits on the vault before any drain**; Base exits can be closed throughout (row 4, or a guardian's door hold cleared by `setGuardian`) | stalemate through signed stamps: the thief may pause and sweep the keys' gas, but every item it schedules dies to the founder's owner stamp, relayed from any account within the delay; principal safe, exits frozen while it pauses; without `stampBySig` this is SOG; **the Safe prevents it** and cannot be installed after a copy | pauses with the guardian account (no owner stamp, so a pending `setGuardian` or Reopen survives), investigates, unpauses, re-schedules; cost: 48 h to 8 d of delay (the loosenings 48 h or 7 d; a pending `transferOwnership`, Ordinary in the Guardian class, 8 d) |
| 2 | `removeFrontDoor` (launch chain's last door) | state O: no progress; direct exits only; re-activation is a Reopen (7 d, owner-cancellable); **bound: indefinite, no loss**; a door the thief removes ahead of the guardian is still the guardian's to remove: it becomes unknown, its Reopen dies and, on the launch chain, the hold becomes the guardian's, so it comes back only after a guardian swap (8 d, then 7) | state G: `setGuardian` unaffected; the door is now unknown (L-6) and no Ordinary activation can be scheduled under the guardian's door hold; the swap on day 8 clears its holds (the door's and, had it also disabled the primary, the bridge's), then the door's Ordinary activation (7 d); a stolen guardian may also remove a door the owner removed (its Reopen dies; the owner's launch-door hold becomes the guardian's, which the swap clears); **bound ≤ 15 days and a block** when the owner holds nothing; up to 22 while the honest owner's own disable of the vault's primary is outstanding: that owner hold refuses `setGuardian` until the primary's Reopen (7 d), so OPEN comes at 15 days and the door's return at 22 (I-26) | as row 1 | stalemate; funds safe | a buggy launch door is paused on Base and replaced from OPEN (7 d) before its removal; a removal meant to last is signed with the **guardian** account (the door becomes unknown; `setGuardian` to the same guardian clears the hold on day 8, a replacement activates on day 15); the **owner** account removes only for a temporary hold, which only a Reopen of that door undoes (7 d) unless the guardian account removes the door too, which makes the removal lasting (row 2 SG's way back, 15 d) |

| 6 | everything else (`setKeeper`, every limit setter, caps, Base `pause`, `setBatchInterval`, `setUnstakeGas`, hotkey management, vesting up) | no exit affected; no stamp; no progress gained (`setBatchLimits(1, 1)` makes each request close its own batch, so each requester pays a bridge fee; no exit is blocked), except hotkey management with both keys: the hotkey freeze (row 1 SOG), every exit refused `StakeMissing` with no stamp once the stake is moved onto a hotkey the thief's validator then swaps (under a whole swap, a payout already queued is refused `UnstakeShort` until someone re-lists the hotkey; under partial swaps it is paid from what the listed hotkeys still hold) | no exit affected | — | — | — |

**Initial values and ownership.** Everything launch-specific is a constructor argument (a `Params`
struct per contract: the front door's oracle, swapper, treasury, pool floors, batch and cap
settings; the vault's `launchChain`, treasury, reward cut, every limit, the initial front door; the
staker's route and fees); the owner is the founder's key from the constructor, so there is no
hand-off; a wrong argument is a redeploy. Every launch value is a constructor argument or a
tightening, never a scheduled item (appendix B).

**Reward vesting** (E4, I4): `rewardVesting` in [1, 30] days, 30 at launch (R4, the vault-favour review: the keeper
claims about monthly on a small vault, so a 7-day vesting let a holder present only during the vesting take about 4.3
times its time share of a claim; was 7), a decrease scheduled, an
increase immediate but bounded. A change applies from the next restart; a carried remainder never
vests faster than its own schedule: each restart of a drip (the claim schedule's: a claim's and a
deposit's write-down at a supply of 0, 3.1; the adoption drip's: an adoption's and that write-down,
Revision 27) snapshots into its period (`vestingPeriod`, `adoptVestingPeriod`) the longer of
`rewardVesting` and what is left of the old drip, so no change moves the price at the change, and a
decrease never releases a remainder sooner than it would have vested. An adoption never restarts the
claim schedule.

**What is scheduled, by contract.**

| Contract | Registry class (7 d) | Guardian class (8 d) | Param class (48 h) | Immediate (owner) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| vault | `activateFrontDoor`, `activateBridge` (Ordinary when new or a guardian-removed door, Reopen when known), `writeOffMissingStake` | `setGuardian` (GuardianSwap), `transferOwnership` (Ordinary) | `moveStake` (its destination in the calldata, 3.10.16); `setStakeHotkey` unless it follows moved stake (gifts count, so an immediate switch is the guardian's alarm, 3.1); the loosening direction of `setOutflowLimits` (up), `setMessageLimits` (values up, `minChunk` down), `setFeeLimits` (up), `setPayoutDelay` (down), `setRewardCut` (bps up or treasury change), `setRewardVesting` (down), `setMaxTotalAssets` (up), `setIdleBuffer` (down) | `removeFrontDoor`, `disableBridge`, `cancel`, `pause`/`unpause`, `setKeeper`, `cancelPayoutBatch`, `addHotkey`, `removeHotkey`, `setClaimGasRequired`, `setHotkeyDustRao`, `setUnstakeMarginRao`, `setUnstakeGas`, `setMinDirectDeposit`, `setPrimaryBridge` (only while the primary is disabled; re-checks the caps), every tightening |
| front door | `activateBridge` (Ordinary or Reopen) | `setGuardian`, `transferOwnership` | `setOracle`, `setSwapper`, `setMaxDeliverySlippage` (up), `setBatchInterval` (down), `setMaxBatchValue` (up), `setMinPoolLiquidity` (down) | `setCaps` (`minDeposit` ≥ 0.03 TAO), `setBatchLimits`, `setRedeemRequestFee`, `setKeeper`, `disableBridge`, `pause`/`unpause`, `setPrimaryBridge` (as the vault), `sweepPot`, every tightening |

**Alternatives rejected.** An external `TimelockController` as owner; keeping the registries plus a
parameter scheduler; delays alone (H-1); a live limits predicate (revisions 3–4: a below-floor cut
deadlocked governance); a heartbeat and a standby pair (revision 4: two more moving parts for a
residual the Safe closes properly); one freeze clock for everyone; reopenings that stamp;
guardian-cancellable bridge reopenings (a 15-day rail lock by a stolen guardian); Reopen for a
guardian-removed door (a stolen owner key brings a rogue door back past the guardian, round 5 L-6);
a per-door pending-batch counter (M-4, 3.5); refusing caps in the tooling without a Safe (a process
check binds nothing a hand-made transaction skips; D1); a nonce on the signed stamp (a replay is
harmless); a guardian-signed stamp (the owner's kills a superset); OpenZeppelin's EIP712 and ECDSA
(0.8 to 1.2 KB); ERC-1271 signatures (delegated code could answer); a stamp on the staker (capped
powers; a fresh staker is a deploy away); a switched-away primary made unknown (`bridgeKnown` also
admits late payouts, H-2 i, and the owner stamp kills a pending Reopen; round 6 L-2, considered).

**Invariants.** I-22 *no loosening's effect, no activation of a new or guardian-removed door or of a
new bridge, no write-off and no ownership change without a `Scheduled` at least its class delay
earlier in time and half of it in blocks (`ceil(delay * 1000 / (2 * MS_PER_BLOCK))`, with
`MS_PER_BLOCK` the chain's block time calibrated so half the delay in blocks takes 5/6 of it at today's speed (1.67 times faster blocks), Revision 28), before it
lapses, with
`exitsOpen()` at scheduling and execution and no stamp between; an ownership change takes effect at
`acceptOwnership`, inside `EXECUTE_WINDOW` of the transfer's execute, with `exitsOpen()` and no
closure or owner stamp since that execute.* I-23 *a tightening by the owner is never refused, and no
setter can reach a value outside its bounds.* I-24 *the guardian can cancel any Ordinary item,
cannot cancel `setGuardian` or a Reopen (a door's Reopen it ends only by removing that owner-removed
door, which makes the door's activation Ordinary, Task 10b), and can schedule nothing; the owner can
cancel anything.* I-25 *`onlyScheduled` and the scheduled path of `onlyOwnerOrScheduled` are
reachable only inside `execute`.* I-26 *reachability:* from every state the instruments can create,
a sequence of owner calls (unpause, `setGuardian`, Reopen items, `setPrimaryBridge` while disabled)
reaches OPEN, with no redeploy and no guardian action; over the four exit records it takes at most
15 days to OPEN and 22 to the launch door's return (the owner's own disable of the vault's primary
with the guardian's door record: `setGuardian` waits for the primary's Reopen; `test_TR_*` asserts
each way back's time). I-27 *a guardian's pause, disable or removal
never delays scheduling or executing `setGuardian`.* I-28 *while both chains are OPEN under the mock
rails, a launch batch closed now is delivered while the front door's rail is coherent, and a payout
sent now is credited while the vault's rail is (each half judged on its own rail: the launch door
active, the rail's primary enabled on its own side, and its other half accepted by the other side:
enabled on the vault for deliveries, known to the front door for payouts). OPEN alone implies none
of these (3.6, after a swap; and the one-sided rail switch, the residual below section 5's
table).* (the two-chain liveness; under the real rails it is covered by section 5's ForeverMoney
and Chainlink rows).
I-29 *every instrument stamps exactly the clock the table says, at `block.timestamp`, and the front
door's pause stamps
nothing; a valid owner-signed stamp sets `lastOwnerClose` to `max(lastOwnerClose, asOf)`, with `asOf
<= block.timestamp`, and changes nothing else, whoever relays it, so a replay changes nothing; an
invalid, future or expired one reverts; the guardian's removal of an owner-removed door writes the
door's state and, when one is pending, deletes the door's Reopen, and on the launch chain under the
owner's door hold it also records the guardian and stamps the guardian's clock; nothing else
stamps.*

**Tests.** Unit per setter (direction, bounds, window, lapse, multi-field, unknown selector,
canonical length, self-call outside `execute` refused, cancel rights per kind, a reverting setter
leaving the item, kinds decided for known, new and guardian-removed targets, `setGuardian` clearing
only a guardian's holds (the door's and the primary's), the guardian's removal of an owner-removed
door (its Reopen deleted, never counted twice, the owner's launch-door hold made the guardian's; the
owner's removal of an unknown door refused), a pending owner that expires and dies to a closure or a
stamp, an item not executable before its `readyBlock` after a halt (a 48-hour item waits 40 hours of
Bittensor's 12-second blocks after a 3-day halt), an 8-day item executable with an `MS_PER_BLOCK` half
the real block time and on blocks 1.9 and 3.7 times slower, the floor at both deploy values (each
class's `readyBlock` and its time at today's speeds), the lapse needing both windows (on blocks ten
times slower than calibrated an item runs after its floor and lapses only after both; on blocks up to
today's it lapses exactly as its time window ends; a halt longer than the window strands nothing), a
scheduled `moveStake` clamped to the position, `activeDoors` and `AlreadyActive`, `setPrimaryBridge` refused while the primary is
enabled, `Scheduled` carrying the calldata, `schedule` refusing an item its clock already covers and
an active target; `stampBySig` with a wrong signer, the other contract's or chain's domain, a future
and an expired `asOf`, a high-s signature, `v` outside {27, 28}, a contract owner, a replay and an
older `asOf` changing nothing, items scheduled at or before `asOf` killed and later ones kept; and,
as an invariant, no pending activation ever names an active door). The scenario table as
`test/governance/ExitsMachine.t.sol`, one test per cell (the list is in section 9), and `T-R` the
reachability sweep (every state the instruments can reach, then the owner's way back). Invariant: an
owner handler that schedules random setters and plays every instrument, a guardian handler that
cancels and plays its instruments, and a stranger that relays signed stamps (fresh and replayed);
I-22 to I-29.

### 3.7 Oracle: pool-depth views, a front-door liquidity floor on deliveries, a cardinality precondition, a buy-leg floor

Covers: scope 9, F-O1, hard question 5, the staker's buy leg, L-5 and L-8.
