**Root cause.** The oracle trusts two pools' mean ticks with no check that the pools are deep
enough for the mean to cost anything to move, and nothing on chain enforces the observation history
the 30-minute window needs (TAO/WETH had 600 slots in use against the 901 of 2-second blocks; 1,801
since Revision 28). The "spot read today" is the
idle pool's last trade standing for the window, not a same-block manipulation.

**Design.** The oracle constructor requires `observationCardinality >= MIN_CARDINALITY` on both pools,
`TWAP_WINDOW + 1` = 1,801 since Revision 28: one per second over the window, plus one. Uniswap writes at
most one observation per block timestamp (`Oracle.write` returns early on a repeated one), and block
timestamps are whole seconds, so at any block time of a second or less (Base's 0.2 seconds included,
five blocks to a timestamp) no pool writes more than one a second, and 1,801 slots always reach back
the window (the 901 of 2-second blocks covered 15 minutes at today's speed). The live pools hold 2,000
and 6,700 in use (2026-11-13), so the precondition already holds; a pool below it grows with
`pnpm ops cardinality --grow` (the in-use count jumps to the requested one at the next index wrap; ops
makes tiny swaps in distinct seconds until then). The oracle stays
ownerless and gains `meanLiquidity()` (harmonic-mean in-range liquidity per pool over the window)
and `taoValue(usdcUnits)`. The floor is a front-door parameter `minPoolLiquidity[0..1]` keyed by
position (constructor-set; tighten at once, loosen scheduled), checked by **`deliver` alone**
(`ThinPool`): the user then claims by hand. No staleness revert (quiet markets); the window stays
30 minutes. The staker's `stake` and `stakeWithPermit` require `taoOut >= oracle.taoValue(usdcIn -
fees) × 95%` (`MAX_BUY_SLIPPAGE_BPS = 500`, constant, every size: 3% was inside the TWAP's lag in
a rally; a size gate would leave most stakes with no floor).

**Alternatives rejected.** A longer window; a max observation age; the max of two windows; an
immutable floor; a 3% or size-gated buy floor; a Chainlink band (none confirmed on Base; open
question 8).

**Invariants.** I-30 *a delivery never settles below the user's, the keeper's or the TWAP floor.*
I-31 *no delivery settles while a pool's mean liquidity is below the floor; no stake settles more
than 5% below the oracle's value.*

**Tests.** Unit over `MockUniswapPool` with cumulative liquidity; the constructor refusing 600;
inverses within rounding; the staker at 6% and 4% short. Fork: the live liquidity read and the
buy floor against the PoC's front-runs.

### 3.8 The root unstake hold (V2, latent)

**Root cause.** `RootStakeUnlockInterval` (0 today, sudo-settable) locks a `(coldkey, hotkey)` pair
after any root add, remove or claim, a third party's `transfer_stake` onto a listed hotkey
re-stamps it, and the hold also gates `move_stake` (so `moveStake` and `removeHotkey` wait too).

**Design (the claim corrected, M-5).** A per-hotkey `try/catch` does not stop the attacker (every
listed pair can be stamped for 0.0004 TAO a day; claims stamp too). The defence is not needing an
unstake: `idleBufferBps` (≤ 1,000; increase immediate, decrease scheduled; 0 at launch) keeps
`idleBufferBps × booked` liquid, and a non-zero buffer at least `minDailyOutflow`; **once the
monitor reports a non-zero interval the owner raises it so the buffer covers `pendingPayouts` plus
a day's floor**.
`_unstake` forwards `max(unstakeGas, _sub0(gasleft(), EXIT_GAS_RESERVE) / positionsLeft)` to each
removal, counting only positions large enough to unstake (L-7), the last one getting the rest less
the reserve; `EXIT_GAS_RESERVE` = 2,000,000 for the post-walk work (Revision 29: 2.6 to 3.3 times the work Task 61's
fork measured, the live precompile's costs included; 1,200,000 before);
`unstakeGas` in [150,000, 5,000,000], immediate both ways, default 400,000; a locked pair is skipped
with `UnstakeSkipped`. Ops: round order payouts, stake, claims; the monitor watches the interval
(critical) and stamps on the pairs the deployment depends on; the upstream report names the
destination stamp, the claim stamp and `move_stake`.

**Operations under a hold (R6b).** The monitor's `root.hold_interval` pages critical, the guardian
pauses the front door's deposits (batches then carry none), and the founder funds the buffer with
`depositDirect`. The hold is 0 on the live runtime (spec 490) and no admin call sets it (a runtime
upgrade or a raw sudo storage write).

**Tests.** The three `RootHoldDoS` PoCs with the buffer sized to the queue; a second free pair;
every pair stamped and no buffer (`UnstakeShort`, bounded gas); `moveStake` and `removeHotkey`
under a hold; the gas split counting only unstakeable positions; `processPayouts` finishing after
the walk. Testnet: a refused `removeStake` under a capped gas.

### 3.9 Caps coherent with the adapters, and the claim gas

`ICascadeBridge` gains `maxMessageValue()`; the front door's `setMaxBatchValue`, the vault's
`setMessageLimits`, both constructors and `setPrimaryBridge` check the cap relations
(`AboveBridgeCap`). `claimGasRequired` is hard-bounded to [`MIN_CLAIM_GAS`, 54,100,000; `MAX_CLAIM_GAS`,
63,400,000] (about +20 B; round 9 L-3). The check reads the gas left before the call, and the call forwards 63/64 of
it, less its own cost, so the floor is the measured 53,200,000 divided by 63/64 (about 54.03M) and rounded up
(Revision 31: 53,200,000 until then, which forwarded only about 52.37M). The top, 60,000,000 until Revision 29, is the
most whose
claim still fits Bittensor's per-transaction limit, about 65,000,000 (75% of the block less 10% for initialization;
Revision 30), with the claim's own overhead (500,000 allowed, twice what was measured) and a 1,000,000 margin, so
the owner can raise the setting at once should the mainnet canary's real claim need more than the launch value.
The bounds set only the least a claim requires left (R6, ChatGPT R2's gas note): what is forwarded is the caller's gas
limit's, 63/64 of what is left, so a transaction at the per-transaction ceiling (64,998,400) reaches the check with
about 64.78M left and forwards about 63.7M at any setting. A real claim that needs up to about 63.6M forwarded can
therefore still be met (at the top setting, with a ceiling-capped transaction); one that needs more can be met by no
setting and no gas limit. A claim
near 55M gas takes about 55% of a block's weight (Frontier weighs a transaction by its gas limit), so it needs a
fairly quiet block.

### 3.10 Implementation decisions

The points the eighth review found open, each settled by the simplest safe choice:

1. **The shortfall check and `_unstake`'s units.** `_unstake` takes the wei to raise and returns
   `unmet` and the walk's `cost` in wei; liquidity is read once before the walk, and the checks are
   on what the walk took (`unmet <= 1,000 rao` at face value) and, for a chunk, on what it received
   (`free + received` funds what is sent), never a re-read balance (3.3), so staked reservations
   cannot block an exit.
2. **`adoptStake` and the timers.** It records `lastAdoptAt`, from which its own `KEEPER_FALLBACK`
   counts, and leaves `lastStakeAt` and the stake ramp alone: the adopted gift enters the outflow
   base at once (a gift cannot be withdrawn, so it is not the borrowed capital the ramp exists for).
   No adopter can keep re-basing the ramp to starve the permissionless `stakeIdle`. Nor the reward
   schedule: an adoption restarts only its own drip (Revision 27), never the claim schedule.
3. **`setStakeHotkey(X)` for an unlisted `X`** reverts at execution (`NotListed`); listing is a
   separate `addHotkey` under the slot rules, never implicit.
4. **Stake thresholds.** The last slot needs at least `minStakeRao()` of the vault's root stake; "no
   stake" in a removal means exactly 0. No dust sweep: it would not close the grief in 3.1 (a
   stranger can place at least the minimum), so I-6 states its precondition instead.
5. **A cancel's carried bounces** keep the batch's `queuedAt` (they have waited the delay and are
   owed regardless, I-19); a cancel that would re-mint nothing reverts (`NothingToCancel`), so it
   never stamps.
6. **Scheduling a non-loosening is allowed.** `schedule` checks the selector, the canonical length
   and the gates, not the direction, so a tightening or an equal value waits its delay and executes
   harmlessly (section 7 step 4's no-op relies on it).
7. **`StampedBySig`** is emitted on every valid call, replays included, so the monitor sees each
   relay; the signature is exactly 65 bytes (`r`, `s`, `v`), never the 64-byte EIP-2098 form.
8. **The window and decoding.** `execute` runs from `readyAt` and `readyBlock` until the item lapses,
   once `block.timestamp > readyAt + EXECUTE_WINDOW` and `block.number > readyBlock +
   ceil(EXECUTE_WINDOW * 1000 / (2 * MS_PER_BLOCK))` (Revision 28), `prune` only then; `schedule`
   decodes an activation's arguments with `abi.decode`, which reverts on dirty bits, so one target
   has one canonical calldata and one id.
9. **`positionsLeft`** in the walk's gas split is counted once before the walk (positions large
   enough to unstake) and decremented as each is tried.
10. **"Full"** means `hotkeys.length == MAX_HOTKEYS`; a stranger's removal also needs
    `stakeMissing()` (round 9 L-2).
11. **The walk's stopping rule.** The walk counts the stake its removals took, at face value,
    against the request, not what the chain paid for it, and both callers stop it once `unmet <= T`:
    a removal fee never sends it to a further position, and no second position is stripped of
    `minStakeRao()` for a remainder under `T`. What the chain kept of the stake taken is the exit's
    cost: `redeemDirect`'s leaver bears it with what is unmet (at most 1,000 rao), and a payout
    chunk's entries bear it pro rata; `processPayouts` asks for `T` more than it needs, which covers
    its second split's rounding unless the walk ended after a whole removal with less than a rao an
    entry of `T` left, other positions untried or not (that chunk refuses `UnstakeShort` until the
    balance or the positions move, 3.3).
12. **What feeds `rampingStake`.** Only `stakeIdle`'s booked delta (`stakeOf(h)` after less before, at
    most what it staked: rev 21),
    re-based from `lastStakeAt` as in v3.1, less `_removeStake` receipts as in v3.1; v3.1's
    `root - bookedStake` term goes, since gifts enter only through `adoptStake`, which does not ramp.
13. **`adoptStake` refuses while paused** (`whenNotPaused`, like `stakeIdle`), so a pause freezes the
    bookings that add capital. `claimRewards` still books while paused, as in v3.1 (decided: claims
    stay open, so rewards keep vesting through an incident; a claim prices no share, and its cut is
    skipped while `stakeMissing()`).
14. **The claim's booking** is `booked += _sub0(after, before)`: an unexpected fall books nothing and
    shows as missing stake (the guard closes minting) rather than reverting the claim; the cut on a
    zero delta is zero.
15. **Carried bounces keep their original order.** The keeper rebuilds the carried list from the
    batch's `DepositBounced` and `Redeemed` events in log order and keeps the last `bouncesCarried`
    of its `DepositBounced` entries (the count in `PayoutBatchCancelled`): bounces are queued first,
    so the unsent ones are always the tail of the bounce list.
16. **`moveStake`'s amount and destination at execute.** A scheduled `moveStake(from, to, rao)`
    moves `min(rao, stakeOf(from))`: any walk in its 48 hours may draw on `from`, and moving less is a
    tightening; an empty position moves nothing (no precompile call, no event) and the item is
    consumed (R1-A L-2). It reverts `InvalidParameter` (the item kept) unless `to` is the stake hotkey
    at execute: `setStakeHotkey` can now run at once (L-1), so the destination the guardian reviewed
    is pinned in the calldata rather than read at execute (R1-A's carry, Task 14a).
17. **The block floor.** Each item records `readyBlock`, the scheduling block plus `ceil(delay *
    1000 / (2 * MS_PER_BLOCK))`, and `execute` also needs `block.number >= readyBlock`, so a halted
    chain cannot consume the veto: after a halt the item still waits half its delay in calibrated
    blocks. `MS_PER_BLOCK` is a per-contract immutable from `Params.msPerBlock` (61 to 60,000 ms, so
    no block time in seconds is taken for milliseconds), which the deploy calibrates for blocks twice
    as fast as the chain's today (the vault 7,200, Bittensor's 12 s; the front door 120, Base's 0.2 s),
    so on Bittensor the floor is about 0.83 of each delay in live blocks today and 0.42 after a 2x
    speed-up (on Base, whose outages are back-filled, it comes with readyAt: there the guardian's cancel
    and the owner's stamp protect); public on both targets so that the lenses can serve it (R1-A L-3,
    Revision 28). An item lapses only once both its window in time and its
    window in blocks (half `EXECUTE_WINDOW` in calibrated blocks after `readyBlock`) have passed, so
    a slowdown, which makes the floor land later, can never strand it; on blocks up to about twice
    the calibrated time apart the lapse is exactly the time window.
18. **The executing flag is a hash.** `_executingHash` is a `bytes32` transient holding the
    executing item's id, the hash of its calldata, not a `bool`; `_scheduledCall` compares it with
    `keccak256(msg.data)`, so only the item's own self-call passes (Task 7a).

## 4. The hard design questions, each with a recommendation

1. **Held or bounced?** Neither in the vault: it reverts `StakeMissing` and stays stateless; the
   adapter parks (native if the vault refused, tokens if the conversion failed or the amount is
   sub-floor, an empty park for a redeem-only batch), under one lock and CEI; anyone delivers or,
   after 14 days, refunds or drops (3.2, section 6).
2. **Visible vs booked without `getStakingHotkeys`?** `getStake` per listed hotkey and
   `getTotalColdkeyStakeOnSubnet(coldkey, 0)`, both live; the ledger is the vault's own, floored
   (3.1).
3. **Origin tracking.** By chain, set at first mint or by any direct deposit, holder-changeable from
   Bittensor; a door burns only its chain's origins and bounces another chain's (3.4).
4. **A replaceable `AlphaVault`.** No; parking plus the convert-during-notice rule; a successor rail
   is a new adapter pair (3.2).
5. **Oracle staleness.** No age gate; a liquidity floor on deliveries and a cardinality precondition
   (3.7).
6. **`closeBatch(expectedId)`.** Yes, with `minValue`; the room rule back since R6b U4 (the founder
   reversed "no room rule" on 2026-11-15: the value-limit hold, 3.5), widened to the half rule in R7
   (the founder's choice: the exact-room hold, 3.5).
7. **The pot surplus.** Rolled forward into the next batch's pot; the owner sweeps it to an
   immutable treasury (3.5).
8. **Bounded tolerances.** Constants; 1 rao per conversion from reserves that never dip into parks;
   1,000 rao of stake a walk may leave untaken, which the leaver bears, and the chain's cut of what
   it takes, which the exit bears; `MAX_FEE_ABS = MIN_REDEEM_VALUE / 2` with 0.03 / 0.015 and a 0.01
   lower bound on the ceiling (3.3).
9. **Owner setters.** One scheduler, three kinds, hard bounds on every exit limit, four instruments
   recorded per actor, a four-state machine with a reachability invariant; the Safe strongly
   recommended for the Base-side residual, and until then the monitor and the signed stamp (3.6,
   section 5).
10. **A global outflow limit.** A second window with its own percentage in [20%, 50%], sharing the
    floor (3.4).

## 5. Who can do what: the governance table

| Party | At once | After notice (veto) | Bound, and the residual |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Owner key** (v3.2-launch: one Ledger, both chains, the guardian its second account; **strongly recommended before significant non-founder money: a 2-of-3 Safe on both chains**) | `pause`/`unpause`; tighten any limit within its bounds (no setting freezes an exit); `removeFrontDoor`, `disableBridge` (holds when they hit the launch door or the primary); `setKeeper`; `cancel` anything; a signed stamp (`stampBySig`), relayed by any account; `cancelPayoutBatch` (each entry's owed value re-minted, never more shares than it burned; bounces carried; a launch-door cancel stamps); hotkey management; `setPrimaryBridge` while the primary is disabled; the staker's routes and fees within their caps | loosen a limit, `moveStake`, `setStakeHotkey` otherwise, `setOracle`/`setSwapper`, `setRewardCut` up, `setRewardVesting` down (48 h, guardian veto); activate a new bridge or door or a guardian-removed door, write off missing stake bounded by `missing - unlisted` (7 d, guardian veto); `transferOwnership` (8 d, guardian veto; the new owner accepts within 7 days of the execute, from OPEN, before any closure or stamp); `setGuardian` (8 d, owner-only cancel); re-activate a known bridge or an owner-removed door (7 d, owner-only cancel); all but the last two only from OPEN and staying OPEN | **On the vault's chain, no user funds move on the owner's say-so without 7 continuous days of open exits** (at the floor, even in a run, first-come, about 36% of a door's base is paid out in them in the worst case found: the Base batch interval raised to 24 h with the open batch rolled every time it falls due empty, a delivery every other day, and the payout delay raised to 48 h, which strands the batches queued under the old delay for a thief with both keys to cancel and take; one request waiting in the batch when it falls due, anyone's, stops the roll, and with every request waiting about 59%; about 67% with the payout delay raised alone, about 74% with neither; all assuming no thief-held shares in the queue: a thief with both keys and capital of its own, its requests first, takes the first-come allowance ahead of honest holders (about 1.4 times the honest holdings empties their share, 0.6 times with the rolls; the measured case first loosens the entry limit, a 48-hour scheduled item, so one entry takes a day's allowance, where at the launch's 10-TAO limit the thief would need many addresses instead), so honest holders may get little or nothing out, and keeping the two keys apart is the defence: section 3.6, row 1 SOG), in which the honest owner or guardian cancels. Residuals: a stolen key can freeze exits indefinitely (no loss of principal, as today); **a stolen single key can close Base-side exits (disable the Base primary) where the vault cannot see it, leaving direct exits (EOA holders on Bittensor) only: no door exit at all before a drain at about day 15, once the thief's `setGuardian` (which the guardian cannot veto) has run (row 4 SO)**; a copied key is a stalemate with funds safe only through the signed stamp (without it, a copy under a gas sweeper is a theft). The single-key residual and its mitigation are below the table. |
| **Guardian key** (v3.2-launch: account 2 on the owner's Ledger; its own device recommended, D2, which buys 8 days against a stolen owner key, not a veto) | `pause`; `removeFrontDoor` (the permanent kind); `disableBridge`; `cancel` any Ordinary item; `addHotkey`; remove an empty hotkey with at most `hotkeyDustRao` unclaimed | nothing | A stolen guardian key holds front-door flows for at most 8 days after a pause or a bridge disable (`setGuardian` on day 8, then the Reopen it cannot cancel) and 15 and a block after removing a door (the swap, then the door's Ordinary activation), even with the primary disabled in the swap's own block (the swap ends that hold too, R1-B M-1; that disable's stamp puts the activation a block later); up to 22 while the honest owner's own disable of the vault's primary is outstanding: that owner hold refuses `setGuardian` until the primary's Reopen (7 d), so OPEN comes at 15 days and the door's return at 22 (I-26), and direct access for at most 8; it cannot move funds. On the owner's Ledger it is stolen or copied with the owner key. |
| **Keeper key** | timing of closes (with id and value), chunking (≥ `minChunk`), `stakeIdle`, claims, `adoptStake`, `deliver` within the floors, the park jobs, `addHotkey`; remove an empty non-stake hotkey with at most `hotkeyDustRao` unclaimed (as the guardian may) | nothing | Cannot move funds; fee splitting bounded per chunk; a delivery at most 1.5% below the TWAP and never below the user's floor; a removal forfeits at most the dust allowance of unclaimed rewards (still owed on chain, claimable once the hotkey is listed again). |
| **A rogue front door or adapter** (7 days of notice from an open vault and a guardian veto to exist) | burn ≤ min(20% per door, 20% global) of the ramped-in base per window (windows are fixed, not rolling: about two allowances can burn seconds apart across a reset, R6 F), only its chain's origins; payouts after the delay; a refund presents its own batch's tokens only | — | The guardian holds payouts at once; the owner cancels the share entries, which gives the burned holders back the value at burn time (Task 34c): the vault's rewards over the hold go to the holders who stayed, and after a fall the burned holders get their shares back. |
| **ForeverMoney's 2-of-3 Safe** (`0xADF6…3546`, the same three signers on Base and Bittensor, no timelock; also the AlphaVault's admin, operator and guardian and the CCIP registry administrator of both tokens; a second Safe with the same signers, `0x2d91…221b`, is an operator too; read 2026-11-05, R3 FM-L2) | halt the rail, the guardian for at most 2 days a shot (it lifts itself, a 12-hour cooldown before the next) and the operator for as long as it likes (`adminHold`, no cap; R3 FM-I2): inbound batches park as tokens, payouts revert and retry (**an open vault with reverting payouts is a stated residual**, bounded by their halt); announce a migration (7-day notice); mint FM-TAO on Base with nothing behind it (`grantRole(MINTER_ROLE)`, one transaction) or revoke the pool's mint and burn roles (the lane stops); withdraw the wTAO locked on Bittensor that backs every FM-TAO on Base and Robinhood (`setRebalancer`, which logs nothing, then `withdrawLiquidity`); swap or remove either token's pool in Chainlink's TokenAdminRegistry (`setPool`); pause or throttle the lanes (rate limits, chain removal, the pool's router) | — | Exposure is the FM-TAO the front door holds (the open batch and unclaimed credits) plus batches and payout chunks in flight; staked TAO is native on Bittensor and unaffected, and no ForeverMoney key can take TAO in the vault. A delisted, throttled or unwired pool is a delay: sends revert and in-flight messages wait in CCIP `FAILURE` until it is wired again. A pool that mints the wrong amount cannot credit anyone: the OffRamp passes the measured amount, and the door (`TokensNotReceived`, the payout sum) and the Bittensor adapter (`AmountMismatch`) refuse it. Unbacked minting and a lock drain are a loss of the exposure above and nothing else; the monitor's `fmtao.role_change_critical` fires on the grant and `fmtao.backing_true` on the drain within the hour, and the guardian's answer is to pause the front door and the vault; Chainlink's registry owner cannot take the pool assignment over (`AlreadyRegistered`). During a notice the keeper flushes the open batch, converts every token park and closes nothing; a converted park still delivers during and after a migration (it needs no ForeverMoney call; only its refund closes, R3 FM-I3); a token park that could not be converted in the notice rides on their token: **the one place a ForeverMoney action can cost a depositor** beyond the exposure above. |
| **Chainlink** | pause the lane; change limits; withhold verification; at worst, run a compromised lane | — | **A paused lane**: nothing moves and nothing is lost; every execution needs their `verifierResults`, a `FAILURE`'s re-execution included: the deployed OffRamp 2.0.0 checks the committee's results every time (Task 52's review; R3's CC-L3 reading, that a `FAILURE` needs none, was wrong). Parking removes every Cascade-side cause but a malformed batch, a bookkeeping fault and a held lock. Anyone can move an `UNTOUCHED` message to `FAILURE`, and the destination pool's inbound rate limit can fail one before `ccipReceive`: nothing is written (R3 CC-L2, CC-L4). At launch the keeper never re-executes on its own: it reports the `FAILURE`, and the founder runs `pnpm ops reexec <messageId>`, which reuses the committee's results from the failed attempt's public calldata, simulates first, and refuses if the simulation fails. Automatic re-execution stays off until the founder turns it on after the rehearsals; until then a failed message's tokens stay locked in the bridge, and nothing is lost. **A compromised lane** (a malicious router or OffRamp) is a residual of having one lane: it cannot mint from the parks: the adapter converts only while its token balance covers its token parks plus the amount named (R4, C-L1), so a forged amount with no tokens behind it is refused `ReserveShort` and the parks are always protected; since R4 (F-1) the cushion is booked and covered by the same check, so a forged amount takes neither a park nor the cushion, and the most it can take is the unswept surplus above both (a mint's rounding, a plain transfer: the treasury's, which the sweep sends on; keep it swept); and (F-3) a conversion that burns other than exactly its amount is refused. It can forge redeems up to the allowance and divert payouts in transit: a forged redeem-only batch carries nothing and still burns holders' shares (F-2, the one case where a message that carries nothing moves value; no check on Bittensor can tell a forged request from a real one, so it stays a stated residual, answered by the monitor and the owner's cancel). With a watching guardian (it disables the primary at the first forged delivery or lost payout; the owner cancels the batches, re-minting each entry's owed value, never more shares than it burned) the loss is bounded by one `payoutDelay` of burns: up to two allowances, not one, since the windows are fixed, not rolling (a window's rest burned just before it ends and a fresh allowance just after, seconds apart, both due a payout delay later: 20 TAO at the launch's 10-TAO floor, half of a 40 TAO vault; R6 F); answered when the forged delivery lands (`bridge.batch_mismatch`), both are still queued for the whole delay, and nothing is lost; and the way off the lane (row 3) must Reopen it for 7 days, under the same watch. **A lane still being exploited** resets that migration with every forged delivery the guardian or owner answers (each answer stamps and kills the new bridge's activation), so the way off it is Chainlink's fix or a redeploy; forged redeem-only parks left behind re-enter at a Reopen as forced exits within the allowance, or are dropped after 14 days. |
| **Bittensor sudo** | disable a precompile (the vault freezes; a disabled staking precompile also makes the owner's `cancelPayoutBatch` revert, where it ran before: since Task 34c it reads the stake to price the re-mint); set the root hold (exits need the buffer; `moveStake` waits: 3.8); raise the floor (sub-floor batches park) | — | Monitored; the buffer and the parks are the mitigations; under a hold the guardian pauses the front door's deposits and the founder funds the buffer directly. |
| **Bittensor's block authors** (20 Aura authorities, read 2026-11-15) | order their own block: sandwich a reward claim (sell a subnet's alpha just before it, buy back after; the chain pays the claim at the same block's pool prices, sells fee-free and gives the author the swap fees), since `claimRewards` takes no minimum (R6b U5) | — | A few percent of some claims, never principal, carried by every root staker; it needs a rogue authority, inside the trust already given to Bittensor's operators. A stated residual: a `minDelta` on the claim or a keeper forwarder that checks the delta is the founder's call, and covers every claim only with U1's guard and a keeper that claims before the schedule ends. |
| **Anyone** | deposit, request, cancel, close, `processPayouts` (paying any fee above the ceiling), `claim`, direct access, `addHotkey` at the chain's minimum while `stakeMissing`, remove an empty hotkey when the set is full and stake is missing, `stakeIdle`, `claimRewards(h)` (since R6b U1 only once the claim schedule has vested; with no keeper, so one stranger claim a vesting period, the first restarting the schedule: Low-4, stated) and `adoptStake` after the 7-day fallback (`KEEPER_FALLBACK` since the last stake, since `h`'s last claim or first listing, since the last adoption), `deliverParked`, `refundParked` after 14 days, `dropParked`, `sweepSurplus`, `prune`, relay a signed stamp | — | None can take anyone else's funds; each is a way out in section 6. |

**The rail between the chains (a residual; Task 39b, I-28).** The rail between the chains is coherent
only by operation. Each chain keeps its own bridges and primary. An owner's switch of one side's
primary alone, or, while the two sides' primaries differ (a migration in progress), either actor's
disable on the vault of a non-primary bridge the front door still uses, leaves both chains OPEN while
every launch batch is refused at one end. Nothing is lost (the Bittensor adapter parks a refused
batch, I-10 refunds it), but Base exits stop until the rails are made coherent again. After a
vault-side switch alone, the front door also refuses every payout sent through the vault's new
primary, and the Base adapter does not park: each such payout's delivery reverts (CCIP `FAILURE`)
and waits for a retry until the front door activates that bridge. This holds only for a new primary
the front door has never activated: a payout through a bridge it knows, even one it has disabled,
is credited. A rail switch is one two-sided
operation: the ops tools refuse a one-sided one, and the monitor's `rails.incoherent` rule alerts
and holds.

**The single-key residual and its mitigation (decisions D1, D2; round 6 M-1).** Until the Safe, one
Ledger owns every contract on both chains and its second account is the guardian, so a copied seed
copies both and the guardian is no check on the owner. A stolen hardware-wallet seed is in practice
a **copied** key, and the thief can sweep both accounts' gas (3.6): **without `stampBySig`, a copied
seed must be treated as SOG** (a rogue door on day 7; Base smart-wallet holders cannot leave). With
it, on every `Scheduled` alert (kind, selector, decoded arguments, `readyAt`; section 7) the founder
signs an owner stamp on the Ledger and relays it from any funded account within the delay (48 h, 7 d
or 8 d), and **funds stay safe only if the founder does**. A Bittensor halt can turn this into a
first-block race at resumption (the thief's ready items against the founder's stamp) when it begins
once an item's floor of blocks is in and spans its `readyAt` (the floor counts from scheduling, R6
G), or when it outlasts the stamp's own day (`STAMP_MAX_TTL`: a stamp signed during the halt expires
before the chain can include it, so the founder signs again at the resume); the relayer then submits an on-demand stamp at once with a high tip (round 8 L-3). The thief
keeps its instant powers: freezing exits (principal safe); yield and fee levers, none of which
reaches principal (`claimGasRequired`, now bounded; forfeiting a non-stake hotkey's rewards up to
`hotkeyDustRao` through its keeper, and as the owner `removeHotkey(h, true)`, which forfeits any
non-stake hotkey's unclaimed rewards whatever their size; the idle buffer up to 10%, vesting up to 30 days and the unstake
margin up to 1 TAO; the request fee up to its 0.001 ETH cap, whose surplus `sweepPot` sends to the
treasury); the staker, whose plain two-step ownership moves at once (up to about 5.5% of each new
stake: the 5% buy floor and the fee caps) until the web points at a fresh staker; and deliveries,
within 1.5% of the TWAP, through its keeper and the staker's delivery route, which is the staker
owner's to set (`setSwapper` itself is scheduled, so a stamp keeps the swapper contract), for as
long as users keep a delivery preference, since in a stalemate the owner never acts again: the web
stops offering USDC delivery and prompts preference holders to `claim()`, which clears it (round 7
Info 3). The treasury (`0x34D1…`) is account 3 on the same seed, so a copy also takes protocol
revenue (the cut's shares, staker fees, the swept pot, adapter surplus), never user principal; it is
immutable in the adapters and the front door, so a treasury on a separate seed has to be chosen
before deploy (open question 9); it must work on both chains (an EOA, or a Safe at the same address
on 964), and its cut shares are redeemed regularly. A separate guardian device buys 8 days against a
stolen owner key, not a veto: only the Safe or the signed stamp is a veto. The Safe prevents a copy
and cannot be installed after one (the thief cancels the move); it is recommended before significant
non-founder money, by `transferOwnership` (Ordinary, 8 d; the staker's two-step is immediate).

**Before other people's money (R6's list; the self-funded launch accepts each).** Every item here
is a stated residual of v3.2, decided for the founder's own money and revisited before anyone
else's; the ones that need a contract change go into one redeploy, and the paid audit's scope
includes each as an open design question:
- **The pro-rata allowance** (3.5's residual): an allowance shared pro rata among a delivery's
  requests, not first-come, so a holder with a day of exposure on twice the capital no longer crowds
  it, and a crowder gets only its share.
- **A leaky bucket for the outflow windows** (3.4, R6 F): `burned` drained at one allowance per day
  instead of reset, so a burst across a reset is about one allowance, not two; designed with the
  pro-rata allowance, since both reshape the same clip.
- **A Base contract wallet's Bittensor twin** (3.4, R6 B): until a contract answer, the site refuses
  contract-account deposits other than EIP-7702 ones, or at least warns properly (another party may
  control the address on Bittensor and can take its shares at any time), and warns before a deposit
  into a `DIRECT_ORIGIN` address; the contract answer, for the audit, is an opt-in "door only" lock
  sent from Base, or a registered Bittensor withdrawal address, never a forced Base consent for every
  direct exit.
- **The two keys apart** (section 5's single-key residual and SOG, the founder's ruling): the
  guardian on its own device and the 2-of-3 Safe as owner on both chains.
- **The door's cap on what the vault reports** (3.1, R6 L-1), in place of `netForwarded`; until then
  the owner raises the cap by the drift.
- **A deposit fee into the pot** (3.5, R6 L-2; the README's item 29), so a full deposit list pays
  its own close. R12's list half rule ends the list's hold without it (Revision 36); the fee would
  price what is left: the under-half hold and the honest closer's fee for a deposits-only batch. Not
  taken now: after the cooldown and both half rules no hold is free, and the fee is an interface
  change (a payable `depositFor` or a TAO skim; the staker, the web, the keeper's economics, I-18).
- **The half-batch hold** (3.5, R7): a holder can still refuse deposits above about half a batch (or
  half the cap's room) by sitting just under half and cancelling as much as each honest deposit adds;
  honest deposits keep landing, and a refused depositor can deposit what fits, which makes the batch
  closable at once. Over the line, the rule binds only through a close that lands before the cancel
  (R8, the R7 review's L-1): the keeper runs once a minute, so a holder could deposit a full batch
  just after one round and cancel before the next, refusing every deposit for most of each minute.
  The cancel cooldown (R11, 3.5) closes that: a deposit cannot be cancelled for five minutes, so the
  keeper's next round commits it; with several addresses the cancels within any five minutes add up
  to less than half a batch, so the batch is near full for at most about one round in five, while
  each keeper close lands (it names a lenient `minValue`, Revision 36). The web
  still offers the refused depositor "close this batch, then deposit" (`closeBatch` is
  permissionless, and that close commits the holder), and the monitor warns on heavy cancelling. A
  deposit fee the cancel does not return would price what is left (the under-half hold and the honest
  closer's fee for a deposits-only batch; the deposit list's hold is closed by Revision 36's list half
  rule); it stays deferred.
- **The keeper-less claim** (R6b U1, the R6b review's Low-4): with no keeper (lapsed, or its key
  gone), the permissionless claim opens only once the claim schedule has fully vested, and the first
  stranger's claim restarts the schedule, so every other hotkey waits another period (`NotKeeper`):
  one stranger claim per vesting period. The rewards stay owed on chain, never lost, and `setKeeper`
  is the owner's and immediate, so no contract change; the monitor's `rewards.uncollected` is the
  trigger.
- **The tolerance's base without the vesting rewards** (3.1, R6 E-fix), if the monitor ever shows
  them mattering.
- **The reward schedule per hotkey, or per-holder reward accounting** (3.5's fourth residual; R6b U1, U2): the exact
  cure for a staggered or backlogged hotkey's claim re-spreading the others' rewards; several hundred bytes, beyond
  v3.2's headroom.
- **A claim minimum** (R6b U5): `minDelta`, or a keeper forwarder that checks the claim's delta, against a block
  author's sandwich.
- **The independent audit** (section 1), after which caps rise above the founder level.

**Code placement (section 8.1).** The vault's only DELEGATECALLs are to its fixed, linked libraries
(`CascadePayoutMath`, L1, L2, L3), whose addresses are baked into its bytecode at deploy. No key can
change them, so they add no party to this table; a library bug is a vault bug, and each library is
reviewed and verified as part of the vault (I-32).

## 6. Where funds can wait, and the permissionless way out

| State | Who gets it out, how | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| Tokens in a front door's open batch | the depositor, `cancelDeposit` (works paused, at once; unpaused, from 5 minutes after its last deposit, R11, and at once while the primary is disabled, R12) | nothing |
| A redeem request in an open batch | the requester, `cancelRedeem` (works paused) | nothing |
| A closed batch in CCIP, not executed on Bittensor | anyone, `OffRamp.execute` | Chainlink's verifiers and the lane |
| A malformed batch | nobody (only a malformed sender can make one, with its own tokens) | — |
| A batch the adapter could not record (`ReserveShort`, a bookkeeping fault) | re-execute once the balance covers the parks | Chainlink's verifiers (one-off) |
| A token park | anyone, `deliverParked` once the conversion works; after 14 days `refundParked` with the tokens as they are (no floor check), or `dropParked` when empty | ForeverMoney's `withdrawLiquid` (delivery); the lane (refund) |
| A native park | anyone, `deliverParked` once the vault accepts; after 14 days `refundParked` | the vault's state (delivery); the lane and ForeverMoney's `depositLiquid` (refund) |
| Shares while `stakeMissing` | anyone lists the hotkey (`addHotkey` at the chain's minimum); the holder, `redeemDirect(shares, true)` | the precompile's reads |
| Shares while the vault is paused | the owner's `unpause`; a stolen guardian's pause ends with its replacement (8 days); a stolen owner's pause has no permissionless end (principal safe) | — |
| Shares whose door was removed | any other door on that chain; a Reopen (7 days) if the owner removed it, an Ordinary activation (7 days from OPEN; ≤ 15 after a stolen guardian's removal) if the guardian did; `setRedeemOrigin` to a chain with one; or `redeemDirect` | — |
| Shares of a Base-side user while the Base primary is disabled | `redeemDirect` on Bittensor (an EOA holder); a Reopen of the bridge (7 days; 8 after a stolen guardian's disable) | the founder's signed stamp on the monitor's alert; the recommended Safe makes the cause unreachable by one key |
| A queued payout | anyone, `processPayouts` after the delay, paying any fee above the ceiling; the owner's cancel re-mints each share entry its owed value, never more shares than it burned, and carries bounces forward | the vault unpaused, the door active, ForeverMoney's `depositLiquid`, an unstake possible (or, when a chain fee would leave an entry nothing, liquid TAO: deposits net it, or the idle buffer; in the walk's whole-removal corner under a chain fee, the balance or the positions moving: a deposit, an exit, a stake or a reward, 3.3) |
| A bounced deposit in a queued payout | anyone, `processPayouts` (bounces first; they survive a cancel in their own batch); never released; a retired door's bounces need that door active again; in deep insolvency it can wait behind an unpayable older batch until the owner cancels that batch | as above |
| A payout chunk in CCIP, not executed on Base | anyone, `OffRamp.execute`; the front door accepts it from any bridge it has ever enabled | Chainlink's verifiers and the lane |
| Claimable TAO on Base | the user, `claim` (works paused) | nothing |
| A failing USDC delivery | the user, `claim` | nothing |
| Stake under an unlisted hotkey | anyone, `addHotkey` while `stakeMissing`; the keeper, guardian or owner at any time | the precompile's reads |
| A sub-minimum stake position | `stakeIdle` tops the stake hotkey up; the owner's `removeHotkey` exits any other fully (waits during a root hold) | — |
| An origin-0 holder (shares only from the cut) | the holder, `setRedeemOrigin` or `redeemDirect` | nothing |
| Adapter surplus | anyone, `sweepSurplus` (to the treasury) | nothing |
| A scheduled item nobody executed | anyone, `prune` once it lapses (its windows in time and in blocks passed, Revision 28) | nothing |

## 7. Migration plan: v3-launch to v3.2

**Path A (decided), in this order:** (1) pause the old front door with the guardian key (on the
v3.1 door a pause makes a close impossible for anyone), then stop the old keeper; (2) from the
depositing wallet, `cancelDeposit(215117444000000000)` on the old front door (works while paused);
(3) hold the TAO (or swap it back to USDC) until v3.2 is live, then stake through the new staker.
If the batch had crossed before the pause: `requestRedeem(all)`, the close, the delay, `claim()`;
about two days; then step 3.

**Deploying v3.2:**

1. Preconditions: both pools' cardinality in use ≥ 1,801 (Revision 28; 2,000 and 6,700 on 2026-11-13;
   `pnpm ops cardinality --grow` for a pool below it); the fork
   rehearsal green (section 9; the testnet rehearsal was dropped on 2026-11-11, and the constants it was to
   measure, `PARK_GAS_RESERVE`, `EXIT_GAS_RESERVE` and `claimGasRequired`'s bounds, were set from the fork at
   Revisions 29 and 30, the mainnet canary checking them with tiny amounts after the deploy); `pnpm ops
   preflight`; the monitor's scheduler alerts
   live (section 9); the pool liquidity floors measured (open question 8); the compiler decision
   (open question 2) and, for solc ≥ 0.8.38, Sourcify and taostats verification confirmed for that
   version, linked libraries included. Ops reads v3-launch's artifacts from its frozen build,
   `ops/builds/preaudit` (built from d21daf3), never `contracts/out`, and the deploy gate rebuilds
   the checkout first.
2. **Addresses by plain `CREATE` from fresh single-use deployer keys at predicted nonces**, as v3.1
   did (M-1): `CREATE2` cannot break the init-code cycles (door ↔ vault immutables, door ↔ staker,
   adapter ↔ adapter `remoteAdapter`), since an immutable's value is in the init code and changes
   the address, and `CREATE3` is rejected (look-alike squatting on the inner deployer). One deployer
   key per chain, funded with a margin over the plan's gas, used once, so the nonces are the plan's.
   A reverted `CREATE` still consumes its nonce and shifts every later address on that chain,
   voiding predictions already baked into the other chain's immutables, so **Bittensor goes first**
   (the Bittensor adapter; then the libraries `CascadePayoutMath`, L2 `CascadeStakeLib`, L3
   `CascadePayoutLib`, linked to the math, and L1 `CascadeBatchLib`, each by plain `CREATE` from the
   same fresh single-use key; then the vault, linked to L1, L2 and L3; then its lens,
   `CascadeVaultLens`: the likelier revert costs nothing on Base), then Base (the Base adapter,
   oracle, front door, staker, then the front door's lens, `CascadeDoorLens`); on each chain the
   adapter comes first because the constructors read its `maxMessageValue()`, and the nonce plan
   follows this order (round 8 L-1); any revert means fresh keys and a new plan. Each takes its
   `Params` and the founder's key as owner from the constructor. Fund the vault's `ED_RESERVE` by
   substrate transfer, the adapter's native reserve through `fundReserve()` and its token cushion
   through `fundCushion(0.001 FM-TAO)` (approved first; a plain transfer is surplus since R4, F-1). The vault's and L3's artifacts carry `__$…$__` placeholders in
   both their creation and runtime code, at different offsets: the deploy tool links them explicitly
   with the plan's addresses before encoding the deployment, and never lets `forge script` deploy a
   library itself (that would add transactions outside the nonce plan). If L4 is ever used, it joins
   the plan before the vault (8.1).
3. Read-backs, every one in `pnpm ops verify`: **each contract's, library's and lens's runtime code
   (`eth_getCode`) equals the runtime code built locally from the committed source with its
   immutables and link references filled in from the plan, never blanked** (today's `verify` blanks
   `immutableReferences` and has no link handling; this replaces it). `immutableReferences` are
   keyed by AST ids: the tool maps each id to its variable through the artifact's AST (built with
   `ast = true`), encodes each value as a 32-byte word and fills every range (the vault's first
   immutable has 8); the vault's `coldkey` is filled with the `ADDRESS_MAPPING` of its planned
   address. The expected set of link references comes from the artifact's
   `deployedBytecode.linkReferences`, and verify fails on any missing or unexpected one; the vault's
   references must name the plan's L1, L2 and L3, and L3's the plan's `CascadePayoutMath`; each
   library's own `library_deploy_address` immutable (under `via_ir`) must equal its own address. The
   comparison is byte for byte with the CBOR metadata stripped from both, or by `extcodehash` only
   after the same filling and when built from the same checkout path (`extcodehash` cannot strip
   metadata, and the path changes it; round 6 Info 9), so a wrong address in any immutable or link
   reference, or a wrong compiler, is caught before any money. Then, read through each contract's
   lens where the getter moved (8.1), values that are non-zero by construction, since a zero passes
   on any empty slot: `guardian`, `keeper`, the treasury, `hotkeys[0]`, `stakeHotkey`,
   `payoutDelay`, `maxEntryValue`, the launch door's state (Active) and `lastStakeAt` (the vault's
   creation time) through the vault lens, and `guardian`, `keeper`, `primaryBridge` and that
   bridge's `bridgeEnabled` through the door lens; and: `owner()` with its kind, `guardian()`,
   `keeper()` on every contract that has them; `vault.launchChain()`, `activeDoors(launchChain) ==
   1`, `exitsOpen() == true` on both, `stakeMissing() == false`, `bookedStake() == 0`,
   `getRejectStakingHotkeyGrowth(coldkey) == true`, `adapter.treasury()` on both, the oracle's
   cardinality and `meanLiquidity`, the front door's floors, the scheduler's class table (`schedule`
   refuses an unknown selector and a non-canonical length in an `eth_call`), the constructor-set
   oracle, swapper, treasury and cut, and the cap relations across the pair:
   `adapterBase.maxMessageValue() >= frontDoor.maxBatchValue()` and `adapterBit.maxMessageValue() >=
   max(vault.maxMessageValue(), vault.maxEntryValue(), frontDoor.maxBatchValue())`; the stamp's
   `DOMAIN_SEPARATOR` and `STAMP_TYPEHASH` (an immutable and a constant, so covered by the code
   comparison, and recomputed by the lens) equal the values computed locally.
4. Nothing to set (appendix B). One single-entry batch of the launch minimum deposit, 0.04 TAO, through
   to delivery (a 0.01 TAO deposit is below that minimum and the 0.03 TAO floor); one 0.03 TAO
   redeem through to a claim; one no-op parameter cancelled by the guardian, a second killed by an
   owner-signed stamp relayed from a fresh account, and a third scheduled after that stamp's `asOf`,
   the stamp replayed, and the third executed after 48 hours; one `sweepSurplus()` with nothing to
   sweep.
5. The founder's stake (A3).

**The Safe before other people's money (recommended, not a gate; D1, D2).** `pnpm ops deploy` and
the sign page **warn loudly, quoting section 5's residual, and never refuse** a caps file above the
founder level while `owner()` is not a contract answering `getThreshold() >= 2` and
`getOwners().length >= 3`; `verify` reports the owner's kind, threshold and signers and warns. A
process check only: it does not bind a hand-made `setCaps`, two signers on one device pass the
threshold, and an EIP-7702-delegated EOA (code `0xef0100…`) counts as an EOA. The move is
`transferOwnership` (Ordinary, 8 days) then the Safe's `acceptOwnership`, per contract, while OPEN,
within 7 days of the execute and before any closure or relayed owner stamp (a stamp as of the
execute or later refuses it; pause the stamp bot or let its last `asOf` precede the execute).
Safe{Wallet} does not list chain 964: the Bittensor Safe is a hand-deployed Safe 1.4.1 (the
singleton factory has code there), signed with the Safe CLI or a transaction builder.

**Runbooks (round 5 L-1, L-6; round 6 M-1, L-1, Info 5, Info 10; round 7 Info 3, 5, 6).**
*Incident:* pause and disable the primary with the **guardian** account (its instruments stamp only
its clock, sparing a pending `setGuardian` and Reopens; Ordinary items die by design); sign a
removal meant to last with the guardian account (the door becomes unknown; the honest way back is
`setGuardian` to the same guardian, 8 days, then a replacement, 15 days) and use the owner account
only for a temporary hold, which only a Reopen of that door undoes; at the first removal alert,
pause Base deposits (that pause stamps nothing). *Ordering:* as in 3.6, stamping actions first, then
the items to keep. *Swap of the stake hotkey:* the owner's `setStakeHotkey` first, then the keeper
frees the slot. *Stolen guardian:* use no owner instrument that stamps until `setGuardian` executes;
schedule it and each Reopen the same day; on day 8 execute `setGuardian` first, then the Reopens
(their window ends on day 14); for a door it removed, cancel the door's payout batches, if at all,
after the swap and before scheduling its activation (day 15). *Copied seed:* on any `Scheduled`
alert the founder did not cause, `pnpm ops stamp` (`asOf` = each chain's latest block time) has the
Ledger sign and a pre-funded relayer submit on each chain, or a bot relays pre-signed daily stamps
(a short horizon, kept only on the relayer; the last `asOf` is the earliest date for any founder
item meant to survive, the Safe's `transferOwnership` included); on Bittensor's resumption after a
halt, an on-demand stamp at once with a high tip; never refill a swept account; point the web at a
fresh staker, stop offering USDC delivery and prompt preference holders to `claim()`.

**Retiring v3-launch:** the old door is paused; owner `setCaps(min, 0)` and `setMaxTotalAssets(0)`;
`removeFrontDoor` on the old vault and `disableBridge` on both; the reserves stay;
`deployments.json` `stage: retired` and a new `v3.2-launch` entry; `SECURITY.md` and the README
tables; Sourcify and taostats verification of the six contracts, the four libraries and the lenses.

**ForeverMoney notice runbook (L-4).** The moment `migrationReadyAt() != 0`: pause the front door
(deposits stop), **close the open batch at once** with the id (anyone can), convert every token park
with `deliverParked`, close nothing afterwards; critical alerts on the notice and on any token park
during it.

**Website changes.** New ABI (`closeBatch(uint64,uint256)`, `redeemDirect(uint256,bool)`,
`setRedeemOrigin(uint64)`, the scheduler's events, `StakeMissing`, `BatchParked`, `REFUND_CHUNK`);
"Send it now" passes the batch id and the user's deposit as `minValue`; the unstake form explains
the window, the 0.033 TAO minimum, forces "all" below it with the price-drop note; the trail reads
the park events as states of a deposit and a refund payout as "returned, not withdrawn"; a
foreign-origin bounce reads "this address already belongs to another chain's Cascade; your deposit
is on its way back". Plain copy for `stakeMissing` ("Staking and unstaking are paused for a short
while: Cascade is reconnecting to its validator after a change on Bittensor. Nothing has been
lost."), a parked deposit, a refunded deposit, a parked withdrawal request ("Still pending; do not
request again until it shows dropped."), a thin pool, a stake refused by the 5% floor, and a paused
door ("New stakes are paused; withdrawals and claims work as usual."). The web never offers the
opt-in direct redeem.

**Monitor changes (indexer).** `stake.missing` (critical, naming the hotkey), `stake.unlisted`,
`stake.surplus_unadopted`, `bridge.parked` (critical after one hour),
`bridge.parked_tokens_during_migration` (critical), `bridge.cushion_low`, `bridge.refunded`,
`bridge.dropped`, `bridge.retire_blocked`, **`admin.scheduled`, `admin.cancelled` and
`admin.executed`: a critical email at once on every `Scheduled`, `Cancelled` and `Executed` event on
both chains, with the item's kind, selector, decoded arguments, `readyAt` and `readyBlock` (from the
lens), and an alert while `pendingOwner() != 0`, with `pendingSince` from the lens** (the indexer
already emails on every admin event and every admin-key transaction; the scheduler's events and
`StampedBySig` (deduplicated by `asOf`, so replays cannot bury `Scheduled` alerts) join that set,
and this alert is section 5's single-key mitigation), `admin.lapsed`, `admin.exits_state` (critical
on any state but OPEN and while `activeDoors(launchChain) == 0`, naming the instrument and who used
it, on both chains: the Base front door's state is read on Base, which is where the vault's residual
becomes visible to people), `admin.owner_kind` (the owner's code, threshold and signer count on both
chains; a warning while the stage is above "founder" without a Safe), `oracle.thin_pool`,
`oracle.cardinality`, `root.hold_interval` (critical), `root.pair_stamped` (the pairs the
deployment depends on), and the scope's backlog items.

**Keeper changes (ops).** `close` passes the id and value it read; a `parked` job (payload from the
Base OnRamp's `CCIPMessageSent`; only parks from a registered door to the vault; `deliverParked`
when it would succeed and on every token park the moment `migrationReadyAt() != 0`; `refundParked`
with `quoteRefund`'s fee after the timeout; `dropParked` for empty parks); an `adopt` job; round
order payouts, stake, claims; the hotkey watch; a `schedule`/`execute` helper that writes the
owner's calls as sign-page files, **the sign page refusing an `execute` whose data does not decode
to a pending `Scheduled` item it has read from the chain, and warning on any caps file above the
founder level without the Safe and on an owner-account `removeFrontDoor` (the door then stays
Reopen-able by the owner alone, without the guardian's veto), and at `execute` comparing each item's
values with the current ones and warning if it would act as a loosening**; `processPayouts` with a
top-up when the fee is above the ceiling (the operator's choice); `verify` reads the scheduler's
pending items, the exits state on both chains, the park totals, the cushion, a door's pending
batches before a removal file, the owner's kind and threshold; `cardinality --grow`; the buffer rule
when the hold interval appears; **`pnpm ops stamp`** (builds the owner's EIP-712 stamp for both
contracts with `asOf` = each chain's latest block timestamp, not the wall clock, has the Ledger sign
it, normalises `v` to 27 or 28 and submits from a relayer pre-funded on both chains whose key is not
derived from the Ledger seed; the sign page shows the contracts, the chains, `asOf`, and the domain
and struct hashes, since the Ledger may blind-sign EIP-712).

## 8. Size budget plan, with measured numbers

All figures are measured with the branch's toolchain (solc 0.8.38, `via_ir`, cancun, 200 runs,
`forge build --sizes`) and supersede every earlier projection, including the per-item byte estimates
in section 3. The gate is **23,076 B**: EIP-170's 24,576 B less 1,500 B kept for audit fixes.

**Today.** The v3.1 vault measures 21,447 B on this toolchain, after its payout math moved to
`CascadePayoutMath`. That library saved 290 B, not the 3,633 B earlier revisions counted on: each
library call stub costs 150 to 250 B, more with struct or array arguments, so only whole subsystems
pay off. The largest subsystems: the payout engine 3,152 B, batch delivery 2,760 B, events 2,488 B,
`CascadeBase` 2,373 B, auto getters about 2,230 B, setters 1,749 B, hotkey management 1,747 B.

**The v3.2 prototype.** A byte-representative, unreviewed prototype of every vault item in section
3, section 3.10 and Appendix A measures **31,035 B** (front door 19,375 B): 6.5 KB over EIP-170. Its
main v3.2 costs: the scheduler 2,738 B (its generic part and the vault's selector table), the signed
stamp 774 B (the front door's: 727 B), the base's holds, clocks and `bridgeKnown` net of the removed
registries 425 B, and the vault features about 5,650 B, of which the two-door features are 1,214 B.

**Measured at gate A2** (Phase 2 done: the layout in place, before the v3.2 features of Phases 3 and
4): vault 15,381 B; L1 3,286 B; L2 1,367 B; L3 4,500 B; `CascadePayoutMath` 1,867 B; front door
14,349 B; vault lens 5,130 B; door lens 1,553 B. The history that chose the layout follows.

**The approved layout (8.1), measured on the prototype: 21,401 B**, 1,675 B under the gate and
3,175 B under EIP-170. Each library on its own (net vault saving, call-stub overhead, library size):

| Library | Vault saving | Stub overhead | Library size |
|---|---|---|---|
| L1 `CascadeBatchLib`, the batch path | 1,784 B | 672 B | 3,613 B |
| L2 `CascadeStakeLib`, the unstake walk | 529 B | 670 B | 1,885 B |
| L3 `CascadePayoutLib`, the payout engine | 2,019 B | 953 B | 4,494 B |

Together with the lens (single-slot `extsload` only; the batch form costs 294 B for nothing a
multicall cannot do), the minimal token and the trims they give 21,401 B. The front door, unchanged
in design, measures 18,495 B in this configuration (its shared base getters behind `extsload` too;
19,375 B with them kept), under the gate either way.

**Trims.** `price()` and `hotkeyCount()` leave the vault with the lens, and `LimitSet` is keyed by
selector (about 235 B).

**The reserve lever.** L4 `CascadeHotkeyLib` (`addHotkey` and `removeHotkey`: eligibility, the last
slot, the three removal paths) saves 877 B more (20,775 B with L1 to L3). It is used only if a size
gate fails during implementation; the implementation's controller decides. A scheduler library was
also measured (about 850 B) and is not planned, since it would move §3.6's code. **Used (step L4,
2026-11-02):** after Task 15 left 1,870 B for Tasks 16 to 23, the controller moved hotkey management
into L4 (8.1); the vault went from 21,206 B to 20,004 B (L4 2,539 B).

**Size gates.** The first gate follows the scheduler, the ledger, the libraries and the lens; every
gate reports the vault, the front door, each library and each lens. The prototype is unreviewed, and
a real implementation may be 5% leaner or grow in review: the margin and the reserve lever are for
that. Not done: a second contract meant to hold value (the libraries and the lens are not; a direct
view or pure call with value, or a `SELFDESTRUCT`, can still force a balance on one, so their
balances are informational: never a verify failure, never a keeper stop), removing direct access,
`optimizer_runs = 1`.

**The compiler (L-C, with the round-4 note).** The IR pipeline on solc 0.8.24 sits inside
`YulOptimizerKnowledgeBaseStaleBookkeeping` (SOL-2026-7; fixed in 0.8.38), and that advisory also
touches the Yul utility functions the legacy pipeline generates, so staying on 0.8.24 is not a way
around it; the second advisory the review cites (SOL-2026-1) is one more reason to take the fixed
compiler. **Option 1, solc ≥ 0.8.38 with `evm_version = cancun` and `via_ir`, is the plan**; the
three Cancun opcodes it may emit all execute on 964 today (section 2); its preconditions are
Sourcify and taostats verification for that version and the full re-run of the suite, the fork
tests and the gas calibration. Option 2 (0.8.24, legacy) stays only as the record of the
alternative.

### 8.1 Code layout (approved 2026-11-01)

Behaviour, governance (3.6) and every invariant are unchanged; only where the code sits changes.
Revision 12 folds in the library-boundary review of revision 11.

- **`CascadeVault`** keeps every external entry point, all storage, the scheduler, the exits machine
  and `stampBySig`, with unchanged semantics. Each entry point runs its own checks and then, for the
  four subsystems below, hands a library the parts of its storage that subsystem writes.
- **Four fixed, linked, external libraries**, run in the vault's context by DELEGATECALL. **The
  link graph:** the vault's bytecode links L1, L2, L3 and L4 (L4 since step L4, the reserve lever);
  L3's links `CascadePayoutMath`; nothing else is linked.
  - **L1 `CascadeBatchLib`, the batch path:** `onBridgeMessage`'s body after the vault's checks
    (deposits, redeems, bounces: decode and sum, origin, mint or bounce, both windows, burn, queue).
    Writes balances and supply, origins and the windows; no external call inside. It takes a
    `BatchIn` (the vault's checks' results and reads, including the message's `sender`) and emits
    `BatchDelivered` first, in v3.1's order.
  - **L2 `CascadeStakeLib`, the unstake walk:** `_unstake` and the per-position gas split. Writes
    nothing (it takes a memory copy of the hotkey list, at most four entries, and returns the ledger
    debits, which the caller applies); calls only the staking precompile. **It is called only by the
    vault's walk, which `redeemDirect` and `processPayouts` use; no library calls it.** The vault
    sets its transient flag (`bool internal transient _unstaking`, not a ledger field; the ledger is
    `{pending, booked, ramping}`) around its own L2 calls and around `removeHotkey`'s direct removal
    (since step L4, around L4's `remove` when the owner calls, the only caller whose removal can
    unstake); no library sets it; `receive()` accepts value only while it is set, and it is never
    set across a bridge call. `removeHotkey` keeps v3.1's direct removal, which reverts when the
    chain refuses it: routed through L2's `try/catch`, a hotkey whose `removeStakeFull` was refused
    under a root hold would be delisted with up to about 0.002 TAO left behind, tripping
    `stakeMissing` in a small vault.
  - **L3 `CascadePayoutLib`, the payout engine,** split for `processPayouts` into `prepare` (no
    state writes: the verification, the chunk, the fee and the unstake the chunk needs) and `finish`
    (the effects, then the bridge's `send`, then `PayoutChunkSent`), plus `cancel` for
    `cancelPayoutBatch` with the carry-forward, which returns the batch's `sourceChain`. The vault
    orchestrates `prepare`, its own walk through L2 under the transient flag, then `finish`; L3
    never calls L2. It takes a `PayoutIn`: the batch as read and the reservation, the primary bridge
    and whether it is enabled, the door check's result, whether the caller is the keeper, and
    `payoutDelay`, `minChunk`, `maxMessageValue`, `maxFeeBps` and `maxFeeAbs`. Writes the payout
    queue, the ledger and, on a cancel, balances and supply; takes the door check's result from the
    vault; calls the bridge (`quoteFee`, `send`) and refunds `msg.sender` last, which under
    DELEGATECALL is the vault's caller, as intended; runs under the vault's `nonReentrant`. The
    launch-door cancel stamp stays in the vault.
  - **L4 `CascadeHotkeyLib`, hotkey management (the reserve lever, step L4):** the bodies of
    `addHotkey`, `removeHotkey` and `moveStake` after the vault's role reads, moved whole: the
    permissionless way back (`NotEligible`), the reserved last slot (`SlotReserved`), the three
    removal paths and their authorization, the owner's leftover handling (moved to the stake hotkey,
    or below the minimum removed into balance by the direct removal, whose two measurements `remove`
    returns and the vault books, as it books L2's walk), `moveStake`'s checks and move, the list's
    writes (push, swap-and-pop), `isHotkey` and `lastClaimAt`. The constructor lists its first
    hotkey through L4's `add` as a privileged add. **Writes exactly** the hotkey list, `isHotkey`
    and `lastClaimAt`, each through a storage pointer the vault passes; never the ledger, the stake
    hotkey or anything else (the library rules' write-set rule holds the set on solc's AST, as the
    vault's call sites bind the pointers); what it only reads comes as values (the booked stake,
    `moveStake`'s listing of `from`). **Calls only** the staking precompile (`getStake`, the floor's
    read, `getUnclaimedRootTaoByHotkey`, `moveStake`, `removeStakeFull`; the library rules pin the
    list), never with value. It reads neither `msg.sender` nor `msg.value`: the vault reads the
    roles and passes them (`privileged` for an add: the owner, the guardian or the keeper; `byOwner`
    and `byKeeperOrGuardian` for a removal), with what L4 cannot read (the coldkey, an immutable;
    the stake hotkey, the booked stake, the floor's fallback and the dust allowance). It reads the
    stake guard as the vault does (`CascadeFormulas.stakeMissing` over the listed hotkeys and the
    booked stake) and only where the vault read it, so every precompile read is made in the same
    order and only when it was. Its events (`HotkeyAdded`, `HotkeyRemoved`, `StakeMoved`,
    `Unstaked`) come from the vault's address, in v3.1's order. The vault keeps the entry points and
    their selectors, `moveStake`'s schedule check, `setStakeHotkey` (its scheduler gating) and
    `claimRewards`. `removeHotkey` stays unguarded by `nonReentrant` (as in v3.1): L4 calls only the
    precompile.
- **`CascadePayoutMath`** stays as it is (pure; linked by L3 only).
- **Shared internal helpers**, compiled into each user (inlined, no call, no size cost): the
  post-walk ledger update (the vault's walk, for `redeemDirect` and `processPayouts`, and
  `removeHotkey`'s direct removal, which L4 makes and the vault books), the ledger debit of one
  stake removal (L2's walk and `removeHotkey`'s direct removal, in L4), mint and burn (the token,
  L1, L3), the peer-id hash, and every derived formula the lens also computes (below).
- **The reserve lever, L4 `CascadeHotkeyLib`** (section 8): in use since step L4 (above). It
  changes I-32's link graph (the vault links four libraries), the nonce plan (section 7: L4 is one
  more `CREATE` before the vault, and every later nonce shifts; Task 43), the link set `verify`
  expects and the libraries verified on Sourcify and taostats (Tasks 44 to 46), and the counts.
- **Two lenses, `CascadeVaultLens` and `CascadeDoorLens`**, read-only and stateless, each
  constructed with **only its target's address** and reading it through a single-slot
  `extsload(bytes32)` view (never a batch form). They share the code for `CascadeBase`'s getters,
  and each has its own generated constants and constructor, because the targets differ: the share
  token's storage takes the vault's first slots (`guardian` sits at slot 5 there and slot 3 in the
  front door), `ADDRESS_MAPPING` exists only on Bittensor, and the hold formulas differ (the front
  door has no `pausedBy` or launch-door hold). The vault lens serves every vault view and derived
  getter that ops, the keeper, the indexer and the web read; it derives the vault's `coldkey` from
  the target's address through the `ADDRESS_MAPPING` precompile, as the vault's constructor does,
  recomputes `DOMAIN_SEPARATOR` from the chain id and the target, and reads the treasury, which is
  storage (scheduled through `setRewardCut`, appendix B), by `extsload`. The vault keeps only what
  wallets, explorers, Safe tooling and `verify` read from it directly (`name`, `symbol`, `decimals`,
  `balanceOf`, `totalSupply`, `owner`, `pendingOwner`, `paused`, and `launchChain`, an immutable
  kept as a getter so that verify is not circular, and `MS_PER_BLOCK`, an immutable the lenses
  ask the target for, R1-A L-3) and `extsload`; no on-chain caller reads anything else (the front
  door is on Base, and the adapters only call `onBridgeMessage`). **Moving to the vault lens:**
  every other public state variable and constant (the limits, `keeper`, `guardian`, the treasury,
  `lastClaimAt`, `redeemOrigin`, `activeDoors`, `doorState`, `launchDoorRemovedBy`, `pausedBy`,
  `primaryDisabledBy`, `primaryBridge`, `bridgeEnabled`, `bridgeKnown`, `scheduled`,
  `lastOwnerClose`, `lastGuardianClose`, `pendingSince`, `STAMP_TYPEHASH`), `coldkey`,
  `DOMAIN_SEPARATOR`, and the computed views `price`, `totalAssets`, `grossAssets`,
  `unvestedRewards`, `visibleStakeRao`, `bookedStake`, `missingStakeRao`, `surplusStakeRao`,
  `stakeMissing`, `unlistedStakeRao`, `minStakeRao`, `minRemainRao`, `hotkeyCount`,
  `getPayoutBatch`, `peerId`, `batchKey`, `exitsOpen`, `ownerHold` and `guardianHold`.
  - **The lenses cannot drift.** Their slot and offset constants, struct members included (the
    slots, offsets and byte sizes inside `PayoutBatch`, `Window`, `Item`, `Ledger` and
    `SharesData`), are generated from `forge inspect … storageLayout` for the vault and the front
    door, and a build test fails on any difference; every derived formula (gross and total assets,
    unvested rewards, the missing tolerance, the minimum-stake fallbacks, the underwater check,
    `exitsOpen` and the holds) lives in shared internal functions used by both the vault and its
    lens, so only the raw reads can diverge; lens parity is an invariant for both lenses, checked
    after every handler step (I-33). A lens bug shows wrong numbers, never moves funds, and a lens
    can be redeployed without its target.
  - The shared `CascadeBase` getters move behind `extsload` on the front door too, read by the door
    lens. The front door's getters that on-chain callers read stay public: `asset` and `usdc` (read
    in the staker's constructor) and `oracle` (read on every stake).
- **A minimal share token** replaces OpenZeppelin's ERC20 (shares are non-transferable anyway): a
  `SharesData` struct, internal mint and burn, and `Transfer` events from and to the zero address.
  The ERC-20 surface for wallets and explorers is `name`, `symbol`, `decimals` (24), `balanceOf`,
  `totalSupply` and the `Transfer` events, plus `transfer`, `transferFrom` and `approve` that revert
  and an `allowance` that returns 0, which wallets handle better than unknown selectors (they are in
  the measured 21,401 B; dropping them would save about 191 B). Testnet 945 checks how Blockscout and
  taostats classify the token. The auditor is told it replaces an audited component and drops
  OpenZeppelin's zero-address checks on mint and burn (the vault never mints to or burns from the
  zero address). I-34 below.
- **The trims** of section 8.
- **The front door** is unchanged in design (18,495 B measured; it fits).

**The DELEGATECALL posture.** The vault's only DELEGATECALLs are to its fixed, linked libraries
(`CascadePayoutMath`, L1, L2, L3, L4, through the link graph above), whose addresses are baked into its
bytecode at deploy; no key can change them. A library is part of the vault for review: it shares the
vault's storage, balance, `msg.sender` and `msg.value`, and its events come from the vault's
address. Each event and each custom error is declared once, at file level (solc ≥ 0.8.22), in shared
files used by the vault and the libraries. A file-level event or error that only a library function
emits appears in that library's ABI, not the vault's (as `PayoutsMismatch` and `AmountMismatch` do),
so **the published vault ABI**, the one ops, the web and the indexer use, is the vault artifact's
ABI merged with its libraries' event and error ABIs. A build script produces it, and a build test
checks that it covers every event and error any library can emit (identity holds by construction,
each being declared once). The indexer's ABI-source parity test uses the merged ABI and covers the
shares, events and library files (and drops `Transfer` from its OpenZeppelin list).

- *Reentrancy.* Every library call happens inside a vault entry point, and library code calls EVM
  code only under the vault's `nonReentrant` (`redeemDirect`, `processPayouts`), whose lock lives in
  the vault's storage, so a re-entrant call into any guarded vault function reverts while library
  code runs; precompile calls cannot re-enter. Inside L3, every EVM call that could re-enter (the
  bridge's `send`, the refund) comes after the state writes, and the refund goes last; L2's
  precompile calls come before the effects as in v3.1, and the TAO they return enters `receive()`
  only under the vault's transient `_unstaking` flag, which is never set across a bridge call. L1
  makes no external call. L4 calls only the precompile, which cannot re-enter, so `removeHotkey`
  and `addHotkey` stay unguarded as in v3.1; the vault sets the flag around the owner's L4 removal
  call, during which L4 makes no call but the precompile's (its views, a move or the direct removal).
- *`msg.value` and access.* No library reads `msg.value` (L3 receives it as a parameter, so it is
  counted once) or makes an access check of its own (under `execute`, `msg.sender` is the vault
  itself; L4 applies the hotkey rules to the roles the vault read and passed); a static check over
  the library sources enforces both, precisely: `msg.sender` appears in library code only as L3's
  refund target, and `msg.value` nowhere. The library checker (`contracts/script/library-rules.mjs`)
  enforces I-32's static half on solc's resolved AST, skipping only a linked library's external
  bodies when it walks a closure; it refuses inline assembly anywhere in a library or the
  first-party code it compiles in (OpenZeppelin's vendored `Math` aside; `deductFee` shrinks its
  lists by copy); its bytecode backstops refuse TSTORE in any library, SSTORE in L2 and the payout
  math and LOG in the payout math; L4 writes exactly its write set of the vault's storage (the
  write-set rule, on solc's AST, binding L4's storage pointers to the vault's state variables at the
  vault's call sites); no field of the `PayoutIn` L3 receives, nor of the batch it holds, is written
  in L3's closure; and no library call carries `{value}` to a cast target, so no call to the staking
  precompile carries value (R1-C F-1, N-1, F-2); the code compiled into L2 and into L4 calls exactly
  its listed precompile functions (never `addStake`, `claimRootWithHotkey` or
  `setRejectStakingHotkeyGrowth`), and no call into a linked library goes through `using … for` (the
  rules read its arguments positionally; fix round 1); a sixth build check
  (`contracts/script/warning-gate.mjs`) requires `src/` to compile with zero solc warnings.
- *Storage pointers.* Each library function takes narrow storage pointers to exactly the structs it
  writes, not one large struct, and memory copies of what it only reads (L2 the hotkey list, L3 a
  `PayoutIn` with the batch as read, the reservation and the door check's result), so "writes only
  its declared fields" is enforced by the types. One shared layout file (`CascadeTypes.sol`) defines
  the structs for the vault, the libraries and the lens; no library declares or addresses storage of
  its own.

**Invariants.** I-32 *fixed libraries:* the vault's bytecode links L1, L2, L3 and L4, and L3's links
`CascadePayoutMath`; these are the only DELEGATECALL targets in the vault's context. No library
contains `SELFDESTRUCT` or `CALLCODE`, DELEGATECALLs anything but a linked library, makes an access
check, or holds inline assembly (OpenZeppelin's vendored `Math` aside); no library's runtime code
has TSTORE, L2's and the payout math's no SSTORE, the payout math's no LOG; L3 never writes the
`PayoutIn` it receives; L4 writes only the hotkey list, `isHotkey` and `lastClaimAt`; L2 and L4 call
only their listed precompile functions; no library call carries value to the staking precompile;
none is upgradeable; each library's external functions are either non-view (guarded against direct
calls) or harmless view or pure; each library's runtime code is verified at deploy with its link
references and immutables filled in (section 7); no library is ever called with a user-supplied
target. I-33 *lens parity:* every value of either lens equals its target's internal value after
every handler step. I-34 *token accounting:* for every account, its `Transfer` mints less burns
equal its `balanceOf`, and their sum equals `totalSupply`.

## 9. Test and review gates before mainnet

1. **Unit tests per finding** (3.1 to 3.9), each written to fail on v3.1 first where v3.1 has the
   behaviour (the PoC files ported into `test/pentest/`), the suite green under the chosen pipeline,
   `forge fmt --check`, `forge lint`, Slither re-run (the linked libraries' DELEGATECALLs, with
   their verdicts written). **The library boundary (8.1):** a direct call to a non-view, non-pure
   library function reverts at the compiler's call guard; a view or pure one runs on the library's
   own empty storage, keeps any value sent and cannot emit events; neither touches the vault.
   Reentrancy across the boundary (a re-entrant bridge, payout recipient or refund target inside L3,
   the precompile's TAO inside L2) hits the vault's lock. Each library writes only its declared
   fields (narrow pointers, plus a Slither or Aderyn check of the storage each library writes and a
   review checklist), and a static check finds `msg.sender` in library code only as L3's refund
   target and `msg.value` nowhere (the library checker on solc's AST, beside the zero-warning build
   check). Both lenses' layout constants, struct members included, match `forge inspect …
   storageLayout` (a build test), and the published vault ABI, merged by the build script, covers
   every event and error any library can emit (a build test). **The exits machine's named tests** in
   `test/governance/ExitsMachine.t.sol`, one per cell of section 3.6's table, 25 plus `T-6` and
   `T-R`: `T-1.SO` the H-1 script; `T-1.SG` the guardian pauses first under a pause bot; `T-1.HO`
   the honest incident; `T-2.SO`, `T-3.SO`, `T-5.SO`; `T-2.SG` (the door unknown, no activation
   schedulable under the guardian's hold, the swap on day 8, the activation on day 15), `T-3.SG` and
   `T-4.SG` (a thief re-closing a Reopen that lands before the swap; swap first, then the Reopen);
   `T-5.SG` (the guardian refused); `T-2.HO` (a buggy door replaced from OPEN before removal; a
   guardian-removed rogue door kept out past a stolen owner; `setGuardian` to the same guardian,
