   then a replacement on day 15; an owner hold undone only by a Reopen of that door), `T-3.HO` (the
   14-day rail migration), `T-4.HO`, `T-5.HO`; `T-4.SO` (what the vault does and does not see of a
   Base-side disable); `T-1.SOG` to `T-5.SOG` (a door only after 7 OPEN days; row 4 as its SO);
   `T-1.CO` to `T-5.CO` (the stalemate under a sweeper that drains the keys' accounts: the founder's
   owner stamps, relayed from a fresh account or a bot with a daily pre-signed stack, kill every
   item the thief schedules); `T-6` (every non-instrument at its bounds, a Base pause included,
   leaves every exit working and stamps nothing); `T-R` (I-26). **The adapter's H-3 tests**: a
   reverting-then-re-entering recipient and a two-level re-entry.
2. **Invariant suite**: the current 11 re-based on the ledger, plus I-1 to I-38 (I-33 checked after
   every handler step through a test-only vault subclass that adds no storage and exposes the
   internal views, with the code-size limit lifted in the test profile only, over the handler's
   tracked users, hotkeys, batch keys, doors, bridges and scheduled ids, for both lenses; I-34
   through `vm.recordLogs` per step into per-account running sums), with the handler extended
   (swaps, strangers' adds, gifts, adoption, write-off, a second door, origin changes, every
   instrument played by an owner and a guardian handler, the adapter campaign with token and native
   parking, short payments, drained cushions, refunds, drops, redeem-only batches, rounding,
   ForeverMoney states, **a re-entrant recipient actor**, the mock's fee and hold switches, and the
   two-chain liveness I-28 under the mock rails). 128 × 64 in CI; a 3,000 × 64 run and a 32-seed
   sweep recorded before review. Two added at Task 42 (R4's stuck-money and vault-favour reviews):
   I-37 *every claim has a non-owner exit:* from every state the campaign reaches, with one more
   blocker applied, the users, anyone and the keeper's routine work alone get each user's money out
   in bounded time (about 19 days: four rounds of exits a day apart, the payout delay, the park
   timeout), but what an explicit table's closers hold (the vault paused; the launch door removed;
   a primary disabled; stake truly lost; a queued payout behind a rail closed for good; and the
   third parties' ForeverMoney migration, the chain's minimum, the founder's cushion; and I-28's
   payout through a vault primary the door never activated, FAILURE until the door's activation),
   nothing the table holds gets out (the table is exactly what the contracts do; the README's "Exit
   liveness"), and what gets out reaches its own user (each user's wallet rises by what left its
   holdings, less the stated rounding).
   I-38 *no one gains except by time held:* across every action the acting actor's wealth does not
   rise (its own reward claim but by its share of the cut's rounding); a holder who may leave gains
   at most its time share of the rewards that accrued while it held (net of the cut), of the gifts
   as they vest and of donations as they land, and of the vest above the accrual that departed
   holders' stake funded (§3.5's fourth residual; the campaign tests only the attacker's own case,
   the one holder there that leaves, its exits' walks crediting what their stake can still add to
   the release; since R7 an owner's move onto the lower-rate standby credits the rate it gives up the
   same way), given every listed hotkey with stake claimed in one round, rounds no further apart
   than `rewardVesting` (30 days at launch; an adoption vests on its own drip and delays no reward,
   Revision 27). That precondition is the keeper's and the chain's to keep, not the contracts' (R6b
   U2): a hotkey the keeper claims apart from the others (a staggered listing) re-spreads their
   rewards a little (about 0.03 of a month's rewards a cycle at 90/10, Fable's model); one whose claims
   the chain refuses (`RootClaimTooHeavy`, as for 15 of 21 validators until spec 484) or that the
   keeper's cost rule skips for longer than the period brings a backlog that vests over one period,
   above its accrual, which an entrant who times it shares (only per-holder accounting removes that;
   a longer period on a backlog claim is unsafe, since a dust claim would set it); the owner's answer
   is to stop new entrants for that period (`maxTotalAssets` and the door's cap, both immediate), the
   monitor's `rewards.uncollected` the trigger. Since R6b U1 no one but the keeper restarts a claim
   schedule that still vests: `claimRewards` reverts `NotKeeper` for any other caller while
   `_claimUnvested() != 0` (before, a stranger's claim of one small hotkey mid-schedule re-spread the
   rest of the month's rewards past their end, up to a quarter, Revision 27's adoption residual through
   `claimRewards`; with one hotkey a claim always brings the whole accrual, so it was harmless there).
   The amount-weighted restart period the review suggested was rejected: it breaks the one-hotkey
   bound (up to 1.5 times the accrual rate, Fable's model). The favour campaign now has a second,
   unevenly accruing hotkey (the standby, `moveToStandby`) and a stranger's claims of either, and since
   R7 an attacker's or a stranger's claim the moment the claim schedule has fully vested
   (`claimAtVestEnd`), with minimums on the claims the vault took (`outsideClaimsMade`) and on those
   at a vested end (`claimsAtVestEnd`), since U1's refusals alone met the claim counts' minimums; and
   every gain is matched by a loss or a reward (the treasury's cut, and its
   fee excess, L3, the stated exception); and across every action the attacker does not make, the
   attacker's wealth does not rise beyond its rounding. I-38's campaign covers one failure branch,
   a delivery the paused vault refused and its retry, and the owner's cancel; it does not cover a
   park and its delivery or refund, a bounce or stake missing, which the README's "Vault favour"
   names with the pins and campaigns that hold each. Each campaign's rare states have a minimum hit
   count asserted in the long run only (ruling B9, `CASCADE_MIN_HITS_RUNS`).
3. **Adversarial review rounds**, two agents at a time, Fable: round 1 the vault and the adapter
   (including the lock and CEI); round 2 the scheduler and the machine (every setter's bounds,
   direction and kind, the self-call surface, the cancel rule, the window, the clocks, every row,
   the reachability sweep) and the oracle/staker/front door; a third round on the fixes; one
   whole-branch review. Every finding gets a test.
4. **Fork rehearsal** (`FORK=1`): Base and Bittensor as before (the real `OffRamp`, the real
   `AlphaVault` over `MockStaking`, parking, the burn rolled back, `deliverParked`, a refund through
   the real Base `OffRamp` to a front door that accepts known bridges); `AdapterGas` re-calibrated
   with the park path, `PARK_GAS_RESERVE` and `EXIT_GAS_RESERVE`, with the library boundary in place
   (the message re-encoded into the DELEGATECALL; the walk two frames deep); the
   `CREATE`-from-fresh-keys deployment on both forks (the libraries and the lenses included) with
   the read-backs of section 7, which must fail for a vault linked to a different L1 build, a
   correct library build at an address other than the plan's, and a lens bound to the wrong target.
5. **Bittensor testnet 945 rehearsal** (dropped on 2026-11-11: what follows is the record of the plan; the live
   checks a fork cannot run are the mainnet canary's, with tiny amounts after the deploy, and the gas constants were
   set from the fork at Revisions 29 and 30) with the real precompile: deploy from fresh keys; stake;
   `getTotalColdkeyStakeOnSubnet` against `getStake`; the reject flag for a contract coldkey (a
   transfer onto an unlisted hotkey refused, onto the listed one accepted, then `adoptStake`, then a
   full exit); **every kind of validator hotkey swap** (all-subnet, per-subnet retaining nominators,
   a per-subnet split), each against `stakeMissing()`, a stranger's `addHotkey` and L-1's immediate
   `setStakeHotkey`; a refused `removeStake` under a capped gas; `moveStake` under a hold;
   `PARK_GAS_RESERVE` and `EXIT_GAS_RESERVE` measured; `TSTORE` from a deployed contract; `PUSH0`
   and `MCOPY` from compiled code; a real `claimRootWithHotkey` for `claimGasRequired`; the 1-rao
   fresh-position read; `removeStakeFull` on an empty position; how Blockscout and taostats classify
   the share token; a third party's `transfer_stake` onto a hotkey the vault removed after claiming,
   and a stranger's `addHotkey` of it refused (round 6 L-3); **whether root dividends can land as
   stake on the vault's hotkey without a vault claim (the auto-claim index)**, which would bypass
   the reward cut and must then be booked by `adoptStake` with the cut applied, or the cut moved to
   the stake-side delta measured at any booking.
6. **Deployment**: section 7, only with the founder, after `pnpm ops preflight`.
7. **The monitor's scheduler alerts** (the single-key mitigation, section 5): on the fork rehearsal,
   a `Scheduled`, a `Cancelled` and an `Executed` item on each chain each produce a critical email
   with the kind, selector, decoded arguments and `readyAt`, and `pnpm ops stamp` relays a
   Ledger-signed stamp from a fresh account that kills a pending item on each chain. Not gates (D1,
   D2): the guardian's own device and the Safe, recommended before caps rise well above the founder
   level.

## 10. Open questions for the founder, each with a recommendation

Answered earlier and removed: cancelling the deposit today (yes, path A); `redeemDirect` while
missing (an explicit opt-in); the treasury's settability; the heartbeat and the standby pair
(dropped by decision in revision 5); in revision 6, the Safe and a separate guardian device as
recommendations, not gates (D1, D2), the fee valve on `processPayouts` (added, round 5 L-3), and a
guardian-removed door re-activated only as an Ordinary item (round 5 L-6, option a); in revision 7,
the signed stamp (`stampBySig`, added, round 6 M-1), no stamp on the staker, and a switched-away
primary left known; in revision 8, an owner-only stamp effective as of its signing time (round 7
Info 9), and no delete on a guardian's removal (unreachable); in revision 11, the one-vault layout
with three linked libraries plus a lens plus a minimal token (approved 2026-11-01, with the
DELEGATECALL posture of section 8.1).

1. **One scheduler replacing the three registries?** Recommended: yes.
2. **The compiler: solc ≥ 0.8.38 with `evm_version = cancun` and `via_ir` (the approved layout
   measures 21,401 B, 1,675 B under the gate; the advisories make it the clear choice), with
   Sourcify/taostats verification for that version confirmed before the first deployment?**
   Recommended: yes; 0.8.24 legacy does not fit, so a verification failure would mean trims, not a
   fallback.
3. **The parameter delay: 48 hours (recommended) or 24?**
4. **Park timeout 14 days (shorter than the longest accepted door hold; a refund then is an exit,
   not a loss), refund fee paid by whoever calls?** Recommended: yes.
5. **Global outflow at launch equal to the per-door limit (20%, sharing the 10 TAO floor), both
   hard-bounded to [20%, 50%]?** Recommended: yes; for two doors, 20% and 30%.
6. **The hard bounds that replace the live floors: outflow ≥ 20% per door and global, its floor in
   [10, 100] TAO; `maxFeeAbs` in [0.01, 0.015] TAO; `maxEntryValue` ≥ 10 TAO; `payoutDelay` ≤ 48 h;
   the front door's `batchInterval` ≤ 24 h and `minDeposit` ≥ 0.03 TAO?** Recommended: yes; every
   launch value sits at a bound or inside, and nothing an owner can set can freeze an exit.
7. **E5, one redeem entry at most half the day's allowance?** Recommended: no.
8. **The front door's initial pool liquidity floors (deliveries only): half the 30-day median,
   measured in the fork rehearsal; and a one-time look for a Chainlink TAO/USD feed on Base?**
   Recommended: yes.
9. **The treasury, immutable where it is immutable: `0x34D1f7DC8Cb62280d0bc617b784Bd2c4668809f6`
   (account 3 of the owner's Ledger seed) or a new address on a separate seed?** Recommended: a
   separate seed before deploy, since it cannot change later and a copied owner seed would otherwise
   take protocol revenue.
10. **Reward vesting in [1, 30] days, default 7, snapshotted per claim?** Recommended: yes.
11. **Should the standby root validator hotkey hold a share of the stake (say 10%)?** Recommended:
    yes once a standby with ≤ 9% take is chosen, as diversification.
12. **Report the root-hold stamping (transfers, claims, `move_stake`) to
    Opentensor?** Recommended: yes.
13. **Launch caps unchanged (40 TAO, 10 TAO batches, the LAUNCH limits)?** Recommended: yes.
14. **`MIN_REDEEM_VALUE` 0.03 TAO and `MAX_FEE_ABS` 0.015 TAO?** Recommended: yes.
15. **The founder's key as owner from the constructor, every launch value a constructor argument,
    addresses by plain `CREATE` from fresh single-use keys, `extcodehash` read-backs, no hand-off?**
    Recommended: yes.
16. **The staker's buy floor at 5% of the oracle's value, at every size?** Recommended: yes.
17. **The front door's `pause` stopping deposits and USDC sales only, never an exit?** Recommended:
    yes.
18. **The Safe and the guardian's own device are recommendations (decided, D1 and D2); when do
    they happen?** Recommended: before `depositCap` or `maxTotalAssets` rise above the founder
    level: the guardian first (a second device, one `setGuardian`, 8 days), then the 2-of-3 Safe on
    both chains (`transferOwnership`, 8 days each, from OPEN); the tooling warns until then.
19. **Reopen items (bridges and owner-removed doors) cancellable by the owner only (a stolen
    guardian bounded at 8 days with the swap executed first; the honest guardian re-disables at
    once), rather than by the guardian too (15 days)? An owner stamp, signed or not, kills a pending
    Reopen.** Recommended: owner only.

## Appendix A: state, functions and events changed, per contract

**`CascadeBase`** (`Ownable2Step` with `renounceOwnership` disabled, guardian, pause, bridges, the
scheduler, the shared part of the exits machine): removes `proposeBridge`, `cancelBridgeProposal`,
`bridgeProposedAt`, `proposeGuardian`, `acceptGuardian`, `cancelGuardianProposal`,
`pendingGuardian`, `guardianProposedAt`, their events; `activateBridge` and `transferOwnership`
become `onlyScheduled`; adds `setGuardian` (`onlyScheduled`; it clears a guardian's bridge hold, and
a virtual hook the vault uses clears a guardian's door hold), the scheduler (`scheduled` as
`Item{scheduledAt, readyAt, class, kind, selector, readyBlock}`, `MS_PER_BLOCK` (an immutable,
public, milliseconds since Revision 28), `pendingSince` with `acceptOwnership` gated as in 3.6, `schedule` with the
canonical-length, already-stamped and active-target checks, `execute`, `cancel`, `prune`,
`EXECUTE_WINDOW`, `_executingHash`, `onlyScheduled`, `onlyOwnerOrScheduled`, `_kindOf`,
`_delayClass`, `exitsOpen()`, `ownerHold()`, `guardianHold()` as virtual views (over
`CascadeFormulas.holds(by, primaryDisabledBy, pausedBy, launchDoorRemovedBy)`, false for `None`; the
base passes `None` for the vault's two holds), `lastOwnerClose`, `lastGuardianClose`,
`primaryDisabledBy`, `bridgeKnown`, `_stamp(Actor)` (`Actor { None, Owner, Guardian }` in
`CascadeTypes.sol`; it reverts on `None`), `stampBySig(asOf, sig)` with an immutable
`DOMAIN_SEPARATOR`, `STAMP_TYPEHASH` (`OwnerStamp(uint64 asOf)`) and `STAMP_MAX_TTL = 1 days`,
`StampedBySig(asOf, relayer)`, `BadStamp`, `StampOutOfWindow`, `Scheduled(id, kind, selector,
readyAt, data)`, `Executed(id)`, `Cancelled(id, by)`, `Lapsed`, `ExitsClosed(by, instrument)`,
`ExitsReopened(instrument)`, `NotScheduled`, `UnknownSelector`, `BadCalldata`, `NotReady`,
`LapsedItem`, `NotCanceller`, `ExitsNotOpen`, `PrimaryStillEnabled`). `pause` and `unpause` record
and stamp nothing here (the vault's overrides record `pausedBy` and stamp, so the front door's pause
stamps nothing, round 7 L-2); `disableBridge` of the primary records and stamps; `setPrimaryBridge`
only while the primary is disabled, clears, re-checks the caps; `_requireBridge` on inbound is
`bridgeKnown` (front door) or `bridgeEnabled` (vault; a rogue inbound to the vault is the registry's
business as today). Its public getters become internal, served through `extsload(bytes32)` and the
lens (8.1).

**`CascadeVault`**: constructor takes `Params` (owner, guardian, keeper, treasury, reward cut,
`launchChain`, initial front door, every limit, `globalOutflowBps`, `rewardVesting`,
`idleBufferBps`, `unstakeGas`, `minDirectDeposit`; the libraries linked). State `bookedStakeRao`,
`lastAdoptAt`, `redeemOrigin`, `activeDoors`, `doorState` (Unknown, Active, Removed; a guardian's
removal returns a door to Unknown, round 5 L-6), `launchDoorRemovedBy`, `pausedBy` (with the
`pause`/`unpause` overrides), `globalOutflowBps`, `rewardVesting`, `vestingPeriod`, `idleBufferBps`,
`unstakeGas`, the adoption drip `adoptVestingAmount`, `adoptVestingStart`, `adoptVestingPeriod`
(Revision 27, last in the layout), `holders` (R4: `Holder{origin, since, fresh}` per holder, in `redeemOrigin`'s slot, which
it replaces: the redeem origin and the door hold of 3.5, the fresh shares written by L1's deposit credit
and `depositDirect`; `STAKE_RAMP` moves to the shared constants); constants `MISSING_TOLERANCE_BPS`, `MIN_MISSING_TOLERANCE_RAO`,
`SHORTFALL_TOLERANCE_RAO`, `DIRECT_ORIGIN`, `MIN_OUTFLOW_BPS = 2_000`, `MIN_MIN_DAILY_OUTFLOW =
10e18`, `MIN_FEE_ABS = 0.01e18`, `MIN_ENTRY_VALUE = 10e18`, `MAX_PAYOUT_DELAY = 48 hours`,
`EXIT_GAS_RESERVE`, `MIN_REDEEM_VALUE = 0.03e18`, `MAX_FEE_ABS = MIN_REDEEM_VALUE / 2`, the two
staking-floor fallbacks; the global window at `burnWindows[bytes32(0)]`. Functions: removes
`proposeFrontDoor`, `cancelFrontDoorProposal`, `frontDoorProposedAt`, `allowFrontDoorRedeems`,
`setFloorFallbacks`, `rootStakeRao` (renamed `visibleStakeRao`); `activateFrontDoor`
`onlyScheduled`, reverting `AlreadyActive`; adds `bookedStake`, `missingStakeRao`,
`surplusStakeRao`, `stakeMissing`, `unlistedStakeRao`, `adoptStake`, `writeOffMissingStake`,
`setRedeemOrigin(uint64)`, `setRewardVesting`, `setIdleBuffer`, `setUnstakeGas`, the hold overrides;
`redeemDirect(uint256, bool)`; `setOutflowLimits(bps, minDaily, globalBps)` bounded; `setFeeLimits`
bounded below; `setMessageLimits` bounded below and against the bridge cap, `minChunk` down
loosening; `setPayoutDelay` bounded above; `setStakeHotkey` immediate only under L-1; `moveStake(from, to,
rao)` clamped to the position and its destination checked against the stake hotkey at execute; `addHotkey` with the last-slot rule and the permissionless
condition (`lastClaimAt[h] == 0`); `removeHotkey` for keeper/guardian (empty, dust) and anyone
(empty, nothing unclaimed, set full, stake missing); `removeFrontDoor` records and stamps;
`depositDirect`, `onBridgeMessage` get `StakeMissing`; `_creditDeposits` writes origin and bounces
foreign origins; `_burnRedeems` the global window and the chain origin; `claimRewards` books
stake-side and skips the cut while missing; `_unstake` the `try/catch` with the per-position gas;
`_removeStake` books stake-side, floored; `processPayouts` `payable` with the fee valve;
`processPayouts` and `redeemDirect` the shortfall rule; `_unstake` returns the walk's cost, which
`redeemDirect` charges, refusing a zero payment (`ZeroAmount`), and `processPayouts` hands to L3's
`finish` (Revision 23); `_deductFee`, `_verifyPayouts`, `_takeChunk`
call the library; `cancelPayoutBatch` carries bounces forward and stamps for a launch-door batch.
Events: `StakeAdopted`, `StakeWrittenOff`, `RedeemOriginSet`, `UnstakeSkipped`, `ShortfallAbsorbed`
(its `amount` everything the leaver bore, `unmet + cost`), `PayoutChunkSent` gains `unstakeCost`,
`DepositBounced` gains `reason`, `RedeemedDirect` gains `acceptedMissing`,
`PayoutBatchCancelled(key, reminted, bouncesCarried)`, `LimitSet` for the new parameters
(`setOutflowLimits` logs `OutflowLimitsSet(bps, minDaily, globalBps)` instead, its three values in one
event; Task 17). Errors:
`StakeMissing`, `NotScheduled`, `AboveBridgeCap`, `SlotReserved`, `UnknownOrigin`,
`WriteOffTooLarge`, `BelowMinAdoption`, `AdoptTooSoon`, `NotEligible`, `AlreadyActive`, `BadValue`,
`NotListed`, `NothingToCancel`, `InvalidParameter` (the bounds). Layout (8.1): the minimal share
token replaces OpenZeppelin's ERC20; `onBridgeMessage`'s body runs in L1, `_unstake` in L2,
`processPayouts` and `cancelPayoutBatch` in L3; the views listed in 8.1 move to the lens; keeps
`launchChain()`; adds `extsload(bytes32)`; the token keeps reverting `transfer`, `transferFrom` and
`approve` and a zero `allowance`.

**`CascadeFrontDoor`**: constructor takes `Params` (owner, guardian, keeper, oracle, swapper,
treasury, the two pool floors, batch interval ≤ 24 h, minimum deposit, deposit cap, request fee,
batch limits, max batch value, delivery slippage); `closeBatch(uint64, uint256)`; `canClose` with
the half rule while the batch holds deposits (R7, 3.5: half the batch value or half the cap's room, or
no room for a minimum deposit; the room rule of R6b U4 widened) and the deposit list's half rule (R12,
Revision 36: `2 × open.deposits.length >= maxDeposits`; the redeem list at its full limit); `_closeBatch` rolls the pot surplus
into the next batch and refunds only `msg.value`; `sweepPot(uint256)` (owner, to the treasury, at most
`potCarried`); `setCaps` refuses `minDeposit` below `MIN_DEPOSIT_FLOOR = 0.03e18` (2 × `MAX_FEE_ABS`)
or above `MAX_BATCH_VALUE` (5,000 TAO, R7); `whenNotPaused` only on `deposit`,
`depositFor`, `deliver`; `MAX_BATCH_INTERVAL = 24 hours`; batch limits 50 + 50, the hard bound since Task 18
(MAX_ENTRIES 50: every legal message fits the gas cap); inbound from `bridgeKnown`; `setOracle`,
`setSwapper` scheduled; `setMinPoolLiquidity(index, value)`; `deliver` checks the floors;
`setMaxBatchValue` checks the bridge cap; `BatchClosed` gains `potCarried`; errors
`WrongBatch(uint64)`, `BatchTooSmall`, `AboveBridgeCap`, `ThinPool(uint8, uint128)`; the cancel cooldown (R11,
Revision 35): constant `CANCEL_COOLDOWN = 5 minutes`, state `lastDepositAt` (`mapping(address => uint256)`,
internal, declared last), written by `depositFor` for its `user`, the view `cancelableAt(address)` (public
since R12, and 0 while the door is paused or its primary bridge is disabled, Revision 36), and
`cancelDeposit` reverting `CancelTooSoon(uint256 cancelableAt)` (file level, `CascadeErrors.sol`) before it.

**`CascadeStaker`**: stays `Ownable2Step` + `ReentrancyGuard`; constructor takes the initial route
and fees; routes and fees owner-immediate within their caps; constant `MAX_BUY_SLIPPAGE_BPS = 500`;
`_stake` requires `taoOut >= oracle.taoValue(usdcIn - fees) × 95%` through `frontDoor.oracle()`.

**`CascadeTwapOracle`**: constant `MIN_CARDINALITY = TWAP_WINDOW + 1` (1,801, Revision 28; 901 before); constructor checks `slot0` on both pools;
new `meanLiquidity()` and `taoValue(uint256)`; error `TooFewObservations(address, uint16)`.

**`CascadePayoutMath`** (new, linked external library, pure): `verify`, `takeChunk`, `deductFee` (no
assembly: it shrinks its lists by copy; no library may hold assembly).

**`CascadeBatchLib`** (L1, new, linked external library): the batch path, `onBridgeMessage`'s body
after the vault's checks, taking a `BatchIn` (with the message's `sender`) and emitting
`BatchDelivered` first; writes balances and supply, the holders' records (the redeem origin and, since
R4, the fresh shares), both windows, and burns only shares held a day, none while `BatchIn.payoutsOpen` is false (S-H1),
leaving a clipped holder 0 or at least `MIN_REDEEM_VALUE` (S-L1); no external call.

**`CascadeStakeLib`** (L2, new, linked external library; called only by the vault's walk, for
`redeemDirect` and `processPayouts`): `_unstake` and the per-position gas split, the walk counting
the stake taken at face value (Revision 23); writes nothing (returns the ledger debits); calls only
the staking precompile.

**`CascadePayoutLib`** (L3, new, linked external library, linked to `CascadePayoutMath` only):
`prepare` (with the full top-up, R4) and `finish` for `processPayouts`, around the vault's walk, and `cancel` for
`cancelPayoutBatch` with the carry-forward (`prepare` takes a `PayoutIn`; `PayoutPlan` gains `free`,
the pre-walk dust count, `received` and `cost`, and `finish` takes the payout list and splits again
after a walk that cost anything, with the coverage check, Revision 23); writes the payout queue, the
ledger and, on a cancel, balances and supply; calls the bridge and refunds the caller last.

**`CascadeVaultLens`** and **`CascadeDoorLens`** (new, read-only, stateless, each constructed with
only its target's address, sharing the code for `CascadeBase`'s getters): the views of 8.1, read
through the target's `extsload(bytes32)` with slot constants, struct members included, generated per
target from `forge inspect … storageLayout`; the vault lens derives `coldkey` through
`ADDRESS_MAPPING` (Bittensor only), and serves `redeemOrigin(holder)` and (R4) `freshShares(holder)` from
each holder's record; the door lens serves the
front door's base state.

**`CascadeHotkeyLib`** (L4, the reserve lever only, section 8): `addHotkey` and `removeHotkey`.

**`CcipAdapterBase`**: `ICascadeBridge` gains `maxMessageValue()`; a `send` variant without
`_checkAmount` for refunds of unconverted tokens.

**`CcipBittensorAdapter`**: `ReentrancyGuard` over `ccipReceive`, `deliverParked`, `refundParked`,
`dropParked`, `sweepSurplus`; state `parked` (`Parked{amount, parkedAt, converted}`), `parkedNative`,
`parkedTokens`; immutable `treasury`; constants `PARK_TIMEOUT`, `PARK_GAS_RESERVE`,
`SHORTFALL_TOLERANCE_WEI`, `RESERVE_TARGET`, `TOKEN_RESERVE_TARGET`, `REFUND_CHUNK`;
`convertExact(uint256)` (self only); `_deliver` decodes, skips conversion for `amount == 0`,
converts through the gas-capped self-call or parks tokens when the balance covers the parks,
delivers with a saturating gas reserve or parks native; `_collect` `>= amount - 1 rao` covered from
the cushion, never from parked tokens; `deliverParked` (CEI: delete and decrement before the call,
restore in the catch), `refundParked` (delete first; the floor check skipped), `dropParked`,
`quoteRefund`, `sweepSurplus`; events `BatchParked`, `ParkedConverted`, `ParkedDelivered`,
`ParkedRefunded`, `ParkedDropped`, `ShortfallCovered`, `SurplusSwept`; errors `AlreadyParked`,
`NotParked`, `ParkedTooYoung`, `NothingToRefund`, `ReserveShort`, `OnlySelf`. R4: immutables
`inboundGasBase` and `inboundGasPerEntry` (constructor arguments after the alpha vault, held to the
base's gas-model checks; the pair includes `PARK_GAS_RESERVE`, and the base must exceed it), the view `parkedDeliveryGas(message)`, `deliverParked`'s gas requirement
(`InsufficientGas`, the shared error) and `ReserveShort` before every conversion or spend of a
token park's tokens (C-L1); `tokenCushion`, `fundCushion(amount)` and `CushionFunded` (F-1: the
cushion booked, covered by C-L1's check, spent only by a short mint, left by the sweep);
`convertExact`'s exact token delta (F-3); the native `ReserveShort` on a converted park's delivery
and refund (F-5); `canSendPayouts()` (S-H1). `IAlphaVault` gains `migrationReadyAt()` and
`migrationFinished()`.

**`CcipBaseAdapter`**: immutable `treasury`; `sweepSurplus` for stray ETH and tokens.

**`CascadeMessages`**: `MAX_ENTRIES` 50 a side since Task 18 (was 100), so every legal message fits the gas cap.

## Appendix C: what this spec measured, and how

- Live reads on 2026-10-31 (read-only `cast call`, throttled, public RPCs): the v3-launch vault
  and front door state in section 2; the staking precompile's `getTotalColdkeyStakeOnSubnet`,
  `getTotalColdkeyStake`, `getRejectStakingHotkeyGrowth`, `getDefaultMinStake`,
  `getNominatorMinRequiredStake`, and the `Unknown selector` revert for `getStakingHotkeys`;
  ForeverMoney's `vaultColdkey` and its stake; `cast code` of the two deterministic-deployment
  factories on 964 and Base; `cast call --override-code` of three tiny programs on 964 proving
  `PUSH0`, `MCOPY` and `TSTORE`/`TLOAD` execute.
- Bytecode sizes: `forge build --sizes` at the project's settings, with `FOUNDRY_OPTIMIZER_RUNS=1`,
  with `FOUNDRY_VIA_IR=true`, and for four scratch copies of `CascadeVault.sol` with the named
  sections deleted (built from `test/scratch/`, discarded afterwards).
- Not measured here, left to the gates: real delivery gas, `PARK_GAS_RESERVE`, `EXIT_GAS_RESERVE`,
  the testnet behaviours in section 9, revisions 2 to 6's byte estimates, Sourcify support for a
  newer solc. Safe{Wallet} does not list chain 964 (the fifth review's reading).

## Revisions 2 to 7 (2026-10-31)

Their full changelogs are in the git history (8c8f094, 399edef, 26a58c3, 9d2759b, 9ff8a0a,
426a367). In short: revision 2 hardened parking and origin by chain; 3 added the exits-open rule
with freeze clocks (H-1) and the floored ledger; 4 made the rule a machine with a scenario table
and Reopen items; 5 cut scope to hard bounds, one adapter lock (H-3), known-bridge payouts (H-2) and
plain `CREATE` (M-1), with states 5 → 4, clocks 3 → 2 and instrument rows 14 → 5; 6 made the Safe
and the guardian's own device recommendations (D1, D2) with the scheduler alerts as the mitigation,
and added the fee valve, the [10, 100] TAO outflow floor, the 0.03 TAO minimum deposit,
guardian-removed doors as unknown, the rolled-forward fee pot and the compromised-lane residual; 7
added the signed stamp (round 6 M-1) with Reopen on the owner's clock, lasting removals by the
guardian account and `setGuardian` to the same guardian, `schedule`'s two refusals, a never-listed
hotkey for the permissionless add, and the runbooks.

## Revision 8 changelog (2026-10-31, after the clean seventh review)

- **The stamp, simplified (round 7 Info 9; decided).** (a) Owner only: `stampBySig(asOf, sig)`,
  type `OwnerStamp(uint64 asOf)`. A guardian stamp killed a subset of what the owner's kills, so the
  role is gone, and with it round 7's L-1 surface. (b) Effective as of its signing time:
  `lastOwnerClose = max(lastOwnerClose, asOf)` with `asOf <= block.timestamp <= asOf + 1 day`, so a
  replay can never kill a later item and the as-of time is never in the future. It is the `deadline
  - TTL` form with the as-of time signed directly, because a short deadline would back-date the
  stamp by almost a day (so round 7 Info 6's 30-minute deadline does not apply). The CO walk holds:
  daily stamps relayed at their as-of time kill every item within about a day, and every item waits
  at least 48 h. The same-second refusal stays, as "the item's clock is not older than now". A
  hand-rolled immutable domain separator, a minimal `ecrecover` (low-s, `v` in {27, 28}), ECDSA
  only. I-29, the tests, `pnpm ops stamp` and the sign page restated.
- **L-2.** The front door's pause stamps nothing: `pausedBy` and the stamp live in the vault's
  `pause` override (Appendix A); `T-6` asserts it.
- **L-3.** One rao-aligned formula for the fee valve and the shortfall rule (3.3): `charged` floored
  to rao, `msg.value` whole rao and never cover; the unstake asks 1,000 rao extra and must then cover
  the chunk, so what a short receipt loses is the holders' idle balance, never a reserve or another
  batch's reservation.
- **L-4.** A rail still being exploited resets its replacement's 7-day activation, so the way off is
  the operator's fix or a redeploy; forged redeem-only parks re-enter at a Reopen as forced exits
  within the allowance (section 5, row 3 HO).
- **Info.** Size re-estimated honestly (the IR registry saving is about 750 B, the library's call
  stubs about 300 B, the stamp hand-rolled): Option 1 about 22,330 B with about 2.2 KB to spare, to
  be measured at the first size gate; Option 2 does not fit. The treasury is account 3 of the same
  seed (section 5; open question 9 now recommends a separate seed before deploy). The delivery skim
  in a stalemate, with the web's answer. The removal's delete of a pending activation dropped as
  unreachable, with an invariant check instead. The ordering rule for the honest owner (3.6) and
  section 7 step 4's sequence. Relayer keys off the Ledger seed and pre-funded on both chains, `v`
  normalised, domain and struct hashes on the sign page, `verify` reading `DOMAIN_SEPARATOR()` and
  `STAMP_TYPEHASH`. I-12 reworded.
- **Complexity, before → after:** states 4 → 4; clocks 2 → 2; kinds 3 → 3; instruments 4 → 4;
  scenario rows 5 + 1 → 5 + 1 (30 cells); invariants 31 → 31; stamp roles 2 → 1; one code path
  dropped (the removal's delete); lines 1,452 → 1,503. Vault projection about 21,540 → 22,330 B
  (Option 1, re-estimated; about 2.2 KB headroom).

## Revision 9 changelog (2026-10-31, after the eighth review: one High, Lows and Info)

Only the listed points changed.

- **H-1, the shortfall rule is receipt-based again.** Liquidity is read once before the walk and the
  check is on the walk's receipt (`unmet <= 1,000 rao`; wei in and out, as in v3.1), never a re-read
  balance that counted other batches' still-staked reservations as liquid. `redeemDirect` asks for
  `out - available` and pays `out - unmet`; the fee-valve block, I-12 (restated per exit) and I-13
  follow. New tests: two or more queued batches at zero idle balance, and a direct exit beside a
  staked payout, both under `setRemoveFeeRao(1)`; the invariant handler checks `processPayouts`'
  liveness at buffer 0.
- **L-1.** Each chain's adapter is deployed first (the constructors read its `maxMessageValue()`):
  Bittensor adapter, library, vault; Base adapter, oracle, front door, staker.
- **L-2.** The last hotkey slot opens only to an add made while `stakeMissing()`; other adds fill at
  most three. A dust sweep was considered and not adopted (a stranger can place at least the
  minimum), so I-6 states its precondition.
- **L-3.** A Bittensor halt longer than about a day is a stated residual (a first-block race at
  resumption), answered by an on-demand stamp with a high tip. (R6 G: the "about a day" is the stamp's
  `STAMP_MAX_TTL`, a stamp signed during the halt expiring first, not the delay; and since Revision 28
  the race also needs the halt to begin once the item's floor of blocks is in and span its `readyAt`.)
- **Info.** `pnpm ops stamp` takes `asOf` from each chain's latest block; pre-signed stacks are short
  and live on the relayer, and the last `asOf` dates the founder's next surviving item; the treasury
  works on both chains and its cut shares are redeemed regularly; I-4 includes the absorbed short
  receipt and I-6 the harmless gift; the parked-redeem web copy; the sign page warns on an
  owner-account door removal; row 3 SG can stretch to about 15 days. The copied-key text keeps the
  staker's delivery route in the skim: the staker is the swapper and its route is its owner's to
  set, so "the keeper only" would understate it (`setSwapper` itself stays scheduled).
- **Implementation decisions (new 3.10).** The receipt check and `_unstake`'s units; `adoptStake`
  leaving `lastStakeAt` and the ramp alone, with its own fallback; no implicit listing in
  `setStakeHotkey`; the stake thresholds; carried bounces keeping `queuedAt` and an empty cancel
  reverting; non-loosenings schedulable; `StampedBySig` on every valid call with a strict 65-byte
  signature; the window's bounds and `abi.decode`; `positionsLeft` counted once.
- **Complexity, before → after:** states 4 → 4; clocks 2 → 2; kinds 3 → 3; instruments 4 → 4;
  scenario rows 5 + 1 → 5 + 1 (30 cells); invariants 31 → 31; lines 1,503 → 1,604. Vault projection
  about 22,330 → 22,370 B (the reserved slot, two errors and the 65-byte check; the receipt check is
  no larger), about 2.2 KB headroom; section 8 is unchanged.

## Revision 10 changelog (2026-10-31, after the clean ninth review; final)

Only the listed points changed.

- **L-1.** I-13's liveness holds as long as the walk can unstake `need - free + T` (a root hold can
  make every batch revert, as in v3.1; the idle buffer answers it); the handler checks it with the
  hold off.
- **L-2.** "Full" is `hotkeys.length == MAX_HOTKEYS`, and a stranger's removal also needs
  `stakeMissing()`, so no stranger can remove an empty `X` during a scheduled `setStakeHotkey(X)`.
- **L-3.** `claimGasRequired` hard-bounded to [the measured value, 60,000,000]; the copied-key list
  in section 5 gains the yield and fee levers, none of which reaches principal.
- **L-4.** I-4 names the 1-rao-short fresh-position read; I-6 names its further preconditions (a
  sub-minimum swapped position in a small vault, two swaps with one free slot).
- **Info.** The walk's stopping rule (`unmet <= T`, both callers); "never needs scheduling"; the
  monitor deduplicates `StampedBySig` by `asOf`; the claim books `_sub0(after, before)`; v3.1's
  `NotRaoAligned` kept in `_deliver`; a bounce behind an unpayable older batch in deep insolvency;
  carried bounces in their original order; the status line; the sign page's check at `execute` that
  an item would not act as a loosening; the failed unlisted read accepted under the write-off's
  notice; the bounces-only cancel test expecting `NothingToCancel`; `setStakeHotkey` before the
  keeper frees a swapped stake hotkey's slot.
- **3.10, items 10 to 15.** "Full"; the stopping rule; what feeds `rampingStake`; `adoptStake`
  refusing while paused; the claim's booking; the carried bounces' order.
- **Complexity, before → after:** states 4 → 4; clocks 2 → 2; kinds 3 → 3; instruments 4 → 4;
  scenario rows 5 + 1 → 5 + 1 (30 cells); invariants 31 → 31; lines 1,604 → 1,666. Vault projection
  about 22,370 → 22,400 B (the `claimGasRequired` bound and the removal's extra condition), about

## Revision 11 changelog (2026-11-01, the code layout; founder-approved)

Behaviour, governance (3.6) and every invariant stay as in revision 10 and the founder decisions;
only where the code sits changes, on the measured size analysis
(`.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-01-cascade-v3.2-implementation/size-analysis.md`, section 5).

- **Section 8 rewritten with measured numbers.** The full v3.2 prototype measures 31,035 B against
  EIP-170's 24,576 B and the 23,076 B gate; section 8's projection was wrong (the payout library
  saved 290 B, not 3,633 B; the scheduler costs 2,738 B and the stamp 774 B). The approved layout
  measures 21,401 B, 1,675 B under the gate; L4 (hotkey operations, 877 B) is the reserve lever.
- **8.1, the code layout.** The vault keeps every entry point, all storage, the scheduler, the
  exits machine and the stamp; three fixed linked libraries run the batch path (L1), the unstake walk
  (L2) and the payout engine (L3); `CascadePayoutMath` stays; a read-only `CascadeLens` over a
  single-slot `extsload(bytes32)` serves the views (listed); a minimal share token replaces
  OpenZeppelin's ERC20 (the surface left: `name`, `symbol`, `decimals`, `balanceOf`, `totalSupply`,
  `Transfer`); the trims; the front door unchanged in design (18,495 B).
- **The DELEGATECALL posture** (status, intro, founder decisions, section 0, section 5, 8.1): the
  vault's only DELEGATECALLs are to its fixed, linked libraries, with the reentrancy and
  storage-pointer rules, and a new invariant, I-32 (no library selfdestructible or upgradeable, each
  verified byte for byte at deploy, none called with a user-supplied target).
- **Deployment and verification (section 7).** The libraries join the nonce plan before the vault,
  from the same fresh single-use key by plain `CREATE`, and each contract gets its lens; the
  read-backs compare each library's code and every link reference, and read moved getters through the
  lens; Sourcify and taostats verify the libraries and the lenses.
- **Tests (section 9).** The library boundary (direct calls, reentrancy across it, each library's
  storage writes limited to its fields, lens values pinned); the invariant suite runs to I-32; the
  fork deployment includes the libraries and lenses.
- **Decisions (founder decisions, section 10, Appendix A).** The one-vault layout recorded as
  approved on 2026-11-01 with the posture change; question 2's headroom figure updated; Appendix A
  gains the libraries, the lens, the token and `extsload`.
- **Complexity, before → after:** states 4 → 4; clocks 2 → 2; kinds 3 → 3; instruments 4 → 4;
  scenario rows 5 + 1 → 5 + 1 (30 cells); invariants 31 → 32 (I-32); linked libraries 1 → 4, plus the
  lens; lines 1,724 → 1,881. Vault 21,401 B measured on the prototype (gate 23,076 B).

## Revision 12 changelog (2026-11-01, after the library-boundary review of revision 11)

Only the listed points changed; behaviour and governance are as in revision 11.

- **M-1, read-backs fill in, never blank (section 7 step 3, 8.1, I-32).** Every contract, library
  and lens is compared with its immutables and link references filled in from the plan; the expected
  link set comes from `deployedBytecode.linkReferences` (missing or unexpected references fail); the
  vault's and L3's references to L2 must agree; each library's `library_deploy_address` equals its
  address; `extcodehash` only after the same filling. The real link graph is stated (vault → L1, L2,
  L3; L3 → L2, `CascadePayoutMath`). The fork rehearsal's verify must fail for a vault linked to
  another L1 build, a correct library at an unplanned address, and a lens bound to the wrong target.
- **M-2, the lens cannot drift (8.1, section 9).** Its layout constants are generated from
  `forge inspect … storageLayout` with a build test; the derived formulas live in shared internal
  functions used by the vault and the lens; lens parity is a new invariant, I-33, checked after every
  handler step; deploy-time lens checks use values that are non-zero by construction.
- **L-1.** The vault's treasury is storage, not an immutable; the lens takes only its target's
  address (`coldkey` through `ADDRESS_MAPPING`, `DOMAIN_SEPARATOR` recomputed, the treasury by
  `extsload`); the vault keeps a `launchChain()` getter so that verify is not circular.
- **L-2.** Events declared once at file level in a shared events file; a build test that library
  events appear identically in the vault's ABI; the indexer's ABI-source parity test covers the
  shares, events and library files.
- **L-3.** L2's callers are exactly `redeemDirect` and L3; `removeHotkey` keeps v3.1's reverting
  removal; the reentrancy rule reads "calls to EVM code only under `nonReentrant`".
- **L-4.** Library and lens balances are informational (never a verify failure or a keeper stop).
- **Info.** The call guard depends on mutability (section 9); no library reads `msg.value` or makes an
  access check, checked statically, and L3 refunds `msg.sender`; narrow storage pointers and memory
  copies per library function; shared helpers for the post-walk ledger update, mint and burn and the
  peer id; the token keeps reverting `transfer`, `transferFrom` and `approve` and a zero `allowance`
  (as measured), with a new invariant, I-34 (`Transfer` mints less burns equal `totalSupply`), the
  dropped zero-address checks noted, and a testnet look at how explorers classify it; explicit
  linking in the deploy tool, never `forge script` library deploys, and L4's effect on I-32 and the
  plan; gas re-measured with the boundary in place; the front door's on-chain-read getters (`asset`,
  `usdc`, `oracle`) stay public; I-32 gains no `CALLCODE`, no other DELEGATECALL, no access checks,
  no storage assembly, and guarded or harmless external functions.
- **Complexity, before → after:** states 4 → 4; clocks 2 → 2; kinds 3 → 3; instruments 4 → 4;
  scenario rows 5 + 1 → 5 + 1 (30 cells); invariants 32 → 34 (I-33, I-34); linked libraries 4 → 4,
  plus the lens; lines 1,881 → 1,978. Vault 21,401 B measured (the token stubs were already in it), plus
  a few dozen bytes for `launchChain()`.

## Revision 13 changelog (2026-11-01, after the clean re-check of revision 12)

Only the listed points changed.

- **N-1, the published vault ABI.** A file-level event or error that only a library emits lands in
  that library's ABI, not the vault's, so the build test of revision 12 could not pass. The published
  vault ABI (for ops, the web and the indexer) is now the vault's ABI merged with its libraries'
  event and error ABIs, produced by a build script; a build test checks that it covers every event
  and error any library can emit; the indexer's parity test uses it. Custom errors are declared once
  at file level too.
- **N-2, two lenses.** `CascadeVaultLens` and `CascadeDoorLens` share the base-getter code, each with
  its own generated constants and a constructor taking only its target (the layouts differ,
  `ADDRESS_MAPPING` exists only on Bittensor, and the holds differ). Both are in the deploy order,
  under I-33 and in the non-zero deploy checks (the door lens's `guardian`, `keeper`,
  `primaryBridge` and its `bridgeEnabled`); Appendix A has both.
- **Info.** The generated constants include struct members (`PayoutBatch`, `Window`, `Item`,
  `Ledger`, `SharesData`); I-33's method (a test-only vault subclass with no added storage, the size
  limit lifted in the test profile only, over the handler's tracked users, hotkeys, batch keys,
  doors, bridges and scheduled ids, for both lenses); I-34 in its per-account form, through
  `vm.recordLogs`; the static check stated precisely (`msg.sender` only as L3's refund target,
  `msg.value` nowhere); `immutableReferences` filled by AST id, every range, with the vault's
  `coldkey` from the `ADDRESS_MAPPING` of its planned address; the shared helper for a stake
  removal's ledger debit, used by L2 and by `removeHotkey`'s direct removal.
- **Complexity, before → after:** states 4 → 4; clocks 2 → 2; kinds 3 → 3; instruments 4 → 4;
  scenario rows 5 + 1 → 5 + 1 (30 cells); invariants 34 → 34; linked libraries 4 → 4; lenses 1 type →
  2; lines 1,978 → 2,030. Vault size unchanged.

## Revision 14 (implementation notes, 2026-11-01)

Wording only: the rulings made while building Phase 2 (Tasks 5a to 5g), recorded where the spec
describes them. Behaviour, governance and invariants are unchanged.

- **The unstake flag** is the vault's transient `_unstaking`, not a ledger field (the ledger is
  `{pending, booked, ramping}`): the vault sets it around its own L2 calls and `removeHotkey`'s
  direct removal, no library sets it, and it is never set across a bridge call (8.1).
- **L3 is split** into `prepare` (no state writes), `finish` (effects, send, `PayoutChunkSent`) and
  `cancel` (returning the `sourceChain`); the vault orchestrates the walk between them and books it
  (the post-walk ledger update is the vault's alone). The link graph is now: the vault links L1, L2
  and L3; L3 links `CascadePayoutMath`; nothing else (7, 8.1, I-32, Appendix A).
- **The interfaces as built:** `PayoutIn` carries the batch, the reservation and whether the bridge
  is enabled (no walk parameters); `BatchIn` carries the message's `sender`, and L1 emits
  `BatchDelivered` first (8.1, Appendix A).
- **The library checker** works on solc's resolved AST and skips only a linked library's external
  bodies; a sixth build check requires a warning-free `src/` (8.1, 9).
- **v3-launch's frozen build** (`ops/builds/preaudit`, from d21daf3) is what ops reads, and the deploy
  gate rebuilds the checkout first (7).
- **Gate A2's sizes** are recorded in section 8, ahead of the prototype figures, which stay as the
  history that chose the layout.

## Revision 15 (implementation notes, 2026-11-01)

Wording only: Phase 3 as built and Checkpoint R1's fixes, recorded where the spec describes them
(the doc-pass lines of the rulings index, the R1 fix report, and the Task 8, 10b and 10c reports).

- **The scheduler (3.6, 3.10.8, 3.10.17, 3.10.18, I-22, Appendix A and B).** `_executingHash` is a
  transient hash of the executing calldata, compared with `keccak256(msg.data)`. Each item records
  `readyBlock` and `execute` also needs `block.number >= readyBlock`, with `SECONDS_PER_BLOCK` per
  contract (`Params.secondsPerBlock`: vault 12, front door 2), so a halted chain cannot consume the
  veto. `acceptOwnership` is the transfer item's tail: within `EXECUTE_WINDOW` of the execute
  (`pendingSince`), from OPEN, with no closure or owner stamp since; `transferOwnership(0)` clears it.
- **The guardian's holds and door removals (3.6, rows 2 and 3, I-24, I-29, section 5).**
  `setGuardian` clears a guardian's bridge hold as well as its door hold, so a contract can read OPEN
  with its primary disabled until the Reopen; the SG bound stays 15 days (up to 22 while the owner's
own disable of the vault's primary is outstanding, I-26). The guardian may remove an
  owner-removed door (it becomes unknown, its pending Reopen is deleted, and the owner's launch-door
  hold becomes the guardian's). A Reopen of any owner-removed launch-chain door clears the launch-door
  hold; a stolen owner key can reopen a guardian-disabled known bridge in 7 days (round 6 L-2's
  trade-off).
- **Instruments (3.1, 3.6, 3.10.16).** A scheduled `moveStake` is clamped to the position at
  execute; `setBatchInterval`'s scheduling is belt-and-braces; row 6 notes `setBatchLimits(1, 1)`'s
  bridge fee per requester.
- **Task 8's interfaces (Appendix A).** `Actor` lives in `CascadeTypes.sol`; `_stamp(Actor)` reverts
  on `None`; `holds` is false for `None`, and the base passes `None` for the vault's two holds.
- **No mint at an empty, underwater vault (3.1, I-1, I-4, 8.1, Appendix B).** The rule is
  `pending + unvested > gross && (unvested != 0 || pending > gross + 1,000 rao)`, round 2's, which
  replaced the first.
- **Libraries (8.1, I-32, Appendix A).** No library holds inline assembly (OpenZeppelin's vendored
  `Math` aside); the bytecode backstops (no TSTORE in any library, no SSTORE in L2 or the payout math,
  no LOG in the payout math); the whole `PayoutIn` is never written by L3; no library call carries
  value to the precompile.

## Revision 16 (implementation notes, 2026-11-01)

- **The block floor is half the delay (3.6, 3.10.17, I-22, Appendix A and B; Checkpoint R1's
  re-review, Low).** `readyBlock` is the scheduling block plus `ceil(delay / (2 *
  SECONDS_PER_BLOCK))`. At the full delay, an item ran only if its blocks arrived inside the window,
  so a `SECONDS_PER_BLOCK` below 8/15 of the real block time, or a 1.9-times slowdown for 15 days,
  made the 8-day class never executable (and at 5 or below on Bittensor, the Registry items too, the
  primary's Reopen among them, against I-26). At half, the guardian still has at least half the
  delay of a live chain's blocks after a halt, a value down to half the real block time runs an
  8-day item at readyAt, and slowdowns up to about 3.7 times are tolerated. The deploy pins
  `SECONDS_PER_BLOCK` to the chain's real block time (Bittensor 12, Base 2).

## Revision 17 (implementation notes, 2026-11-01)

- **`moveStake` names its destination (3.1, 3.6, 3.10.16, Appendix A; Checkpoint R1-A's carry, Task
  14a).** `moveStake(bytes32 from, bytes32 to, uint256 rao)` reverts `InvalidParameter` at execute
  unless `to` is the stake hotkey. Since `setStakeHotkey` is immediate when it follows moved stake
  (L-1), a move the guardian reviewed as "A to S" could otherwise have executed as "A to S'". An item
  whose destination changed is re-scheduled.
- **The hotkey rules' check order (3.1).** `addHotkey` checks the caller's eligibility (`NotEligible`),
  then the zero hotkey, a listed hotkey, a full set, and only then the last slot (`SlotReserved`), so
  a re-add of a listed hotkey reads `HotkeyKnown` at any count. `removeHotkey` refuses an unlisted
  hotkey and the stake hotkey first, for every caller (`UnknownHotkey`, `HotkeyInUse`), then a
  non-owner's removal of a hotkey holding stake (`NotAuthorized`), before reading the unclaimed
  rewards. `setStakeHotkey` checks the listing (`NotListed`) before the direction (`NotScheduled`).
- **Round 9 L-2 as it holds (3.1; the Task 14a review).** A stranger's removal needs the set full and
  stake missing, so it cannot touch an empty `X` under a scheduled `setStakeHotkey(X)` while nothing
  is missing or a slot is free. Full and missing it can; the kept item fails `NotListed` until the
  owner lists `X` again, and no pending-item check is added (it would block I-6's way back whenever
  `X` is the only empty hotkey).
- **`HotkeyRemoved`'s `forfeit` (3.1, Appendix A).** True only for the owner's forfeit; the keeper's,
  the guardian's and a stranger's removals emit `false` whatever flag they pass, since the dust rule
  decides them, so the indexer never reads a forfeit the owner did not make.
- **The immediate switch's cost model (3.1, 3.6).** Gifts count toward `setStakeHotkey`'s immediate
  test: a stolen owner key that gifts more than the stake hotkey holds, and at least half the booked
  stake, onto a listed `X` switches at once and can then consolidate through its own removals; on a
  fresh vault a dust gift suffices. The reject flag limits `X` to the coldkey's staking set and no
  principal is at risk (the validator's take and the swap griefs are the harm), so the guardian
  treats any immediate switch as an alarm and the monitor alerts on it (T54/T55).
- **The last slot rests on the reject flag (3.1, section 9).** No stranger can squat the reserved
  slot with a hotkey of its own only because the flag refuses stake onto hotkeys outside the
  coldkey's staking set; the mainnet canary confirms it for a contract coldkey (section 9's testnet gate was dropped).

## Revision 18 (implementation notes, 2026-11-02)

- **Reward vesting, owner-set and snapshotted (3.6, Appendix A and B; Task 15).** `rewardVesting` is
  a constructor argument and `setRewardVesting(uint64)` (Param class, Ordinary; down loosens, so it
  is scheduled; up, or the same, at once; `InvalidParameter` outside 1 to 30 days, at execute for a
  decrease), logged `LimitSet(setRewardVesting.selector, period, 0)`. Each restart of the schedule
  (a claim's, Task 16's adoption's, and the write-down below) writes the amount, the start and
  `vestingPeriod`, the longer of `rewardVesting` and what is left of the old schedule (the review's
  I1: after a decrease, a restart re-spread the whole carried remainder over the new short period,
  handing it within a day to whoever deposited before the claim; now a carried remainder never vests
  faster than its own schedule); `unvestedRewards()` reads `vestingPeriod`, so a change reaches only
  the next restart. The remainder a restart carries is read under the old period first. The
  constant `REWARD_VESTING` is gone; the lens serves `rewardVesting()` and `vestingPeriod()` (0
  before the first claim).
- **The supply-0 write-down (3.1, I-1; Task 15, the T10c carry).** At a supply of 0, `depositDirect`
  and a delivery write the schedule down to `sub0(gross, pending)` (gross before their money) before
  their underwater check, restarting it there through the same snapshot; so at a supply of 0 a
  deposit is underwater only when `pending > gross + UNDERWATER_TOLERANCE`. It never runs with
  shares outstanding and logs nothing. It never raises the unvested amount at its moment and never
  speeds what it keeps (the snapshot keeps at least the old end), though it can stretch its pace
  when the period in force is the longer (at a supply of 0 no holder's). The cut's skip is
  unchanged. The keeper's and the web's bounce predictions at a supply of 0 apply the same
  reduction (Phases 10 and 12).

## Revision 19 (implementation notes, 2026-11-02)

- **The reserve lever is used: L4 `CascadeHotkeyLib` (8, 8.1, I-32; step L4).** Hotkey management
  (`addHotkey`, `removeHotkey` and `moveStake` after the vault's role reads; the constructor's first
  listing too) moved into a fourth fixed linked library, with no behaviour, selector, event, error
  or storage change. The vault sets its transient unstake flag around the owner's L4 removal call:
  the window is wider than v3.1's (it also covers the owner's move of a leftover above the minimum
  and the precompile's views), but no call in it reaches anything but the precompile, whose
  `moveStake` and views pay the vault nothing. Vault 21,206 B to 20,004 B; L4 2,539 B. The library
  rules gain L4's call target (the precompile), its opcodes (CALL, STATICCALL) and a write-set rule
  (WRITE_SETS: the hotkey list, `isHotkey`, `lastClaimAt`; fix round 1: L4 takes no ledger pointer,
  and the owner's direct removal returns its two measurements, which the vault books with
  `CascadeLedger.applyWalk` after clearing the flag, as it books L2's walk). Fix round 1 also pins
  the precompile functions each library may call (L2: `getStake`, `getDefaultMinStake`,
  `getNominatorMinRequiredStake`, `removeStake`, `removeStakeFull`; L4: `getStake`,
  `getDefaultMinStake`, `getUnclaimedRootTaoByHotkey`, `moveStake`, `removeStakeFull`, and
  `removeStake`, compiled in with the shared removal but never taken by L4), and refuses any call
  into a linked library through `using … for`; fix round 2: the staking interface inherits nothing,
  and a call on it to a function it does not declare itself is refused. A later hard-coded gas limit
  for `addHotkey`, `removeHotkey` or `moveStake` needs about 1/64 more headroom for the library call
  (Task 64). Off-chain carries: the deploy links L4 (one more `CREATE` before the vault in the nonce
  plan, Task 43), `verify` expects its link and its runtime code (Tasks 44 and 45; the read-backs,
  Task 46), and it is verified on Sourcify and taostats with the other libraries (the testnet and
  launch verification steps).

## Revision 20 (implementation notes, 2026-11-02)

- **`adoptStake` and the bounded write-off (3.1, 3.6, 3.10.2; Task 16).** `adoptStake()` checks, in
  order, the keeper or `KEEPER_FALLBACK` since `lastAdoptAt` (`NotKeeper`), a `STAKE_RAMP` since it
  (`AdoptTooSoon`), then `surplus >= minStakeRao()` (`BelowMinAdoption`; while stake is missing the
  surplus is 0, so nothing is adopted); it sets the booked stake to the visible stake (booked plus
  the surplus), restarts the schedule through `_vest` on what is unvested plus the surplus, no cut,
  records `lastAdoptAt` and emits `StakeAdopted(rao)`; `lastStakeAt` and the ramp are untouched.
  `lastAdoptAt` (a `uint64`, set by the constructor) is the vault's last state variable, in a slot of
  its own, so no other slot moved; the lens serves it. `writeOffMissingStake(rao)` is `onlyScheduled`
  and classified Registry, Ordinary, one word; `w = min(rao, missing)`; `unlisted` from
  `getTotalColdkeyStakeOnSubnet` in a `try` capped at 100,000 gas, 0 when the call fails (a call that
  succeeds with fewer than 32 bytes of return data still reverts, as Solidity decodes it outside the
  `catch`); `WriteOffTooLarge` unless `unlisted <= missing - w`; then the booked stake falls by `w`
  and `StakeWrittenOff(w)` is emitted. A write-off of nothing (nothing missing, nothing unlisted) runs
  and emits `StakeWrittenOff(0)`. The keeper fallback of `stakeIdle`, `claimRewards` and `adoptStake`
  is one internal check, and `CascadeFormulas.sub0` subtracts unchecked (it subtracts only when `a >
  b`): no behaviour change, about 250 B off the vault (and 83 B off L1, L2, L4 and the vault lens).

## Revision 21 (implementation notes, 2026-11-04)

- **Checkpoint R2 (four adversarial reviewers at `f2fe8f6`, two seats, two models each).** No High and
  no Medium. The controller chose six items: four code fixes (below), the README's corrections and
  operator notes, and this note; everything else the reviewers raised is ruled or carried (the
  ledger's R2 lines). Sizes after the round: the vault 21,401 B (+18 B, F3 alone), L2 1,966 B (+15
  B), L4 2,574 B (+46 B), the vault lens 8,463 B (+245 B, F4); L1 unchanged at 3,723 B.
- **F1: the unclaimed-rewards read is optional (3.1; A-fable L-3).** `removeHotkey` (L4) reads
  `getUnclaimedRootTaoByHotkey` in a `try`, as 3.1 has the other optional reads, after the stake
  checks (unchanged). When the call fails, the owner's `removeHotkey(h, true)` still removes and
  `HotkeyRemoved` logs `unclaimed` 0; the owner's non-forfeit path reverts `HotkeyHasRewards`, and
  the keeper's and guardian's dust path and a stranger's path revert `NotAuthorized`, as for rewards
  above the dust, so I-6's way back needs the owner while the read is gone (a full set after a
  second swap: the owner's forfeit frees the slot). No gas cap: a working read is never cut short
  (its cost on the live runtime is unmeasured, unlike the floors' single reads), and a dispatch
  failure that burns the gas it is given leaves the rest of the call a 64th of it, so the owner's
  forfeit needs a gas limit about 64 times its tail (a few million). A call that succeeds with fewer
  than 32 bytes of return data still reverts every path, the forfeit included: Solidity decodes it
  outside the `catch`, as for the floors' and the total's reads, and the library rules allow no
  low-level call or assembly that could catch it.
- **F2: `Unstaked` logs the position a full removal took (3.3's walk; B-fable I-1).** The walk's
  `Unstaked(h, wantRao, receivedWei)` logs the whole position `s` when the removal is full (`want >=
  s` or `s - want < minRemainRao()`), the request otherwise; what was received and the ledger are
  unchanged (the owner's direct removal already logged its position). Off-chain, the indexer's
  `roundingWei` and `unexplainedFall` (price rules) read `wantRao`; on a full removal they now see the
  stake that left, not an over-ask (no off-chain change was made).
- **F3: `stakeIdle` books at most what it staked (3.1, 3.10.12; A-opus I-2).** It books and ramps
  `min(measured, rao)`: the one-rao-short read of a fresh position still books the read, and a
  chain-side credit landing on the stake hotkey inside `addStake` (a root-claim settlement on a stake
  change, which the mainnet canary watches for; section 9's testnet gate was dropped) stays an unbooked surplus, which
  only `adoptStake` books, vested
  over `rewardVesting` with no cut. `Staked` still logs the measured gain, so a monitor sees
  `measured > rao`.
- **F4: the lens's current window and the ramp (8.1; B-opus L-3).** L1's window rule moved into
  `CascadeFormulas.currentWindow` (a window 24 hours past its start reads as a fresh one opening now
  with nothing burned), which L1 burns against and the vault lens's `burnWindows(peer)` now applies,
  so an expired or never-written window reads as L1 would start it, not as consumed. The lens serves
  the ledger's ramp as `rampingStake()` (wei, as last written; what still ramps is it spent linearly
  over `STAKE_RAMP` from `lastStakeAt()`), so off-chain code can compute the outflow base from the
  lens alone; the lens-surface check serves `ledger.ramping` (SERVED_AS) and lists no internal-only
  state. Lens bytes only: the vault and L1 are unchanged in size.
- **Section 5's rows (A-opus I-4, A-fable I-1).** The keeper row now names its `removeHotkey` power
  (an empty non-stake hotkey with at most `hotkeyDustRao` unclaimed, the guardian's rule), and the
  Anyone row the 7-day fallbacks of `stakeIdle` and `claimRewards(h)` beside `adoptStake`'s (code:
  `_requireKeeperOrAfter`, since `lastStakeAt`, `lastClaimAt[h]` and `lastAdoptAt`).
- **I-3 holds at the ledger floor (A-fable L-2).** "Gifts do not move the price until adopted or
  unstaked into balance": a gift a removal takes into balance is priced at once only where the debit
  exceeds the ledger (M-2's floor). Where the ledger holds more (a full removal of a gifted position
  in a vault with other stake), the gift's rao come off real booked stake, the price does not move,
  and an equal surplus stays on the remaining positions for `adoptStake`; a residue below
  `minStakeRao()` is never adoptable alone, so that much stake stays unpriced until a larger surplus
  arrives (bounded by the chain's minimum, 0.002 TAO today).
- **I-6 assumes the chain's minimum stake of 0.002 TAO (A-opus L-3).** "A swapped position below
  `minStakeRao()` (in a vault with less than 2 TAO booked)": with a sudo raise of `DefaultMinStake`
  (a stated sudo power) the minimum rises above the tolerance in larger vaults too, and a swapped
  position between the tolerance and the new minimum cannot be listed by a stranger (`NotEligible`),
  nor by anyone into the last slot (`SlotReserved`) until a privileged actor removes a hotkey first.
  No loss; mints stay closed until then. Monitor carry: alert on stake missing on a sub-minimum
  swapped position, and watch `DefaultMinStake`.
- **A second chain's door cannot be paid (B-opus L-1).** Payouts route only through the primary
  bridge, and a CCIP adapter serves one lane (`remoteChainId` is immutable; `_checkSend` reverts
  `WrongDestination` otherwise), so a door on a chain the primary does not serve can be delivered
  from but never paid: its redeems wait for the owner's cancel and its bounces lock. No launch
  configuration reaches it (a second chain's door is a non-goal, behind a 7-day activation). Until
  payouts route per chain, no door may be activated on a chain the primary does not serve (the
  README's operator note 21; the runbook carries it).
- **Queued entries meet the cap only when queued (B-fable L-2, B-opus I-4).** An entry queued under
  an older, larger `maxEntryValue` that is above a later primary's `maxMessageValue()` makes that
  batch's quote or send revert (`MessageTooLarge`) for good: a redeem waits for the owner's cancel,
  and a carried bounce above the cap can be neither sent nor cancelled. Only the owner's own primary
  switch reaches it. Before `setPrimaryBridge` to a smaller cap, drain every queued batch (the
  README's operator note 21; T47/T53, and a monitor rule for a queued entry above the primary's cap).
- **I-13's ceiling is exact up to one rao per entry (B-opus I-2).** Each entry pays `ceil(assets ×
  charged / sum)` and its net is floored to rao, so a chunk's entries may bear up to one rao each
  above the ceiling; the rounding stays in the vault for the holders (the documented direction). I-13
  now says so.

## Revision 22 (implementation notes, 2026-11-04)

- **A supply-0 underwater delivery reverts (3.1, 3.2, I-1, I-4; Task 25's carry from Task 10c).** 3.1's
  "`onBridgeMessage` bounces the batch's deposits (its redeems still burn)" now holds only with shares
  outstanding. At a supply of 0, when `CascadeFormulas.underwater(grossBefore, pending, unvested)` is true
  after the write-down (so a payout shortfall past `UNDERWATER_TOLERANCE`), `onBridgeMessage` reverts
  `VaultInsolvent` and the Bittensor adapter parks the batch whole (native, reason
  `VaultInsolvent.selector`; delivered later, or refunded after `PARK_TIMEOUT`, Task 27). A bounce there
  queues its deposits' TAO as reserved payouts, and that TAO would fill the reservation hole: an older
  queued payout processed first spends it, and the bounced depositor's own payout is then the short one. At a
  supply of 0 no redeem can burn, so the revert costs no leaver anything. The revert undoes the
  supply-0 write-down with everything else, so a parked batch does not write the schedule down (Task 15's
  carry), and it writes no replay record, so the same message delivers once the reservation is whole. With
  shares outstanding nothing changes: underwater means assets 0, which the half-launch line refuses, so the
  delivery's `insolvent` is now that line alone and the underwater check runs only at a supply of 0 (same
  behaviour, fewer bytes); deposits bounce and redeems burn. Whether a vault with shares outstanding that is
  underwater should also refuse rather than bounce is left to a later review. The README's operator notes
  gain the pre-existing case of a payout cancel at a supply of 0 after a new deposit there (Task 15's review):
  since Task 34c's re-mint, the half where the batch's price was below the new depositor's is gone (the
  redeemers get back the value they were owed), and the half where it was above remains (they get back their
  burned shares, worth less, and the new depositor gains the difference).
- **Parking in the Bittensor adapter (3.2, I-7, I-11; Task 25).** `_deliver` follows 3.2's four steps. The
  lock (`ReentrancyGuard`'s `nonReentrant`) sits on the adapter's `_deliver` override, which every inbound
  path runs, so `ccipReceive` stays the base's and the Base adapter takes no lock (ruling A2); the later
  park functions join the same lock. A record exists when `parkedAt != 0`, so an empty park (a redeem-only
  batch) still holds its key. Step 1's sum check reverts the file-level `AmountMismatch()`, the vault's
  selector. A park's `reason` is read from the first 4 bytes of the return data without copying the rest,
  so no length of revert data can eat `PARK_GAS_RESERVE`; measured with a recipient and with a conversion
  that burn all their gas, the park's writes (the record, the total, `BatchParked`, `Delivered`) used about
  57,300 and 53,000 gas of the reserve (the native park's figure includes its value call's own cost),
  leaving at least 92,000 spare at every limit tried (1, 3 and 7 million). `ccipReceive` still emits
  `Delivered` after a park. The gas-capped recipient call and its native park are one helper,
  `_payOrParkNative(key, parkedAt, ...)`, which writes nothing before the call and restores in its catch,
  so Task 26's `deliverParked` can reuse it after deleting the record and decrementing (ruling B7c). Still
  reverting the message: a malformed batch, `AlreadyParked`, `ReserveShort`, a recipient with no code
  (Solidity's code check precedes the `try`, as in v3.1; only a malformed sender names one) and a delivery
  given too little gas to write the park. The adapter's own delivery work (the lock, the decode, the key,
  the self-call) adds about 19,000 of `AdapterGas` need at 1+1 and 46,000 at 50+1, and the reserve withheld
  from the vault's call is not in the model yet: Task 61's recalibration adds it (about 150,000 on the
  Base adapter's `gasBase`; 2 x `MAX_ENTRIES` then gives 6,730,000, within `GAS_CAP`). The v3.1 inbound
  stall PoC (a withdraw 1 wei short) now delivers, the wei from the reserve, and one past the 1-rao
  tolerance parks the tokens. `CcipBittensorAdapter` 7,046 B to 8,858 B.
- **Task 25's review round 1.** The live pre-deploy simulation (`script/simulate_inbound.sh`, run by
  `pnpm ops preflight`, which passes only on a stdout line `SIMULATION OK`) printed OK whenever
  `ccipReceive` returned, which a park now does. Its Router stand-in is now
  `test/fork/SimulationRouter.sol`, which after forwarding reverts `SimulationParked` (both totals and
  the batch's record) unless `parkedNative` and `parkedTokens` are both 0, so a park prints
  `SIMULATION FAILED: the delivery PARKED ...` and exits 1. Dry-run on 2026-11-04 against a local anvil
  standing in for the node, with the mocks at the live addresses: a delivery passes, and a halted alpha
  vault, a conversion 2 rao short, a refusing sink and an amount not in whole rao each fail (ops
  unchanged). Returndata bombs are tested: a recipient reverting with 1 MiB still parks native with its
  selector, and a conversion's bomb parks tokens, its selector when `convertExact` can afford to bubble
  it, else 0x00000000; a catch that copied the whole payload runs out of gas in both tests (checked
  with that mutant). A stranger's recipient can call `OffRamp.execute` on another verified message during its
  own delivery; the lock fails that inner delivery (`ReentrancyGuardReentrantCall`, `FAILURE`,
  re-executable): the README's operator note 22; whether `OffRamp 2.0.0` allows nested execution is
  unverified.
- **`deliverParked` (3.2, H-3, I-10, I-11; Task 26).** Locked by the same `nonReentrant` as `_deliver`;
  `convertExact` is not, so `this.convertExact` runs inside the lock. The order is 3.2's: the key
  recomputed (`NotParked` without a record), a token park converted with no `try` (its revert is the
  call's, the record intact), then the record deleted and its total lowered before the recipient's
  call. 3.2's "move `amount` from `parkedTokens` to `parkedNative`, set `converted`" followed by
  "delete the record and `parkedNative -= amount`" is written as its net effect (`parkedTokens -=
  amount` for a token park, `parkedNative -= amount` for a native one, then the delete); the state
  before the call and after either outcome is the same. "Restore the record" is read as the same
  record, `parkedAt` included: anyone can call `deliverParked`, so a restore that reset the clock
  would let any caller postpone the refund forever (I-10). The recipient's call is `_deliver`'s own
  helper (`_payOrParkNative`, ruling B7c), so it carries `_deliver`'s gas cap (all but
  `PARK_GAS_RESERVE`), which 3.2 does not name here: a recipient that burns all its gas leaves about
  117,000 unused after the restore at 1, 3 and 7 million, and a conversion already done is kept.
  `ParkedConverted(key)` is emitted on every refused delivery, an already-converted park's included.
  A token park whose recipient has no code cannot be delivered (Solidity's code check precedes the
  `try`, so the whole call reverts, its conversion with it); it is refunded as tokens after the
  timeout. Mutants without the lock, and without both the lock and the delete-first order, fail the
  H-3 tests (the second drains another batch's native park); one that restores with a fresh
  `parkedAt` fails five tests. `CcipBittensorAdapter` 8,858 B to 9,475 B.
- **`refundParked`, `quoteRefund`, `dropParked` (3.2, I-8 to I-10; Task 27).** *Both refunds skip send's
  amount checks (the controller's ruling).* Preflight ruling A6 had a converted refund call `_collect` and
  then `_ccipSend(…, checkAmount: true)`. 3.2 says the refund's send skips the conversion-floor check, and
  I-10 needs refunds live: a floor the root key raised above a converted park, or a park above this
  adapter's `maxMessageValue` (inbound delivery checks no cap), would otherwise trap the refund for ever.
  So a converted refund still converts back through `_collect` (`depositLiquid`, which refuses on its own
  if ForeverMoney's rail is closed or its own minimum is not met, and where Task 28's tolerance and cushion
  go), then sends with no amount check; an unconverted refund approves the router for the tokens it holds
  and sends them, never touching the native balance or `parkedNative`. *`checkAmount` removed.* With
  `send` passing false (it checks before it collects) and no refund passing true, Task 24's flag had no
  caller passing true, so `_ccipSend` lost it and makes no amount check; `_checkSend` makes send's two
  inline again (sizes unchanged). *As written.* The checks are `NotParked` (no record), `NothingToRefund`
  (an empty park, which is dropped instead), then `ParkedTooYoung` while `block.timestamp < parkedAt +
  PARK_TIMEOUT`, so the refund opens at exactly 14 days after the first park (a refused `deliverParked`
  keeps that `parkedAt`, Task 26, so no retry postpones it). The message is built from the parked BATCH's
  deposit entries as they are (`_deliver` checked they sum to the amount before writing the record), the
  payload `(door, vault, PAYOUTS(key, REFUND_CHUNK, deposits))`. The record is deleted and its total lowered
  before the first call; the calls reach only the immutable alpha vault, token and router, all under the
  lock. The fee is `msg.value`, the excess kept; `quoteRefund` is the router's fee for exactly the message
  sent (not time-gated). `dropParked` reads "after the timeout, only `amount == 0`" as `ParkedTooYoung`
  before the timeout (as the refund) and `NotParked` for no record or a non-empty one. A token park that
  `deliverParked` converted (its recipient refused it, or a caller starved it of gas, Task 26's carry) is
  refunded as a converted park, through `depositLiquid`: never a loss, but it needs the rail. A park's
  refund names the park's recipient as the payload's sender, so a park a stranger sent through the Base
  adapter goes back to that stranger, whose delivery on Base fails unless it is a receiving contract: it
  strands only itself. I-8 (mint XOR refund) rests on CCIP executing a message at most once: after a refund
  or a drop no record holds the key, so a second execution of the same message would deliver again; the
  OffRamp never re-executes a message in SUCCESS. `CcipBittensorAdapter` 9,475 B to 10,582 B
  (`CcipBaseAdapter` 6,299 B and the vault 21,441 B unchanged).
- **The send-side tolerance, the token cushion and the Bittensor sweep (3.2, 3.3, I-7; Task 28).** As
  written: `_collect` makes `depositLiquid` of exactly `amount`, then reverts `ConversionShort` unless the
  mint is at least `amount - SHORTFALL_TOLERANCE_WEI` **and** the token balance after it is at least
  `parkedTokens + amount`, so the rao comes from the cushion (the token balance above the token parks, a
  plain transfer funding it), never from a park. Three readings: *both converting paths* (the controller's
  ruling): 3.3 names "the payout send", but a converted refund converts back through the same `_collect`
  (Task 27), so it has the same tolerance and the same cushion; with the cushion drained it reverts
  `ConversionShort` and its record stays (I-10 is unchanged: a transfer that tops the cushion up unblocks
  it). *An over-mint is accepted* (3's "at least `amount`, never exactly"): the router still takes exactly
  `amount`, and the extra stays as surplus, which until swept also serves as cushion. *`ShortfallCovered(amount)`*
  carries the wei the cushion paid, `amount - minted`, emitted only when the mint is short; the native
  reserve's cover of a delivery's shortfall (`convertExact`, Task 25) emits nothing, so the monitor sees
  it only through the balance. `sweepSurplus()` is 3.2's: under the one lock (unlike the Base adapter's,
  ruling B12, because this adapter owes its parks), it sends `_sub0(balance, RESERVE_TARGET + parkedNative)`
  native and `_sub0(tokenBalance, TOKEN_RESERVE_TARGET + parkedTokens)` tokens through Task 24's shared
  `_sweep` (tokens first, then the one call into the treasury's code), so below a target it sends none of
  that kind and a surplus first refills the reserve or the cushion. A treasury that re-enters `sweepSurplus`
  or `deliverParked` from its native payment is refused `ReentrancyGuardReentrantCall`; one that bubbles the
  refusal reverts the sweep `TransferFailed`, and nothing moves. `bridge.cushion_low` (Task 56's rule) is
  "the native reserve or the token cushion below its target": `balance - parkedNative < RESERVE_TARGET` or
  `tokens - parkedTokens < TOKEN_RESERVE_TARGET`. The v3.1 send-side PoCs (a mint 1 wei short or over) and
  the surplus PoC are inverted: a 1-wei under-mint is covered once the cushion is funded, an over-mint is
  accepted, and a withdraw's surplus is swept. `CcipBittensorAdapter` 10,582 B to 11,240 B
  (`CcipBaseAdapter` 6,299 B and the vault 21,441 B unchanged).
- **Close by id, the pot rolled forward, `sweepPot` (3.5, I-18, I-21; Task 29).** As written: `closeBatch(expectedId,
  minValue)` checks `WrongBatch(open.id)`, then `BatchNotReady`, then rolls an empty batch (refunding `msg.value`,
  whatever `minValue`), then `BatchTooSmall()` while `open.totalDeposits < minValue`, then closes; `canClose` drops the
  room rule; a close pays `potUsed = min(feePot, fee)` from the pot and `fee - potUsed` from the closer's value (else
  `InsufficientFee`), refunds the closer `msg.value - (fee - potUsed)` and opens the next batch with `feePot =
  potCarried = feePot - potUsed`; a self-close pays from the pot alone, its request's own `msg.value` being that
