  request's fee. Seven readings and additions. *`_closeBatch(uint256 budget)` keeps v3.1's argument*, the pot plus the
  closer's value (the pot alone for a self-close), in place of the plan's `_closeBatch(uint256 extra)`: with `extra`,
  the self-close's constant 0 made via_ir inline a second, specialized copy of the function (18,780 B against 17,959 B,
  measured); the refund is `budget - fee - remainder`, the same `msg.value - (fee - potUsed)`, so nothing observable
  differs. *`whenNotPaused` stays on `closeBatch`* (ruling A13b): 3.5's "every exit call works while paused" is Task
  30's, which removes it with its own test. *`PotSwept(uint256 amount)`* (ruling B13): 3.5 names no event for
  `sweepPot`; the monitor's I-18 accounting needs one. *A sweep above `potCarried` reverts `InvalidParameter()`* (an
  existing error; 3.5 names none); a sweep of 0 is allowed and moves nothing. The sweep lowers `potCarried` and
  `open.feePot` together before it pays, under `nonReentrant`, so `potCarried <= open.feePot` always (a request only
  raises the pot, and a close sets both); a treasury that refuses ETH reverts the whole sweep
  `TransferFailed`, nothing stranded. *`Params.treasury`* sits after `swapper` (Appendix A's order), non-zero
  (`ZeroAddress`), stored as `address public immutable treasury`; `potCarried` is a new state variable, declared
  last so that no earlier slot moves (the door layout regenerated with one variable more; the door lens serves
  neither, the front door keeps both getters).
  *What is carried*: `potCarried` is the whole remainder at the last close (earlier carried wei and that batch's own
  surplus alike), so a batch's request fees become sweepable only once its close has paid from them; a roll leaves the
  pot and `potCarried` as they were. The invariant suite gains I-21 (`invariant_aCloserNeverReceivesMoreThanItSent`,
  a handler count of closes after which the closing handler held more ETH than before); I-18 was already
  `invariant_frontDoorIdentities`. Off-chain consumers still use v3.1's `closeBatch()` and six-field `BatchClosed`
  (ruling A3; the event's topic changes with its seventh field): the keeper, `rehearse`, the web and the indexer, for
  Tasks 50, 54 and 58; `canClose` also changes meaning for them (no early close by room, which the keeper's own fullness check in
  `state.ts` still derives). `CascadeFrontDoor` 17,598 B
  to 17,959 B (the vault 21,441 B, `CcipBaseAdapter` 6,299 B and `CcipBittensorAdapter` 11,240 B unchanged).
- **A pause never stops an exit, payouts from any known bridge, the front door's bounds (3.2, 3.5, 3.9, I-20;
  Task 30).** As written: `whenNotPaused` stays on `deposit`, `depositFor` and `deliver` alone, so `closeBatch` (ruling
  A13b: Task 29 had kept it), `requestRedeem` and `requestRedeemWithDelivery` (v3.1's, which 3.5's list names) work
  while paused, the self-close inside a request included; the front door's `_requireBridge` reads `bridgeKnown`, its
  base version (now `virtual`) keeps `bridgeEnabled` for the vault, whose runtime code is unchanged byte for byte
  without its metadata; `MAX_BATCH_INTERVAL` is 24 hours; `setCaps` and the constructor (one `_setCaps`) refuse a
  `minDeposit` below `MIN_DEPOSIT_FLOOR`; `_setMaxBatchValue`, which the constructor and `setMaxBatchValue` share,
  checks `_requireBridgeCap(primaryBridge, value)`, and the door's `_checkBridgeCaps` does it for `setPrimaryBridge`
  (the vault's Task 23 pattern). Five readings. *The floor's error and getter*: 3.5 names no error, so a minimum below
  the floor reverts the door's existing `InvalidParameter()`, and `MIN_DEPOSIT_FLOOR` is a public constant like the
  door's other bounds (the door keeps its own getters, 8.1). *One source for 2 × `MAX_FEE_ABS`*: `MAX_FEE_ABS` moved
  to `CascadeConstants.sol` (`MIN_REDEEM_VALUE / 2`, as before), the vault's internal constant now names it, and the
  floor is `2 * Constants.MAX_FEE_ABS`, so the bounce rule of 3.3 cannot drift from the fee ceiling. *The cap's
  order and reach*: `MAX_BATCH_VALUE` (`InvalidParameter`) is checked before the bridge's cap (`AboveBridgeCap`), as
  the vault orders its limits; the check runs against `primaryBridge` even while it is disabled, and on a tightening
  too (a cap that fell refuses a tightening above it, as on the vault; a CCIP adapter's cap is immutable, so I-23
  holds there). *The pause's reach*: the only other call a paused door refuses `EnforcedPause` is OpenZeppelin's
  second `pause`; every other entry point (the scheduler, `stampBySig`, `disableBridge`, the setters, `sweepPot`,
  `creditRedeemFee`, `setDelivery`) gets past the pause, which a sweep over the door's whole state-changing surface
  checks call by call (`test_pauseStopsDepositAndDeliverOnly`). *A disabled bridge is no primary*: known is not
  enabled for a send: `setPrimaryBridge` of a known, disabled bridge still reverts `BridgeNotEnabled`, and a close
  through a disabled primary `PrimaryBridgeDisabled`, the self-close included; only a Reopen re-enables it. The spec's
  "a refund through a disabled Base twin" (3.2's tests, ruling B16a) is
  `test_refundThroughADisabledBaseTwinCreditsEachDepositor` beside Task 27's end-to-end refunds, through the real
  adapter pair. The fixtures and fork setups that built a door with a 0.01 TAO minimum now build it at the floor
  (construction only); the v3 ops settings' `SETTINGS.minDeposit` (0.01 TAO) would now fail a deploy, and the
  guardian's lever to stop third-party closes is the primary's disable, not the pause (the indexer runbook's
  `rail.alpha_vault` text and the web's paused-batch messages say otherwise): no off-chain change was made, for
  Tasks 43, 50, 53, 56 and 58. `CascadeFrontDoor` 17,959 B to 18,156 B (the vault 21,441 B, `CcipBaseAdapter` 6,299 B
  and `CcipBittensorAdapter` 11,240 B unchanged).
- **The oracle's depth and inverse views, at least 901 observations (3.7, I-31; Task 31).** As written: the
  constructor reads each pool's `slot0` after its token check and reverts `TooFewObservations(pool, cardinality)` below
  `MIN_CARDINALITY = 901` slots **in use** (`observationCardinality`; the slots requested, `observationCardinalityNext`,
  do not count: TAO/WETH read 600 in use and 2,000 requested on 2026-10-31); `taoValue(usdcUnits)` is USDC → WETH at the
  WETH/USDC mean tick, then WETH → TAO at the TAO/WETH mean tick, through `usdcValue`'s `_quoteAtTick` and its
  `SafeCast` limits (the input and the WETH leg at most 2^128 - 1); `meanLiquidity()` returns (TAO/WETH, WETH/USDC),
  each `OracleLibrary.consult`'s harmonic mean (0.8 branch). The price and depth views share one helper, `_observe`,
  not one call: each of `_meanTick` and `_meanLiquidity` calls the pool's `observe` itself, so a caller of both a
  price view and `meanLiquidity` (Task 32's `deliver`) observes each pool twice, gas only (`usdcValue` unchanged).
  Six readings where 3.7 is silent. *Rounding*: both price views round down at each
  hop, so `taoValue(u)` is a floor of the exact inverse `u × R1 / R2` (R1, R2 the pools' prices times 2^192), under it
  by less than the TAO/WETH price in raw units (TAO wei per WETH wei, 9.2 at the live tick) plus one wei, and
  `usdcValue(x)` a floor of `x × R2 / R1`, under it by less than the WETH/USDC price plus one unit; a TAO round trip
  through USDC loses less than about one USDC unit's worth of TAO (4.3e9 wei at the live ticks), a USDC round trip at
  most 3 units. The unit tests derive both bounds and check them at the live ticks and by fuzzing amounts and ticks;
  Task 33's buy floor reads a value rounded down by those wei. *No growth* (a seconds-per-liquidity cumulative that
  did not move over the window) divides by zero, as in the library: the view reverts (Panic 0x12) rather than answer
  `type(uint128).max`, which would pass any floor. *A mean above 2^128 - 1* reverts in `SafeCast` where the library's
  `uint128(...)` would wrap it to a small number. No Uniswap v3 pool gives either reading: `Oracle.transform` adds
  `(seconds << 128) / liquidity` (liquidity 1 for an empty range), at least 1 a second for any uint128 liquidity, and
  `observe` interpolates the window's start rounded down, which only adds growth; so the window's growth is at least
  1,800 and the mean at most 2^128 - 1, which the library's product `T × (2^160 - 1)`, not a shift, keeps in range (the
  largest liquidity reads as itself; an exact growth reads one below). Both refusals fail closed on a reading only a
  non-Uniswap pool makes, and the price views do not read the liquidity cumulative, so neither touches `usdcValue` or
  `taoValue`. *The cumulative's difference is checked*, as in the 0.8 library and as the tick's already was: the
  pool's uint160 cumulative wraps only after 2^32 seconds of an empty range. *A thin stretch counts in full*: the
  mean is per pool and harmonic, so one second of an empty range in the window reads at most 1,800, and a pool pushed
  thin for one block stays under Task 32's floor for up to the window. *No age gate* (3.7's "no staleness revert"): a
  pool whose history reaches the window answers however long ago it last traded; a shorter history still reverts
  "OLD" in all three views. A18: `OracleIdleTwapPentest` is inverted. At its pinned block 53,341,000 (TAO/WETH 600 in
  use and 2,000 requested, WETH/USDC 5,000) the constructor reverts `TooFewObservations(TAO/WETH, 600)`; the v3.1
  file's idle-pool assertions (the newest observation older than the window, the mean tick equal to the spot tick)
  are dropped, as 3.7 accepts that reading and they read an oracle that cannot now be built there. C7: the fork
  suites that build the oracle (`BaseForkV3`, `FrontdoorPentest`) skip in `setUp`, with the reason, when either pool
  is below 901, through `ForkBase.skipBelowMinCardinality`; `BaseForkV3` gains the live depth read
  (`test_fork_depthAndInverseAtTheLivePools`: each mean positive, and a pool with no observation in the window reads
  its liquidity within the rounding; at block 53,360,000, TAO/WETH, idle for 2,932 s, read 7 above its liquidity of
  2.67e21, inside the bound of 12). `MockUniswapPool` gains `slot0` (cardinality 1 until set) and liquidity levels,
  `MockTwap` both views (deep pools until set; "OLD" in all three when set to revert). `ICascadeTwap` gains both
  views; the front door calls only `usdcValue`, so its metadata-stripped runtime is unchanged. Off-chain, no change
  was made: the readers of the oracle's ABI are listed in the task report for Tasks 46, 54, 57 and 58.
  `CascadeTwapOracle` 3,160 B to 3,715 B (the vault 21,441 B, the front door 18,156 B and `CascadeStaker` 8,051 B
  unchanged).
- **Task 31's review.** *Either token order*: the rounding bounds above are stated for the live order, WETH token0 of
  both pools; the unit tests now also build the oracle with TAO before WETH, USDC before WETH, and both (mock
  addresses below WETH's), where a pool quotes the inverse price at the opposite tick, and hold `usdcValue`,
  `taoValue`, both round trips and `meanLiquidity` to bounds derived from each pool's own order (a hop's rate R /
  2^192 or its inverse); an oracle that assumed the live direction passes every live-order test and fails these.
  *The PoC forks its own block*: `OracleIdleTwapPentest` no longer sets `FORK_BLOCK` for the whole forge process
  (v3.1's `vm.setEnv`, which could pin every fork a combined run made after it, so that C7 would skip `BaseForkV3`
  and `FrontdoorPentest`); it forks block 53,341,000 in its own `setUp` (`BASE_RPC`, default the public archive
  base-rpc.publicnode.com) and ignores `FORK_BLOCK`. *The 901 copy*: Solidity 0.8.38 refuses
  `CascadeTwapOracle.MIN_CARDINALITY` from another contract (error 9582), so `ForkBase` keeps its copy, and each suite
  that builds the oracle asserts it equals `MIN_CARDINALITY()`. The README's oracle row, its depth paragraph and
  checklist item 32 now say what the oracle serves and that the constructor enforces 901. Tests and docs only: the
  oracle's, the front door's and the vault's runtime code is unchanged.
- **The front door's pool-liquidity floor on USDC deliveries (3.7, I-31; Task 32).** As written: `Params` gains
  `uint128[2] minPoolLiquidity` (after `treasury`, Appendix A's order), stored as `uint128[2] public minPoolLiquidity`
  (0 TAO/WETH, 1 WETH/USDC, the order of `meanLiquidity()`), declared last so no earlier slot moves (both floors in
  one slot, 32; the door layout regenerated with one variable more; the door lens serves neither, as for
  `potCarried`); `deliver` reads `oracle.meanLiquidity()` once and reverts `ThinPool(index, liquidity)` for the first
  pool below its floor, TAO/WETH first, then reads `usdcValue` once as before, both after the preference checks and
  before any token moves; `claim` is untouched; `setMinPoolLiquidity(uint8 index, uint128 value)` is `onlyOwner`, a
  raise or an equal value at once, a cut only as execute's self-call (`_requireScheduledIfLoosens(value < current)`),
  classified Param and Ordinary at two words. The readings, and the controller's rulings, where 3.7 is silent. *The
  comparison*: a pool exactly at its floor passes (`liquidity < floor` refuses). *A floor of 0* is accepted (the
  constructor and a scheduled cut; no lower bound, the controller's ruling) and checks nothing, the emergency path if
  liquidity moves to other pools; `deliver` does not skip the oracle call when both floors are 0 (a skip measured 51 B
  more, 18,735 B, and would save about 47,500 gas, the warm second observe of each pool, only in that state; a
  `meanLiquidity` that reverts, unreachable on a Uniswap pool, Task 31, still blocks a delivery, failing closed). Task
  44's verify will check that the deployed floors are non-zero. *An index above 1* names no pool: `InvalidParameter`
  whatever the value, directly and at execute (scheduling checks no value, 3.10.6, so the item is kept); the setter
  checks the index before the direction, since an index with no floor has nothing to cut, so a direct cut of a real
  floor still meets `NotScheduled` as its first refusal (the setter has no other check). *The event* is the door's
  `LimitSet("minPoolLiquidity", index, value)` (its limit setters' pattern), one per floor, the constructor's two
  included. *The pause*: the setter works while paused, like the door's other setters (a row in the pause sweep;
  `doorEntryPoints()` gains it). *The harmonic mean's sensitivity* (a question for Checkpoint R3): one block of a
  near-empty range (Uniswap divides by max(L, 1)) collapses the 30-minute mean, so whoever pushes TAO/WETH into a thin
  range for one block (about two swap fees) holds every delivery for up to 30 minutes, repeatably; no one loses (users
  claim by hand), and the reading measures organic depth that is there, not a proven cost to move the price (anyone
  can raise it with narrow liquidity of their own), so the window stays the main defence; documented in the NatSpec
  and the README. *The gas*: `usdcValue` and `meanLiquidity` each observe both pools, so `deliver` now observes each
  pool twice (no combined view: a later decision, the controller's ruling). Measured on a Base fork at block
  53,546,400 (`test_fork_deliverThroughUniswapWithTwapFloor`, gas report, each call cold): `deliver` 510,901 to
  558,386 gas (+47,485; the reverting call 485,112 to 532,595), the second observe of each pool, warm, accounting for
  nearly all of it (`usdcValue` read after `meanLiquidity` costs 47,323 where it cost 152,323 cold); over `MockTwap`,
  252,429 to 257,850 (+5,421). *No launch value* (3.7's "half the 30-day median" is a deploy calibration, Tasks 61, 43
  and 44): Task 31 saw TAO/WETH's mean swing about fivefold (6.2e20 to 3.1e21), and half the median would have held
  deliveries at 2 of 7 sampled blocks. The fixtures' floors (`DefaultParams`, 1e20 and 1e17) are non-zero, so every
  delivery test runs the check against `MockTwap`'s default depth (the uint128 maximum); the live-shaped fork fixtures
  (`BaseForkV3`, `FrontdoorPentest`) name 1e20 and 1e17, below the live means (6.30e20 and 8.76e17 at block
  53,547,368). The plan's Step 1 (inverting `OracleIdleTwapPentest`) is dropped: Task 31 inverted it, the constructor
  refusing that pool at its pinned block. Tests: the thin pool blocking the delivery and not the claim (each pool, and
  both), the setter's directions, the index, the floor of 0, the delivery at the floor, the setter's direction rows in
  `SetterDirections` (with its canonical length, its dirty index and floor, and the writes of each half of the slot),
  the constructor's read-back and logs, the lens's layout read, and on the fork
  `test_fork_thinPoolBlocksDeliveryNotClaim` (a floor one above a live mean reverts `ThinPool` with the claim intact
  and paying; both floors at the live means deliver). The off-chain readers (the ops deploy's `Params`, the keeper's
  revert classifier, the indexer's `LimitSet` names) were not changed: the task report lists them for Tasks 43, 50 and
  54. `CascadeFrontDoor` 18,156 B to 18,684 B (+528 B against a 250 B budget: the check about 201 B, the setter and
  its write about 229 B, the public getter 75 B, the class entry 23 B; 4,392 B under the gate), the vault 21,441 B
  unchanged.
- **The staker's buy floor, and its route and fees from the constructor (3.7, I-31; Task 33).** As written:
  `_stake` requires the TAO its swap paid, `taoOut` by balance and before the floor to whole rao, to be at least
  `frontDoor.oracle().taoValue(usdcIn - fees) * (10_000 - MAX_BUY_SLIPPAGE_BPS) / 10_000` (`uint16 public constant
  MAX_BUY_SLIPPAGE_BPS = 500`), else `BelowOracleFloor()`, on `stake` and `stakeWithPermit` alike (both run `_stake`)
  and never on `sellFor`, which the front door's delivery floors bound; the router's own minimum stays the caller's
  `minTaoOut`, unchanged and not folded into the floor. The constructor takes `Params { usdc, tao, frontDoor, owner,
  treasury, route0, deliveryRouteId, swapFeeBps, depositFeeUsdc }`: today's zero-address and `TokenMismatch` checks
  and `TreasurySet`, then route 0 through `setRoute`'s own helper (`_setRoute`), the delivery route through
  `setDeliveryRoute`'s (`RouteInactive` unless the route is active, so only 0 can pass: the field is redundant at
  construction) and the fees through `setFees`'s (`FeeTooHigh`), each emitting its setter's event (`RouteSet`,
  `DeliveryRouteSet`, `FeesSet`, in that order after `TreasurySet`), so off-chain readers see one history and a
  deploy needs no owner call before the first stake. Routes and fees stay owner-immediate within their caps; no owner
  switch skips the floor. Seven readings where 3.7 is silent. *Where the oracle is read*: once per stake, after the
  cheap checks (deadline, beneficiary, route, the deposit fee) and before any token moves, the comparison after the
  swap. The value does not depend on the order: a swap in the same block, the stake's own or a front-run, leaves the
  30-minute mean where it was (a pool's cumulative for the current second carries the tick from before the block's
  first tick-moving swap), which the inverted PoC asserts on the fork; reading first lets a stake with no oracle, or one
  that cannot answer, fail before its USDC moves. *No oracle, no stake*: while the front door's oracle is the zero
  address (its constructor accepts one, and a scheduled `setOracle(0)` is how deliveries are stopped) a stake reverts
  `NoOracle()`, an error of its own (15 B), because a call to a codeless address reverts with empty data, which the
  web could only word as unknown; a non-zero codeless oracle still reverts with empty data, and an oracle that
  reverts ("OLD") passes its own reason through unchanged. Both fail closed, as I-31 requires. *The comparison*: a
  payment exactly at the floor stakes (`taoOut < floor` refuses). The floor rounds down twice (`taoValue`, Task 31's
  floor of the exact inverse, then the 95%), so it is never above the exact 95% of the oracle's value; a value large
  enough to overflow the multiplication reverts (fails closed), unreachable at real prices. A floor of 0 (a value of
  at most 1 wei) refuses nothing, and the front door's own minimum (`ZeroAmount`, `BelowMinDeposit`) is what refuses
  such a stake. *Which error first*: the router's own minimum ("Too little received") and the staker's
  `InsufficientOutput` (a router paying short of `minTaoOut` whatever it reports) come before `BelowOracleFloor`, so
  `BelowOracleFloor` means the caller's minimum was met and the floor was not: the pool's price is far from its
  30-minute average, or the stake's own impact and fees pass 5% (Tasks 57-58 word it apart from "the price moved").
  *The path check on memory*: the constructor's route is in memory, so `_checkPath` now reads `bytes memory` (the
  first token by `bytes20(path)`, the last by one memory-safe `mload` of the word ending at the path's last byte,
  inside the path since its length is at least 43) and `setRoute` copies its calldata route into memory; `_setRoute`
  reads the token immutables, which the constructor sets before it runs the helper. Measured, the shared helpers are
  20 B smaller than a constructor with its own copies of the checks (8,370 B). *The fixtures*: `DefaultParams.defaultStakerParams` (route 0 Uniswap-kind and active through the named
  router and paths, also the delivery route, and the launch fees `STAKER_SWAP_FEE_BPS` 15 and
  `STAKER_DEPOSIT_FEE_USDC` 50,000), used at every construction site (`Staker`, `EndToEnd`, `BaseForkV3`,
  `FrontdoorPentest`, the invariant suite, which keeps its deposit fee of 0); the post-deploy `setRoute(0, ...)`,
  `setDeliveryRoute(0)` and `setFees` calls are gone (`BaseForkV3` still adds its Slipstream route 1 with
  `setRoute`); `EndToEnd` builds its router before the staker, and the staker suite's front door names a `MockTwap`
  at 250 USDC per TAO from its constructor. *The gas* (Base fork, block 53,549,000, gas report, each call cold): the
  oracle's `taoValue` costs 153,900 (both pools' `observe`); `stakeWithPermit` through SwapRouter02, whose swap then
  touches the same pools warm, 500,776 to 644,416 (+143,640), and `stake` through Slipstream's TAO/USDC pool 443,628
  to 604,265 (+160,637); over `MockTwap` a stake costs about 11,000 more. Tests: `test_sixPercentShortReverts` and
  `test_fourPercentShortStakes` (both entry points), `testFuzz_floorAtEverySize` (one wei under the floor reverts,
  at it the front door decides, at the fixture's TWAP from one unit above the deposit fee to $1M and then at any
  price from 1 to 1e30 USDC units a TAO, reaching the floor of 0), `test_constructorSetsRouteAndFees` (the read-backs,
  the four events, a stake and a sale with no owner call, and every setter refusal), `test_zeroOracleRefusesStake`,
  `test_revertingOracleRefusesStake` and `test_sellForReadsNoBuyFloor`. A18/C6: `FrontdoorPentest`'s buy-leg PoCs
  are inverted. At block 53,549,000 an honest $1,800 stake filled at 99.37% of the oracle's value (4.40% above its
  floor); with the web's 0.5% minimum the router refuses the larger front-runs and none that fills profits the
  attacker (kept); with no minimum of the victim's own, a $25,000 front-run buy still fills (96.70% of the value, the
  victim at least the floor, the attacker's round trip losing about $125 against the victim's $59), and every
  front-run from $50,000 (94.86%) is refused `BelowOracleFloor` with nothing moved. Off-chain, nothing was changed:
  the ops deploy still builds the staker with v3's five arguments and sets its route, delivery route and fees by
  three calls after it, and the web and the indexer know neither new error; the task report lists the readers for
  Tasks 43, 54, 57 and 58. `CascadeStaker` 8,051 B to 8,350 B (+299 B: the floor 281 B, the constructor's helpers and
  the memory path check +18 B); the front door 18,684 B, the vault 21,441 B and the oracle 3,715 B unchanged, byte for
  byte without their metadata.
- **Checkpoint R3 (2026-11-05; the fix round after three seats, a gap critic and a second wave on ForeverMoney's and
  Chainlink's deployed code; no High, no Medium).** What changed:
  *The send-side tolerance reaches the adapter (B-1).* ForeverMoney's deployed `depositLiquid` checks the stake it
  measured against `minAlphaRao` (`Slippage`), so `_collect`'s `amount / RAO` made a stake 1 rao short revert inside
  ForeverMoney before 3.3's tolerance and the cushion were read; it now passes `amount / RAO - 1` (both callers pass a
  non-zero whole-rao amount, `NotRaoAligned` first, so no underflow), the adapter's own check binds, and a stake 2 rao
  short still fails inside ForeverMoney. *The constructor refuses a zero `remoteSelector` and a gas model of 0 and 0*
  (`InvalidParameter`; A-I4). *A held lock's refusal reverts, it never parks (CC-L1).* A stranger could execute an
  honest batch's CCIP message from code the vault runs under its lock (`redeemDirect`'s payment, `processPayouts`'
  refund), where the adapter's lock is free and the vault refuses `ReentrancyGuardReentrantCall`; the batch parked
  native on its first delivery, its message spent. Reading 3.2: 3.2 parks a recipient's refusal, and a lock the
  recipient holds is transient, not a refusal of the batch. So `_payOrParkNative` reverts with that selector: CCIP
  records `FAILURE` (the deployed OffRamp writes nothing for it, and anyone re-executes it without committee results),
  and `deliverParked` reverts too, the park as it was. Every other refusal parks. Fix round 2 extends it to the
  conversion: a `withdrawLiquid` refused by ForeverMoney's own lock (OpenZeppelin v5's selector, a stranger delivering
  from its payment) reverts the delivery instead of parking the tokens (the send side has no park and already reverts). *The ForeverMoney double is
  faithful (FM-L1, FM-I1, TH-1).* `MockAlphaVault` now stakes and unstakes through the staking double and checks what
  it measured on both legs, with ForeverMoney's error names, no floor of its own, a halt that is a clock and a
  migration that closes deposits only; on the live rail a delivery more than 1 rao short parks with ForeverMoney's
  `Slippage` (the adapter's `ConversionShort` only at 1 rao short with the reserve empty). *The delivery-sandwich PoC
  measures a sandwich (C-I2)*: the victim's sale stays inside the round trip; at 10 TAO, a size the keeper would not
  send, the attacker's round trip comes out ahead by back-running the victim's own impact, within the floors. *Docs*:
  the staker's routes, delivery route and fees change at once within their caps (C-L3), and the keeper delivers once
  its quote less 0.5% clears both floors; the gas model's headroom once `PARK_GAS_RESERVE` is counted, 25,106 gas at
  1+1 and 8,543 with four hotkeys (A-L1, recalibrated by Tasks 61 and 64, no launch value changed), and today's slack
  figures (A-I1); `PARK_GAS_RESERVE` is an estimate until Task 64 (A-I2; Appendix B); `MIN_CARDINALITY` assumes
  2-second blocks (C-I3); the 5% buy floor is the bound, not the expectation (C-I5); `Delivered` follows a park too
  (B-I3); the harmonic-mean hold's cost, about $88 per 30 minutes (C-L1); deliveries all at once, about 5 TAO the
  keeper's limit and 13 TAO the contract's on today's pools (C-L2); OffRamp 2.0.0 refuses a nested `execute` itself,
  a stranger or the inbound rate limit can move an `UNTOUCHED` message to `FAILURE`, and re-executing a `FAILURE`
  needs no committee results (CC-L2 to CC-L4); section 5's ForeverMoney row (FM-L2, FM-I2, FM-I3). Not changed
  here, carried: the keeper's and web's halves of C-L1 and C-L2, A-L2, B-I2, B-I4, C-I1, C-I4, C-I6, the keeper's
  and monitor's halves of CC-L2 and CC-L4, and FM-L2's monitor rules. `CcipBittensorAdapter` 11,240 B to 11,295 B
  (`CcipBaseAdapter` 6,299 B, the front door 18,684 B, the vault 21,441 B, the staker 8,350 B and the oracle 3,715 B
  unchanged, byte for byte without their metadata; both adapters' initcode +73 B).
- **A payout cancel re-mints the value owed, never more shares than were burned (3.5, 3.1 I-4; Task 34c, the
  founder's decision of 2026-11-05).** `cancelPayoutBatch` re-minted each entry's burned shares, so a cancel after
  the price rose moved the delay's rewards from the holders who stayed to the redeemers (0.69% of the price at
  Task 34's scripted cancel). Each remaining entry with shares now gets `min(shares, sharesForDeposit(assets,
  assetsBefore, supplyBefore))`: the vault passes its `totalAssets()` into L3 and L3 reads the supply, both once,
  before anything is released, so entry order does not matter; no special case at a supply of 0 and no write-down.
  After a rise the redeemer gets back the value owed in fewer shares; after a fall, the burned shares, as before.
  The formula rounds down, so the stayers keep the rounding: at an unchanged price the re-mint can sit up to one
  rao's worth of shares below what was burned (the delivery floored the value to whole rao), worth the value owed
  less 1 wei. `pendingPayouts` still falls by every re-minted entry's whole value, `NothingToCancel` is still
  decided by the entries' shares, and no event, error, ABI or lens layout changed (`RedeemReversed.shares` is now
  the shares re-minted; an entry whose value buys no share mints nothing). I-4's cancel exception is back to dust
  (Task 34's interim bound is gone). `CascadePayoutLib` 5,057 B to 5,301 B, the vault 21,441 B to 21,458 B.

## Revision 23 (2026-11-06, the founder's ruling: the leaver pays)

- **Holders never pay for someone else's exit (3.3, 3.1 I-4 and the new I-35, 3.10.1, 3.10.11; Task 36b, the
  founder's ruling of 2026-11-06, design-leaver-pays.md).** Every cost an exit causes falls on the party exiting,
  rounded in the vault's favour, and an exit that cannot cover its own cost is refused, never subsidised. The walk
  (L2) now counts the stake each removal takes at face value against the request (`unmet -= min(unmet, debit ×
  RAO)`), not what the chain paid for it, so a removal fee never sends the walk to a further position and one partial
  removal always ends it. `_unstake` returns the walk's cost, what the chain kept of the stake it took,
  `ceilToRao(sub0(debit × RAO, received))`, with `unmet` and what it received. `redeemDirect` pays
  `floorToRao(sub0(out, unmet + cost))`, reverts `ZeroAmount` when that is 0 (the dust exit that used to burn its
  shares for nothing keeps them), and logs `ShortfallAbsorbed(unmet + cost)` (the parameter renamed `amount`). A
  payout chunk's entries bear the walk's cost pro rata with their share of the bridge fee: L3's `finish` takes the
  payout list and, when the walk cost anything, runs `CascadePayoutMath.deductFee` again on `charged + cost` (each
  share rounded up, each net floored to rao, a bounce included: the founder's answer 3), refusing `FeeTooHigh` when
  that leaves an entry nothing the first split did not (an entry with shares reverts in the split; a bounce by the
  dust count), and then refuses `UnstakeShort` unless the liquidity read before the walk and the walk's receipts fund
  `net + charged` (I-12 as code: the corner where the walk ended after a whole removal with less than a rao an entry
  of T left untaken, whether or not other positions remain, since the walk stops once `unmet <= T`, and the second
  split's rounding left the entries more than the receipts; that chunk waits until the balance or the positions
  move, I-13). `PayoutPlan` gains `free`, the pre-walk dust count, `received` and `cost`; `PayoutChunkSent` gains
  `unstakeCost`. A chunk whose cost would leave an entry nothing is paid once liquid TAO covers it without a walk
  (the founder's answer 2: never the caller or the keeper). No minimum direct exit (answer 1); housekeeping costs
  stay the holders' (answer 4: the fresh read's rao, the owner's `removeHotkey` cut, a future re-stake fee). No new
  error, parameter or constant; `unstakeMarginRao` no longer moves a fee (leave it 0). At today's zero fee no
  outcome changes but the dust exit's refusal.
- **The four spec lines the code contradicted, fixed here.** 3.3's "pay `_sub0(out, unmet)` ... the leaver bears
  it" and 3.10.11's "the leaver bears at most 1,000 rao" (a whole-position receipt larger than the ask, or a later
  position's, zeroed `unmet`, and the holders paid those removals' fees); I-4's "a short unstake receipt of at most
  1,000 rao that the holders absorb" (they absorbed any fee above T on a whole-position removal or a walk that went
  on); 3.3's "that cushion, or the rao it lost, is idle balance, which the holders bear" (at a fee of T none of it
  stayed idle, and above T a partial chunk walk refused: a freeze); and Appendix B's missing `unstakeMarginRao` row.
- **I-4 narrows, I-35 is new, I-13 and I-14 restated (3.1, 3.3).** I-4 loses the short-receipt, above-T and
  cancel-dust exceptions; what stays is the swap, the move read short, the simulated loss, the fresh read, the
  owner's `removeHotkey` cut, and the mint or cancel that absorbs a hole one of these left. I-35: no direct exit,
  sent chunk or cancel lowers the price exactly (`(A' + 1)(S + 1e6) >= (A + 1)(S' + 1e6)`) or widens the hole,
  except a cancel that starts at `totalAssets()` 0 over a hole I-4's other exceptions left. I-13: a direct exit is
  paid exactly `floorToRao(out - unmet - cost)`; a chunk is sent as queued less each entry's share of `charged +
  cost`; its liveness needs no stake-missing precondition. I-14 adds "none from a walk's cost". The emptied vault's
  tail now drains at any fee (each chunk pays its own fee, so no supply-0 payout gap or schedule hole forms; the
  last chunk no longer waits for a deposit or a stake gift, so the runbook's "the last batch of an emptied vault may
  need a stake gift of the payout hole plus the removal fee" retires).
- **Carried, not done here:** the web's quote and break-even line (one more term: "less its share of any unstake fee
  the chain charges, none today"; Tasks 58 and 59), the keeper's payout planner (the face-value coverage rule, the
  walk-cost `FeeTooHigh`, process right after deliveries under a large fee; Task 50), the indexer's payout
  cross-check (`deductFee(charged + unstakeCost)`) and its price rules (a fall across an exit is now unexplained;
  Task 54), and the monitor's "the chain charges a root removal fee" alert (it reads `PayoutChunkSent.unstakeCost`
  and `ShortfallAbsorbed` directly, alerting on any non-zero `unstakeCost`; an `Unstaked` with `receivedWei <
  wantRao × 1e9` is a secondary signal, exact only when the chain takes exactly what was asked), dropping its
  emptied-vault rule (Task 57). Sizes: the vault 21,458 B to 21,695 B (1,381 B under the gate), `CascadePayoutLib`
  5,301 B to 6,245 B, `CascadeStakeLib` 1,966 B to 1,963 B; the front door, the other libraries and the lenses
  unchanged. The published vault ABI changes by the event field and the parameter rename.

## Revision 24 (implementation notes, 2026-11-07)

- **Task 38's carried docs pass (3.1 I-4, 7; the contracts README).** I-4 says what the code does since Task 36b: no
  exit lowers the price at any per-removal fee the chain charges, since every removal's fee is the exit's own, so a
  walk costs the holders nothing however many positions it takes. The 1,000-rao-per-receipt bound the earlier wording
  carried (it assumed the fee at most T, and above T a walk cost the holders up to receipts × fee) went with the
  exception it bounded (Revision 23). At a supply of 0 the owner's cancel writes nothing down, so the holders it
  re-mints absorb a hole another of I-4's exceptions left and then receive the rewards still vesting; a deposit writes
  the schedule down first (Task 15). Section 7's step 4 sends one batch of the launch minimum deposit, 0.04 TAO: its
  0.01 TAO was below that minimum and the 0.03 TAO floor (Task 43's concern 3). In the contracts README, the lists of
  what still reverts a delivery name the conversion refused by ForeverMoney's own lock beside the recipient's (R3 fix
  round 2); an execution from inside a lock is filed with the failures that reach `ccipReceive`; and "a FAILURE's
  retry needs `verifierResults`" is gone from the README, the Bittensor adapter's header (a comment) and checklist
  item 27, which now asks Task 61 to confirm against the real verifier that a FAILURE re-executes with no committee
  results (R3, CC-L3). Section 3.2's root-cause paragraph keeps its design-history wording.
- **The adapter campaign (3.2, I-7 to I-11; Task 38).** As written, on AdaptersFixture's two chains (the two CCIP
  adapters over two mock routers, the faithful `MockAlphaVault`, the vault and the front door as their test-only
  subclasses, the handler their keeper), every message relayed by the handler as the lanes carry it, a `FAILURE`
  re-executed. Its readings: *the ghost map* is each message's state (in flight, failed, delivered, parked as tokens or
  as native TAO, refunded, dropped), moved only by the handler's calls and the events they log, with each park's record
  and the totals the ghost's own; after every step it is compared with `parked(key)`, `parkedNative`, `parkedTokens`,
  both balances (I-7), each door batch's `processed` flag (I-8: minted exactly when delivered, so a parked, refunded or
  dropped batch never minted) and the Base adapter's token balance (0). *I-8's deposits*: a delivered batch's deposits
  each minted or bounced exactly once; a delivery or a park call that leaves no state, or two, is a failure. *I-9* is
  checked twice: at the refund's send (PAYOUTS of the parked batch's deposits, from the handler's own record of the open
  batch, carrying the park's amount) and on Base (each depositor credited exactly, `netForwarded` lower by the refund).
  *I-10 by snapshot*: every park past the timeout, refunded by a stranger at the quoted fee (or dropped, when empty),
  may refuse only as a converted park whose rail is closed, with the error its state names (`IsHalted`,
  `MigrationInProgress`, the precompile's `BelowMinStake` under a raised floor, `Slippage`, `ConversionShort` past the
  cushion); and a converted park to an accepting vault is always delivered. *I-11*: the re-entrant recipient (Task 26's
  `ReentrantRecipient`) is a stranger sending its own batches through the Base adapter; at each call that reaches it,
  its behaviour is drawn (accepting, refusing, re-entering any park function or the sweep before or after refusing, two
  levels deep, bubbling the lock's refusal), and no other record or total moves, no park event names another key,
  nothing is swept, it is paid exactly its batch or nothing, and every re-entry it records is refused
  `ReentrancyGuardReentrantCall`; its Base-side deliveries (refunds of its batches) run with it re-entering nothing, since
  one EVM holds both chains and a Base delivery cannot reach the Bittensor adapter. *The drains* are cheats (the native
  reserve dealt down to the ghost's native parks, the cushion burned down to its token parks), standing for a reserve
  spent on covered roundings; they never go below what is owed. Not reachable here: `VaultInsolvent` (no loss),
  `AboveBridgeCap`, `NoOracle`, `BelowOracleFloor`. The adapters' half of the looped-rounding test runs 500 trips at the
  minimum, not 1,000: a trip costs about a million gas and a thousand pass the test's 2^30 gas limit. The send side's own
  1-rao check is pinned on the lenient `RoundingAlphaVault` (documented as rounding-only), and `MockAlphaVault` runs
  ForeverMoney's gates before its lock, as deployed. The campaign's files, sorting ahead of `test/vault/`, pushed
  `VaultCoreTest` over via-IR's jump-tag limit again in a forced build, so its constructor section moved unchanged to
  `VaultDeploy.t.sol`, as Task 37 moved its setters. No contract change. Task 42a's grown `test/libraries/`, which also
  sorts ahead of `test/vault/`, pushed it over once more (solc's "Tag too large for reserved space"), so its six
  direct-deposit tests moved unchanged to `test/vault/VaultDirectDeposits.t.sol`, with the imports only they used. No
  contract change.
- **Task 38's fix round (3.2, I-7, I-8, I-10, I-11).** *Every key, not only the parked ones.* The campaign compared the
  adapter's records only under the keys its ghost had parked, so an adapter whose delivered batch also left a record (a
  refactor to "record first, delete on success" that forgets the delete) passed it, though anyone could then refund the
  minted batch out of the other parks' TAO. After every step, and between the calls of a composite one, the record under
  every batch's key the handler has seen must now be the ghost's, a key with no live ghost park holding none (I-7; I-8:
  delivered and still parked is two states), each total must equal the sum of the adapter's own records over those keys,
  and the park functions' dead-key draws name any batch's key, a delivered one's included (`NotParked`). The re-entrant
  recipient also re-enters through the router, executing another pending batch from inside its delivery: that execution
  fails at the lock (CCIP `FAILURE`), writes nothing, and the batch is re-executable. *I-10's text* now names what a
  converted park's refund also needs: the token cushion's rao (Revision 22's Task 28 note), beside `depositLiquid`'s
  gates, the chain's minimum stake under it (the precompile's `BelowMinStake` under a raised floor: removing the
  adapter's own floor check from the refund freed an unconverted refund from every floor, not a converted one from the
  chain's minimum) and its slippage. ForeverMoney's held lock, which the ruling also names, is not a campaign state: it
  is held only inside a stranger's own transaction (`test_aStrangerUnderForeverMoneysLockCannotParkAnHonestBatch` pins
  the adapter's side). No contract change.

## Revision 25 (2026-11-11, Checkpoint R4's contract fixes; the founder's and the controller's rulings)

- **The door hold on fresh shares (3.4, 3.5, I-36; R4 B-H1).** One address could take a whole day's first-come
  allowance with a deposit and a redeem in one batch, at no market risk, and defer every honest Base exit that day.
  A door's burn now takes only shares held a day: each holder's fresh shares, in its `Holder{origin, since, fresh}`
  record (the redeem origin's slot, so a new depositor writes no extra slot: a slot of its own priced a 50-deposit
  batch past the gas model), which a
  door's deposit and `depositDirect` add to (restarting the day), and a cancel's re-mint and the reward cut never
  touch. A request held whole is the existing `RedeemDeferred`; no event changes, and the lens serves the bucket.
  The strict per-holder timestamp first ruled was dropped before any code: anyone's `depositFor` could have held any
  holder's whole balance for 0.04 TAO a day. `redeemDirect` is not held (it takes no allowance). The residual, a day
  of exposure on twice the capital still crowding the allowance, is stated in 3.5; a pro-rata allowance is the deeper
  fix, before other people's money.
- **The adapter converts only tokens that arrived (3.2, section 5's Chainlink row, I-7; R4 C-L1).** `_deliver`
  checks `token.balanceOf(this) >= parkedTokens + amount` before the conversion, where the check had run only after a
  failed one; `deliverParked` and an unconverted refund check the same for their park. The parks are always
  protected; a forged amount could then take at most the token cushion (about 0.001 TAO), which F-1 below closes.
- **A parked delivery requires the vault's gas (3.2; R4 A-M1 and C-M1, the contract half).** `deliverParked`
  requires `gasleft() >= PARK_GAS_RESERVE + inboundGasBase + inboundGasPerEntry × entries` right before the vault's
  call, else `InsufficientGas` and the park stays exactly as it was. The pair is the lane's inbound model, the Base
  adapter's `gasBase`/`gasPerEntry`, as two new constructor immutables (the adapter's own pair models the payouts
  toward Base, 140,000 / 29,900, far below the vault's need); the deploy plan feeds both adapters from one setting
  and `verify` fails when they differ. `parkedDeliveryGas(message)` is the keeper's floor. `_deliver` gets no such
  requirement: a starved CCIP delivery parks, by design.
- **The forged-message review's checks (3.2, section 5; R4 F-1, F-3, F-5, the founder's review).** The token
  cushion is booked (`tokenCushion`, `fundCushion`) and C-L1's check covers it, so a forged amount takes neither a park
  nor the cushion, only unswept surplus (the treasury's); `convertExact` refuses a conversion that burned more than its
  amount of tokens (the vault-favour review: not exact equality, which a 1-wei rounding of ForeverMoney's would trip);
  a converted park's delivery and refund check the native books too. F-2, a forged redeem-only batch, stays a stated
  residual (section 5). The adapter campaign gains three compromised-lane actions.
- **The stuck-money review's three (3.5, I-36; R4 S-H1, S-L1, S-L3, the founder's review).** No door burn while the
  vault's primary bridge cannot send payouts (its new `canSendPayouts()`, false from ForeverMoney's migration notice and
  during its pause): door redeems are deferred, deposits still mint, direct exits are unaffected. A clip to the entry
  limit or the allowance leaves the holder 0 or at least `MIN_REDEEM_VALUE`. The fresh bucket's clock moves to the
  share-weighted mean on a mint within the day, so a stranger's gifts cannot hold a holder's own shares past about a
  day. S-L2, S-L4 and S-M1 go to the off-chain round.
- **The vault-favour review (3.6, Appendix B).** `rewardVesting` launches at 30 days, not 7: the keeper claims about
  monthly on a small vault, and a 7-day vesting let a holder present only during it take about 4.3 times its time
  share (ops' settings follow here: the gas recalibration below touches them). Its conditions on R4's rules are met: the weighted clock rounds
  up and the re-mint and the cut stay out of the bucket; the entries' fee share is 0 only when the caller sends the
  whole fee; the clip burns less, never past `min(maxEntryValue, allowance)`; the rail's reads are gas-capped; and the
  burn check refuses only an over-burn.
- **The full top-up (3.3, I-13; an outside review the controller checked).** A raised conversion floor between a
  chunk's net and its gross would strand it (`BelowConversionFloor` on the net), a bounce-only batch for good. A
  caller whose value covers the whole fee now pays all of it and the entries are sent their gross. The residual, a
  floor above the gross, waits for the floor or a new route. The spec notes the founder's call that the treasury
  keeps a small holding, so the supply never reaches 0 (a runbook line, off-chain).
- **The lane's gas model toward Bittensor, recalibrated (Appendix B's inbound row; `AdapterGas`).** 820,000 + 60,000
  an entry, from 780,000 + 58,000. The door hold's day of age in `AdapterGas`'s setUp put its points on a real clock,
  where a day's first burn writes the door's and the global window's starts (at t = 1 an unwritten window read as
  current, so the old points missed about 40,000 mock gas): at 88b056a that alone put every one-redeem point past
  the old model (1+1 needed 902,046 of 896,000). R4's reads add about 21,500 at 1+1 (the rail read, the token check,
  the over-burn check) and about 970 a deposit (the fresh shares). 100 entries give 6,820,000, within `GAS_CAP`, with
  room for Task 61's park reserve (about +150,000, 6,970,000); ops' settings carry the pair until Tasks 61, 64 and 65
  replace it with measurements.

## Revision 26 (2026-11-11, the R4-K review's fixes; the controller's rulings)

- **`canSendPayouts()` never reverts (3.2, 3.5; Important 2).** Its three AlphaVault reads are low-level, gas-capped
  `STATICCALL`s that copy back at most a word, after a code-size check: no code, a revert, an empty or short answer, or
  a read out of its 30,000 gas each reads false. Before, a `try` caught only the callee's revert, so an AlphaVault with
  no code or a fallback answering a missing getter with empty data reverted the view, which the vault read as open,
  and burned a door redeem into a queue that could not pay. The vault's fail-open stays, for a primary bridge with no
  such view: the one open path, tested and stated.
- **The inbound pair includes the park reserve (Appendix B; the launch settings).** 1,300,000 + 57,000 an entry, from
  820,000 + 60,000: `AdapterGas`'s need now counts `PARK_GAS_RESERVE` on top of the measured delivery (the adapter keeps
  it back from the recipient's call), and a pair on the `GAS_CAP` line with a higher base and a lower per-entry figure
  leaves at least 228,600 gas of slack at every measured point (50+1 the least) where 970,000 + 60,000 would leave
  13,232. `parkedDeliveryGas` therefore no longer adds the reserve (counted once, in the pair), and the constructor
  refuses an inbound base at or below it. `maxDeposits` and `maxRedeems` stay 50. Task 61's fork kept the pair
  (Revision 29).
- **The forged-lane invariant made sound (test code; Important 1).** Surplus moving to the immutable treasury (a sweep a
  reached recipient sets off) is allowed and nothing else; F-2's named burn is excluded as its text says; forged copies
  toward Base now run (their messages recorded); every forged action runs through a self-call whose revert is counted,
  never dropped; the review's four-call replay is a regression test. And the review's minors: a direct exit leaves
  `fresh` as it is (3.5), the keeper's top-up rule (3.3), a same-day request after a direct exit pinned, an independent
  floor in the core campaign (a delivery never burns what it minted), an honest action after a forged park, a 50+50
  point in the gas rule's bisection.
- **The cushion's funding step** (`deploy.ts`, `verify.ts`, the runbook: approve + `fundCushion`, and verify reading
  `tokenCushion()`) is done at the merge, as a mandatory switch that gates the deploy (Important 3). Done at merge 7b
  phase 2a: the deploy's step 3 and runbook 3.2 fund it by approve then `fundCushion` (`pnpm ops fund-cushion`, a call
  file for the sign page), and verify fails while `tokenCushion()` is 0. The same round states S-L1's re-mint sliver
  as a residual (3.5).

## Revision 27 (2026-11-13, the adoption drip; the founder's decision)

- **An adoption vests on its own drip (3.1, 3.5, 3.6, I-38; Task 16's adoption, B7b's schedule).** `adoptStake`
  called `_vest(unvested + surplus)`, which restarted the one reward schedule over `max(rewardVesting, what was left)`:
  rewards that holders had already earned (claimed and vesting) were pushed later, and whoever held afterwards, a timed
  entrant included, took a share of rewards earned before it arrived (per adoption at most its share x the rewards left
  x the time since the last restart / the period, up to a quarter of the schedule; anyone could adopt after 7 days
  with a 0.002 TAO gift). Task 42's vault-favour campaign found it, and it was stated as a fifth residual in 3.5. The
  founder decided to fix it before the self-funded launch ("it should be its own drip"): the contracts are immutable,
  so the alternative was a later redeploy. The vault gains a second schedule, the adoption drip (`adoptVestingAmount`,
  `adoptVestingStart`, `adoptVestingPeriod`, last in the layout, laid out as the claim schedule's three, so no other
  variable moved; the lens serves each). `adoptStake` restarts only the adoption drip, on what is left of it and the
  surplus, by the claim schedule's rule (`_restartPeriod`: the period in force, or what is left of the drip if
  longer); the claim schedule (`vestingAmount`, `vestingStart`, `vestingPeriod`) is restarted only by a claim, which
  carries its own remainder alone, never the drip's, and by the write-down. `unvestedRewards()`, on the vault and the
  lens, is both drips' sum (`CascadeFormulas.unvestedRewards` per drip), which `totalAssets()` leaves out, so the price
  steps at neither restart. The write-down at a supply of 0 covers both: the adoption drip first, to what the gross
  holds beyond the claim schedule and the queued payouts, then, when the claim schedule alone exceeds that, the claim
  schedule to it, each restarting by its own rule (neither vests faster than its own schedule). No event changes:
  `StakeAdopted(rao)` stays as it was, and the drip is read from the lens. The fifth residual is removed from 3.5
  (fixed), and I-38 no longer credits what an adoption's restart delayed: the vault-favour campaign's adoption credit
  (fix round 4) is gone and departures are its only credit; its premise, that an adoption leaves the claim schedule
  as it was, is asserted with the time-share bound (`adoptionsMovedClaims`), and the core campaign holds the same
  (`unexpectedAdoptions`). Seeds 8111, 8222 and 8333 at 128 x 64 pass the favour, core and ledger campaigns with 0
  reverts and no vest above the accrual left unfunded. Two mutations, run in a scratch copy, restore the old
  behaviour: the old code (an adoption restarting the claim schedule on everything unvested and the surplus) and the
  delay alone (the claim schedule restarted on its own remainder, the surplus on the drip); each fails the favour
  campaign on every seed (the premise), the core campaign, the unit tests and the adoption pin (first on the vest above
  the accrual: 0.0053 TAO for the delay alone, as fix round 4 measured). Without the premise check the delay alone
  passed the favour invariants on all three seeds, its excess showing only in the logged `unfundedVest` (0.009 to
  0.115 TAO a seed, 0 with the fix). Sizes:
  `CascadeVault` 22,396 B (+222 B, 680 B under the gate's 23,076 B), `CascadeVaultLens` 8,808 B (+215 B); the
  restart's period rule is one internal function both drips share, and nothing moved into a library. The published
  ABI is unchanged (the drip is internal state, served by the lens). Off-chain carry: readers of `unvestedRewards()`
  or `totalAssets()` are unchanged; a reader of the raw claim-schedule fields that means "what still vests" (the
  monitor's snapshot of `vestingAmount`/`vestingStart`/`vestingPeriod`, the web FAQ's "still being added over"
  period, the ABIs that list the lens's views) adds the adoption drip's three.

## Revision 28 (2026-11-13, Base's 0.2-second blocks; the founder's direction)

- **Base's blocks are ten times faster.** Base went from 2-second to 0.2-second blocks at block 53,556,128
  (2026-11-05 18:00 UTC; on chain, 1,000 blocks per 200 seconds since then, five to each whole-second timestamp, and
  2 seconds each before). The contracts read block numbers in two places, the oracle's minimum history and the
  scheduler's block floor; everything else reads time. The founder's direction: fix both now, future-proof, and make
  the floor stronger than it was, not just restored.
- **The oracle's minimum history is 1,801, one per second (3.7, section 7, Appendix A and B).** `MIN_CARDINALITY` was
  901, one observation slot per 2-second block over the 30-minute window, plus one. It is `TWAP_WINDOW + 1`, 1,801: one
  per second, not one per 2-second block. Uniswap writes at most one observation per block timestamp (`Oracle.write`
  returns early on a repeated one), and block timestamps are whole seconds (Base's 0.2-second blocks come five to a
  timestamp, read on 2026-11-13), so at any block time of a second or less no pool writes more than one a second, and
  1,801 slots always reach back the window: future-proof by construction. 901 was too low today: a pool trading every
  second filled them in 15 minutes, so anyone could overwrite the window's history and make the oracle refuse ("OLD"),
  holding stakes and deliveries. The live pools hold 2,000 (TAO/WETH) and 6,700 (WETH/USDC) slots in use (2026-11-13),
  so the deploy's precondition already holds. (The controller's first draft asked for 18,001, one per 100 ms block;
  the one-per-timestamp rule makes anything above 1,801 unused, and the off-chain review corrected it.)
- **The block floor is in milliseconds, calibrated at twice today's speed (3.6, 3.10.17, I-22, Appendix A and B).**
  `SECONDS_PER_BLOCK` (1 to 60 s) becomes `MS_PER_BLOCK` (61 to 60,000 ms, else `InvalidParameter`; `Params.msPerBlock`
  on the vault and the front door, in the same position and type), and `readyBlock` the scheduling block plus
  `ceil(delay * 1000 / (2 * MS_PER_BLOCK))`. The deploy values, calibrated so half the delay in blocks takes 5/6 of it at today's speed (1.67 times faster blocks), are Base 120 and
  Bittensor 7,200: the floor is 720,000, 2,520,000 and 2,880,000 Base blocks, and 12,000, 42,000 and 48,000 Bittensor
  blocks, for the 48-hour, 7-day and 8-day classes. On Bittensor, the vault's chain, whose blocks stop in a halt, that
  is about 0.83 of each delay in live blocks at today's speed (40, 140 and 160 hours), stronger than the half it was,
  and about 0.42 after a 2x speed-up. On Base it protects nothing after an outage, as under R1-A L-3 before it: Base's
  block numbers have fixed timestamps and an outage's blocks are filled in at their own timestamps, so the front door's
  `readyBlock` comes with its `readyAt`; there the guardian's cancel (by L1 forced inclusion too) and the owner's stamp
  protect, and the front door's window in blocks matters only if a fork slows Base's blocks past twice the calibrated
  time (a point for the auditor). A slowdown makes loosening changes wait longer (the floor binds once blocks come
  more than twice the calibrated time apart, 1.2 times today's: Bittensor above 14.4 s, Base only by a fork; at Base's
  old 2-second blocks a 48-hour item would wait about 16.7 days, its window in blocks about 58), in proportion to the
  slowdown, while exits, pauses, tightenings, cancels and stamps read no blocks and are unaffected. The bound is 61 ms,
  not 1, so that a block time in seconds is never taken for milliseconds (2 on Base would hold a 48-hour item about
  100 days, 12 on Bittensor about 1,000).
- **An item lapses only once both its windows have passed (3.6, 3.10.8, section 6).** With the floor calibrated faster
  than today, a slower chain reaches `readyBlock` later, and under the time-only lapse an item whose floor landed after
  `readyAt + EXECUTE_WINDOW` could never run (at ten times the calibrated block time, every class). `_lapsed` is now
  `block.timestamp > readyAt + EXECUTE_WINDOW && block.number > readyBlock + ceil(EXECUTE_WINDOW * 1000 / (2 *
  MS_PER_BLOCK))`: its window in blocks is half the window in calibrated blocks (2,520,000 on Base, 42,000 on
  Bittensor), as the floor is half the delay; `execute` refuses `LapsedItem` and `prune` admits an item on that rule
  alone. Why it is sound: a lapse needs `block.number > readyBlock + W_b`, so at `readyBlock` an item is never lapsed,
  and the first block in which both its clocks have passed can find it lapsed only when one gap between blocks, longer
  than the 7-day window, spans `readyAt` after its window in blocks has passed (a halt of more than 7 days at that
  moment, which the time-only lapse killed too). Once ready, an item keeps at least its whole time window after
  `readyAt`, or at least `W_b` blocks after `readyBlock` when its floor was the later clock. On blocks up to about twice
  the calibrated time apart (today's speeds and any faster), `readyBlock + W_b` comes before `readyAt +
  EXECUTE_WINDOW`, so the lapse is exactly the time window, as before; on slower blocks the window in blocks lasts
  longer in time the slower they come; a halt holds the blocks, so an item whose window in blocks was open when the
  chain halted keeps the rest of it after the resume. Half the window in blocks, not all of it: with all of it, the
  lapse at today's speeds would stretch from 7 days after `readyAt` to about 10.3 to 11.3, and a ready, unexecuted item is
  a pre-approved change that a stolen key could run at once, so its exposure stays what it was. The pending owner's
  acceptance (`pendingSince + EXECUTE_WINDOW`) stays in time: it has no block floor, so no slowdown can strand it; the
  gates, the clocks, `cancel`, `stampBySig` and the lens read no lapse.
- **The lens and the ABI.** The forwarded getter is `MS_PER_BLOCK()` (selector `0xa7a093c5`, was
  `SECONDS_PER_BLOCK()`, `0xc1d74258`) on both targets and both lenses (`IExtsload`, lens-surface's `FORWARDED`), and
  the constructor's `Params.secondsPerBlock` is `Params.msPerBlock` (its value now milliseconds). The published vault
  ABI changes in exactly those two names (`merge-abi.mjs`).
- **A capped read is always given its whole cap (3.1, 3.8; the off-chain review's finding, and the audit it asked
  for).** `writeOffMissingStake` reads the coldkey's total in a `try` capped at 100,000 gas and counts a failed read
  as 0, which lets the `WriteOffTooLarge` bound pass; a caller who sets the gas so the read is starved could write off
  stake that listing could still bring back, and a wallet's estimate could pick such a limit by itself (Bittensor's
  estimator bisects to the least gas that succeeds). EIP-150 alone does not close it for a precompile: a starved
  read leaves its caller only a 64th of the gas if it runs out, too little to finish, but a precompile that checks
  its cost up front and refuses with a revert returns the rest. `CascadeFormulas.requireGasFor(cap)` reverts
  `InsufficientGas` unless `gasleft() >= cap * 64 / 63 + 5,000` (the margin for the call's own cost, a cold account
  included), so the read gets all of `READ_GAS` (100,000) or the call reverts (the item kept). The audit of every
  capped `try` and low-level call in `contracts/src` whose failure changes a decision:
  - the write-off's total read (vault; fail-open: 0 unlisted): the owner's transaction sets the gas: guarded;
  - the floors' reads, `minStakeRao` and `minRemainRao` (`CascadeFormulas`, compiled into the vault, the walk, the
    hotkey library and the lens; fail-open: the constant fallbacks, lower than the chain's minimum whenever the chain
    raises it above 0.002 / 0.02 TAO): anyone's `addHotkey` and `adoptStake`, the keeper's `stakeIdle`, and the walk
    in a user's `redeemDirect` or anyone's `processPayouts` set the gas: guarded, so a stranger cannot list a hotkey,
    or adopt a surplus, below the chain's minimum by starving the read;
  - the vault's rail read, `_payoutsOpen` (a staticcall capped at `RAIL_VIEW_GAS`; fail-open: open): read only in a
    delivery, which only the primary adapter makes, with the lane's model gas (CCIP's limit, which our own sender
    sets and manual execution cannot lower) or, in `deliverParked`, behind that model's floor (R4), both far above the
    cap's 64/63: not starvable, unchanged;
  - the adapter's three rail reads in `canSendPayouts` (capped at `RAIL_READ_GAS`; fail-closed: cannot send): a starved
    read only defers a burn, inside the vault's capped rail read above: unchanged;
  - the adapter's conversion and recipient calls (`convertExact`, `onBridgeMessage`, all the gas but
    `PARK_GAS_RESERVE`; fail-closed: a park anyone delivers later): CCIP's model gas, and `deliverParked`'s floor:
    unchanged;
  - the walk's removals (`removeStake`, `removeStakeFull`, each its share of the gas, at least `unstakeGas`;
    fail-closed: the position skipped, and more than `SHORTFALL_TOLERANCE` unmet reverts): the caller only hurts its
    own call: unchanged;
  - `removeHotkey`'s unclaimed read (no cap; fail-closed: every path but the owner's forfeit refuses): unchanged;
  - the staker's `permit` (`try`, no cap; the caller's own allowance either way) and every native send (a failure
    reverts): no decision to flip.
  The cost: 44 B on the vault, 43 B on the walk (`CascadeStakeLib`), 25 B on the hotkey library and 43 B on the
  vault lens.
- **Tests.** `test/governance/BlockFloor.t.sol` (new, 7 after the review): each class's `readyBlock` at both deploy values and its time
  at today's speeds (5/6 of the delay) and after a 2x speed-up (5/12), on today's chain run at readyAt and not a second
  before; a 3-day halt after one live hour holds a 48-hour item NotReady until its floor's blocks come (39 hours of
  Bittensor's 12-second blocks), the guardian cancelling in the resume block; a halt longer than the window strands
  nothing; on Base an outage's back-filled blocks meet the floor with readyAt (the review's I-1); on blocks ten
  times slower than calibrated each class runs from `readyBlock` to its window in blocks' last block and lapses, and
  prunes, only after both windows; on the calibrated blocks, twice as fast and today's, each lapses exactly a second
  after its time window; and the rule at its edges. `CascadeBase.t.sol`'s floor tests in milliseconds (the bounds 61
  and 60,000 taken, 0, 60 and 60,001 refused, after the review; the R1 ports at 6,000 ms on 12-second blocks and 100 ms on Base's 0.2 seconds;
  the slower chain), the vault's and the front door's constructor bounds, `Scheduler.t.sol`'s halt at 7,200 ms (12,000
  blocks, 40 hours of 12-second blocks), the oracle refusing 1,800 and 901 and taking 1,801 (and the live pools' 2,000
  and 6,700), and two gas scans in `VaultLedger.t.sol` (no gas limit from 60,000 to 600,000 starves the write-off's
  read into a write-off, or the floor's read into a stranger's listing; the mock's reads refuse short of 90,000 gas
  and return the rest, `setReadGasFloor`). The fixtures at the deploy values (`DefaultParams`, `SchedulerHarness`,
  `ExitsFixture`, `LensParity`), the warps in milliseconds (`TrackedHandler._warp`; `Schedule.warpTo` at 500 blocks
  a second, enough for any `MS_PER_BLOCK`), and the governance campaigns' model of `readyBlock` and the lapse
  (`GovernanceHandler`, `LivenessHandler`, `CopiedKeyHandler`, the core handler's Reopen), with two reach counters
  (`executesInTheBlockWindow`, `prunesRefusedInTheBlockWindow`); no draw changed then (the review's directed halt step later did, and re-derived the minimums).
  The forced parity suite: 109 suites, 1,437 passed, 0 failed, 27 skipped (108 and 1,428 before, with the seed-112
  pin in between). Seeds 9101, 9202 and 9303 at 128 x 64 pass the governance, liveness, copied-key, core and exits
  campaigns, and seed 112 the exits campaign. Six mutations in a scratch copy, each caught: `MIN_CARDINALITY` back to
  901 (2 oracle tests), the floor back to seconds (9) and without its half (10), the lapse in time alone (3), and
  either gas check removed (the write-off then goes at a 60,000 gas limit, the stranger's listing at 90,000).
- **Sizes.** `CascadeVault` 22,655 B (+259 B: about 215 B the lapse's window in blocks, 44 B the gas check), 421 B
  under the gate's 23,076 B; `CascadeFrontDoor` 19,102 B (+418 B, the shared scheduler's lapse); `CascadeVaultLens`
  8,851 B, `CascadeStakeLib` 2,006 B, `CascadeHotkeyLib` 2,599 B; the rest unchanged. Nothing moved into a library.
- **Off-chain carry** (v3.2-wire's, not here): every reader of `SECONDS_PER_BLOCK` or `secondsPerBlock` (ops' deploy
  plan and settings, `code.ts`'s expected getters, `verify`, the keeper's ABI and deployment pins, `lens.ts`, the sign
  page and the scheduler's block estimates, which become `ceil(delay * 1000 / (2 * ms))`), of `MIN_CARDINALITY` or 901
  (ops' `cardinality.ts`, the keeper's `MIN_OBSERVATIONS`, `deploy.ts`, the indexer's `oracleMinCardinality` and
  `deps.ts`, `LAUNCH-SETTINGS.md`), and of the lapse as `readyAt + EXECUTE_WINDOW` alone (the indexer's `scheduler.ts`
  and `rules/admin.ts`' `admin.lapsed` and record pruning, ops' `scheduler.ts`, `sign.ts`, `rails.ts` and `verify.ts`'
  `windowEnd`) adds the window in blocks: an item may still run past its time window while `block.number <= readyBlock
  + ceil(EXECUTE_WINDOW * 1000 / (2 * MS_PER_BLOCK))`.
- **The fresh review's fixes (2026-11-14: 0 Critical, 2 Important, 5 Minor; the floor's arithmetic, the half window in
  blocks, the readers, the oracle's 1,801 and the gas check judged sound).**
  - I-2: `MS_PER_BLOCK` takes 61 to 60,000 ms. Every value a block time in seconds took before (1 to 60) is refused
    with `InvalidParameter`, so seconds passed as milliseconds cannot freeze the scheduler behind an immutable (2 on
    Base made a 48-hour item wait about 100 days, 12 on Bittensor about 1,000).
  - I-1: the floor's live-block protection is the vault's chain's. Base's block number is a fixed function of its
    timestamp and an outage's blocks are filled in at their own timestamps (the sequencer's catch-up, or derivation
    past the sequencing window), so the front door's `readyBlock` comes with its `readyAt`;
    after a Base outage the guardian's cancel (by L1 forced inclusion too) and the owner's stamp protect, as they did
    under R1-A L-3, and the front door's window in blocks matters only if a fork slows Base's blocks past twice the
    calibrated time. 3.6, 3.10.17, the block floor's bullet above, `CascadeBase`'s comment and the README say so; a
    point for the auditor.
  - M-1: the vault's and the front door's constructor comments state 61 to 60,000 ms, and the oracle's "every block"
    is "every second".
  - M-2: the governance campaign's directed step `haltPastTheWindow` (a halt past an item's window in time with the
    blocks held, then a prune, an execute with the blocks held, or one after they catch up to `readyBlock`), with
    minimum hits by the slowest-seed rule from seeds 9101, 9202 and 9303: `executesInTheBlockWindow` 265 and
    `prunesRefusedInTheBlockWindow` 176 (and, re-derived with the new draw, `guardianRemovalsOfRemoved` 381 and
    `reopensDeleted` 220, from 474 and 272). The lapse in time alone now fails the campaign itself, not only the
    unit tests.
  - M-3: the slowdown margin, stated in 3.6 and above: delays lengthen only on blocks more than twice the calibrated
    time apart, 1.2 times today's (Bittensor above 14.4 s; Base only by a fork: back at 2-second blocks a 48-hour item
    would wait about 16.7 days, its window in blocks about 58).
  - Considered and not taken: M-5, a gas check before `_payoutsOpen`'s rail read (a delivery always has the lane
    model's gas, so it cannot be starved; the vault keeps its headroom). M-4 is the off-chain agent's.

## Revision 29 (2026-11-14, Task 65: the gas constants from the fork; the controller's rulings)

- **Why now, and from what.** The testnet tasks (62 to 64) were dropped by the founder's decision (2026-11-11), so
  the constants rest on Task 61's fork measurements (the real OffRamp, Router, pools and ForeverMoney's AlphaVault over
  `MockStaking`) and on the T61a review's live read-only estimates from an account on Bittensor (`addStake` 104,208
  gas, `removeStake` 144,167, `getStake` 27,427, each with the 21,000 intrinsic). The contracts are immutable once
  deployed, so each constant errs high: too high costs a little gas limit; too low can strand an exit or a message.
- **`EXIT_GAS_RESERVE` 1,200,000 to 2,000,000 (3.8, Appendix B).** A removal that burns its gas leaves the exit only
  the reserve, so the work after the walk must fit in it whatever gas the caller sends. On the fork, at the keeper's
  gas with the paying removal burning its share, a payout's work after the walk is about 549,000 at one entry and
  648,000 at fifty over the mock precompile (1,450,930 and 1,352,465 of the reserve spare); the live chain adds about
  60,000 to 110,000 (the real `addStake` in the send, the walk's last `getStake`), so 2,000,000 is 2.6 to 3.3 times
  the live work. Every exit test now asserts the reserve holds at least twice its work with a 150,000 live allowance
  (over the mocks and on the fork), which 1,200,000 failed. The cost is only a higher gas limit per exit: the caller's
  gas rule becomes 2,000,000 + positions × `unstakeGas` + the exit's own work (3.6M with four hotkeys at the launch
  values), and at the setter's top 22.0M, inside the 75,000,000 block and the per-transaction limit, about 65,000,000
  (Revision 30). Nothing else moves with it: the walk's split,
  the lens's constant (it serves `EXIT_GAS_RESERVE` from the same file), the 63/64 holdbacks between the vault, L3 and
  the bridge (the send still receives the reserve within 100,000, `VaultExits`), and every exit test (one assertion now
  in terms of the reserve, one payout's gas raised by the reserve's 800,000 so its shares stay above `unstakeGas`).
- **`MAX_CLAIM_GAS` 60,000,000 to 73,500,000 (3.9, Appendix B; superseded by Revision 30: 63,400,000, against the
  per-transaction limit, about 65,000,000, since no Bittensor transaction can carry the 75,000,000 block limit this
  bullet used).** The most `claimGasRequired` whose claim still fits
  Bittensor's 75,000,000-gas block (read live 2026-11-14) with the claim's own overhead and a 1,000,000 margin, so the
  owner can raise the setting at once should the mainnet canary's real claim need more than the launch value. The
  overhead, the vault's gas outside the forwarded `claimRootWithHotkey` (the intrinsic gas, the checks, eight
  `getStake` reads with four hotkeys listed, the booking, the treasury's first cut, the restart and the events, every
  slot cold), measured 218,947 at its dearest over the mocks; 500,000 is allowed, twice that: 73,500,000 + 500,000 +
  1,000,000 = 75,000,000 (`VaultClaimGas.t.sol`, which also runs a claim at the top that needs all it is given in a
  transaction of 74,000,000). A rogue setting at the top costs a claim more fee but still fits a block, so the
  permissionless claim after `KEEPER_FALLBACK` is never too large for a block (round 9 L-3's lever, unchanged in kind).
- **Kept.** `MIN_CLAIM_GAS` 53,200,000 (no fork can run the claim; the canary measures one); `PARK_GAS_RESERVE`
  150,000 (the fork's worst park write 59,344, 2.5 times under it, with no precompile after the failure; raising it
  would raise every inbound message's CCIP gas); the inbound pair 1,300,000 / 57,000 and the 50 + 50 batch limits.
  Off the contracts: the toward-Base pair (the Bittensor adapter's constructor, an ops deploy setting) rises from
  140,000 / 29,900 to 180,000 / 32,000 (the T61a review's I-2: the thinnest margin, and the live door's non-zero
  `netForwarded` write costs 2,800 more than the forks measured; the fork test counts it now): one payout then needs
  126,003 of 180,000, fifty 1,373,140 of 1,780,000; the contract tests use the new pair.
- **Comments.** The Bittensor adapter's NatSpec on a FAILURE's re-execution says what section 5 and the README say
  since 2026-11-10 (it needs the committee's results, as every execution; at launch a report and a re-execution by
  hand), and every src comment that waited for "Task 64" or "the testnet gas tests" names Task 61's measurement and
  the canary.
- **Tests and sizes.** Test-first: the tests pinning the new values and their margins failed at 2d33d0d (11 of 90 in
  the five suites touched: the claim bound's transaction short of the block by 13,500,000, the exits' reserve short of twice its
  work, 1,776,960 and 1,738,300 needed over the mocks, the pins at 1,200,000 and 60,000,000) and pass at 3bb6617.
  New: `VaultClaimGas.t.sol` (the overhead within its allowance, the bound the most that fits, a claim at the top);
  the reserve's floor in `AdapterGas` and `BittensorForkV32`; the toward-Base pair at 180,000 / 32,000 in the
  fork and calibration tests with the netForwarded write; the stranger's empty-proof attempt asserted to return and
  write FAILURE; the token park's native reserve and the vault's balance; the payout's fee quote cold (its least gas
  821,665 and 1,310,954, about 53,500 more than with the quote warm). `AdapterGas`'s three exit tests move to their own
  suite, `AdapterGasExitsTest`, unchanged but for the reserve and its floor: the calibration's contract, with them,
  hit via-IR's "Tag too large for reserved space" when compiled beside a vault suite (the whole forced build
  among them). The forced parity suite: 113 suites, 1,452 passed, 0 failed, 48 skipped (111, 1,449 and 48 before);
  `FORK=1`: `BaseForkV32` 9 of 9 (block 57,607,777) and `BittensorForkV32` 13 of 13 (block 9,508,000); the build
  checks 7 of 7 (`LibraryLinks.sol` regenerated: the vault's link offsets moved by its byte); seeds 9401, 9402 and 9403
  of the whole invariant folder at 128 x 64 pass (308 tests each, 0 failing). Sizes: `CascadeVault`
  22,656 B (+1 B: the claim bounds' span no longer fits three bytes; 420 B under the gate), `CascadeVaultLens`
  8,825 B (-26 B); every other part unchanged.
- **Off-chain carry** (the off-chain Task 65 on v3.2-wire, not here): the keeper's payout gas rule
  (`ops/src/keeper/core/v32/payout.ts` `EXIT_GAS_RESERVE`, 1,200,000) moves to 2,000,000, or burning removals at the
  floor can eat the reserve; the claim's bound (`ops/src/v32/settings.ts` `BOUNDS_V32.maxClaimGas`,
  `ops/src/keeper/core/rewards.ts` `CLAIM_GAS_REQUIRED_MAX`) to 63,400,000 (Revision 30), with the keeper's highest
  gas limits (`MAX_KEEPER_GAS`, `keeper/src/proof.ts` `MAX_PROOF_GAS`, claimGasRequired + 2,000,000 today) held
  within the per-transaction limit, 64,900,000 (`BITTENSOR_TX_GAS_CEILING` in `ops/src/keeper/core/ceiling.ts`,
  mirrored in `indexer/src/thresholds.ts`); the toward-Base deploy pair (`settings.ts` `gas.towardBase`) to 180,000 /
  32,000; and the tests that read these constants from the source (`ops/test/keeper-v32.test.ts`, `ops/test/v32-plan.test.ts`,
  `ops/test/keeper-r4.test.ts`, `keeper/test/node/pins.test.ts`) and the web's fixtures at 1,200,000.

## Revision 30 (2026-11-15, Task 65's fix round: Bittensor's per-transaction gas limit; the controller's ruling)

- **The limit a transaction meets is about 65,000,000, not the 75,000,000 block (3.9, Appendix B; the Task 65 review's
  I-1).** Revision 29 sized `MAX_CLAIM_GAS` against Bittensor's block gas limit, 75,000,000. A single transaction
  cannot carry that. Subtensor (spec 490, the mainnet runtime; `runtime/src/lib.rs`) has a 4e12 ref-time block, a
  normal dispatch ratio of 75% and `BlockWeights::with_sensible_defaults`, and Frontier's `WeightPerGas` is
  75% × 4e12 / 75,000,000 = 40,000 ref time a gas. `frame_system`'s `with_sensible_defaults` reserves 10% of the block
  for initialization, so the normal class's `max_extrinsic` is 3e12 − 0.4e12 − the base extrinsic weight, about
  2.6e12. Frontier weighs an Ethereum transaction by its gas limit (not its gas used), and `CheckWeight` refuses any
  weight above `max_extrinsic` with `ExhaustsResources`, both in the pool and when the block is built. That gives a
  ceiling of about 65,000,000 gas, a little less. The live chain agrees (2026-11-15, read-only): the runtime's own pool
  check, `TaggedTransactionQueue_validate_transaction`, by `state_call` on a legacy transaction signed by an unfunded key (never submitted),
  passes the weight check up to a gas limit of 64,998,400 and answers `ExhaustsResources` from 64,998,600 up
  (65,000,000, 73,500,000, 74,000,000 and 75,000,000 all refused).
- **`MAX_CLAIM_GAS` 73,500,000 to 63,400,000.** The same rule as Revision 29, against the real limit: 63,400,000 + the
  500,000 overhead allowance + the 1,000,000 margin = 64,900,000 ≤ 64,998,400 (the margin also covers a runtime
  upgrade that moves the base weights). At 73,500,000 the owner could have set a value between about 64.7M and 73.5M
  that no transaction can meet, stopping every claim (reversible at once, yield only, but the bound's point, round 9
  L-3's "nor ask more than a block gives", was lost). `VaultClaimGas.t.sol` now holds the claim at the top against
  `TX_GAS_LIMIT` = 64,998,400 (its source and the reading cited there): the bound is the most that fits to 100,000, and
  a claim at the top that needs all it is given completes in a transaction of 63,900,000. The test EVM has no
  per-transaction ceiling, so the constant read from the chain stands in for it.
