- **What it means for the launch.** The live headroom of `claimGasRequired` runs from 53.2M to 63.4M: if the mainnet
  canary's real claim needs more than that left inside the vault, no setting fixes it, and only a redeploy with a
  different claim path would. And since Frontier weighs a transaction by its gas limit, a claim near 55M gas takes
  about 55% of a block's weight: it needs a fairly quiet block (the block's other normal transactions plus the claim
  within 3e12), and the pool may hold it a few blocks. The canary should record the claim's gas limit, that the pool
  took it, its block and how many tries it took.
- **Everything else stands.** `EXIT_GAS_RESERVE` 2,000,000 (its largest walk, 22.0M at the setter's top, is far under
  the 65,000,000 limit), `MIN_CLAIM_GAS` 53,200,000 (its keeper transaction, 55.2M at the 2,000,000 margin, is under
  the limit), `PARK_GAS_RESERVE`, the inbound pair and the batch limits. The vault is back to 22,655 B (the bounds' span,
  10,200,000, fits three bytes again; 421 B under the gate), so `LibraryLinks.sol` is regenerated.
- **Off-chain carry** (the off-chain Task 65 on v3.2-wire): a per-transaction ceiling, about 64,900,000, beside the
  block limit (`ops/src/keeper/core/v32/payout.ts` `BITTENSOR_BLOCK_GAS_LIMIT`); `claimGasLimit`, `MAX_KEEPER_GAS` and
  `MAX_PROOF_GAS` capped at it (with `MAX_CLAIM_GAS` 63,400,000 the 2,000,000 margin gives 65,400,000: cap the limit,
  or cut the margin to 1,500,000, and move `indexer/src/thresholds.ts` `claimGasMargin` and its pin with it);
  `BOUNDS_V32.maxClaimGas` and `CLAIM_GAS_REQUIRED_MAX` to 63,400,000 (`ops/src/v32/plan.ts` follows); and the
  canary's claim line above. Today's values are safe: 55M + 2M = 57M, under the limit.

## Revision 31 (2026-11-15, Checkpoint R5's contracts review: its Minors; the controller's rulings)

- **`MIN_CLAIM_GAS` 53,200,000 to 54,100,000 (3.9, Appendix B; M-4).** The bound promises that no setting lets a claim
  start short of what the chain needs, but the vault checks the gas left before the call, and the call forwards 63/64
  of it, less its own cost: at 53,200,000 the precompile got about 52.37M, below the 53.2M (about 53.18M) section 2
  measured, and the repo's own `MockStaking` refused that claim. 53.2M over 63/64 is about 54.03M; with the call's cost
  between the check and the call, 54.1M left forwards about 53.25M. A new test,
  `test_aClaimAtTheFloorForwardsWhatThePrecompileNeeds` (`VaultClaimGas`), sets `claimGasRequired` to the floor,
  bisects the least gas the check lets through to 1 gas, and lands the claim there against MockStaking's 53.2M need (at 53,200,000 the least passing gas, 53,240,444, burned).
  The launch value, 55,000,000 (about 54.14M forwarded), stays inside the bounds; the live headroom is 54.1M to 63.4M
  left, about 53.25M to 62.4M forwarded. The pins move with it (`VaultBounds`, `VaultClaimGas`, `ExitsMachine`'s floor,
  `VaultDeploy`'s lower-bound construction; `SetterDirections` and `LensParity` used 54,000,000 as an in-bound value,
  now 54,500,000). The vault's size is unchanged (both bounds keep their byte widths).
- **Comments that go into the verified source (M-1, M-8).** "Twice as fast" misdescribed the block-time calibration:
  7,200 ms is 12 s over 1.67, and 120 ms is 0.2 s over 1.67. The constructors' NatSpec (`CascadeBase`, the vault's
  and the front door's `Params`) and this spec now say "calibrated so half the delay in blocks takes 5/6 of it at
  today's speed (1.67 times faster blocks)", which is what Revision 28 measured (0.83 of each delay in live blocks
  today). ForeverMoney's `cancelMigration()` sets `migrationReadyAt` back to 0, so an announced migration closes
  `depositLiquid` only until it is cancelled: `IAlphaVault` and `canSendPayouts`' NatSpec say so (the view reads it
  live; the behaviour was already right). All src comment edits and the constant are in one commit, which the frozen
  build follows.
- **A test of the rail read that cannot be starved (M-5).** `VaultExitRail`'s three `noGasStarvesAClosedRailIntoABurn`
  tests (a view costing nothing, the live adapter's 10,731, and 140,000 near the cap): on a closed stub rail,
  `onBridgeMessage` of a redeem-only batch is bisected to its least succeeding gas and swept downward from just above
  it (every 32 gas through the 2,000 below it, every 500 to 30,000); every outcome is the redeem deferred, or a revert
  that changed nothing; never `Redeemed`.
- **Appendix B.** A row for the toward-Base pair (180,000 / 32,000, immutable in the Bittensor adapter); the inbound
  pair's row says Task 61's fork kept it.
- **The README's side** (M-2, M-3, M-6, M-7, M-8's checklist line): the retained hotkey swap's freeze as an open risk
  (Task 14's two-way model the record; the keeper never frees a slot whose credits read above 0); the auto-claim index

## Revision 32 (2026-11-15, R6: ChatGPT's second-round review and Fable's whole-system review; the founder's and the controller's rulings)

Each ChatGPT finding was checked twice (Opus, then Fable); the founder: "make any fixes you want before we deploy".
Four behaviour changes and one comment-only src change in R6, two behaviour changes in R6b (U1, U4); the rest stated.

- **A direct exit lowers the door hold's bucket (3.5, I-36; ChatGPT R2 claim A, Low/Medium).** `redeemDirect` left
  `fresh` as it was, and the weighted clock (S-L3) counted shares that had left: fifty one-block round trips of
  100 TAO left about 5,000 TAO of burned shares at t0, so a door deposit just short of a day later was held under an
  hour (R4 B-H1's crowding back for gas). After the burn the bucket is capped at the balance, so `fresh <= balance`
  everywhere; 3.5's "conservative" sentence was wrong for the clock and is replaced. Tests: the scenario flipped
  (`VaultDoorHold`: the bucket ends at 0, the later deposit held the full day), the aged-shares case (aged shares
  stand in for the burned fresh ones), `VaultDoorHold`'s `fresh` 9e24 (was 1e25), `CoreHandler`'s model capped after
  every direct exit, and the invariant `freshOf <= balanceOf` (it failed in its first run before the fix).
- **A disabled primary bridge reads closed (3.5's S-H1, I-36; ChatGPT R2 claim D, Low).** `_payoutsOpen` asked the
  primary's own view, which cannot see the vault's disable, so in the half-switched replacement (the front door's
  primary switched first) a batch through the new bridge burned into a queue nothing could send until the owner's
  switch. The vault now reads `bridgeEnabled[primaryBridge]` first and defers (S-H1's path). Stated with it: a bounce
  in that window still queues behind the disabled primary (as S-H1 already accepts), and a deferral drops the
  batch's requests, so holders pay the request fee again; under ForeverMoney's pause a redeem-only batch is
  delivered and dropped while a mixed one parks whole and keeps its redeems (Fable's I-2), and under a disabled
  primary a mixed batch's redeems are dropped too. The runbook switches the vault's primary first. Tests:
  `VaultExitRail`'s six (the window deferred and the switch paying through the new primary; the guardian's disable;
  a mixed delivery; the bounce residual; a Reopen; a disabled primary with no rail view); `ExitHandler` counts a
  disabled primary as a closed rail.
- **The write-off's coldkey read has its own cap (3.1, Appendix B; ChatGPT R2 claim C, Low).** Bittensor meters
  `getTotalColdkeyStakeOnSubnet` by the coldkey's positions, which a stranger can add under the vault's own hotkeys
  (0.0001 TAO each, never removable by the vault), and about 67 priced it past `READ_GAS`'s 100,000 for good, so the
  `WriteOffTooLarge` bound read 0 at every gas limit. `WRITE_OFF_READ_GAS` = 45,000,000, the chain's ceiling for
  this read (256 hotkeys on 128 subnets and root, 44,801,875, rounded up), in both `requireGasFor` and the `try`;
  `catch {}` stays (a vanished function, or metering raised past any constant by a later runtime, is the stated
  residual under the notice and veto); every other read keeps `READ_GAS`. A dust position pins its hotkey in the
  coldkey's staking set for good, so every hotkey the vault ever listed can be pinned. The execute's least frame
  gas, measured over the mock: 46,455,972 (46,479,084 as a transaction), and a write-off whose read burns its whole
  cap uses 45,014,348; it runs within a 64,900,000 transaction. Tests (`VaultLedger`): reads priced at 110,000 and at
  44,801,875 still bound the write-off; the constant pinned to the ceiling and the transaction limit; too little gas
  reverts `InsufficientGas` with the item kept; the cap and starvation tests moved to the new cap.
- **The missing-stake tolerance's base is the stake backing shares (3.1, I-6, Appendix B; ChatGPT R2 claim E, the
  founder's concern after Fable's check; superseding the first triage's "no contract change").** The bps part was
  0.1% of the booked stake, which also holds the stake behind queued, unprocessed payouts: with the queue most of it
  (100 booked, 99.8 queued) a 0.1 TAO gap read as nothing missing while mints ran at half the price. The base is now
  `sub0(booked, pending / RAO)`, with the 0.001 TAO floor; all of the queue comes off (part may be liquid, which only
  tightens), no balance is read, and the vesting rewards stay in the base. The same rule at every reader: the vault's
  guard, the claim's two reads, the delivery, L4's `add` and `remove` (a new `pending` argument) and the lens. The
  residual left: a minter inside the tolerance gains at most max(0.1% of the stake backing shares, 0.001 TAO); and
  (I-6) where less than 2 TAO backs shares a sub-minimum swapped position now closes mints until a privileged actor
  lists it. Fable's further points stand under the new base: a minter can exit and then list the swapped hotkey
  itself, and a small gift can pull a gap just past the tolerance back inside. Tests (`VaultLedger`): Fable's example
  now refuses a direct deposit and a batch; the boundary at a 10% queue; the formula's fuzz with a queue (no queue:
  the old rule); a fuzz of the minter's gain and the stayers' loss; `VaultModel` and `LibraryBoundary` follow.
- **The block floor counts from scheduling (3.6, the single-key residual, Revision 9's L-3; ChatGPT R2 G, Info).**
  "A halt longer than the remaining delay cannot make it executable in the first block after resume" was false for a
  halt that begins once the floor's blocks are in and spans `readyAt`: the item is then ready in the first block
  after the resume. The guarantee is the floor's live blocks since scheduling (about 0.83 of the delay today), and
  the founder's response budget is those blocks. The "about a day" of L-3 is the stamp's `STAMP_MAX_TTL`. No
  behaviour change; `BlockFloor.test_aHaltAfterTheFloorSpanningReadyAtLeavesItReadyAtTheResume` pins it, and the
  `CascadeBase` NatSpec and the README say it.
- **Fixed outflow windows (3.4, I-15, section 5; ChatGPT R2 claim F, known since v2).** About two allowances can burn
  seconds apart across a reset, so the Chainlink row's "one `payoutDelay` of burns, a day's allowance at launch" is up
  to two allowances (20 TAO at the 10 TAO floor), the rogue-door row says "per fixed window", and the README's "at
  most about 20% of the vault per day" becomes about 40% within seconds. The run and SOG figures stand (a fixed window
  never allows less than a rolling one) but are best-timing figures. No change to `currentWindow`: the leaky bucket is
  queued with the pro-rata allowance (section 5's new list). `VaultDelivery.test_claimF_...` pins the doubling.
- **A Base contract wallet's Bittensor twin (3.4, section 5's list; ChatGPT R2 claim B; the founder's decision: a
  known risk, no contract change).** `redeemDirect` pays whoever controls an address on chain 964, door-credited
  shares included, with no deadline; a twin can also lock the wallet out of its door and harvest later deposits. The
  site must refuse or properly warn contract wallets before other people's money; a later fix is an opt-in "door
  only" lock sent from Base (or a registered withdrawal address), never a forced one; an audit item.
  `SmartWalletTwin.t.sol` pins the take, the lock-out and the harvest.
- **Stated from Fable's whole-system review (no change).** L-1: `depositCap` counts `netForwarded`, which a direct
  exit never lowers, so door deposits can stop `CapExceeded` until the owner's `setCaps` (`EndToEnd`); L-2: the
  deposit list can be held with cancellable minimums for gas only (README item 29; `EndToEnd`; closed by Revision
  36's list half rule); I-2 above; I-3: the
  treasury's `DIRECT_ORIGIN` step is mandatory if the treasury is ever a contract (3.4).
- **Smaller notes.** The claim-gas bounds set what a claim requires left; a ceiling-capped transaction forwards about
  63.7M at any setting (3.9, `CascadeConstants`; the docs said 62.4M, "no setting"). The outflow base holds the stake
  behind queued payouts too, so a door's daily cap is looser than its bps while the queue is large (I-5). Fee
  credits are not withdrawable, by design, and two other fees are not returned (README). A stranger's gas limit
  flips no payout decision (`VaultExits.test_aStrangersGasLimitFlipsNoPayoutDecision`). Section 5 gains the
  before-other-people's-money list.
- **Test-model corrections the E-fix needed.** `CoreHandler` judged a delivery's I-1 guard by reading
  `stakeMissing()` after it ("a delivery changes no stake"); a delivery's queued payouts now tighten the tolerance, so
  it reads the state before the attempt, as it reads the underwater state (the forced suite's campaign found it).
  `test_aMintAtAnEmptyVaultAbsorbsASwapsRecordedGap` and `test_aMintAtAnEmptyVaultUnderwaterIsRefused`: with every
  booked rao behind queued payouts the tolerance is its 0.001 TAO floor, so the first's 4 bps swap now reads as
  missing until its gift brings the gap inside (asserted both ways), and the second swaps 1 bp of 10 TAO, exactly the
  floor. The library-rules script tests follow L4's new `add`/`remove` signatures.

R6b (the multi-agent review's confirmed findings, each checked again by Fable; the founder approved U4's contract
change):

- **Only the keeper restarts a claim schedule that still vests (U1; Medium before other people's money, Low at
  launch).** `claimRewards` reads `own = _claimUnvested()` first and reverts `NotKeeper` for any other caller while it
  is non-zero; the vesting rule is unchanged, so every one-hotkey proof and the adoption drip stand, and a non-keeper
  claim always starts a fresh period on its own amount. The permissionless claim then opens at the later of 7 days
  since that hotkey's claim and the schedule's end (rewards stay owed on chain meanwhile). The amount-weighted period
  was rejected (it breaks the one-hotkey bound). Not covered, by design: the keeper's own staggered claims and a stolen
  keeper key (the keeper row's "can time claims badly"). +50 B. Tests: `VaultRewards` (a stranger refused mid-schedule
  with the claim record and the schedule untouched; the keeper unaffected; allowed once vested; the finding's
  two-hotkey scenario), the favour campaign's standby hotkey and the stranger's claims of either (`claimsHeldWhileVesting`),
  its two-hotkey pin, and the re-entry pin's mid-schedule attacker claim replaced by the keeper's (the same restart).
- **I-38's precondition is the keeper's and the chain's (U2, Low).** Every listed hotkey with stake claimed in one round,
  rounds no further apart than `rewardVesting`; a refused or skipped hotkey's backlog vests over one period above its
  accrual, which only per-holder accounting removes; the owner stops new entrants for that period. No contract change
  (a gap-long period would let a dust claim stretch later ones); the keeper claims every listed hotkey each round,
  whatever it costs, at 29 days.
- **Operations under a root hold (Low, latent: the hold is 0 today).** Under a hold the
  guardian pauses the door's deposits and the founder funds the buffer directly (3.8, section 5's sudo row, the
  README's sudo row). Runbook 7.6 is the off-chain agent's.
- **The value-limit hold and the room rule (U4, Low; the founder reversed decision 6).** `canClose()` is true when no
  minimum deposit fits under `maxBatchValue` or `depositCap`; one address can no longer hold every batch just under a
  limit and cancel before each close (qualified by Revision 34: only where a close lands between its deposit and its
  cancel; Revision 35's cancel cooldown makes the keeper's next round land there). The old grief is gone (the pot
  rolls forward, `minValue`). Door 19,058 B (-44 B).
  Tests: `FrontDoorClose` (the flipped room-rule test, the 9.96 hold closed at once and committed, the cap-room case,
  an honest batch with room still waiting); the mutant (the old clause, no cap clause) fails three.
- **A block author's sandwich of a claim (U5, Info to Low).** The chain pays a claim at the same block's pool prices
  and `claimRewards` takes no minimum: a rogue Aura authority can take a few percent of some claims, never principal.
  Stated (section 5's new row); `minDelta` or a keeper forwarder is the founder's call, with U1's guard.
- **ForeverMoney's validator hotkey swap (U6, Low).** `canSendPayouts()` cannot see it, so door redeems burn into a
  queue that pays once ForeverMoney repoints (or the owner cancels); stated beside S-H1, I-36's S-H1 clause narrowed to
  the causes the vault reads, and the README's "Nothing users see" corrected. A fourth rail read was not taken.
- **The token cushion and the sweep (U7, Info).** The README said a surplus first refills the cushion; since R4 the
  sweep leaves the booked cushion, so only `fundCushion` refills it (README corrected; the code and 3.2 were right).
- **Tokens sent straight to the front door (U8, Info).** FM-TAO or USDC transferred to the door is credited to no one:
  stray USDC is lost, stray FM-TAO comes back only by an owner's 7-day activation of a contract that credits its
  sender. A README line; no sweep added.
- **Sizes.** The vault 22,937 B (22,655 at Revision 31; +282 B: A, D, C, the E-fix and U1; 139 B under the 23,076 B
  gate); the front door 19,058 B (19,102); the lens 8,867 B (8,825); L4 2,611 B (2,599). `LibraryLinks.sol`
  regenerated (the vault's link offsets).
- **Verified.** The forced parity suite at 55feabf: 115 suites, 1,487 passed, 0 failed, 49 skipped (114, 1,456 and 49
  at Revision 31). Build checks 7 of 7; the script tests 140 of 140; `forge fmt --check` clean. Seeds 9401 and 9402
  pass (310 tests each); seed 9403 found the favour campaign giving an attacker's retry no tolerance for another
  holder's mint rounding (1 wei; the same sequence fails on fd1442c, so the model's, not R6's): the retry now gets
  the per-entry dust another actor's retry gets, the sequence is a pin, and 9403 passes (311 tests). Every behaviour
  change was test-first, and each fix undone fails its tests (A 3, D 5, C 5, the E-fix 2, U1 2 and the favour
  campaign, U4 3). Delivery gas rose 355 a delivery (D's enabled read, the E-fix's queue read), a claim's overhead
  about 150 (U1), a door close up to about 2,400 (the room rule's reads); every gas pin holds.

## Revision 33 (2026-11-15, R7: the R6b review's Lows; the founder's half rule)

A fresh Fable review of R6 and R6b (0 Critical, High or Medium) found two Lows in the room rule, an Info in its sums
and a coverage gap in the favour campaign; the founder chose the half rule for the first two. One behaviour change in
`canClose`, one bound in `_setCaps`; the rest stated.

- **The half rule (3.5; the review's Low-1 and Low-3; the founder's choice).** Besides its interval and a full list,
  `canClose()` is true, while the open batch holds deposits, at (a) `2 × totalDeposits >= maxBatchValue`, (b)
  `totalDeposits + minDeposit > maxBatchValue` (R6b's room rule), (c) `netForwarded + 2 × totalDeposits >=
  depositCap` (no subtraction, so a cap lowered below `netForwarded` cannot underflow) or (d) `netForwarded +
  totalDeposits + minDeposit > depositCap` (R6b's cap room rule). Low-1: a holder kept the room at exactly
  `minDeposit` (9.96 of 10 TAO at launch), which the room rule left waiting while every larger honest deposit
  reverted `BatchValueFull`, and cancelled each 0.04 that landed; the same under the cap. In general a rule
  "closable when the room is below X" lets a holder keep the room at X, so the close is tied to the holder's own
  capital instead. Stated: a holder can still refuse deposits above about half a batch (or half the cap's room),
  and to stay under half it must cancel as much as each honest deposit adds, so honest deposits keep landing
  (qualified by Revision 34: over the line, only a close landing before the cancel binds the holder; Revision 35's
  cancel cooldown holds the cancel back five minutes). Low-3:
  with no deposits the cap clause read `netForwarded + minDeposit > depositCap`, true for good at a filled cap, at a
  cap of 0 and with a minimum above `maxBatchValue`, so anyone closed every redeem-only batch at once (each close
  drawing the pot) and rolled every empty one; a batch without deposits now waits for its interval or a full list.
  At launch a batch reaching 5 TAO is closable at once, so a heavy flow of deposits sends up to twice the batches;
  the keeper's and the monitor's copies of the rule are the off-chain agent's (v3.2-wire's `doorLimitsFull` already
  reads the four clauses). The checks are reordered (the interval first, then the lists, then the deposits), so a
  close by the timer reads neither `minDeposit` nor the limits.
- **The minimum deposit's ceiling (Appendix B; the review's Info).** `_setCaps`, which the constructor shares,
  refuses `minDeposit > MAX_BATCH_VALUE` (5,000 TAO) with `InvalidParameter`: no larger deposit could be sent, and
  `setCaps(type(uint256).max, ...)` made `totalDeposits + minDeposit` overflow, so every `closeBatch` panicked until
  the owner set it back. I-23 asserts the ceiling; the governance campaign probes one past it (`pastABound`, 35
  bounds) and draws `minDeposit` within it.
- **U1 in keeper-less mode (the review's Low-4; stated, no change).** With no keeper the permissionless claim
  allows one stranger claim per vesting period: the first restarts the schedule and every other hotkey waits for its
  end (`NotKeeper`). The rewards stay owed on chain, and `setKeeper` is the owner's and immediate (section 5's
  list; the README's claim fallback).
- **The favour campaign proves the vested claim (the review's coverage note).** Under U1 the `holderClaims` (399)
  and `strangerClaims` (350) minimums could be met by refusals alone. A new action, `claimAtVestEnd`, passes time
  to the claim schedule's end (when it is no later than the keeper's round due then, which follows at the same
  moment, so no claim gap overruns) and has the attacker or the stranger claim one hotkey first; the campaign's
  prediction requires it to succeed. New minimums: `outsideClaimsMade` (every claim the vault took from a
  non-keeper) 1,510 and `claimsAtVestEnd` 1,040, by the MinimumHits rule over the seeds 9401-9403 (a quarter of the
  slowest projection for 3,000 runs, 6,040 and 4,162); the older minimums are kept, each under a third of the new
  campaign's slowest projection. A model mutant that refuses a non-keeper's claim once any schedule existed fails
  the campaign now and passed it before (seed 9401). Two pins (`test_aClaimTheMomentTheScheduleHasVestedIsTaken`,
  the standby case with the round following).
  The keeper's gap was not stretched past the vesting period: that would break I-38's stated precondition.
- **A rate fall vests above the accrual, as a departure does (the favour campaign's model; seed 9402 of R7's
  calibration; the controller's ruling to confirm).** The owner's move of stake onto the standby, which accrues at
  half the rate, lowers the vault's accrual mid-schedule, while what is still to vest and still unclaimed was earned
  at the old rate, so for up to a period the schedule vests above the accrual and a holder through it takes its share
  of the difference: the shrunk sequence's attacker (24.9 of 44.9 TAO, entered after the month's rewards accrued)
  0.0027 TAO beyond its bound, the vest above the accrual 0.0049 TAO. It reproduces on the R6 campaign's actions alone
  (two warps in place of the claim at the vested end), so it predates R7; R6b's standby brought it, and R6's seeds
  had runs with unfunded vest (9 to 36 a seed) without crossing the bound. Not a contract fault: the vault vests what
  it claimed, and a fall in the reward rate (an owner's move to a weaker validator, the chain's own rates moving) is
  the fourth residual's shape (3.5) with a rate in place of stake; per-holder accounting is the cure (section 5).
  The model now credits the pool at each move, as at a departure: the rate the moved stake gives up x that stake x
  the vesting period, net of the cut (`movedCredit`), so the attacker's allowance takes its share of that vest above
  the accrual and nothing else; the sequence is a pin
  (`test_aMoveOntoTheLowerRateStandbyMidScheduleIsCreditedLikeADeparture`, both ways), and without the credit it
  fails on the bound. Over the seeds 9401 to 9405 the runs with unfunded vest fell to 0 to 2 a seed.
- **Pins that encoded the old rule, changed.** `FrontDoorClose`: `test_aBatchWithRoomStillWaitsForItsInterval`
  (room for exactly one minimum deposit, waiting) becomes `test_aBatchUnderHalfItsLimitsWaitsForItsInterval` (half
  less a rao, waiting); `test_closeBatchEarlyWhenFilledExactlyWithTheLeastMinimum` (3,999 of 4,000 TAO is now
  closable before the last deposit); `test_closeBatchAfterMaxBatchValueLoweredBelowTotal` (1,500 TAO under the
  4,000 limit, then lowered to 1,000; 3,000 TAO is now closable before the lowering). `FrontDoorBatches`: the
  constructor's bounds test constructs at a 5,000 TAO minimum (was `type(uint256).max`) and refuses one above.
  `GovernanceHandler`'s `setCaps` model refuses a minimum above the ceiling.
- **Sizes.** The front door 19,041 B (19,058 at Revision 32: -17 B, the reordered checks outweigh the two clauses and
  the ceiling); the vault (22,937 B, 139 B under the 23,076 B gate), both lenses (8,867 and 2,688 B) and every library
  unchanged.
- **Verified.** The forced parity suite at 9ecf726: 115 suites, 1,501 passed, 0 failed, 49 skipped (115, 1,488 and 49
  at Revision 32: the door's 10 new tests and the favour campaign's 3 pins). Build checks 7 of 7; the script tests
  140 of 140; `forge fmt --check` clean. Seeds 9401, 9402 and 9403 pass (314 tests each; 310, 310 and 311 at Revision
  32); the favour campaign alone also passes 9404 and 9405. Every behaviour change was test-first (11 door tests and
  the governance campaign failed on the unfixed code), and each fix undone fails its tests: the half value clause 4,
  the half cap clause 2, the deposits condition 4, the ceiling 2 and the governance campaign; the model's move
  credit, its pin, on the bound. Gas, per test against Revision 32: a close by the interval 96 to 2,231 cheaper (the
  interval is read first, so the deposits and the limits are not), an empty roll 13,077 and a full redeem list's
  close 7,149; a close by the room clauses 142 to 377 dearer (the half clauses' reads); deposit tests 17 to 65 (code
  placement: `depositFor` is unchanged); the vault's gas tests unchanged; every gas pin holds. Slither: one new
  result, `canClose`'s `total == 0` as a strict equality (a false positive: `open.totalDeposits` is the door's own
  accounting, which no transfer moves, and 0 exactly when the deposit list is empty); Aderyn: the same 5 High and 21
  Low kinds, nothing new in the front door.

## Revision 34 (2026-11-15, R8: the R7 review's L-1, L-2 and I-1; no contract change)

A fresh Fable review of R7 (0 Critical, High or Medium) found two Lows and five Infos, none needing a contract
change. One test and doc wording; `contracts/src` is Revision 33's (`git diff 1bad5ce -- contracts/src` is empty).

- **The hold between the keeper's rounds (3.5, section 5; the review's L-1, Low, availability only; not new in
  R7; closed by Revision 35's cancel cooldown).** The half rule binds a holder only through a close that lands while
  the batch is over the line, before the holder's cancel. The keeper runs once a minute, so a holder can deposit a full batch (10 TAO at launch) just after
  one round and cancel before the next: every honest deposit reverts `BatchValueFull` for most of each minute, at two
  Base transactions a minute to the holder, and the monitor's five-readings `batch.deposits_blocked` misses the
  flapping. A mistimed cancel leaves the holder's capital committed, which is no loss. Answered off-chain:
  `closeBatch` is permissionless, so the web offers the refused depositor "close this batch, then deposit" when a
  deposit does not fit and `canClose()` is true, and that close commits the holder; the monitor warns on heavy
  cancelling. The contract cure, a deposit fee that a cancel does not return (the contracts README's item 29), stays
  deferred (section 5's list). 3.5's "the keeper closes a closable batch at once and the holder's capital is
  committed" and its stated residual (a holder refuses only deposits above about half a batch) now carry the
  qualification, as do section 5's half-batch hold, Revisions 32 (U4) and 33 and the README (the close's step 3 and
  item 29). `canClose`'s NatSpec says the same as Revision 33 ("anyone's close (the keeper's) commits its deposits")
  and is left as it is: R8 changes no src.
- **An independent check of `canClose` (the review's L-2).** The campaigns' handlers predict `BatchNotReady` from
  `fd.canClose()` itself, so a wrong clause would pass every campaign. `FrontDoorClose.testFuzz_canCloseMatchesTheRule`
  recomputes the rule from 3.5 (the interval, the lists, a batch without deposits waiting, then clauses (a) to (d))
  on the state the test built: up to three deposits that stay and one cancelled in full (its entry removed),
  `netForwarded` from a real close of an earlier batch, `minDeposit`, `depositCap`, `maxBatchValue` (down from the
  5,000 TAO ceiling), the list limits and the clock, each drawn freely or set far from its edge, with one clause aimed
  at its edge (a wei below, at or above it; the interval a second either side). At four points (as deployed, with the
  limits set, with the list limits set, after the clock moves) the door holds the model's state and `canClose()`
  answers the rule without reverting; then a closable batch closes for the bridge fee (its deposits sent, or an empty
  batch rolled) and any other reverts `BatchNotReady`. Each of 16 mutants of `canClose`, applied one at a time to
  the clean source, fails it under each of the seeds 1, 2 and 3 (512 runs each): each of (a) to (d) dropped, and its
  comparison moved by one (`>=` to `>`, `>` to `>=`); `netForwarded` left out of (c), and of (d); the deposits
  condition dropped; the interval dropped or made strict; the lists dropped, or either made strict. The interval's
  two are mostly caught by the test's earlier close at exactly its interval (refused `BatchNotReady`), once at the
  clock's own edge; the rest by the rule's assertion. Unmutated it passes the same seeds and 20,000 runs (seed 8801).
- **Clauses (b) and (d) decide alone only after a raised minimum (3.5; the review's I-1).** Every entry is at least
  `minDeposit` when made (a partial cancel must leave the minimum), so while the deposits are at least the minimum
  (b) implies (a) and (d) implies (c): (b) or (d) decides alone only after `setCaps` raises `minDeposit` above a batch
  already holding deposits, and (a) and (c) cover normal operation. No change; the fuzz aims at that case.
- **Verified.** `contracts/src` unchanged (`git diff 1bad5ce -- contracts/src` empty before and after the
  mutations), so no forced suite or seeds were run: Revision 33's stand. `FrontDoorClose` and `FrontDoorBatches`
  pass, 74 of 74 (40 and 34: Revision 33's 73 and the new fuzz). Build checks 7 of 7 (the front door 19,041 B and
  the vault 22,937 B, unchanged); `forge fmt --check` clean. Logs: the workspace's `run/r8/`.

## Revision 35 (2026-11-16, R11: the cancel cooldown; the founder's choice)

ChatGPT's third-round review (claim 3) named the fill-and-cancel between the keeper's rounds, Revision 34's stated
residual. Both checks (the first check, and Fable's adversarial second check) ruled it a known residual, availability
only, and the cooldown cheap in bytes but not in process; both put it in the next door build. The founder chose to do
it now, before the self-funded deploy: one behaviour change in the front door, a constant, an error, one state
variable and a view.

- **The cooldown (3.5).** `CANCEL_COOLDOWN = 5 minutes` (a constant, Appendix B). `depositFor` (and `deposit`
  through it), after its checks, writes `lastDepositAt[user] = block.timestamp` for the entry's owner, not the payer
  (the staker calls `depositFor` for its buyer). `cancelDeposit` first computes the caller's `lastDepositAt +
  CANCEL_COOLDOWN` and, while `block.timestamp` is before it, reverts `CancelTooSoon(cancelableAt)` (declared at file
  level in `CascadeErrors.sol`; selector `CancelTooSoon(uint256)`), ahead of every other check, a zero amount
  included. `cancelableAt(user)` returns the same sum (`CANCEL_COOLDOWN` itself for a user that never deposited).
  `lastDepositAt` is `mapping(address => uint256)`, internal, declared after every earlier state variable (slot 33;
  no earlier slot moves; `DoorLayout.sol` regenerated). The door lens is unchanged: it serves CascadeBase's state and
  the keeper, and the front door keeps its own views. The error's parameter is `cancelableAt`, not `at`, which a later
  Solidity reserves (the warning gate: src compiles with no warning; the selector is the same either way).
- **Why (3.5, Revision 34).** A deposit was free to cancel until its batch closed, and the half rule makes a batch
  over half closable at once, so a close that landed first committed the holder; but the keeper closes once a minute,
  and a holder could deposit a full batch just after one round (every honest deposit refused `BatchValueFull`) and
  cancel before the next. Now its cancel waits five minutes, so the keeper's next round, or anyone's permissionless
  `closeBatch`, sees the full batch and commits it. One address cannot get round it: a top-up restarts its clock for
  the whole entry. With more addresses a lift over half can still be cancelled back within a round, but only out of
  deposits five minutes old, which were in the batch at a round at least five minutes earlier, under half: over any
  five minutes the cancels add up to less than half a batch (or half the cap's room), so the batch is near full for at
  most about one round in five, between under-half holds, instead of most of every minute (while each keeper close
  lands: Revision 36's keeper-voiding pin).
- **What is left (stated; the Revision 34 residual updated).** The between-rounds hold of a full batch is closed. The
  under-half hold remains (a holder just under half refuses only deposits above about half a batch, and to stay under
  it must cancel as much as each honest deposit adds); so does the honest closer's bridge fee for a deposits-only
  batch (the pot is fed by request fees only); and the deposit list's hold (R6, L-2) is not closed by the cooldown: a
  holder keeps the list one entry short at each round, fills it with a fresh entry after the round and cancels an aged
  one before the next. The contracts README's item 29, a deposit fee the cancel does not return, stays the cure for
  all three, deferred. (Revision 36 closes the list's hold with the list's half rule; the fee stays deferred for the
  other two.) The web's "close this batch, then deposit" and the monitor's cancel warning stay.
- **I-20, qualified.** A paused front door still refuses no exit call with `EnforcedPause`; a deposit's cancel waits
  up to five minutes after the user's last deposit, paused or not, and no deposit can restart the clock while paused
  (`depositFor` is `whenNotPaused`). Section 6's row says the same. (Revision 36: whole again, no cooldown while
  paused or with the primary disabled.)
- **The griefing note.** `depositFor(victim, amount)` restarts the victim's clock, so anyone can hold a victim's cancel
  back five more minutes at a time, each time gifting the victim the deposit (at least `minDeposit`: 0.04 TAO at
  launch, about 0.48 TAO an hour), bounded by the batch's room and the cap's room (Revision 36's correction: a gift
  tops up the victim's entry and needs no list slot; the contract's floor for a gift is `MIN_DEPOSIT_FLOOR`, 0.03 TAO,
  should `minDeposit` be lowered); the pause stops it, and the victim's cancel goes five minutes after the last one
  (at once since Revision 36). No loss: the gift is the victim's, and the batch carries it to the vault
  as the victim's deposit if it closes first.
- **Pins and tests.** New (each failed on the code without the cooldown: the declarations alone, no write and no
  check; run/r11/new-before.log): `FrontDoorBatches.test_aCancelWaitsFiveMinutesFromTheLastDeposit` (the exact
  `cancelableAt` in the revert, a second before, at it, before the amount's checks),
  `test_aTopUpRestartsTheCancelClock`, `test_depositForRestartsTheVictimsClock` (and the pause stopping the grief),
  `test_aPausedDoorTakesACancelOnceTheCooldownHasPassed`;
  `FrontDoorClose.test_aFullBatchCannotBeFlappedBetweenTheKeepersRounds` (a full batch at the launch's 10 TAO, its
  cancel refused at once and a second before the keeper's next round, which commits it) and
  `test_aLiftOverHalfCancelsOnlyAnOldDeposit` (the multi-address lift, once, then committed).
- **Pins changed (each existing cancel that follows its own deposit now waits out the cooldown first, or expects the
  refusal; no assertion weakened).** `FrontDoorBatches`: `test_cancelDepositPartialThenFullFreesSlot` (twice),
  `test_cancelDeposit_partialBelowMinimumReverts`, `test_cancelDeposit_partialToExactMinimumSucceeds`,
  `test_cancelDeposit_fullCancelAlwaysSucceeds`, `test_cancelDepositFreesSlotForOthers` (twice). `FrontDoorClose`:
  `test_emptyRollIgnoresMinValue`, `test_halfTheBatchValueIsTheEdge`, `test_halfTheRoomUnderTheCapIsTheEdge`,
  `test_theRoomClauseAtItsEdge`, `test_theCapRoomClauseAtItsEdge`,
  `test_aHoldJustUnderHalfTakesHonestDepositsAndTheFirstMakesItClosable` (the holder's deposit five minutes old, so
  the under-half hold it pins is the one left), and `testFuzz_canCloseMatchesTheRule` (its canceller cancels five
  minutes into the batch, far from the 12-hour interval; the clock draw before the interval starts from the time
  already passed, so it never runs back). `FrontDoorPayouts.test_balanceIdentityWithOpenDepositsAndDonation`,
  `FrontDoorPause.test_everyExitCallWorksPaused` (the paused cancel once the cooldown has passed).
  `EndToEnd.test_theDepositListCanBeHeldWithCancellableMinimums` in its R11 shape: 49 entries aged while the list is
  one short, the 50th fills it and is refused `CancelTooSoon`, an aged one frees the slot before the keeper's round,
  and the next round needs another aged entry. The test helper `waitCancel(user)` (FrontDoorTestBase) warps to the
  door's `cancelableAt`, which the new tests pin against the deposit's own time.
- **The campaigns.** FrontDoorTracker keeps each door's users' last deposit times from the handlers' own deposits that
  went through (a deposit, a `depositFor`, the staker's `depositFor` for its buyer), never from the door's view, and
  predicts each cancel's `CancelTooSoon` by its exact revert data; a predicted refusal that does not happen, or any
  other, is unexpected. `CoreHandler`, `DoorsHandler`, `FavorHandler` and the exits routine predict it. The routine's
  `_cancelOpen` swallowed every refusal before; it now counts any but the predicted one. The exits routine also makes
  a cancel refused within the cooldown again at its next round (its warps pass the five minutes): seed 9401 found a
  deposit left in the open batch under the scenario with the door's primary disabled, where no close can take it, so
  the cancel is the only way out and the table's row reads "from five minutes after its last deposit". The doors pin
  `test_aPausedDoorTakesEveryExitCall` sees the paused cancel refused `CancelTooSoon` first, then waits five minutes.
  New named states, reach only (minimum 0, logged, never asserted): `depositCancels` and `cancelsTooSoon` (core,
  doors, favour), `pausedCancelsTooSoon` (doors).
- **Mutants (each applied alone to the fixed source, then restored byte for byte; run/r11/mutants.log).** The door
  suites (`FrontDoorBatches`, `FrontDoorClose`, `FrontDoorPause`, `FrontDoorPayouts`) and `EndToEnd`, then the doors
  campaign on seed 9401. The cooldown check dropped: 7 tests fail (the six new and the list-hold pin), and the
  campaign's `invariant_noUnexpectedReverts` and its pause pin. The clock written for `msg.sender` instead of `user`:
  `test_depositForRestartsTheVictimsClock`, and the campaign's `invariant_noUnexpectedReverts` (its `depositFor`
  draws). The edge moved (`<=` for `<`, so a cancel at exactly `cancelableAt` is refused): 21 tests, and the
  campaign's pause pin (its warps are an hour or more, so its random runs never land on the second itself).
- **Sizes.** The front door 19,209 B (19,041 B at Revisions 33 and 34: +168 B, 3,867 B under the 23,076 B gate; the
  first check estimated 80 to 150 B); the vault 22,937 B (139 B under the gate), both lenses (8,867 and 2,688 B) and
  every library unchanged.
- **Gas.** Per call, with the door's and the token's storage cold (`vm.cool`, as in its own transaction), against the
  same source without the write, the read and the check: a user's first deposit 195,490 (+22,165), a top-up an hour
  later 57,200 (+5,065), a `depositFor` for a new user 140,336 (+22,166), a partial or a full cancel 43,836 and 68,389
  (+2,244 each). Per test against Revision 33's forced suite (its bytecode, Revision 34 changed comments only): tests
  whose users deposit rise about 22,100 to 22,600 per first-time depositor; a test that deploys a front door about
  33,700 (the 168 B); the rest within a few hundred (code placement; `AdapterGas`'s door delivery +264, its headroom
  pin holding; the ledger campaign's pins up to 236 less); the fuzz means move with their inputs. The vault's own gas
  tests (`Gas`'s, `AdapterGas`'s vault paths, `VaultClaimGas`) are unchanged but the size test (it deploys a door),
  campaign pins that deposit at a door rise as above, and every gas pin holds.
- **Verified (at 98051ee; logs: the workspace's `run/r11/`).** The forced parity suite: 115 suites, 1,508 passed, 0
  failed, 49 skipped (115, 1,502 and 49 at Revision 34: the six new tests). Build checks 7 of 7 (`DoorLayout.sol`
  regenerated: `lastDepositAt` at slot 33, after `minPoolLiquidity` at 32); the script tests 140 of 140; `forge fmt
  --check` clean. Seeds 9401, 9402 and 9403 pass, 314 tests each (as at Revision 33). Seed by seed, the core
  campaign's cancels landed 1,520, 1,734 and 1,437 times and were refused `CancelTooSoon` as predicted 1,253, 1,059
  and 1,475 times; the doors campaign's landed 220, 303 and 241 and were refused 217, 187 and 144 (16, 14 and 42 of
  them on a paused door); the favour campaign's landed 195, 183 and 124 and were refused 176, 236 and 154.
  The Base fork suites (`FORK=1`, the public Base RPC: `BaseFork`, `BaseForkV3`, `BaseForkV32`, `FrontdoorPentest`): 29
  of 29. Slither: 173 results (172 at Revision 33); the new one is `timestamp` on `cancelDeposit`'s `block.timestamp <
  ready`, by design (a five-minute clock; a Base block's timestamp can lean seconds, not minutes). Aderyn: the same 5
  High and 21 Low kinds, with the same instances per kind and file (lines shifted).
- **Off-chain, for the off-chain agent (not done here).** The web's hand-written door ABI (`web/src/lib/cascade/abi.ts`)
  and the keeper's, the monitor's and ops' door ABIs need `cancelableAt(address)`, `CANCEL_COOLDOWN()` and the error
  `CancelTooSoon(uint256 cancelableAt)` (decode by position: the parameter is not `at`); the web's cancel button waits
  for `cancelableAt`; the frozen build is rebuilt (the door's bytecode changed); runbook 7's "tell those depositors to
  cancel" waits five minutes after their last deposit.

## Revision 36 (2026-11-16, R12: the cooldown's bypass and the deposit list's half rule; the design check's ruling)

ChatGPT's third round, in its follow-up 3, answered Revision 35's cooldown: it fixes the fast-cancel race given a live
keeper, but blocks cancels where no close can land (the bridge paused, disabled or unable to send), weakens "cancel
works while paused", lets `depositFor` reset a victim's clock, and leaves the deposit list's hold; it preferred a
deposit fee, beneficiary consent and a maximum duration. The design check (Opus, adversarial, at 98051ee; Fable was
overloaded) and Fable's second check of the DoS triage ruled: ship a bypass for the pause and the disabled primary,
and a half rule for the deposit list; ship none of the rest. One behaviour change in the front door's view and one
clause in `canClose`; no storage, getter, event, error or layout change; nothing in the vault.

- **The bypass (3.5).** `cancelableAt(user)` returns 0 while `paused()` or `!bridgeEnabled[primaryBridge]` (the close's
  own condition: `_primary()` reverts `PrimaryBridgeDisabled` on it), else `lastDepositAt[user] + CANCEL_COOLDOWN`;
  it is `public` now, and `cancelDeposit` reads it (`ready = cancelableAt(msg.sender)`; `CancelTooSoon(ready)` while
  `block.timestamp < ready`, ahead of every other check, as before). `depositFor` is unchanged; its NatSpec says the
  pause stops the gifts and frees every cancel at once. While paused closes still land but no deposit can
  (`depositFor` is `whenNotPaused`), so there is no fresh deposit to hold; while the primary is disabled no close
  with entries can land, while deposits still do (a disable is not a pause), so the cooldown would only hold
  depositors, whose clocks a
  `depositFor` could restart for as long as the disable lasts. Either way the cancel goes at once and I-20 is whole
  again. An unpause, or the primary enabled again (its reopen, a scheduled `activateBridge`, or `setPrimaryBridge` to
  an enabled bridge), brings the cooldown back from the user's last deposit; another bridge's disable changes nothing.
  Not `exitsOpen()`: a scheduled `setGuardian` clears a guardian's hold while the primary stays disabled until its
  reopen, so no close can land yet and the cooldown must stay waived; the condition is the close's own
  (`FrontDoorBatches.test_theBypassFollowsThePrimaryNotTheHold`, which a bypass on `!exitsOpen()` fails).
- **No overdue-batch bypass.** The launch keeper holds a non-full batch up to 24 hours (until its fees cover the
  bridge fee), so a bypass for a batch past its interval would reopen the hold most of each day.
- **The deposit list's half rule (3.5).** `canClose` reads `2 * open.deposits.length >= maxDeposits ||
  open.redeems.length >= maxRedeems` (the redeem list stays at full: a full one closes itself on its pot). The proof:
  a keeper round that leaves the batch waiting found at most 24 of 50 entries (2 × 24 < 50), so reaching 50 takes at
  least 26 pushed since, each under a minute old and uncancellable for five; the next round finds at least 26 (2 × 26
  ≥ 50) and closes. `>=`, so an odd limit's half rounds up (25 of 49; 24 of 49 waits). The R6 list hold (R11: 49 aged
  entries, one cycled a round) ends: other depositors are refused `BatchFull` for at most a round, at 26 minimum
  deposits (1.04 TAO at launch) committed a round, which fills the 40 TAO cap in under 40 minutes. The cost: honest
  batches of 25 depositors or more close at the next round (it matters only under about 0.2 TAO a depositor, where the
  bridge fee per depositor is about 30 to 40% higher than in a full list); a forced close costs its forcer 25 entries
  (1 TAO) where it cost 2 TAO; and the keeper's daily Base cap (0.01 ETH) is spent by about 7 TAO of forced closes
  instead of about 10.
- **Not taken.** *The latch* (Fable's second check): a flag that keeps a batch closable once it was over the line
  makes forced closes 50 to 125 times cheaper: age 4.97 TAO for five minutes, add a fresh 0.04 TAO from a second
  address (the batch latches), cancel the 4.97, and a 0.04 TAO batch is closable at once, about ten an hour, three of
  which exhaust the keeper's Base cap; it also leaves a stale flag after an emptied batch and needs a new getter. *The
  amount lock* (a per-entry lock of the amount added within the window) must accumulate, so a gift inside the
  victim's own window still extends it: partial protection for more state and audit surface. *Beneficiary
  signatures* (EIP-712 consent for `depositFor`): the clock must stay on the entry's owner, since exempting
  third-party deposits is an escape (B's `depositFor(A, 5.02)` on A's aged 4.95, then A cancels all 9.97), and the
  staker's route is a third-party deposit; a signature changes the staker and the web for a grief that pays its
  victim. *A maximum duration no stranger can reset*: the bypass covers every state where no close can land, and
  elsewhere the batch's own close (24 hours at most) ends the grief. *The deposit fee* (the README's item 29):
  after the cooldown and both half rules no hold is free, and the fee is an interface change (a payable `depositFor`
  or a TAO skim; the staker, the web, the keeper's economics, I-18); it stays on section 5's list for the redeploy
  before other people's money.
- **What is left (stated).** (1) Liveness: the cooldown binds only through a close that lands within five minutes
  (the keeper's round, or anyone's `closeBatch`); an aged entry's one-rao cancel between the keeper's read and its
  close voids a close whose `minValue` is the total it read (`BatchTooSmall`), so the keeper names a lenient
  `minValue` on a batch it closes early, and the "about one round in five" bound holds while each keeper close lands
  (the new keeper-voiding pins, both half rules). That keeper is the one built from v3.2-wire (the off-chain fix):
  `canClose`'s NatSpec, which says the keeper names a lenient `minValue`, is true of it only. (2) The under-half
  holds: of the value, of the cap's room and of the list (24 entries of 50 wait); honest deposits keep landing. (3)
  The bounded flap: lifts cancelled back add up to under half a batch over any five rounds. (4) Gifts: a `depositFor` of at least `minDeposit` (0.04 TAO at launch;
  `MIN_DEPOSIT_FLOOR`, 0.03 TAO, the contract's floor should it be lowered) restarts a victim's clock, about 0.48 TAO
  an hour, all the victim's, bounded by the batch's and the cap's room (a top-up needs no list slot) and by the
  batch's close, and ended at once by the pause. (5) Forced closes: 25 entries (1 TAO) or 5 TAO of value each, all
  committed, and about 0.0016 ETH of pot or keeper cost a close (an interpolated figure), bounded by the 40 TAO cap;
  the pot pays a close's fee first, so the owner sweeps the carried pot regularly (`sweepPot`); the honest closer's
  bridge fee for a deposits-only batch stays, and the deposit fee is deferred. (6)
  One-shot escapes: a pause or a disable of the primary frees every fresh deposit's cancel at once, so a lift over the
  line made just before it escapes that close; only the owner or the guardian makes that moment, and the unpause or
  the restore brings the cooldown back. (7) No bypass for a close that fails outside the door (a bridge whose quote or
  send reverts while it stays enabled, an underfunded keeper): the pause is the answer, and it frees every cancel.
  (8) UX: `CancelTooSoon` comes before `InsufficientRequest`, so the web reads `depositOf` first, and `cancelableAt`
  reads 0 while paused or disabled.
- **I-20, whole again.** A paused front door refuses no exit call, a deposit's cancel at once (3.5, the invariant's
  text, section 6's row).
- **Appendix B.** With `batchInterval` at or under `CANCEL_COOLDOWN` every deposit is closable by the timer no later
  than it is cancellable, so cancels would in effect be gone; the launch's interval is 1 hour, and lowering it is a
  scheduled loosening. The batch limits' row says the deposit list closes at half.
- **Corrections to Revision 35.** Its griefing note said the gifts are bounded "by the batch's room and the list"; a
  gift tops up the victim's existing entry and needs no list slot, so the bounds are the batch's room and the cap's
  room. Its "about one round in five" holds only through keeper closes that land (above). Its list-hold residual and
  its "I-20, qualified" are marked closed here. Revision 32's L-2 is marked closed too.
- **Tests (new; each failed on b39a724, run/r12/new-before.log, new-before3.log and new-before4.log).** `FrontDoorBatches`:
  `test_aPausedDoorTakesACancelAtOnce` (replaces `test_aPausedDoorTakesACancelOnceTheCooldownHasPassed`: `cancelableAt`
  0 while paused, a partial cancel inside the five minutes at once, the unpause bringing back `CancelTooSoon` from the
  same deposit), `test_aDisabledPrimaryTakesACancelAtOnce` (another bridge's disable keeps the cooldown; the
  primary's frees it, a fresh deposit's cancel too; `setPrimaryBridge` to a standby and the primary's own reopen,
  executed in a fresh deposit's second, each bring `CancelTooSoon` back), and
  `test_theBypassFollowsThePrimaryNotTheHold` (the review of ae474d1: the guardian disables the primary, a scheduled
  `setGuardian` clears its hold with the primary still disabled, and `cancelableAt` still reads 0 and a cancel inside
  the five minutes goes; the reopen brings the cooldown back; a bypass on `!exitsOpen()` fails it). `FrontDoorClose`:
  `test_theDepositListsHalfIsTheEdge` (24 and 25 of 50, closed with 25 entries; 24 and 25 of 49; a cancel back to 24
  waits again, no latch), `test_anAgedOneRaoCancelVoidsAnExactMinValueOnAHalfFullList` (an aged entry's one-rao cancel
  after the keeper's read: an exact `minValue` reverts `BatchTooSmall`, `minValue` 0 commits all 25).
  `EndToEnd.test_theDepositListHoldEndsAtTheKeepersNextRound` (rewritten from
  `test_theDepositListCanBeHeldWithCancellableMinimums`: 49 aged entries are closed at the keeper's round; 24 aged at a
  round, 26 fresh after it, every aged one cancelled, the fresh ones refused `CancelTooSoon`, and the next round
  commits 26 to the vault; the honest deposit lands in the next batch).
- **Characterisation tests (unchanged behaviour, so they pass on b39a724 too; each caught by a mutant).**
  `FrontDoorClose.test_aRedeemListStillClosesOnlyWhenFull` (25 and 49 of 50 wait, 50 close; 48 and 49 of 49),
  `FrontDoorClose.test_anAgedOneRaoCancelVoidsAnExactMinValueButNotALenientOne` (the value half rule's keeper-voiding
  pattern at the launch's values: an aged 0.04 TAO plus a rao, a fresh fill to a rao under 10 TAO, the one-rao cancel;
  the exact `minValue` reverts `BatchTooSmall`, the fill is refused `CancelTooSoon`, `minValue` 0 commits it), and
  `EndToEnd.test_fiftyFakeRedeemRequestsPayTheirOwnClose` (an outside review's question: at the fixtures' 1e14 wei fee,
  the launch's until the founder's 0.0002 ETH below, fifty shareless addresses fill the redeem list, `canClose` is true, the 51st self-closes on the pot and lands in the
  next batch, the pot carries fifty fees less the bridge fee, and at the vault fifty `RedeemSkipped`, nothing burned,
  no `PayoutBatchQueued`, pending payouts as they were with a real payout queued).
- **Pins changed (no assertion weakened).** `FrontDoorBatches.test_depositBatchFullUnlessMerging` (its limit 2 to 3:
  one entry of 2 is now half the list; one of three waits, two close, three are full, the fourth refused `BatchFull`,
  a merge still lands); `FrontDoorBatches.test_depositForRestartsTheVictimsClock` (the pause frees the victim's
  cancel at once, inside the five minutes, where it waited them out); `FrontDoorPause.test_everyExitCallWorksPaused`
  (the paused cancel at once: no `waitCancel`, `cancelableAt` 0); `FrontDoorClose.testFuzz_canCloseMatchesTheRule`
  (its model's list clause is the half rule, and the deposit limit's draw reaches 7, so each of its up to three
  entries meets its half edge, 2n against 2n and 2n + 1); `Doors.invariants.test_aPausedDoorTakesEveryExitCall` (the
  paused cancel goes at once, counted `cancelsWaived`, no `CancelTooSoon`).
- **The campaigns.** `FrontDoorTracker._cancelTooSoon` predicts `CancelTooSoon` only when the handler's own record
  puts the cancel inside five minutes and the door holds the cooldown: unpaused (`paused()`) with its primary enabled,
  read from the door's storage at the generated layout's slots (`primaryBridge`, then its `bridgeEnabled` entry),
  never from `cancelableAt`. `_waivedNow` marks a cancel inside the five minutes that goes because the door waives the
  cooldown; `cancelsWaived` counts them (the doors campaign, which pauses its doors, and the exits routine, which pauses
  the door and disables its primary), and `cancelsWaivedPaused` and `cancelsWaivedPrimary` count each cause apart,
  so the exits campaigns show both reached. `DoorsHandler.pausedCancelsTooSoon` is gone (no paused cancel is refused now);
  the doors campaign logs and names `cancelsWaived` in its place, and both exits campaigns name it (reach only).
  `ExitHandler._usersExit` lost its unused argument. The minimums of the R11 and R12 named states are set by
  MinimumHits' rule (a quarter of the slowest of the three seeds' projections to 3,000 runs; 0 under 8), the lowest of
  this revision's derivations on the seeds 9401 to 9403 at 128 x 64 (the sweep at ae474d1, three exits-only runs for
  the waiver's two causes, and the final sweep at f6b306f; run/r12/final/minimums.txt, exits-split-minimums.txt,
  final2/minimums-recheck.txt: MinimumHits notes a total can come out short when campaigns run in parallel, and the
  totals vary from run to run): the core campaign's `depositCancels` 314 and `cancelsTooSoon` 265; the doors
  campaign's 117, 61 and `cancelsWaived` 5 (every waiver there is the pause's); the favour campaign's 105 and 129; the
  exits campaign's `cancelsTooSoon` 294, `cancelsWaived` 35, `cancelsWaivedPaused` 17 and `cancelsWaivedPrimary` 14,
  and at the launch's limits 231, 34, 16 and 16. Until now they were 0, so a handler change that stopped cancels
  landing passed a long run.
- **Mutants (each applied alone to the fixed source, then restored byte for byte; run/r12/mutants.log).** The door
  suites (`FrontDoorBatches`, `FrontDoorClose`, `FrontDoorPause`, `FrontDoorPayouts`) and `EndToEnd`; the bypass's
  mutants also against the doors campaign (pauses) and the exits campaign (pauses and disabled primaries), seed 9401.

  `2 * b.deposits >= l.maxDeposits`, the web's `isFull` and its units and the monitor's copy of the rule follow; the
  keeper names a lenient `minValue` (0, or the batch's fresh part) on a batch it closes early, so an aged one-rao
  cancel cannot void its round; the web's cancel button reads `cancelableAt` (0 while paused or disabled: cancel at
  once). No ABI change (`cancelableAt`'s selector is the same, now `public`); the frozen build is rebuilt (the door's
  bytecode changed).
- **The launch's request fee (the founder, 2026-11-16).** Appendix B's `redeemRequestFee` is 0.0002 ETH, where it was
  0.0001 ETH: fifty requests, a full redeem list, pay 0.01 ETH into the pot, about 2.3 times the bridge fee to close a
  full 50 + 50 batch (about 0.0043 ETH toward Bittensor), where 0.005 ETH paid about 1.2 times, a margin a rise in
  TAO/ETH or Bittensor's gas price would take away; a list filled with shareless requests then closes itself on its
  pot. A constructor argument, within `MAX_REDEEM_REQUEST_FEE` (0.001 ETH): no contract change, and the frozen build is
  unchanged (`DefaultParams`' 1e14 wei stays the fixtures').
